# Architecture

Code map for orienting quickly in the project. One section per system; keep headings stable and update the section that matches the code you change. Conventions and rules live in [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md); this file describes what exists.

All C# types are in the global namespace (no `namespace` declarations). Paths below are relative to this file.

## Overview

A Unity 6 (URP 2D) top-down action RPG prototype. The player boots into a main menu, starts a new game, and is placed in a seamless world, a grid of chunk scenes streamed around the players (Testlands, the hand-painted test world, at its `start` entry), where they walk in 8 directions and fight with a sword, a greatsword or a longbow (switched with a dev key for now). Weasel mobs idle, patrol around their spawn, spot the player with line-of-sight checks, path around walls on their world's navigation, crowd around the player without overlapping, and melee attack with a telegraphed windup a player can step out of. Damage shows floating numbers and a HUD health bar, and every hit flashes, holds and nudges the struck unit with an impact where it lands, kicking the camera of the player who landed or took it. Mobs target whichever player of their world they spot (nearest visible). A dead player respawns on its own after a delay at the nearest waystone it discovered in its world, whatever the other players do: nothing restarts when every player is dead. Offline and online play (host and join) run in seamless worlds, each player in its own world and travelling between worlds through exits (see Networking and Worlds and streaming). A dev-only stress world spawns hundreds of mobs on a procedurally generated map to profile AI and pathfinding.

## Packages

Notable entries in [Packages/manifest.json](../Packages/manifest.json):

| Package | Used for |
|---|---|
| `jp.hadashikick.vcontainer` (git, pinned to tag `1.19.0`) | Dependency injection, `LifetimeScope`s, entry points |
| `com.unity.inputsystem` | `InputSystem_Actions.inputactions`, `PlayerInputService`, `InputBindings` (rebinding), UI input module |
| `com.unity.render-pipelines.universal` | URP 2D renderer, `Light2D` |
| `com.unity.2d.tilemap` / `.extras` | Tilemaps for terrain, collision and navigation data; `RuleTile` base of the dual-grid walls tile |
| `com.unity.2d.aseprite`, `com.unity.2d.animation` | Sprite import and animation |
| `com.unity.ugui` + TextMesh Pro | Menu, HUD, damage popups |
| `com.unity.test-framework` | EditMode and PlayMode tests |
| `com.unity.netcode.gameobjects` 2.13.3 | Netcode for GameObjects: `NetworkManager`, host/client sessions, network prefab handlers |
| `com.unity.transport` 2.6.0 | `UnityTransport`. Pinned to the version Netcode declares: 2.7.x throws a Burst `NullReferenceException` from its `AnalyticsLayer` job every network update in this editor version. |
| `com.unity.multiplayer.playmode` 2.0.2 | Multiplayer Play Mode (up to 3 extra virtual players in the editor) |
| `com.unity.multiplayer.tools` 2.2.12 | Network Simulator (latency, jitter, packet loss) and network profiling. Simulation only runs in the editor and development builds. |
| `com.coplaydev.unity-mcp` (git) | UnityMCP editor automation |

## Assemblies

| Assembly | Folder | References | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TopDownRPG.Core` | `Assets/Scripts/Core` | VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, UnityEngine.UI (for the `EventSystem` pointer-over-UI check), Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.Networking.Transport | Boot, scene flow, input, clock, randomness, network session, camera follow, screen fader. Must not reference Gameplay. |
| `TopDownRPG.Gameplay` | `Assets/Scripts` (everything outside `Core` and `Dev`) | Core, VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras, Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.2D.Runtime (the spell lights' `Light2D`) | AI, navigation, combat, player, character animation, UI, effects, world (including the dual-grid walls and the blended terrain renderer), composition roots. |
| `TopDownRPG.DevTools` | `Assets/Scripts/Dev` | Gameplay, Core, VContainer, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras | `defineConstraints: UNITY_EDITOR \|\| DEVELOPMENT_BUILD`; not auto-referenced. Its editor-only code sits behind `#if UNITY_EDITOR`. |
| `TopDownRPG.Editor` | `Assets/Editor` | Core, Gameplay, VContainer, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Editor, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras.Editor, Unity.2D.Sprite.Editor (slicing the walls sheet), Unity.2D.Aseprite.Editor (reading hand-drawn characters', effects' and projectiles' files), Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.2D.Runtime (the stress scene's Global Light 2D), Unity.TextMeshPro (the UI's font assets and the theme's fonts) | Editor-only platform. Includes the dual-grid tooling, the terrain brush and material tools, the walls sheet generator, the character baker, the effect and weapon sprite recipes, the Aseprite character, effect and projectile recipes, the prop placement tool and the scene generator. |
| `TopDownRPG.Editor.Testing` | `Assets/Editor/Testing` | UnityEditor.TestRunner, UnityEngine.TestRunner | Editor-only, not auto-referenced: `ProjectTestRun`, the test runner for tools that drive the editor (see Tests). |
| `TopDownRPG.EditModeTests` | `Assets/Tests/Editor` | Gameplay, Core, Editor (for the prop snapping rule, the scene generator and the terrain tools), DevTools (for the stress map), VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.InputSystem.TestFramework, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Editor (for the grid brushes), Unity.2D.Aseprite.Editor (to read back the `.aseprite` files tests write), Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.2D.Runtime (for the 2D lights the light render tests make) | Editor-only test assembly. |
| `TopDownRPG.PlayModeTests` | `Assets/Tests/PlayMode` | Gameplay, Core, DevTools, VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.2D.Runtime (for a copy's cast light) | Needs DevTools for the stress-scene test. |

Dependency direction: `Core <- Gameplay <- DevTools`, with `Editor` and both test assemblies on top. Core and Gameplay each have an `AssemblyInfo.cs` ([Core](../Assets/Scripts/Core/AssemblyInfo.cs), [Gameplay](../Assets/Scripts/AssemblyInfo.cs)) granting `InternalsVisibleTo` to both test assemblies; `internal` members are test seams. Gameplay also grants it to `TopDownRPG.Editor`, whose dual-grid tools set `internal` members of the dual-grid types and whose terrain brush calls the terrain renderer's `internal` preview API, and to `TopDownRPG.DevTools`, whose lighting panel drives the lighting's `internal` dev seams (see Dev tools); Gameplay still references nothing of DevTools. Editor's own [AssemblyInfo.cs](../Assets/Editor/AssemblyInfo.cs) grants it to the EditMode tests.

Six regions, the six area generators' seed 1 layouts with their props, on an island in the sea, joined by stone and gravel roads through the wilds' grass and its forest; a waystone per region on a plaza at its spawn cell, `clearing` the default entry, and a gate on each linked port. | Made with the World Editor's steps (`WorldAuthoring.CreateWorld`, `CreateChunk` for every chunk, `ListInGameScenes`) and written through the World Generator's code path (`WorldGeneratorSession`) from `WorldGenerator_Overworld.asset`, then synced, baked, repaired and validated: every chunk 5 to 14 layers in the style's order, all eighteen materials laid, the Coast region's plateau of raised ground with its ramp, 48 spawn points of the Weasel under the chunk grids' `GeneratedMobSpawns` (28 in the regions, 20 in the wilds) with derived ids, 5102 props under the chunk grids' `GeneratedProps` (2117 the regions', 2985 the wilds'; PropGeneration.md's Phase 6), 14 baked coast maps beside the chunk scenes, a `ChunkSky` on the grids of the 16 chunks a region's sky reaches (Lighting.md's Phase 8), no walls; 19 entries, the six waystones then the 13 gates. Regenerate it from its generator asset only (WorldGeneration.md: Generating a world), locking a chunk there before painting it by hand. |
| [Dev/Worlds/StressTest/World_StressTest.unity](../Assets/Dev/Worlds/StressTest/World_StressTest.unity) and its four chunks under `Chunks/` | The stress world, a dev-only world of 2 x 2 chunks at origin (0, 0), the first free slot of the layout when it was made. Not listed in `GameScenes` and not in the build list: its `SceneDefinition`s lie under `Assets/Dev/`, which `SceneBuildValidator` skips and `WorldAuthoring` keeps out of the build. Played by opening one of its scenes and pressing Play. | Made with the World Editor's steps (`WorldAuthoring.CreateWorld` into `Assets/Dev/Worlds`, `CreateChunk`, `AddWalls`). Root scene: `WorldLifetimeScope`, whose auto-inject objects list `StressTest` (at the world's middle, (64.5, 64.5)) holding `StressTestSpawner` and `StressTestOverlay`, and the lighting. Each chunk's grid has an empty `GrassTilemap` layer, walls and a `StressTestMap`, which paints the chunk's part of the 128 x 128 map at runtime. Entry `start` (a waystone) at the world's middle. No mob spawn points: the spawner makes the mobs. |

Chunks get their walls from [Walls.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/World/Walls.prefab) (`WorldAuthoring.AddWalls`, the World Editor's Add walls): an empty `CollisionTilemap` on the `Obstacles` layer with its dual-grid walls module (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`) and grid colliders, and its `RenderTilemap` at order 10 on the `Terrain` sorting layer, saved from the area template as the areas went, and since Lighting.md's Phase 7 a Shadow Caster 2D on the `CollisionTilemap`, shaped by its grid colliders through URP's collider shape, untrimmed, on the ground's sorting layers alone since KnownGaps.md's Phase 7, so the walls stop the prop lights and units and props read the walls' shadows at their feet (`WorldAuthoring.AddWallShadows` gave the walls already in the worlds theirs, and brought them onto the ground's sorting layers) (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9). The Scene Generator writes into standalone scratch scenes (`WorldAuthoring.CreateScratchScene`, see Editor tools), never into a world's chunk.

Scene identity is data, not strings: [SceneDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/SceneDefinition.cs) assets under `Assets/Data/Scenes` (and beside each world's scenes under `Assets/Worlds`) hold a scene GUID + path, and [GameScenes](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/GameScenes.cs) (`Assets/Data/Scenes/GameScenes.asset`) lists MainMenu, Gameplay, the seamless worlds (`worlds`, `FindWorldOwning(scenePath)`: the listed world whose root or chunk scene that is; `FindWorldById`), and the starting world and entry (`StartingWorld`, `StartingWorldEntry`: the Overworld's `clearing`), where New Game and Host Game start and where a joining player the host does not know arrives. Its internal `CopyStartingAt(source, world, entry)` makes a copy of the asset starting elsewhere (a `GameScenesCopy`, every serialized field copied through `JsonUtility`, `DontSave`; a type of its own, since VContainer refuses a second singleton of the same concrete type), which `SceneBootTestHelper` registers into the Main scope for every PlayMode boot, so the tests start at Testlands' `start`; the asset itself is never written at runtime or by a test.

Composition roots find a scene's own objects with [SceneQuery](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/SceneQuery.cs) (`FindFirst<T>(scene)`, `FindAll<T>(scene)`, inactive objects included), which reads the scene's root objects into an array of its own on every call, so it keeps no state between calls.

### Load hierarchy

```
Main (MainLifetimeScope)
├── MainMenu (MenuLifetimeScope, parent = Main)            -- while in the menu
└── Gameplay (GameplayLifetimeScope, parent = Main)        -- while in a session
    └── World_* (WorldLifetimeScope, parent = Gameplay)    -- each world with a player in it
        └── World_*_x_y (ChunkLifetimeScope, parent = its world) -- the chunks streamed around the players
```

Every session runs in seamless worlds (see Worlds and streaming). The area path (one area scene at a time under Gameplay) went in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9.

### Boot sequence

1. Play (or a build) starts in `Main`. In the editor, [PlayModeBootstrapper](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/PlayModeBootstrapper.cs) forces `playModeStartScene = Main` (toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Boot From Main`) and stores the scenes that were open via [EditorBootRequest](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/EditorBootRequest.cs) (`SessionState`). Test Runner sessions are detected by their `InitTestScene*` scene and left alone: the runner raises its `RunStarted` callback only after play mode has started, too late to skip the start scene, which is still read after the play mode change, so the bootstrapper clears the start scene and the boot request for that session and applies them again on the return to Edit Mode. Play loads scenes from disk, so it saves the modified scenes first (cancelling Play when the user declines); without that, unsaved edits would silently be missing.
2. `MainLifetimeScope` builds; its entry point [BootFlow](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Boot/BootFlow.cs) (`IAsyncStartable`) runs.
3. Editor only: `BootFlow` consumes the boot request. If a world's root or chunk scene was open (a world `GameScenes` lists, else any `WorldDefinition` asset in the project, such as the dev stress world), it starts a new game there (`SessionSave.StartThrowaway`, then `GameFlow.StartWorldGameAsync(world)` at the world's default entry); if only Gameplay was open, a new game in the starting world at its entry; either is a throwaway session, logged as one, which never writes the save file (KnownGaps.md's Phase 2): only a game started from the main menu saves. If MainMenu was open, the menu. Any other scene is no world's: it warns and shows the menu.
4. Otherwise `GameFlow.ShowMainMenuAsync()`.
5. Every session from the menu runs in seamless worlds (see Save data). Menu "New game" -> `MainMenuController.StartNewGame()`: `SessionSave.StartNew()`, then `GameFlow.StartWorldGameAsync` offline in the starting world at its entry (`GameScenes.StartingWorld`, `StartingWorldEntry`: the Overworld's `clearing`). "Continue" loads the save into the `SessionSave` and starts offline with the local player at its saved world and position (`PlayerStarts`), moved on to walkable ground once the chunks there are up when the player can no longer stand there (`WalkablePlaces`, see Save data). "Host Game" starts hosting first, then continues the save the same way when there is one, or starts a new game. "Join Game" connects to the address in the field and then starts `GameFlow.StartJoinedGameAsync`, which waits for the host to place this player in a world (see Networking).

### GameFlow

[GameFlow](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/GameFlow.cs) (singleton in Main scope) owns all scene transitions. Uses [SceneLoader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/SceneLoader.cs) (additive load/unload, `Resources.UnloadUnusedAssets`) and [ScreenFader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/ScreenFader.cs).

- Every transition: reject if one is running -> `TransitionStarted` -> fade out -> work -> fade in -> `TransitionFinished`. Cancelled with `Application.exitCancellationToken`. A trip between worlds is a transition too (`IsTravelling`), so it never runs beside another one, either way round.
- `ShowMainMenuAsync`: leave any world session, unload gameplay and unused assets, load MainMenu under Main.
- `StartWorldGameAsync(world, position)` (and `StartWorldGameAsync(world, entryName)`, which starts at that entry's position, `WorldDefinition.EntryPosition`): unload menu, leave any world session, unload gameplay and unused assets, load Gameplay under Main (made the active scene), set `CurrentWorld`, then await the Gameplay scope's [IWorldSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/IWorldSession.cs) `EnterAsync(world, position)`, which completes once the ground around the players is loaded, before the fade in. Logs an error and enters nothing when the scope provides no `IWorldSession`. Leaving a world session first raises `WorldSessionEnding` (the session's last save), then (`LeaveAsync`, awaited before anything unloads) unloads its chunks and worlds one at a time.
- `StartJoinedGameAsync()`: a joined client's session. Like `StartWorldGameAsync` up to the Gameplay scene, then awaits the Gameplay scope's `IWorldSession.JoinAsync()`: the host spawns this machine's player in a world, which the client loads around it before the fade in, and that world becomes `CurrentWorld`. When the session ends first it warns and leaves the Gameplay scene without a world; `NetworkSessionLifecycle` then takes the game back to the menu. Leaving a world session also leaves one that is still joining (the world session is active though no world is recorded yet).
- `TravelAsync(world, entryName)`: the local player's trip to another world (WorldStreaming.md's Travelling between worlds), run by `LocalTripTraveller` once the trip's authority has approved it and put the player in that world. A transition flagged `IsTravelling`: fade out, set `CurrentWorld`, await the Gameplay scope's `IWorldSession.ArriveAsync(world, entryName)` (the local player moved to the entry, then the ground around it loaded), fade in. It loads and unloads nothing itself, and `TransitionTimeFreeze` leaves game time running. Logs an error outside a world session.
- `WorldSessionEnding` event: raised as a world session is about to end (the menu, a new session), with its players still in their worlds; `GameSaveRecorder` saves then.
- State: `IsTransitioning`, `IsTravelling` (the transition running is a trip), `IsInMenu`, `IsInGame` (Gameplay loaded and a world recorded), `CurrentWorld` (the local player's world: the one the session started in, then each one it travelled to), `GameplayScene`.

## Composition / DI

VContainer scopes live one per scene. Each Gameplay-assembly scope logs an error and registers nothing if it has no parent (i.e. the scene was opened without booting through Main).

### MainLifetimeScope ([MainLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Boot/MainLifetimeScope.cs), Core)

| Registration | Kind |
|---|---|
| `GameScenes` | instance (inspector) |
| `Camera` (main camera), `ScreenFader`, `CameraFollow2D` (+ as `ICameraKick`) | components (inspector) |
| `IClock` -> `UnityClock` | singleton |
| `IRandom` -> `new SystemRandom()` (time-seeded) | instance |
| `SceneLoader`, `GameFlow` (gets this scope as `LifetimeScope` parameter) | singletons |
| `UISelection` (with the inspector's `EventSystem`) | singleton |
| `RuntimeInputActions` (the inspector's `InputActionAsset` and `EventSystem`) | singleton |
| `PlayerInputService` as `IPlayerInput`, `IMenuInput` + self (gets the `RuntimeInputActions` copy and the scene's `EventSystem`; a missing `EventSystem` logs an error) | singleton |
| `GameplayInputGate` (+ self), `TransitionTimeFreeze` (with `ISessionStatus`, so it freezes offline only) | entry points |
| `TimeFreeze` (owns `Time.timeScale`, back to 1 on dispose) | singleton |
| `MenuStack` (+ self) | entry point |
| `BootFlow` | entry point |
| `NetworkSession` as `IGameAuthority`, `INetworkObjectSpawner`, `IClientReadiness`, `IChunkHandshake`, `IChunkHolds`, `ISessionStatus`, `IPlayerIdSource`, `INetworkClock`, `IHostLink` + self (gets the inspector's `NetworkManager` prefab and `NetworkSettings`, and `UserSettings` for the player id) | singleton; logs an error and is skipped if either is missing |
| `NetworkSessionLifecycle` | entry point |
| `ISaveFolder` -> `FileSaveFolder` at `Application.persistentDataPath/Saves` (`Saves.Player2` and so on for a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player, `InstanceFiles`), with the earlier builds' `save.json` beside it (a virtual player's own) | instance |
| `IWallClock` -> `SystemWallClock` | singleton |
| `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts` | singletons |
| `ISaveStore` -> `FileSaveStore` at `Application.persistentDataPath/settings.json` (a virtual player's own, as above), keyed `UserSettings.FileName` | instance |
| `UserSettings` (takes the keyed store) | singleton |
| `UiScaleSettings` (inspector, `Assets/Data/UI/UiScale.asset`; a missing one logs an error and the defaults from `UiScaleSettings.Create` stand in) | instance |
| `UiScale` (+ self) | entry point |
| `InputBindings` as `IInputBindingLabels` + self (gets the `RuntimeInputActions` copy and the inspector's `RebindableActionList`; a missing list logs an error) | entry point |

### MenuLifetimeScope ([MenuLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/MenuLifetimeScope.cs))

- `OptionsMenuView` via `RegisterComponentInHierarchy`, and `OptionsMenuController` as an entry point + self.
- `MainMenuController` via `RegisterComponentInHierarchy` (injected with `GameFlow`, `NetworkSession`, `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts`, `OptionsMenuController` and `UISelection`).
- Auto-inject objects: `MainMenuCanvas`, for its `UiPixelScale`.

### GameplayLifetimeScope ([GameplayLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/GameplayLifetimeScope.cs))

| Registration | Kind |
|---|---|
| `PlayerRegistry` as `IPlayerRegistry` + self | singleton |
| `PlayerControlSettings` (the player prefab's own asset, so there is one source of truth; a prefab without one logs an error and registers nothing) | instance |
| `LocalPlayerTracker`, `PointerTargetPicker`, `LocalPlayerCommandSource`, `PlayerNavigation`, `PlayerBinder`, `PlayerRespawner` | singletons |
| `PlayerSpawner` (with the inspector's player prefab and the Gameplay scene as parameters; places a joining player on a free entry slot with `GameplaySettings`) | singleton |
| `GameplayPlayers` | entry point (spawns players in `Start`, see Player) |
| `DamagePopupLayer` | component |
| `PointerFeedbackLayer` (inspector; a missing one logs an error and skips `PointerFeedbackPresenter`) | component |
| `GameplaySettings` | instance |
| `CombatSettings` (inspector; a missing one logs an error and registers nothing, like a missing player prefab) | instance |
| `HudSettings` (inspector; a missing one logs an error and registers nothing, like a missing player prefab; UiRevamp.md's Phase 1) | instance |
| `PlayerHudView`, `AbilityBarView`, `StatusBarView`, `NoticeView`, `OptionsMenuView`, `GameMenuView` | components in hierarchy |
| `CombatEvents`, `DamageService`, `HitService`, `AbilityService`, `AttackService` | singletons |
| `EffectPool` (with the Gameplay scene) | singleton (disposed with the scope) |
| `ProjectileVisualPool` (with the Gameplay scene) | singleton (disposed with the scope) |
| `ProjectileService` (+ self) | entry point (`ITickable`; disposed with the scope) |
| `StatusVisualPool` (with the Gameplay scene) | singleton (disposed with the scope) |
| `StatusEffectService` (+ self) | entry point (`ITickable`) |
| `PropFadeSettings` (inspector) and `PropFading` (+ self) | instance and entry point (`ITickable`); without the settings an error is logged and `PropFading` is registered without them, so the chunks' props still have it to go to and nothing fades |
| `HitFeedbackSettings` (inspector) and `HitFeedbackPresenter` (+ self) | instance and entry point; both skipped, with an error, when the settings are missing |
| `DamagePopupPresenter`, `GameplayEntryPoint`, `PlayerDeathHandler` (`ITickable`), `PlayerHudPresenter` (`ITickable`), `AbilityBarPresenter`, `StatusBarPresenter`, `NoticePresenter` (+ self, `ITickable`), `PointerFeedbackPresenter`, `OptionsMenuController` (+ self), `GameMenuController` (+ self) | entry points |
| `Worlds`, `PlayerWorlds`, `ChunkNetworkPrefabs` | singletons |
| `CharacterOutfits` (the players' composed outfits, PlayerCharacter.md's Phases 6 and 7; the spares kept and the sprites built a frame from `GameplaySettings`' Outfits) | singleton (disposed with the scope, destroying the outfits) |
| `NavigationPagePool` (`DefaultSlots`: 24 chunk pages, shared by every loaded world's `WorldNavigation`) | instance |
| `SharedTerrainArrays` (the texture arrays of each terrain style, shared by every chunk's `TerrainBlendRenderer`) | singleton (disposed with the scope, destroying the arrays) |
| `RegionSkies` (the region skies of the loaded chunks, Lighting.md's Phase 8) | singleton |
| `TripArrivals`, `WorldExits`, `LocalTripAuthority`, `NetworkTripAuthority` as `ITripAuthority` + self, `PlayerIds`, `WalkablePlaces`, `WaystoneNotices`, `KnownWaystones` | singletons (outside the settings check below: every player's `PlayerWorldSync` takes the trip authority, the notices and the known waystones, and the respawns go through the trip authority) |
| `TripProtection` as `IUnitProtection` + self | entry point (`ITickable`, its time limit; disposed with the scope): the travellers kept out of play, which `LocalTripAuthority`, every world's `WorldPlayers`, `HitService` and `StatusEffectService` take |
| `WaystoneDiscovery`, `WaystoneMarkers` (+ self) | entry points (see Worlds and streaming, UI) |
| `GameSaveRecorder` (+ self), `HostSessionSync` (+ self) | entry points (see Save data) |
| `WorldStreaming` as `IWorldSession` + self (with the inspector's `WorldStreamingSettings`, this scope as the `LifetimeScope` parent of every world, `GameplaySettings` for the entry slots, and `WalkablePlaces` and `PlayerIds` for saved places no longer walkable), `LocalWorldPresentation` (with the same settings) | entry points; both skipped, with an error, when the settings are missing, and so is every trip registration below |
| `LocalTripTraveller` (+ self) | entry point |
| `DayNightSettings` (inspector), `DayClock`, `DayClockSync` (+ self), `DayNightPresenter` (+ self, with `RegionSkies` and `LocalPlayerTracker`), `PropLighting` (+ self) and `JoinedSaveRecorder` (+ self) | instance, singleton and entry points; all but `PropLighting` and `JoinedSaveRecorder` skipped, with an error, when the settings are missing, and the worlds keep their authored light; those two are then registered without a day clock, so the chunks' lights still have it to go to, burning at full without flicker, and a client's copy of its place keeps the time zero |
| `LightWarmUp` (+ self) | entry point (LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): runs URP's 2D light and shadow code once as the session starts |

Auto-inject objects: `PlayerHudCanvas`, `DamagePopupCanvas` and `GameMenuCanvas`, for their `UiPixelScale` (DesignSystem.md's Phase 3) and, since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, the health text's `PixelTextScale`.

[GameplayEntryPoint](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/GameplayEntryPoint.cs): points `CameraFollow2D` at the local player and snaps whenever `LocalPlayerTracker` assigns one (on a client that happens when the host's spawn arrives); clears the target on dispose.

### WorldLifetimeScope and ChunkLifetimeScope ([WorldLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/WorldLifetimeScope.cs), [ChunkLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/ChunkLifetimeScope.cs))

- A world's root scene holds a `WorldLifetimeScope`, built under the Gameplay scope with the world's `WorldDefinition` enqueued by `WorldStreaming`; each chunk scene holds a `ChunkLifetimeScope`, built under its world's scope with a `ChunkIdentity` (world, chunk coordinates) enqueued. Both log an error without a parent scope and then register nothing; the chunk scope also logs one for every `MobController` placed in the chunk: mobs come from spawn points. Other scene objects needing injection go in a scope's `autoInjectGameObjects` (the stress world's root scene lists its `StressTest` object).
- `WorldLifetimeScope` registers the world's `WorldNavigation` (as itself and `INavigation2D`, with the Gameplay scope's `NavigationPagePool`), disposed with the scope, which frees its pages' slots. `WorldStreaming` resolves it into the world's `LoadedWorld`. It also registers the world's `WorldPlayers` (as itself and `IPlayerRegistry`, built by a factory from the Gameplay scope's registry, `PlayerWorlds` and the world's definition, since a constructor taking `IPlayerRegistry` would resolve itself), disposed with the scope: everything injected in the world's scope and its chunks' scopes, the mobs above all, sees only the players in that world. And it registers the world's `WorldState`, from the Main scope's `SessionSave` (`WorldOf(world id)`), which outlives the scope, so what its chunks record survives the world unloading.
- `ChunkLifetimeScope` registers, when the chunk has props (any `PropFade` in its scene), the `ChunkPropFading` entry point with them: in `Start` it hands them to the Gameplay scope's `PropFading`, and when the scope is disposed it takes them out again (see World, Props). It registers the `ChunkNavigation` entry point with the chunk's `NavigationGrid2D` (an error when the chunk has none, and then nothing below either): in `Start` it adds the chunk's page to its world's navigation, and when the scope is disposed (the chunk unloading) it takes it out. It also registers the `ChunkMobSpawner` entry point, resolvable as itself (with every `MobSpawnPoint` of the chunk and the chunk's scene; its `WorldState` and `SessionSave` come from the parent scopes), plus a build callback that calls its `RegisterNetworkPrefabs`. When the chunk has an `AreaTerrain` (every chunk of the worlds; `WorldAuthoring.SetUpTerrainEdits` gives one) it registers it as a component and the `ChunkTerrainSync` entry point, resolvable as itself (with the chunk's scene), plus a build callback that calls its `RegisterNetworkPrefab`; these come before the grid check. When the chunk has a `TerrainBlendRenderer` it registers that as a component too, so it is injected with the Gameplay scope's `SharedTerrainArrays` before it first builds (its `WorldChunk` keeps it disabled until the presentation shows it). When the chunk has prop lights (any `PropLight` in its scene), props' light shadow casters (`ChunkPropLights.CastersOf` its props, Lighting.md's Phase 7) or a renderer, it registers the `ChunkPropLights` entry point with them and the renderer (null without one): in `Start` it hands them, the casters, the renderer's ground glow and the chunk's world (its `ChunkIdentity`) to the Gameplay scope's `PropLighting`, and when the scope is disposed it takes them back (see Lighting). When the chunk has a `ChunkSky` (written by the world generator where a region's sky reaches it) it registers the `ChunkRegionSky` entry point with it: in `Start` it hands it, with the chunk's world and coordinates, to the Gameplay scope's `RegionSkies`, and when the scope is disposed it takes it back (see Lighting).

### How components get injected

- Plain classes: constructor injection (`GameFlow`, `BootFlow`, presenters, spawners, `WorldStreaming`).
- MonoBehaviours: `[Inject] public void Construct(...)`, reached via `InjectGameObject` build callbacks or `IObjectResolver.Instantiate`.

| Component | `Construct` parameters | Injected by |
|---|---|---|
| `PlayerWeaponController` | `AttackService`, `ProjectileService`, `IClock` | `PlayerSpawner` (`resolver.Instantiate`), or `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` on clients |
| `PlayerNetworkSync` | `PlayerBinder` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerWorldSync` | `PlayerWorlds`, `GameScenes`, `NetworkTripAuthority`, `IClientReadiness`, `WaystoneNotices` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerController` | `IClock` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerMotor2D` | `PlayerNavigation` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerAbilities` | `IClock`, `AbilityService` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `CharacterVisuals` | `IClock` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` (on the player's `Visuals` child) |
| `MainMenuController` | `GameFlow`, `NetworkSession`, `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts`, `OptionsMenuController`, `UISelection` | Menu scope |
| `MobController` | `INavigation2D`, `IPlayerRegistry`, `IRandom` | `ChunkMobSpawner` or `StressTestSpawner` (`resolver.Instantiate` through a chunk's or the stress world's scope), or `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` on clients (through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`). The navigation is its world's `WorldNavigation` and the players its world's `WorldPlayers` |
| `MeleeDamageDealer` | `IClock`, `AttackService` | same as `MobController` |
| `CharacterVisuals` (a mob's) | `IClock` | same as `MobController` (on the mob's `Visuals` child) |
| `DestroyMobOnDeath` | `EffectPool`, `IGameAuthority` | same as `MobController` |
| `NetworkHealth` | `DamageService` | player: same as `PlayerWeaponController`; mob: same as `MobController` |
| `NetworkStatusEffects` | `CombatSettings`, `StatusEffectService`, `IClientReadiness` | same as `NetworkHealth` |
| `StressTestSpawner` | `IObjectResolver`, `WorldNavigation`, `WorldDefinition`, `LocalPlayerTracker` | The stress world's `WorldLifetimeScope` `autoInjectGameObjects` |
| `AreaTerrain` | `IGameAuthority` | Chunk scope `RegisterComponent` |
| `TerrainBlendRenderer` | `SharedTerrainArrays` | Chunk scope `RegisterComponent`; in edit mode `EditModeTerrainArrays` hands one editor-wide set to the renderer of every world chunk opened (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5); elsewhere (tests that build renderers, scratch scenes) nothing does and it builds its own arrays |
| `NetworkAreaTerrain` | `AreaTerrain` | `ChunkTerrainSync` (`resolver.Instantiate`) on the host, or a client's chunk handler through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs` |

`MeleeDamageDealer`, `PlayerController`, `PlayerAbilities`, `PlayerWeaponController` and `CharacterVisuals` have no clock until one is injected, and each logs an error from `Start` when none was (`CharacterVisuals` then animates nothing); every scope that builds them registers an `IClock`, and tests construct them with a `ManualClock`. A `PlayerMotor2D` that was never injected has no navigation and moves in straight lines.

## Networking

Netcode for GameObjects, host-and-play. See [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md) for the plan and [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md#networking) for worlds online; this section describes what exists. Players, mobs, health and runtime terrain edits are replicated. The host runs all game logic (mob AI, damage, deaths, respawns, trips between worlds); clients move their own player and mirror the rest. Sessions run in seamless worlds: each player is in one world at a time, every machine knows each player's world, the host streams for every player and a client for itself, and what a client receives follows the chunks it holds.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [NetworkSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/NetworkSettings.asset`): default join address (`127.0.0.1`), host listen address (`0.0.0.0`), port (7777), client connect timeout (10 s), max players per session including the host (4, range 1–4). |
| [NetworkSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSession.cs) | Main-scope singleton that owns the `NetworkManager`: instantiates it from `Assets/Prefabs/Network/NetworkManager.prefab` (`NetworkManager` + `UnityTransport`, scene management off, no player prefab) and destroys it on dispose, so it never outlives the Main scope despite Netcode moving it to `DontDestroyOnLoad`. `StartHost()`, `JoinAsync(address, timeout, token)` (connects with this build's version and `LocalPlayerId`, the install's player id from `UserSettings`, waits for the connection, shuts down and returns false on failure/timeout), `Shutdown()`, `RegisterPrefab`/`UnregisterPrefab`, `JoinRefusal` (the host's reason when it refused the last join, else null), `IsActive`/`IsHost`/`IsServer`/`IsConnectedClient`/`LocalClientId`, event `ConnectionLost` (local client stopped without `Shutdown` being called). Makes its `NetworkManager` the Netcode singleton before starting, since Netcode falls back to the singleton when spawning. Implements `IGameAuthority` (`IsAuthoritative` = server, or offline when not following a host: a client whose connection dropped stays non-authoritative until `Shutdown()`, which `NetworkSessionLifecycle` calls once it is back in the menu, so a stranded client never saves a session or runs the game on its own) and [IHostLink](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IHostLink.cs) (`IsFollowingHost`: joined, connected or not; `IsHostLost`: following a host whose connection dropped without this machine leaving, until `Shutdown()`), which a client's copy of its place reads (`JoinedSaveRecorder`, see Save data). Player ids: the host keys every approved client by the id in its join payload (`PlayerIdOf(clientId)`; the host's own is `LocalPlayerId`); a client without one, or with one already in the session (a copied `settings.json`; Multiplayer Play Mode's virtual players have files of their own, `InstanceFiles`), gets a new id for the session, with a warning. Readiness (`ClientChunkReadiness`, below, with this session as its `IClientVisibility`), over named messages on `MessageDelivery` (`ReliableFragmentedSequenced`, the channel Netcode creates and destroys objects on, so an acknowledgement arrives after the hides before it): a client's `NotifyJoined()` (`ClientJoinedMessage`) once its Gameplay scope is up; host `JoinedClients`, `IsClientJoined`, event `ClientJoined`; a client's `ReportChunkReady(trip, chunk)` (`ChunkReadyMessage`) and `ReleaseChunkAsync(chunk)` (`ChunkReleasingMessage`, then waits for `ChunkReleasedMessage` or the session's end); host `IsClientReady(clientId)` (settled in its current trip), `IsClientReady(clientId, chunk)` (reported during its current trip), `BeginTrip(clientId, trip, worldId)`, `IsChunkHeld(chunk)`, all true for the host's own client where they ask about one; `Readiness` (internal, for tests). Spawning, all destroyed with their scene: `SpawnPlayerObject(instance, owner)` and `Spawn(instance)` are visible to every joined client; `Spawn(instance, homeChunk)` to the clients that hold that chunk, whichever trip they reported it in, and, when the prefab's handler here is a `NetworkPrefabInstanceHandlerWithData<NetworkChunkId>` (the chunks register theirs on the host too), it sets the chunk as the instantiation data. `ShowNowVisible`/`HideNoLongerVisible` walk the spawned objects with a visibility check and `NetworkShow`/`NetworkHide` the ones whose check changed for that client. `Despawn(instance)` (host) despawns and destroys one object, destroying it when it is not spawned. `RegisterPrefab(prefab, handler)` installs a handler of the caller's own (`ChunkNetworkPrefabs`' routers), with an error when the prefab already has one. `HasPlayerObject(clientId)`. Also implements `INetworkObjectSpawner`, `IChunkHandshake`, [ISessionStatus](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/ISessionStatus.cs) (`IsActive`, `IsHost`) for the in-game menu, the trips and the time freeze, and [IPlayerIdSource](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IPlayerIdSource.cs) (`LocalPlayerId`, `PlayerIdOf`) for `PlayerIds`. A client stopping while the application quits or the editor leaves Play Mode is not reported as `ConnectionLost`. |
| [JoinApproval](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/JoinApproval.cs) | Static admission rule, free of Netcode types. `CreatePayload(version, playerId)` is the connection data a client sends (its `Application.version`, a line break, its player id; line breaks inside either are dropped) and `ReadPayload` reads both back (a payload with only a version has no player id); `Evaluate(payload, hostVersion, playerCount, maxPlayers)` returns null to approve, or a reason: another or missing version first, then a full party. `EncodeRefusal`/`TryDecodeRefusal` tag the reason so a client can tell it from Netcode's own disconnect text. |
| [ClientChunkReadiness](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/ClientChunkReadiness.cs) | Plain C#, the host's per-client readiness and the release handshake (WorldStreaming.md's Networking). `Join(client)` (shows it what it may see: the players), `Leave`, `Clear`, `JoinedClients`, `IsJoined`; `BeginTrip(client, trip, worldId)` (a newer trip only: reports tagged with an earlier one are ignored from then on, and the client is unsettled until it reports a chunk for the trip; the chunks it holds in other worlds are released at once, what it may no longer see hidden from it, while those of the trip's world stay held but are ready for the trip only once reported during it; KnownGaps.md's Phase 4), `TripOf`; `ReportReady(client, trip, chunk)` (ignored before joining, for another trip or no chunk; else the chunk is held and ready for the trip, the client settled and, for a chunk it did not hold, shown what it may now see); `Release(client, chunk)` (forgets the chunk, hides what the client may no longer see, then acknowledges, held or not); `Holds(client, chunk)` (held, whichever trip reported it: what it may see), `IsReady(client, chunk)` (reported during its current trip), `IsSettled(client)`, `IsHeld(chunk)` (by any client). Showing, hiding and acknowledging go through an [IClientVisibility](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IClientVisibility.cs), so `ClientChunkReadinessTests` runs it without Netcode. |
| [ChunkId](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/ChunkId.cs), [NetworkChunkId](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkChunkId.cs) | A chunk as every machine names it: its world's `WorldId` and its coordinates (`Of(world, coords)`, `IsValid`, value equality), and its wire form (`INetworkSerializable`, the id a `FixedString64Bytes`), which the chunk messages and chunk objects' instantiation data carry. |
| [IChunkHolds](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IChunkHolds.cs), [IChunkHandshake](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IChunkHandshake.cs) | The handshake as the world streaming sees it: `IsChunkHeld(chunk)` (host: some client holds it, so the streamer keeps it), `ReportChunkReady(trip, chunk)` and `ReleaseChunkAsync(chunk)` (client only; nothing offline or on the host). |
| [PlayerWorldState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/PlayerWorldState.cs) | A player's world as `PlayerWorldSync` replicates it: `WorldId` (empty for none), `Trip` (its trip count) and `Entry` (where its latest trip leads), fixed strings and value equality. |
| [NetworkWaystone](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkWaystone.cs) | A waystone a client's player discovered as `PlayerWorldSync` replicates it to its owner (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3): its world's place in `GameScenes.Worlds` and its entry's in that world's entries, the same on every machine of one build, two ints so the list's element is unmanaged without the Gameplay assembly seeing fixed strings; `TryFrom(scenes, worldId, entryName)`, `TryResolve(scenes, out worldId, out entryName)` (false for a world or entry the build lacks), value equality. |
| [INetworkObjectSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/INetworkObjectSpawner.cs) | `IsActive`, `IsServer`, `RegisterPrefab` (with a resolver and a target scene, or with a handler of one's own, which `NetworkSession` installs and refuses with an error when the prefab already has one, since Netcode keeps one handler per prefab), `UnregisterPrefab`, `Spawn` (visible to joined clients) and `Spawn(instance, homeChunk)` (visible to the clients holding that chunk), `Despawn` (host: despawns and destroys; destroys an object that is not spawned): the slice of `NetworkSession` chunk code needs, so EditMode tests can fake it. |
| [IGameAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IGameAuthority.cs) | `IsAuthoritative`: whether this machine decides game state. Implemented by `NetworkSession`; tests use a fixed fake. |
| [INetworkClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/INetworkClock.cs) | `ServerTime`: the host's network time in seconds, which every machine of a session reads alike (a client's estimate of it), zero outside a session. Implemented by `NetworkSession` (`NetworkManager.ServerTime`); the day clock counts a client's time of day on with it. |
| [IClientReadiness](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IClientReadiness.cs) | `IsClientReady(clientId)`: whether a client reported a chunk ready since its player's latest trip began (the host's own client always has); `IsClientReady(clientId, chunk)`: whether it reported that chunk ready during its player's current trip (a respawn waits for it); `BeginTrip(clientId, trip, worldId)` (host: the trip's world, so the client's holds of every other world are released). Implemented by `NetworkSession`; `NetworkStatusEffects` holds a client's player until it is ready, and `PlayerWorldSync` begins its trips. |
| [InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler.cs) | `INetworkPrefabInstanceHandler` that creates network prefab instances through an `IObjectResolver` so their `[Inject]` methods run, moves them into a target scene when one is given (players into Gameplay, a chunk's objects into their home chunk through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`), and destroys them on despawn. Netcode only calls it on clients; the host must create its instances with `resolver.Instantiate` itself before spawning. |
| [NetworkSessionLifecycle](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSessionLifecycle.cs) | Main-scope entry point: shuts the session down whenever a transition ends in the menu, and returns to the menu (with a warning) when the connection is lost anywhere but the idle menu. A loss during a transition (a client still joining, mid-trip, or loading) is remembered and acted on when that transition finishes, since `GameFlow` ignores a second transition. A loss in the idle menu just ends the session. |

- `Assets/DefaultNetworkPrefabs.asset` is Netcode's auto-maintained network prefab list, referenced by the NetworkManager prefab: the player, the Weasel, the stress-test mob and `AreaTerrainSync` (the prefab of `NetworkAreaTerrain`).
- Runtime terrain edits, per chunk: only the host (or an offline game) changes a chunk's editable tilemaps, through its `AreaTerrain.TrySetCells`. Each changed cell is kept in a per-chunk list (last write per cell wins) that the host's `NetworkAreaTerrain` of that chunk mirrors into a `NetworkList<TerrainEdit>`; a client applies the whole list when the object reaches it (once it holds the chunk, so a client arriving there catches up as a late joiner does) and each change after that. Every machine's navigation follows the tile changes on its own. A client moves its own player, so between an edit and its arrival that player can still walk where the host just built a wall.
- Scene management is disabled: each machine loads its own worlds and chunks (`WorldStreaming`), so nothing networked is placed in a scene and nothing networked moves between scenes (players live in Gameplay, a chunk's objects in their home chunk).
- Netcode sets `Application.runInBackground` while a `NetworkManager` exists.
- Connection approval is on in the NetworkManager prefab (`NetworkSession` logs an error if it is off). The host approves itself, and approves a client through `JoinApproval` against `Application.version` and `NetworkSettings.MaxPlayers`, counting connected clients plus clients approved this frame but not yet connected. A refused client gets the reason as its disconnect reason; `JoinAsync` exposes it as `JoinRefusal` and the menu shows it. Approval is part of Netcode's config hash, so every build that should connect must have it on. Bump the Version in Player Settings whenever a change breaks compatibility between builds.
- Join order on a client: connect (version and player id), load Gameplay (its scope registers the player prefab in a build callback) and send joined (`GameplayPlayers.Start`); the host spawns its player where `PlayerStarts` places it (its saved place by player id, else the starting world's entry), once its own streaming has loaded that world and the chunks around the place (`IWorldSession.PrepareAsync`, which returns the place moved on to walkable ground when the player can no longer stand there, `WalkablePlaces`: the host alone decides, so the client, a late joiner included, gets the corrected place with its player's spawn and never corrects it itself), with its world set, and shows it every player, whatever their world; the client's `PlayerWorldSync` puts its player in that world, `WorldStreaming` loads the world and the chunks around it, registering each chunk's mob and terrain sync prefabs (`ChunkNetworkPrefabs`), and reports each chunk ready once it is up; only then does the host show it that chunk's mobs and terrain edits, and stop holding its player. As the client's joined message arrives the host also tells it the id of its session (`HostSessionSync`, a named message the client takes from the host only), under which the client keeps a copy of its own place (see Save data). A late joiner goes the same way.
- Walking and trips: a client releases each chunk before unloading it (`WorldStreaming`), and the host hides that chunk's objects from it before acknowledging; the host never unloads a chunk a client holds (its streamer's `IChunkHolds`), except when the session ends. A trip ends what the client held before it (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4): as the host begins a client's trip it releases the client's chunks of every other world, hiding their objects from it, and keeps those of the trip's world held, their objects shown, but ready for the trip only once the client reports them during it. A client's trip is an RPC naming the exit (`PlayerWorldSync.RequestTripRpc`, with the client's own position); the host checks it (`LocalTripAuthority`), streams the entry, and puts the player in the target world for its next trip, which reaches the client as its player's networked world: the client fades, moves its own player there (a teleport snaps on the other machines), reports the new world's chunks for that trip and fades in, then tells the host so (`PlayerWorldSync.TripArrivedRpc`, naming the trip), which ends the protection the host gave the player for it (`TripProtection`: from the approval on, no mob sees it, no hit lands and its statuses stand still). Nobody else fades. No session ever restarts when every player is dead: each player respawns on its own, and a respawn is a trip within the player's own world to its waystone, decided by the host and run by the player's own machine like any trip; the host restores the player's health once it sees it there with the waystone's chunk reported by its client during that trip (see Player), as its own respawn waits for its own streaming to have the chunk loaded. A discovery of a waystone by a client's player reaches its owner as `PlayerWorldSync.WaystoneDiscoveredRpc`, for its popup. The protocol gained that RPC in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9, so the build version went from 1.3 to 1.4. World content is part of the build too, a host and its clients loading the same chunk scenes: WorldGeneration.md's Phase 5 made the Overworld the starting world and took the version to 1.5. Animation.md's Phase 2 changed what a player's replicated move means (the actual speed over the base move speed, where it was a share of the slowed speed) and took it to 1.6. Its Phase 3 replaced a player's swing message (`AttackRpc`) with an attack's start, contact and early end (`PlayerNetworkSync`), and took it to 1.7. Its Phase 4 added a player's equipped weapon (a network variable), the weapon to the attack and cast messages and the target to the contact, and took it to 1.8. Its Phase 6 added the hit's source unit to `NetworkHealth.HitRpc`, and took it to 1.9. Its Phase 5 added the cast cancel (`PlayerNetworkSync.CastCancelledRpc`), and took it to 1.10. Its Phase 7 replaced a mob's attack message (`MobNetworkSync.AttackRpc`) with an attack's start, contact and early end, and its moving flag with its move (its actual velocity over its move speed), and took it to 1.11. PropGeneration.md's Phase 6 wrote the props into the Overworld's chunks, moving its regions' mobs and some of their late terrain, and took it to 1.12. Lighting.md's Phase 3 added the host's day clock message (`DayClockSync`) and took it to 1.13, its Phase 5 rewrote the Overworld for its light props and took it to 1.14, and the Sandbox's lighting showcase took it to 1.15. On 2026-11-01 it became the pre-alpha `0.1.0-prealpha`: the game is pre-alpha, real multiplayer tests with another client wait for the first playable vertical slice, and until then the version changes only when the user asks (CLAUDE.md), so it no longer follows protocol or world changes (Known gaps): KnownGaps.md's Phase 2 added the trip's fade-in report (`TripArrivedRpc`) and a player's saved weapon (a server network variable on `PlayerNetworkSync`), its Phase 3 a client's discoveries (a network list on `PlayerWorldSync`), and its Phase 4 the point a hit came from (`NetworkHealth.HitRpc`) and a cast's completion to every machine but its owner (`PlayerNetworkSync.CastCompletedRpc`, which went to the host alone), and its Phase 5 the host's session id sent to each joining client (`HostSessionSync`, a named message), and PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5 a player's colours (an owner network variable on `PlayerNetworkSync`), each with the version unchanged.
- Unity Transport timeouts on the NetworkManager prefab: heartbeat 500 ms, disconnect 10 s (down from the 30 s default). The disconnect timeout is how long a client waits before giving up on a host that vanished without a clean disconnect (a crash, or it dropping off the virtual LAN), and the same for the host about a client. 10 s still rides out short Wi-Fi or Tailscale hiccups; a heartbeat counts as traffic, so an idle but live connection never times out.
- The NetworkManager prefab carries a `NetworkSimulator` set to the `None` preset. It drives every transport in the process, so only one should be enabled (the in-process test clients remove theirs). Unity Transport's simulator stage, which it drives, delays and drops what a driver sends and what it receives alike, so with two drivers in one process (the in-process tests) each way goes through it twice: a 150 ms preset gives a round trip of about 600 ms. The PlayMode tests delay their connections with `NetworkDelay` instead (see Tests), which applies each driver's stage to what it sends only.

### Transform smoothing

Networked transforms use `LegacyLerp` position interpolation with unreliable deltas and the default buffer (`NetworkTransform.InterpolationBufferTickOffset` 0, tick rate 30). This was chosen by measurement, not by the package docs' suggestion: the explicit benchmark `NetworkSmoothnessPlayModeTests.Benchmark_RemotePlayerSmoothness_UnderStress` moves an in-process client's player for 2 s at a time under 150 ms latency, 20 ms jitter and 4% loss and records, per rendered frame of the host's copy, how far the speed strays from the owner's, the longest freeze and visible backward jumps. Results on this machine (6 runs per setting, run in both orders):

| Setting | Start lag | Mean speed error | Runs with a freeze over 150 ms |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas, offset 0 or 1 | ~390 ms | 22–26% | 0–2 of 6 |
| `Lerp`, unreliable deltas, offset 1 or 2 | ~380 ms | 57–63% | 0–2 of 6 |
| `SmoothDampening`, unreliable deltas | ~350 ms | ~100% | up to 3 of 6 |
| Any type with reliable deltas | 450–550 ms | 60–150%, with backward jumps for `Lerp`/`SmoothDampening` | frequent |

Unreliable deltas are the biggest win (a lost update is replaced by the next one instead of resent behind it). Rerun the benchmark by name after changing tick rate, speeds, movement code or transform settings.

Rerun after click-to-move replaced WASD (the client's player now walks on far-away move orders, with the mobs despawned): `LegacyLerp` unreliable offset 0 gave 385 ms start lag, 21% mean speed error and no stall over 150 ms in 6 runs; offset 1 26%; `Lerp` 59–67%; `SmoothDampening` ~106%; reliable deltas 76% with stalls in 5 of 6 runs. The ranking and the shipped setting are unchanged. The 3-run regression test is noisier than the 6-run benchmark (38% in the same session).

These runs set the Network Simulator's preset, which in one process applies on both drivers, sending and receiving (see Networking): each way had about 300 ms of delay, up to 40 ms of jitter and about 8% loss, a 609 ms round trip as KnownGaps.md's Phase 4 measured it, rather than the 150 ms the preset names. The numbers stand as measured under those conditions; newer tests delay with `NetworkDelay`.

## Save data

A save file per session in a folder, written by the machine that decides game state (offline or the host), holding the world sessions (WorldStreaming.md's Persistence, Phase 8; KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), and on a joined client a copy of its own place per host session, which it plays on alone once that host is gone. See [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#phase-6-services-and-persistence) for the decisions. Per-machine preferences live in a separate settings file (see Settings below).

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [ISaveStore](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/ISaveStore.cs) | `TryRead`/`Write` of one file's text, so save logic is tested without the disk, and `KeepCopy(out copy)`, a copy of what is stored, readable or not, under a name of its own before something replaces it. |
| [FileSaveStore](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/FileSaveStore.cs) | Writes `<path>.tmp`, then swaps it in (`File.Replace`, or a move for the first save) so a crash never leaves half a save; logs IO failures as errors and returns false. `KeepCopy` copies the file to `<path>.bak` (`.bak2`, `.bak3` and on when those exist). |
| [ISaveFolder](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/ISaveFolder.cs), [FileSaveFolder](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/FileSaveFolder.cs) | The save files, one per session: `Names()` (the `.json` files of the folder, sorted; not the `.bak` copies or `.tmp` files), `Open(name)` (a file's `ISaveStore`, a `FileSaveStore` in the folder, made at its first write), `Legacy` (the single `save.json` of earlier builds, beside the folder) and `RetireLegacy(out moved)` (renames it `save.json.imported`, numbered after the first, once imported). The Main scope's is `Saves` in the persistent data folder (`Saves.Player2` for a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player, `InstanceFiles`). |
| [GameSaveData](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/GameSaveData.cs) | Serialized form (`JsonUtility`), version 4 (`CurrentVersion`; `IsReadable` takes only it): `id` (the session's), `savedAt` (when it was written, UTC in the round-trip format, `Stamp`, `SavedAtUtc`), `host` and `hostGone` (set on a joined client's copy of its place, `SetHost(hostSessionId, gone)`, `IsCopy`; `SetSession(id)` makes it a session's), `time` (the world clock, game seconds played on the session, which the time of day follows, see Lighting), `players` (per player id: `worldId`, `position`, `waystones` (the waystones it discovered, world id and entry name each; the world's default entry counts as discovered without one), `weapon` (the id of the catalog weapon it holds, its asset's GUID, `WeaponCatalog.IdOf`; empty for none, the starting weapon)) and `worlds` (per world id visited: its `chunks`, each with `coords`, `deadMobs` (spawn point id and `diedAt` on the world clock) and `terrainEdits` (the layer named by what it draws, `AreaTerrain.KeyOf`: its material's id or `walls`; cell; filled)). Nested records `Player`, `Waystone`, `World`, `Chunk`, `MobDeath`, `CellEdit`. `TryParse` returns false on malformed JSON. `IsImportable`: versions 2 and 3, the single save file of earlier builds. |
| [GameSave](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/GameSave.cs) | Main-scope singleton over the `ISaveFolder` and an `IWallClock` ([SystemWallClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/SystemWallClock.cs) in play). A session's file is `session-<id>.json`, a joined client's copy of its place `joined-<host session id>.json` (`FileNameOf`, `CopyNameOf`). `TryLoadLatest(out data)`: of the readable files, the sessions and the copies whose host is gone, the one saved last by its `savedAt` (a tie to the name that sorts last); a copy whose host may still be there is never loaded, being used only while joined to it. `HasSave` (whether there is one), `TryLoadCopy(hostSessionId, out copy)`, `Write(data)` (stamped with the time, into its file; refuses data naming no session or host). A malformed file or a format version this build does not read is ignored with a warning (once a run), and kept (`ISaveStore.KeepCopy`, with a warning naming the copy) before anything writes over it; when the copy cannot be made nothing is written. Once a run, before anything else, the earlier builds' `save.json` is imported: a version 2 or 3 file becomes a session of its own (a new id), its players, waystones, weapons and dead mobs kept and its terrain edits, which named layers by index, dropped with a warning (`Import`), and is renamed; any other version is ignored with its warning and left alone. Writing a copy whose host is gone leaves the menu a notice (`TakeHostGoneNotice`). |
| [SessionSave](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/SessionSave.cs) | Main-scope singleton: the running session's saved state in memory, used offline and on the host. `Id`, the session's, which names its file and which a host tells its clients; a new one from the moment it is made, so a session started without the menu (a test hosting from code) saves into a file of its own too. `IsThrowaway`, set by `StartThrowaway()` (an editor boot straight into a world: a new game whose state nothing writes to a file) and cleared by `StartNew()` and `StartFrom`. `StartNew()` (New Game and a joining client, which writes no session of its own; a new id) or `StartFrom(data)` (Continue, Host Game with a save; the save's id, or a new one for a joined client's copy, which goes on as a session of its own, the copy kept). The world clock `Time`: the saved time plus the game time (`IClock`) since the session started, so a death time from one session means the same in the next. Places by player id (`TryGetPlace`, `SetPlace`) and, apart from them, the waystones each player id discovered (`Discover(playerId, worldId, entry)`, once each, true when new; `WaystonesOf`), and the id of the weapon each holds (`SetWeapon(playerId, weapon)`, an empty id forgetting it; `WeaponOf`, empty for none), which a new place keeps and `ToData` writes into each player's record (a player with discoveries or a weapon but no place yet is left out; every player in a world has its place recorded before each save), the `WorldState` of every world visited (`WorldOf(worldId)`, made empty on first visit; `TryGetWorld`), `ToData()` (under its id). It outlives the Gameplay scope. |
| [WorldState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/WorldState.cs), [ChunkState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/ChunkState.cs) | What persists of a world: a `ChunkState` per chunk coordinates (`Chunk(coords)`, made on first use; `TryGetChunk`; empty ones are not saved), `ToData`/`FromData`. A chunk's record: killed mobs by spawn point persistent id (`RecordMobDeath(id, time)`, `TryGetMobDeath`, `ForgetMobDeath`, `StaysDead(id, now, respawns, delay)`, which forgets a death whose delay has passed so the mob spawns again; the world's `RecordMobDeath(coords, id, time)` records it in the home chunk's record and drops the same id from any other, and `TryFindMobDeath(id, home, out holder)` finds it in any chunk's, home first, since regenerating a world can move a spawn point with its id to another chunk) and terrain edits, the last write per layer (named by what it draws) and cell kept in place (`RecordTerrainEdit`, `TerrainEdits`). |
| [PlayerStarts](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/PlayerStarts.cs) | Main-scope singleton: where a player starts. `TryGetStart(playerId, out world, out position)`: its saved place in the `SessionSave` when its world is listed (`GameScenes.FindWorldById`) and the position lies on one of that world's chunks (`WorldDefinition.HasChunkAt`), else, with a warning for a place gone, `GameScenes.StartingWorld` at `StartingWorldEntry` (an error and false without a starting world). The menu asks it for the local player, the host for each joining one. A place on its world's chunks that the player can no longer stand on, or cannot leave, is moved later, once the chunks around it are up (`WalkablePlaces`). |
| [WalkablePlaces](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/WalkablePlaces.cs) | Gameplay-scope check of the place a player starts at (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 5, KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), run by `WorldStreaming` offline and on the host only, once the chunks around the place are up: `EnterAsync` for the local player's start (an offline Continue, the host's own place), `PrepareAsync` before the host spawns a joining player. `TryMove(world, place, playerId, out moved)`: a place on a cell its world's navigation lets the players' movement profile (`PlayerControlSettings`) stand on and leave stays; one on a cell it does not (the ground there changed since it was saved: water, a wall, the face of raised ground, or ground cut off from the world's entries) moves to the centre of the nearest such cell by Manhattan distance within `GameplaySettings.SavedPlaceSearchCells` (16), in rings as `INavigation2D.TryGetNearestWalkableCell` searches, or else to the player's nearest discovered waystone of that world (`WaystoneRules.TryFindNearest`, the default entry counting), with one warning naming the world, the old and the new place (world-local) and whether the old one was unwalkable or cut off; a world with no entry keeps it, with a warning. A cell is one a player can leave (`IsReachable`, internal) when its region, joined across the loaded chunks, holds one of the world's entries (its default, waystones and gates) or runs on into a chunk of the world not loaded (`INavigation2D.RunsBeyondLoadedPages`); a region doing neither is a pocket (an islet in a lake, a plateau with no ramp, a walled yard), whose cells are skipped, each pocket checked once. A pocket larger than the loaded chunks cannot be told from the rest of the world and counts as reachable; in a world with no entry every walkable cell does. A place whose chunk has no navigation page loaded is kept. The rule itself is `Find` (internal, static, over any `INavigation2D`, for `WalkablePlacesTests`). The corrected place is the player's place from then on, so the save keeps it at its next write. |
| [GameSaveRecorder](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/GameSaveRecorder.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`). Writes the `SessionSave` through `GameSave`, into the session's own file, only while `IGameAuthority.IsAuthoritative`, a world session runs (`GameFlow.CurrentWorld`) and the session is no throwaway (`SessionSave.IsThrowaway`: an editor boot straight into a world writes nothing): once a frame at most after any player enters a world (`PlayerWorlds.Changed`: the session's start, a trip, a player joining), every `GameplaySettings.AutosaveIntervalSeconds` (60) of game time, and at once on `GameFlow.WorldSessionEnding` (the menu, the host leaving, a new game) and `Application.quitting` (in the editor, leaving Play Mode). `Save()` first records each registered player's place under its player id (`PlayerIds`): where it stands, or the entry of its trip while `TripArrivals` holds one it has not reached, since a traveller is put in the target world before it moves (`TryGetPlace`, internal), or, for a dead player on no trip, the waystone it will respawn at (`TryGetRespawnPlace`, internal: `WaystoneRules.TryFindNearest`), so leaving or quitting while dead neither skips the respawn nor brings it back beside its killer; nothing when it stands on none of its world's chunks. With it the id of the catalog weapon the player holds (`WeaponCatalog.IdOf`), as this machine sees it (a client's player's copy follows its owner's switches). A player leaving the registry (a client disconnecting) has its place and weapon recorded as it goes, for its next join. |
| [HostSessionSync](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/HostSessionSync.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IInitializable`), in a session only: the host sends its `SessionSave.Id` to each client as it joins (`NetworkSession.ClientJoined`, a late joiner included; the named message `TopDownRPG.HostSession` on `MessageDelivery`), and a client keeps the id it hears from the host, any other sender ignored (`HostSessionId`, null before). `Send` and `Listen` are internal statics, which `InProcessHost` sends with. |
| [JoinedSaveRecorder](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/JoinedSaveRecorder.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`), on a joined client that knows its host's session (`HostSessionSync`) only: writes the client's copy of its own place in that session through `GameSave` (`joined-<host session id>.json`): its own player's record alone (its world, place, the waystones it knows it discovered, `KnownWaystones`, and its weapon) and the host's world clock (`DayClock.WorldTime`, the time of day; zero without a day clock), no world's state, which is the host's. It keeps its player, and the world and trip entry `PlayerWorlds` last gave it, itself, since the tracker and `PlayerWorlds` forget them before it hears the player leave. Its place is where its player stands, the entry of the trip it is on while `GameFlow.IsTravelling`, or, while dead, the waystone it will respawn at (`TryGetPlace`, internal; `GameSaveRecorder.TryGetRespawnPlace`). It writes when its player enters a world, every autosave interval while the host is there, on `GameFlow.WorldSessionEnding` and on `Application.quitting`, the copy saying whether the host is gone (`IHostLink.IsHostLost`): its player's last record is taken as the player leaves the registry, since Netcode destroys a client's copies as its connection drops, and the copy written as the session then ends (`NetworkSessionLifecycle` returning to the menu) says the host is gone; a client whose player never stood in a world this session rewrites an earlier copy for that host, if any, only to say so. Joining that host session again writes it with the host there. |
| [InstanceFiles](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/InstanceFiles.cs) | Static: `NameFor(fileName)`, the save folder's and the settings file's names of this instance. In the editor a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player (launched with `-name Player2` to `-name Player4`, per the package's FAQ) gets its own (`Saves.Player2`, `settings.Player2.json`), so it keeps its own player id, key bindings and saves, as a separate install would; the main editor and builds keep the plain names. |

Worlds are saved by world id (their asset GUID), never by path, and terrain edits name their layer by its material's id (`TerrainMaterial.MaterialId`, the asset's GUID, kept by the editor as a weapon's is), never by its index, so a chunk's layers can be reordered or pruned between builds. Each session has a file of its own: New Game starts one (written at its first save), Continue loads the latest (a session, or a joined client's copy of its place whose host is gone, which goes on as a new session of its own), and Host Game continues the latest or starts one; the menu shows no slot list, and old sessions are kept. Booting the editor straight into a world (or the Gameplay scene) starts a throwaway new game there: its state lives in the `SessionSave` like any session's, but nothing writes it, so the save files stay as the last game started from the main menu left them. A joined client writes nothing but its copy of its own place in the host's session, which it uses only once that host is gone: losing the connection while joined (the host left, stopped, crashed or timed out) returns it to the menu, whose status line says the host is gone and that Continue carries on alone from where it was, Continue selected; leaving by choice leaves the copy saying the host is there, so Continue does not take it. A killed mob comes back after its config's `RespawnDelaySeconds` of world-clock time, and only as its chunk loads again: chunks load outside every player's view (the interest rectangle is the view plus the load margin), or behind a fade (a session's start, a trip, a join), so a mob never appears in front of a player. Multiplayer Play Mode virtual players share the main editor's data folder; `InstanceFiles` gives each its own files.

### Settings

The local machine's preferences, in `settings.json` beside the save folder (a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player's own `settings.Player2.json` and so on, see `InstanceFiles`). Every machine reads and writes its own, clients included; nothing is replicated. Starting a new game never touches it. Specified in [KeyBindings.md](KeyBindings.md#saving).

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [UserSettingsData](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettingsData.cs) | Serialized form (`JsonUtility`): `version` (`CurrentVersion` 1), `inputBindingOverrides` (the Input System's override JSON as a string), `playerId` (the install's player id), `characterLook` (the name of the look the local player is drawn with, AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 2), `characterColours` (a [UserColourPick](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettingsData.cs) per colour choice picked, the choice's and the swatch's names, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5) and `characterParts` (a [UserPartPick](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettingsData.cs) per part slot picked, the slot's and the option's names, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6) `uiSizeStep` (the UI size step above Normal, DesignSystem.md's Phase 3), `reduceFlashing` and `reduceMotion` (the display preferences, DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), and `allyPopups` (how allies' popups show, an `AllyPopups` value: `Shown`, `Smaller` or `Hidden`, UiRevamp.md's Phase 3; a value this build does not know reads as `Shown`); a file from before any of them reads with an empty one (the step at Normal, the preferences off, allies' popups shown), so the format version stayed 1. `TryParse` returns false on malformed JSON. |
| [UserSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettings.cs) | Reads the file once when created, through the `ISaveStore` registered under the `UserSettings.FileName` key (so tests swap it as they swap the save folder); a malformed file or an older format version is ignored with a warning and replaced on the next change, while a file from a newer build is ignored with a warning and never overwritten (`IsReadOnly`: changes stay in memory for the session). `InputBindingOverrides`, and `SetInputBindingOverrides`, which writes the file unless it is read-only and returns whether it did. `CharacterLook` (empty for the player's own) and `SetCharacterLook(name)`, which on a change writes the file (unless read-only) and raises `CharacterLookChanged`. `CharacterColour(choice)` (the swatch's name, empty for the choice's own) and `SetCharacterColour(choice, swatch)` (an empty swatch forgets the pick), which on a change writes the file (unless read-only) and raises `CharacterColoursChanged`. `CharacterPart(slot)` (the option's name, empty for the slot's first) and `SetCharacterPart(slot, part)` (an empty name forgets the pick), which on a change writes the file (unless read-only) and raises `CharacterPartsChanged`. `UiSizeStep` (0, Normal, by default) and `SetUiSizeStep(step)` (never below 0), which on a change writes the file (unless read-only) and raises `UiSizeStepChanged`. `ReduceFlashing` and `ReduceMotion` (off by default; DesignSystem.md's Phase 6) and `SetReduceFlashing(on)` and `SetReduceMotion(on)`, which on a change write the file (unless read-only) and raise `ReduceFlashingChanged` and `ReduceMotionChanged`; they change only what this machine shows (Effects, Lighting, Damage popups). `AllyPopups` (`Shown` by default; UiRevamp.md's Phase 3) and `SetAllyPopups(value)`, which on a change writes the file (unless read-only) and raises `AllyPopupsChanged`; only this machine's `DamagePopupPresenter` reads it. `PlayerId`: this install's player id (WorldStreaming.md's Player identity), a GUID made the first time it is read and written to the file with the other settings (kept for the session only when the file is read-only). |

## Input

Click-to-move, specified in [PlayerControls.md](PlayerControls.md). Mouse only for gameplay; the gamepad still works in menus. Every gameplay control but the pointer can be rebound ([KeyBindings.md](KeyBindings.md)); these are the defaults.

| Input | Action (`Player` map) |
|---|---|
| Right mouse button | `MoveClick`: pressed gives an order at the cursor, held re-evaluates it |
| A | `AttackMove`: an attack move to the cursor's point at once (no confirm click) |
| Pointer position | `Point` (`<Pointer>/position`, pass-through) |
| S | `Stop` |
| Q W E R, 1 2 | `Ability1`…`Ability6`: cast the ability in that slot toward the cursor (quick cast) |

- [IPlayerInput](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IPlayerInput.cs): `GameplayEnabled`, `PointerScreenPosition`, `IsPointerOverUI`, `MovePressedThisFrame`, `MoveHeld`, `AttackMovePressedThisFrame`, `StopPressedThisFrame`, `WasAbilityPressedThisFrame(slot)`. The local device seam; gameplay reaches it only through `LocalPlayerCommandSource` (see Player). [AbilitySlots](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/AbilitySlots.cs)`.Count` is 6.
- [PlayerInputService](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/PlayerInputService.cs): wraps the `Player` action map of [InputSystem_Actions.inputactions](../Assets/InputSystem_Actions.inputactions) (action names are public constants; `AbilityActionName(slot)`). Returns neutral input while the map is disabled. `IsPointerOverUI` asks the injected Main-scope `EventSystem` (`IsPointerOverGameObject`, so only graphics with `raycastTarget` block clicks: the ability bar's slot frames, and `ScreenFader` while visible; every other HUD graphic has it off). Logs an error if the map or any action is missing. The `Player` map holds only `MoveClick`, `AttackMove`, `Point`, `Stop` and `Ability1`…`Ability6`, one `Keyboard&Mouse` binding each. A `Menus` map holds `Menu` (Escape, gamepad Start) for the in-game menu; `SetMenusEnabled`/`MenusEnabled` (the gate drives it).
- [GameplayInputGate](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/GameplayInputGate.cs): Main-scope entry point (+ self) that enables the `Player` map only when `GameFlow.IsInGame && !IsTransitioning` and nothing holds a block (`Block(owner)`/`Unblock(owner)`, `IsBlocked`; the in-game menu blocks it while open), and the `Menus` map whenever in game outside transitions; listens to `TransitionStarted`/`TransitionFinished`.
- [RuntimeInputActions](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/RuntimeInputActions.cs): Main-scope singleton holding this session's `Instantiate` copy of the project input asset (ids kept), which `PlayerInputService`, `InputBindings` and the `EventSystem`'s `InputSystemUIInputModule` use (it disables the module, points it at the copy and enables it again, so the module enables the copy's UI actions). The project asset is never changed at runtime; the copy is destroyed on dispose.
- [InputBindings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/InputBindings.cs): Main-scope entry point that owns the binding overrides on the session's copy, for the actions listed in [RebindableActionList](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/RebindableActionList.cs) (`Assets/Data/Input/RebindableActions.asset`: `MoveClick`, `AttackMove`, `Stop` and `Ability1`…`Ability6`, each with the name and section the options screen shows; actions are matched by id, so a copy of the asset works too). On `Initialize` it loads the overrides from `UserSettings`, drops any on other actions or on forbidden inputs and both sides of a duplicate (with one warning, then rewrites the file); malformed overrides are ignored with a warning, and two actions sharing a default input are logged as one error. `TryAssign(action, path, out swappedWith)` binds any key but Escape, or a mouse button (left, right, middle, back, forward), swapping with the rebindable action that had the input, removing the override when the path is the default, saving and raising `BindingsChanged` once. `Reset(action, out swappedWith)` assigns the default the same way; `ResetAll`. `GetShortLabel` (the layout's key name, or LMB, RMB, MMB, M4, M5), `GetAbilityKeyLabel(slot)` (the same for an ability slot's action, found once at construction) and `GetLabel` (mouse buttons spelled out); `GetDisplayName` and `GetSection` from the list.
