A keyboard layout change raises `BindingsChanged`. `StartRebind(action, finished)` listens for the next key or allowed mouse button (`PerformInteractiveRebinding` restricted to `<Keyboard>` and the five mouse buttons, any-key excluded, Escape cancelling) and routes it through `TryAssign`; `finished` gets `RebindOutcome.Assigned` with the swapped action, or `Cancelled` (also for a refused input). One rebind at a time, never on an enabled action (logged as an error); `CancelRebind`, `IsRebinding`. `Dispose` cancels a running rebind. It only ever changes the session's `RuntimeInputActions` copy.
- [IInputBindingLabels](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IInputBindingLabels.cs): `BindingsChanged` and `GetAbilityKeyLabel(slot)`, the seam the HUD uses.
- [IMenuInput](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IMenuInput.cs): `BackPressedThisFrame`, the `UI` map's Cancel (Escape, gamepad East) while that map is enabled, and `MenuPressedThisFrame`, the `Menus` map's Menu (Escape, gamepad Start) while that map is enabled; `PlayerInputService` implements it and logs the missing action with the others.
- UI input uses the `UI` map through the `EventSystem` in Main.

## Clock

- [IClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/IClock.cs): `float Time`. Used for cooldowns and gameplay timers.
- [UnityClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/UnityClock.cs): `Time.time`; the Main scope registers one as the `IClock` singleton, and the in-process test client registers its own.
- Clock consumers: `MeleeDamageDealer` (a mob's attack timeline and cooldown, `AttackState`), `PlayerWeaponController` (the attack timeline and cooldown, `AttackState`), `PlayerController` (order timing: hold re-evaluation, chase repaths, cast times, the cast buffer window), `PlayerAbilities` (cooldowns), `ProjectileService` (projectiles' flight, by the clock's delta each frame), `CharacterVisuals` (the player's and the mobs' character animation, which so holds still while game time is frozen and runs on a `ManualClock` in tests). Mob AI timers are driven by the `dt` passed to `MobController.TickStateMachine`, which tests control. Purely visual, per-client animations (pooled effects (`EffectPlayer`), damage popups, the hit flash and nudge (`HitFeedback`), the camera kick) advance with `Time.deltaTime` on purpose; a character's hit-stop holds on its animator's game clock. `StatusEffectService` ticks statuses with the clock's delta each frame.
- [TimeFreeze](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/TimeFreeze.cs) (Main-scope singleton): the only writer of `Time.timeScale`: 0 while any owner holds it (`Hold`/`Release`, `IsFrozen`, on a [HoldSet](../Assets/Scripts/Core/HoldSet.cs), the owner set `GameplayInputGate` blocks with too), 1 after the last release and on dispose. Holders: `TransitionTimeFreeze`, and `GameMenuController` in solo play.
- [TransitionTimeFreeze](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/TransitionTimeFreeze.cs) (Main-scope entry point): offline, holds the `TimeFreeze` from `GameFlow.TransitionStarted` to `TransitionFinished` (and releases it on dispose), except during a trip (`GameFlow.IsTravelling`), which is a local fade only, so nothing on the game clock, `Time.deltaTime`, physics or `Awaitable.WaitForSecondsAsync` advances behind the fade of a new game, Continue or the menu. In a session (`ISessionStatus.IsActive`) it never holds (`Freezes(transitioning, travelling, inSession)`): a machine's own start, join or leave stops nobody's game (WorldStreaming.md's Simulation and presentation). `ScreenFader` uses unscaled time.
- [IRandom](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Random/IRandom.cs): `Range(min, max)`, `InsideUnitCircle()`. [SystemRandom](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Random/SystemRandom.cs) wraps `System.Random`, time-seeded or with an explicit seed (tests). Gameplay code never uses `UnityEngine.Random`.
- Randomness consumers: `MobPatrolAnchor` (roam destinations, idle durations via `MobConfig.NextIdleDuration(IRandom)`), passed down from `MobController`.

## Camera and sprite sorting

- [CameraFollow2D](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Camera/CameraFollow2D.cs) (Core, on Main Camera): `LateUpdate` SmoothDamp follow of the target's Rigidbody2D position, separate smooth times for moving/idle, can snap to the pixel grid (32 PPU; `snapToPixelGrid` is off in `Main`, see Known gaps). `SetTarget`, `SnapToTarget`. The camera's kick ([ICameraKick](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Camera/ICameraKick.cs), Animation.md's Phase 6): `Kick(offset, seconds)` knocks it `offset` off the followed position, whole on the frame it starts and falling as the cube of the time left (`Time.deltaTime`), ignored while a larger kick still runs (`KickOffset`; internal `AdvanceKick` for tests); the smoothing runs from the followed position, so a kick never moves where the camera settles.
- The Main Camera's Pixel Perfect Camera (32 assets PPU, reference resolution 640 x 360) uses no crop frame: it picks the largest whole zoom that fits the 640 x 360 reference and shows the rest of the screen as more of the world, instead of drawing the reference into a centred box with a black border. At 1280 x 720 and 1920 x 1080 that is exactly the reference view. With a crop frame it renders into an offscreen texture blitted into the box, and Unity draws the Game view's gizmos as if the camera filled the whole window, so the spawn points' gizmos landed up to about 1.6 times farther from the screen centre than the world under them. The Pixel Perfect Camera warns in the Game view when the window has an odd width or height.
- Sprites sort by the 2D renderer's custom axis ([WorldScale.md](WorldScale.md), Phase 1): `Assets/Settings/Renderer2D.asset` sorts with Transparency Sort Mode Custom Axis along (0, 1, 0), so within a sorting layer and order a lower sprite draws in front, by its height in floats, with no range to run out of. Nothing writes an order per object: within the `Entities` layer everything draws at order 0, but a carried lantern's layer, at order 1 inside the character's sorting group so it draws over the body (Lighting.md's Phase 9; the group itself sorts at 0; no character carries one since the player's was removed). Units, the arrow and the effects sort as a `SortingGroup` on their root, which Unity sorts at the root's position: the player and the Weasel at their feet, so a hit's nudge of the sprite on `Visuals` never changes their sorting; the arrow at its point on the ground under its sprite; an effect at the ground height it is played at (`EffectPlayer`), an impact nudged 0.05 toward the camera so it draws over the unit it hits. A prop's `Visuals` sprite sorts at its pivot (Sprite Sort Point Pivot), its base on the root, with no script on it, so nothing runs when a chunk's props start. The Weasel's `HealthBar` has a group of its own on the `Overlay` layer that sorts at the root (`sortAtRoot`), since a renderer inside a unit's group draws on the group's layer, so each unit's bar draws over every unit and over the other bars as one. A group just made in the editor sorts only once the editor updates its groups (`SortingGroup.UpdateAllSortingGroups`), which a test rendering one at once calls first.
- Sorting layers, back to front: `Default`, `Terrain` (ground: the blended terrain's chunks at order 0, the walls' render tilemap at 10; the markers also use order 10), `Entities` (units, projectiles, effects and props, all at order 0, sorted by the custom axis at their feet or base; a weapon is drawn into its character's frames), `Overlay` (world-space bars that draw over every unit).
- The camera culls the `Obstacles` layer, so the collision tilemap itself is never drawn in the game; in the editor `CollisionTileGizmo` (see Editor tools) shows its cells. It is the data tilemap of a DualGrid walls module (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`): the module's `RenderTilemap` child, on the Default layer and the Terrain sorting layer at order 10 (above the ground), draws the 32 px wall art, while the data tiles carry `Grid` colliders, so physics, navigation and the art all follow the same cells.

## Lighting

Lighting is presentation each machine draws on its own ([Lighting.md](Lighting.md), Phases 1 to 11 so far): URP's 2D lights, read by our own shaders once per art pixel, so the light changes only from one whole art pixel to the next and the art stays crisp, spells that light the world around them, a day and night every player of a session sees alike, the sun's shadows, which swing and lengthen with it, the shadows of clouds drifting over the ground by day, a sky of each region's own, and campfires, lamp posts, torches, braziers and lava that light the night, their flames and glass glowing, the flames moving, embers glowing in the charred wood, water that reflects the lights, and cobbles and props whose relief answers where each light comes from.

- Lights: each world's root scene holds one Global Light 2D under its `WorldLighting`, authored white at 0.85 (an outdoor world's then follows the day, below), on the `Default`, `Terrain`, `Entities` and `Overlay` sorting layers (`LocalWorldPresentation` shows the lights of the world on screen and hides the others'). The only other lights are the spell lights, the lights that are not spells, the light props' and the glowing ground's. The 2D renderer (`Assets/Settings/Renderer2D.asset`) draws the light textures at the screen's resolution (LightingGaps.md's Phase 4: at half of it a light's shadow edge flickered by an art pixel as the camera moved at one screen pixel an art pixel, the smallest window the Pixel Perfect Camera allows), with an HDR emulation scale of 1.
- [PixelLight.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Include/PixelLight.hlsl): `PixelLit(colour, worldPosition)` reads the light texture of blend style 0 (`_ShapeLightTexture0`) once, the texel holding the screen position of that world position (not a blend of four, Lighting.md's Phase 7, so a light's shadow reaches each art pixel whole) (`PixelLightScreenUV`, as `Lit2DCommon` computes a sprite's), and combines it as URP's `CombinedShapeLightShared` does, `_HDREmulationScale * (colour * modulate + additive)` by the style's blend factors; `PixelLightAt(worldPosition)` is that texel's light as it is, the lights' colours times their intensities (black without the keyword), which water's reflections read too; `PixelLitShadowed(colour, worldPosition, shadowed)` first takes the sun's light (`_SunColour`) away from it by the share a sun shadow covers, leaving the sky and the spells' light (`PixelLit` is it with none); without `USE_SHAPE_LIGHT_TYPE_0` (no light reaches the sorting layer) the colour is left as it is, as URP leaves it. `PixelGlowColour(colour, glow, tint, texel)` is a sprite pixel's colour before it is lit, from its glow (alpha how much it glows, colour the one it flickers to, black for none): its flicker colour, tinted as the sprite is, at the steps of the global `_GlowFlicker` (x the step, y the share of flickering pixels shown, z 1 to draw no pixel glowing, which the sprite shader applies to the glow it reads; zero, as in the editor, shows no flicker and every glow) a hash of its texel and the step picks (`PixelLightHash(pixel, step)`, the hash `PropLighting` uses too), its own otherwise; `PixelGlowLit(lit, colour, glow)` is the lit colour, no less than the colour times its glow. `PixelFlameFrame(uv, origin, frames)` (Lighting.md's Phase 6) moves a moving flame's texture coordinates up to the frame it shows: its sheet holds `frames` frames in rows over the sliced first, and a sprite standing at `origin` shows the frame the flicker's step has reached from the one a hash of the art pixel it stands on starts it at (`PropLighting.FlameFrameAt`), the first while nothing flickers (the editor) and for a still sprite. `PixelLightTexelCentre(uv, texels, worldPosition)` is the world position of the centre of the texel a sprite's fragment draws, from the screen-space derivatives of its texel coordinates and its position, so flipped, scaled, rotated and atlas-packed sprites find it too. A lit pass declares the keyword itself (`#pragma multi_compile _ USE_SHAPE_LIGHT_TYPE_0`): the sprite shader's 2D lit pass, and `TerrainBlend.hlsl` under `#if SHADERPASS == SHADERPASS_SPRITELIT`, so no other pass has light variants. Only blend style 0 (Multiply, URP's default for a new light) is read, so every light uses it: one light keyword instead of URP's four, two light variants of a lit pass instead of sixteen. Sprite masks and normal maps are not read.
- The ground: the terrain graph is on the Sprite Custom Lit target, and `TerrainBlend_float` lights its composite, and the debug view's colours, at the centre of the art pixel it snapped to (see World), the composite without the sun where the sun shadow mask covers that art pixel (`SunShadowAt`); where its topmost layer shows, raised ground drawing nothing over it, that layer adds the lights it reflects (below).
- Water's reflections (Lighting.md's Phase 10): water, the shallows and the sea reflect the lights (`TerrainMaterial.Reflection`: 1, 0.5 and 1, nothing else), in [TerrainBlendReflection.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlendReflection.hlsl)'s `TBReflection`: each row of art pixels sways sideways by a whole art pixel at 8 frames a second, and from there a pixel looks up the screen, north, over 20 art-pixel centres from 0.2 to 8 units for the brightest light other than the sky (the light texture less `_TerrainReflection.xyz`), fading to 0.3 of it at the far end; it reflects that light only as far as it is stronger than at the pixel itself, so a light's glitter path runs from it down the screen toward the camera, as a reflection does in this view; sharpened to its bright core (full at 0.7, cubed), in the light's own hue, at 0.7, and broken into dashes of 4 pixels, 30 % of them hidden by a hash of the dash and the step 4 times a second. Its glints and highlights (unlit colours brighter than 0.3) also catch the light falling on them, 3 times their colour, so the sea's surf lights up by a light on the beach. A pixel finds no light on 5 points over the reach and at itself before it reads all 20, which leaves out only a pool too faint there to show. Every light reflects, spells too, whole art pixels at once. `DayNightPresenter` hands the shaders `_TerrainReflection`: the light the world shown's global light puts in the light texture (its sky and sun outdoors, its own light indoors, `WorldLighting.GlobalLight`) and how strongly water reflects, fully at night and indoors, `DayNightSettings.DayReflection` (0.5) by day, between by the night lights' share; none in the editor, so water draws as before there.
- Relief under the lights (Lighting.md's Phase 11): [PixelRelief.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Include/PixelRelief.hlsl) reads where a local light comes from without URP's normals pass. `PixelLocalLight` is the light texture less `_LightRelief.xyz`, the light the world shown's global light puts there; `PixelLocalLightReaches` counts it only above 0.02 in its brightest channel, which leaves out the light texture's rounding of the sky; `PixelLocalLightDirection` is the way it grows, from its brightest channel 8 art pixels to either side, divided by its brightness there, with z 1, so a light shines from the side at the edge of its pool and from above at its middle. The cobbles and the plaza (`TerrainMaterial.Relief`) turn their bevels in [TerrainBlendRelief.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlendRelief.hlsl): `TBInterior` hands up, with a layer's colour, the bevels its stone pixel shows (`TBRelief`: the stone's own, `TBStoneBevel`, which way it faces from the height texture's alpha, how strongly from its distance to its stone's edge and centre in the cell field and the material's settings in the lookup texture's relief columns; and where the stone is cut along a rim the cut's), and `TBReliefLight` takes the painted bevel away and draws it again in each light's share: the sky keeps it, the sun draws it from its own side where no sun shadow falls, the local lights from where they come, 3 times the painted strength from the side (`TB_RELIEF_LOCAL`) and none from straight above, all by the share of the pixel's colour the stones' layer makes. The afternoon, whose sun shines within a few degrees of the painted side, draws today's pixels within a few steps, and the night away from the lamps exactly. Gravel's chips and the planks keep their painted bevels. A sprite's normals (`_NormalTex`, the props', below) light it under the local lights alone (`PixelSpriteRelief`): as much more as its normal faces where a light comes from, up to twice that light, and as much less as it faces away, to none; the sky and the sun keep its painted light, and a sprite without normals (the units, the effects) draws as before. `DayNightPresenter` hands the shaders `_LightRelief`: the same light as the reflections' and 1 while relief is drawn; none in the editor, so nothing changes there.
- Lamps' shadows on the casters (LightingGaps.md's Phase 2): the props' Shadow Caster 2Ds, and since KnownGaps.md's Phase 7 the walls', shadow only the ground's sorting layers, so URP draws the `Entities` layer's light in a batch of its own without their shadows, and a sprite there darkened by screen pixel would take patches where it overlaps a shadow on the ground behind it. Instead `LampShadowPass` hands the sprite shader the ground's light texture, and [LampShadow.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Include/LampShadow.hlsl) keeps of a caster's lights other than the sky (`_LightRelief.xyz`) the share the ground keeps half an art pixel above its feet (`LampShadowKept`: the ground's local light over the caster's own there, brightest channels, all of it where no local light reaches): a unit (the units' rendering layers) under each column of the sprite, so a trunk's narrow shadow crosses it, and a prop (the props') under its middle, whole, since by column a crown read the trunk's own shadow beside it. `LampShadowLit` lights it as `PixelLitShadowed` does, with only that share of the local lights, exactly as before where it keeps them all; its relief by its normals takes the same share. So a unit south of a wall, in its shadow, darkens whole, its head drawn over the wall's top, which the wall leaves lit, too (KnownGaps.md's Phase 7). Every other sprite of the `Entities` layer (the flat props, the small stones and the doormats, on neither caster layer; the arrow; the death puff) keeps at each art pixel it draws the share the ground keeps there, as the ground does, so none shines through a wall's or a prop's lamp shadow. Every sprite where no caster near a burning light is in view (URP then draws every sorting layer in one batch, the light handed over being the sprite's own) draws as before.
- [SpritePixelLit.shader](../Assets/Shaders/Sprites/SpritePixelLit.shader) (`TopDownRPG/Sprite Pixel Lit`), the one lit sprite shader: its 2D lit pass (`Universal2D`) lights each fragment at the centre of the texel it draws (the texture's size read from `_MainTex` itself), without the sun where a sun shadow falls (a renderer on the casters' rendering layers, `_SunShadowFeetLayers`, a prop reads it once in the vertex stage two art pixels toward the sun from its feet, so it darkens whole or not at all, and a unit under each of its columns on its feet' row, from the mask without the units' shadows (`SunShadowColumn`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 3); any other reads it at each art pixel it draws, as the ground does: the walls' tilemaps, the markers, the auras, the arrow, the puff), keeping of the local lights the share a lamp's shadow at its feet leaves a caster, or at the art pixel it draws any other sprite of the `Entities` layer (`LampShadowKept`, `LampShadowLit`, above; the vertex stage hands the fragment its origin and how it reads them, `CasterReading`: none, a prop, a unit, which reads its sun shadows by column too, `CasterReadsByColumn`, or any other sprite), its relief under the local lights by its normals (the sprite's secondary texture `_NormalTex`, x right, y up the screen and z toward the camera as 0 to 255, clear for a sprite without one: `PixelSpriteRelief`), a glowing pixel at no less than its own colour (its glow, the sprite's secondary texture `_GlowTex`, black for a sprite without one: `PixelGlowColour`, `PixelGlowLit`), a player's colours before the light (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5, [CharacterPalette.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Include/CharacterPalette.hlsl), `CharacterPaletteColour`: where the sprite's secondary texture `_PaletteMap`, black for a sprite without one, names a dye and tone, the colour of the material's palette `_PaletteTex` there, tinted as the sprite is, where the palette is opaque; the sprite's own colour elsewhere, and under a material without a palette, so only a player's `CharacterPalette` copy recolours), then mixes in the flash colour by the flash amount (`_FlashColor`, `_FlashAmount`, in its per-material buffer, set by `HitFeedback` through a property block); its vertex stage picks a moving flame's frame (`_FlameFrames`, in the per-material buffer, 1 for a still sprite: `PixelFlameFrame` at the renderer's origin); its unlit pass (`UniversalForward`) draws the same unlit, in the palette's colours too, on its first frame. SRP Batcher compatible. Both passes compile synchronously in the editor (`#pragma editor_sync_compilation`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5), so the first sprites drawn with it in a session draw at once. Its material, [SpritePixelLit.mat](../Assets/Materials/SpritePixelLit.mat), draws the player's and the Weasel's bodies, every prop (`Prop.prefab`'s `Visuals`, which every prop prefab inherits), the arrow, the death puff, the status aura, the markers and the walls' render tilemap (`Walls.prefab` and the copies in the chunks); it is also the 2D renderer's default material (Default Material Type Custom), which a new sprite or tilemap renderer gets, so one a tool makes lights the same way. [SpritePixelLitFlames.mat](../Assets/Materials/SpritePixelLitFlames.mat), the same shader with `_FlameFrames` 6 (`LightRecipe.FlameFrames`), made by the prop writer, draws the campfires, torches and braziers, whose flames move (Lighting.md's Phase 6). Unlit on purpose: the impact (`Sprite-Unlit-Default`) and the world-space bars on `Overlay`.
- Under the global light alone the ground and every sprite draw exactly the pixels URP's Sprite Lit target and `Sprite-Lit-Default` drew (Lighting.md's Phase 1 notes).
- Spell lights (Lighting.md's Phase 2): point lights on blend style 0 and the `Default`, `Terrain` and `Entities` sorting layers (not the bars' `Overlay`), without shadows, off until they show. Each machine plays them from what it already plays, so nothing about them is sent and nothing in the game reads them.
  - A cast's light: `CharacterVisuals`' cast light, the player's `Visuals/CastLight` at 1.2 and radius 3, shows the light of the frame each update shows (`CharacterAnimator.Light`). The character baker writes each frame's light into the animation set (`CharacterFrameLight`): from where it drew the glow, at the strength it drew it (0.5, 0.75 and 1 over the gather, 1 on the release, 0.5 on the ring and 0.25 on the sparks), in the cast's rim tone (Bolt (90, 180, 240), Mend (90, 210, 110), Smite (240, 170, 60)). It holds with its sprite through a hit-stop, follows the legs under a cast drawn over the run, and plays on a copy from its owner's cast messages as the copy animates. Only the class casts Bolt, Mend and Smite glow.
  - An impact's light: `EffectPlayer`'s, the `Light` under `Impact.prefab`'s `Sprite`, at 1.2 and radius 2, in the middle colour of the Fire, Frost, Lightning and Poison sparks (`EffectSprites.LightColour`, written by the effect writer), fading to nothing over the impact's 0.16 s and off in the pool. Physical and True impacts, so every weapon and Weasel hit, cast none.
  - At 1.2 nothing burns to white by day; 32 lights on screen add about 0.04 ms to a frame in a player (Lighting.md's Phase 2 notes).
- A lantern in hand (Lighting.md's Phase 9): removed from the player on 2026-11-05, with its key, `PlayerLantern` and its replicated state. What carried it stays for a character that carries one later: the baker's and the Aseprite recipes' lantern layer and light per frame (see Character animation and Editor tools, Characters/), and `CharacterVisuals`' lantern renderer and light, drawn and lit only while `ShowLantern` has it burning.
- Day and night (Lighting.md's Phase 3), cosmetic: nothing in the game's rules reads the time of day. The code is under `Assets/Scripts/Lighting`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [DayNightSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayNightSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Lighting/DayNight.asset`): the day's and the night's length on the world clock (1200 and 300 s), the start time a new game begins at (0.16, the morning), the light keys ([DayLightKey](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayNightTypes.cs): a time of the cycle, the sky's colour and the sun's, and the night lights' share, how strongly the lights that burn at night shine (none from sunrise through the evening at 0.7, 0.7 at sunset, full from dusk at 0.84 to before dawn at 0.96, Lighting.md's Phase 5); ten, from sunrise at 0 through the afternoon at 0.56, exactly today's 0.85 white together, and sunset at 0.8, to the night sky (0.13, 0.16, 0.30) at 0.88), the sun's shadow (0.5 per unit of height at noon, at most 2, leaning toward the camera by half its way across at either end, Lighting.md's Phase 4) how often the night's lights and glowing pixels flicker (`FlickerSteps`, 8 a second) and the share of flickering pixels shown at a step (`GlowFlickerShare`, 0.36), Lighting.md's Phase 5, the clouds (Phase 8: `CloudSize` 14 units, the most cover `CloudCover` 0.45, `CloudSpellSeconds` 240, `CloudShade` 0.75 of the sun, `CloudEdge` 0.03 of the noise, `Wind` (0.6, -0.15) units a second), how strongly water reflects the lights by day (`DayReflection`, 0.5 of at night, Phase 10), and how often the host resends its clock (60 s). The cycle runs from 0 at sunrise to 1; `CycleSeconds`, `DayShare` (where sunset lies). `PhaseAt(worldSeconds)`: the start time plus the cycles played, in [0, 1). `LightAt(phase)`: a [DayLight](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayNightTypes.cs) (`Sky`, `Sun`, `Total` the two together, opaque, and `NightLights`, the night lights' share), each colour and the share blended linearly between the keys either side, round past the last to the first, exactly a key's at its time. `SunAt(phase)`: a [SunState](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayNightTypes.cs) (`IsUp`; `Elevation`, the sine of the day's progress; `Shadow`, where a point one unit up lands on the ground on the screen: away from the sun, to the left at sunrise and the right at sunset, as long as the noon length over the elevation up to the longest; none at night), which the sun's shadows follow. `OnValidate` warns about keys out of order. `Create` for tests, without clouds, and `WithClouds` to give a test's settings some. |
| [DayClock](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayClock.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton over the settings, the Main scope's `SessionSave`, `IGameAuthority` and `INetworkClock`. `WorldTime`: `SessionSave.Time` where game state is decided (offline, the host), so the time of day is saved with the world clock and a loaded save resumes it; on a client the host's world clock last heard (`Anchor(worldTime, serverTime)`, `IsAnchored`) plus the network time since, and its own before it hears it. `SessionPhase` (the settings' phase at the world time), `Phase` (with this machine's `LocalOffset`, a share of the cycle, wrapped, and its local rate), `Light`, `Sun`, `Settings`. The dev panels' seams: `internal` `LocalRate` (how fast this machine's time of day runs against the world clock: 1 with it, 0 held, more ahead; changing it keeps the time shown, and the time is worked out when read, so nothing runs per frame), `ShowPhase(phase)` (this machine's time of day there, running on at the local rate) and `FollowSession()` (no offset, rate 1). |
| [DayClockSync](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayClockSync.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IInitializable`, `ITickable`, `IDisposable`) over the clock and `NetworkSession`. In a session, on `Initialize` (before any entry point tells the host a client joined), the host follows `ClientJoined` and sends that client its world clock and the network time it read it at (`MessageName`, two doubles, on `NetworkSession.MessageDelivery`), and every joined client again each `ResyncSeconds` of its world clock; a client listens (internal static `Listen(manager, clock)`, also used by tests on an in-process client's manager) and anchors its clock on messages from the host only. Offline it does nothing. |
| [DayNightPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/DayNightPresenter.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, `IDisposable`) over the clock, `Worlds`, `RegionSkies` and `LocalPlayerTracker` (the last two may be null: the day's own sky): writes the clock's `Light.Total`, the sky and the sun together, the light where no sun shadow falls, tinted by the region skies at the local player in the world shown (`Sky`, a `RegionSkyBlend`, Lighting.md's Phase 8), to every loaded world's `WorldLighting.ApplySky` once it has moved by 1/512 in a channel, and to a world as it loads (`WorldLoaded`); `Shown`, the light last written. It hands the sun to the shaders (`SunShadowGlobals.Set`: its light, in the same tint, and shadow vector, casting while the sun is up and the world shown, `WorldLighting.IsShown`, is outdoors) once it has moved by 1/512 in a channel or 1/1024 of its shadow vector, or started or stopped casting (`Casting`), and takes it away when disposed. While the sun casts it hands the clouds (`Clouds`, its `SunClouds`) to the shaders every frame (`SunShadowGlobals.SetClouds`): drifted by the clock's world time, at the level of the day's cover then times the region skies' (`CloudCover`), and clears them once when it stops casting or no cloud would show. `internal` `SunShadowsOverride` (a `LightingOverride`, `DayNightTypes.cs`: `Follow`, `On` or `Off`; the lighting panel's): off, the sun casts nothing on this machine while the world keeps the sky and the sun together; forced on it follows the day, since there is nothing to cast with while the sun is down or indoors. `internal` `CloudsOverride`: forced on, the clouds cover the most the day and the region allow; off, none. It hands water's reflections (Lighting.md's Phase 10) the global `_TerrainReflection` (`ReflectionId`): the light of the world shown (outdoors the light it writes the world, indoors `WorldLighting.GlobalLight`) and how strongly to reflect (outdoors `DayNightSettings.DayReflection` eased to 1 by the night lights' share, indoors 1) once either has moved by 1/512, and zero when disposed; `internal` `ReflectionsOverride`: forced on, as at night; off, none. It hands relief under the lights (Lighting.md's Phase 11) the global `_LightRelief` (`ReliefId`): the same light of the world shown and 1 while relief is drawn, once either has moved by 1/512, and zero when disposed; `internal` `ReliefOverride`: off, none; on, as when it follows. Allocates nothing as it ticks. |

- Sun shadows (Lighting.md's Phase 4): by day outdoors the props, the units and raised ground's cliffs cast the sun's shadow, each caster's own silhouette laid on the ground away from the sun, and the lit shaders take the sun's light away where one falls, so a shadow takes the sky's colour and two never darken each other. An outdoor world's global light keeps the sky and the sun together (`DayNightPresenter`): the light textures hold 10- and 11-bit floats, so this is what keeps the afternoon's unshadowed pixels exactly today's. Casters are found by their rendering layers, defined in the project's tags and layers: `Sun Shadow Props` (`Prop.prefab`'s `Visuals`, so every prop but those lying flat), `Sun Shadow Units` (the player's and the Weasel's `Visuals`) and `Sun Shadow Cliffs` (the terrain chunks of a grid with raised ground, `TerrainStyle.CliffShadowLayers`). Nothing runs per prop. Shadows know the ground's height (Lighting.md's Phase 11): raised ground's top stands the cliff's height above the level ground in front of it and is drawn that height up the screen, so a caster on a top casts into the mask apart from one on level ground, a shadow running off a top drops to the level ground below (`SunShadowDrop`: the cliff's height times the sun's shadow vector further and that height lower on the screen), a level caster's shadow stops at a plateau's side but for its parts higher than the cliff, which fall on the top, and a cliff face, which faces away from the sun in this view, takes only the clouds'; every lit shader reads its shadow that way (`SunShadowAt`), the ground and the sprites alike.

| Type / asset | Role |
|---|---|
| [SunShadowFeature](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/SunShadowFeature.cs) | The 2D renderer's feature (`Assets/Settings/Renderer2D.asset`'s `SunShadows`): the caster material, the props', units' and cliffs' rendering layers, the cliffs' shader, the tallest caster's height above its feet (5 units, `TallestCaster`), the margin the mask reaches past the view (4 units), how far the tallest shadow's tip may move before the props' shadows are drawn again (1 art pixel) and the clouds' shader (Lighting.md's Phase 8), whose material it makes the first time (`CloudMaterial`) and destroys with itself. It keeps a `SunShadowMask` per game or scene camera, readied in `RenderPipelineManager.beginCameraRendering` (culling needs the render context), and released once its camera is destroyed; a scene loading or unloading marks every mask's props' shadows out of date. It enqueues its `SunShadowPass` for those cameras, and does nothing while the sun casts nothing. For the lamps' shadows on the casters (LightingGaps.md's Phase 2) it also holds the casters' sorting layer (`Entities`), enqueues its two `LampShadowPass`es for those cameras day or night, and clears `_CasterLayers` as each camera begins, so a camera without them reads no lamp shadows and its units read the sun's whole, as props do. `InvalidateAll` marks every mask's props' shadows out of date (a scene loading or unloading, and `SunShadowGlobals.RedrawPropShadows`). |
| [LampShadowPass](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/LampShadowPass.cs) | `ScriptableRenderPass2D` on the casters' sorting layer, through the render graph (LightingGaps.md's Phase 2), two of them: before that layer's sprites (`RenderPassEvent2D.BeforeRenderingSprites`), it reads blend style 0's light texture of the first batch (`Universal2DResourceData.lightTextures[0][0]`: the ground's sorting layers, `Default` and `Terrain`, come first and every light lights both, so URP draws their light, with the props' and the walls' shadows, in the first batch) and sets it as `_LampShadowGround` after the pass, with `_CasterLayers` (the props' and units' rendering layers, which read it at their feet, every other sprite of the layer reading it at each art pixel; zero before the layer, as each camera begins, reads none) and `_CasterUnitLayers` (the units', which read the lamps' and, since LightingGaps.md's Phase 3, the sun's shadows by column) set in its render function; after them (`AfterRenderingSprites`) it only reads the texture, so the render graph keeps it alive until the sprites have drawn. |
| [SunShadowPass](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/SunShadowPass.cs) | `ScriptableRenderPass2D` at `RenderPassEvent2D.BeforeRendering`, through the render graph: when the camera's mask is out of date, clears the ground's texture and draws into it the terrain chunks on the cliffs' layers with the cliffs' shader's second pass (Lighting.md's Phase 11), then clears the props' texture and draws into it the culled sprites on the props' layers with the caster material and the terrain chunks on the cliffs' layers with the cliffs' shader over their own material (`overrideShader`); then every frame copies it into the frame's texture and draws the units over it, and the clouds over the whole mask while any cloud takes the sun away (`SunShadowGlobals.Cloudy`), each texel keeping the most shadow of either. It sets `_SunShadowMask`, `_SunShadowProps` (the props' texture, which the units read by column, LightingGaps.md's Phase 3), `_SunShadowGround`, `_SunShadowRect`, `_SunShadowMaskMatrix`, `_SunShadowTallest` (the tallest caster's height) and `_SunShadowFeetLayers` (the props' and units' layers) as globals, and an empty `_SunShadowRect` when no mask is drawn. |
| [SunShadowMask](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/SunShadowMask.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`), one camera's mask: two RGBA8 textures (`RTHandle`s, point filtered: the props' and the frame's, red the shadows on level ground, green those on raised ground's tops, blue those of level casters' parts higher than a cliff, alpha the clouds alone; Lighting.md's Phase 11) and an RG8 one (`Ground`: what the ground is, and the cliff's height near raised ground) of one texel per art pixel over `Area`, in whole art pixels of the world, so it never moves against the ground. `Prepare(context, shadow, casts, tallest, margin, redrawPixels)`: `Follow` (internal) centres the area again, with the margin and sizes rounded up to 64 texels, once the view needs more than it covers (the view, the feet of casters up to the tallest's height below it, and the art pixels sprites read at), and marks the props' shadows out of date (`PropsCurrent`) when it moved or the sun's shadow vector moved by the redraw distance at the tallest caster's tip (`MarkPropsDrawn`, `Invalidate`); then it culls the renderers standing where their shadows can reach the area (the area and the area moved toward the sun by the tallest shadow, `Culling`). Nothing while the sun casts nothing or the camera is not orthographic (`IsCasting`). `DrawMatrix`, `ShaderRect`. |
| [SunShadowGlobals](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/SunShadowGlobals.cs) | Static: the global shader properties' IDs; `Set(sun, shadow, casts)` sets the sun's light (`_SunColour`) and its shadow vector with whether it casts (`_SunShadow`), both zero while it casts nothing; `Shadow(out casts)` reads them back for the feature. `GroundId` and `TallestId` name the mask's ground and the tallest caster's height (Phase 11). `SetClouds(drift, level, shade, size, edge)` sets the clouds (`_SunClouds`: the drift and the level above which the field is a cloud, and the share of the sun a cloud takes away, none for no cloud; `_SunCloudShape`: the size, the edge's fade and the tile's cells), `Cloudy()` whether any cloud takes the sun away. `RedrawPropShadows()` marks the props' shadows of every camera out of date in each `SunShadowFeature` of the active pipeline's renderers (LightingGaps.md's Phase 3), and `PropsMaskId` names `_SunShadowProps`. |
| [SunShadow.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Include/SunShadow.hlsl) | The globals, `SunShadowMaskAt` and `SunShadowGroundAt` (the mask's and the ground's texels at an art pixel, zero outside them or while the sun casts nothing), `SunShadowDrop(cliff)` (how far a shadow drops off a top, in whole art pixels), `SunShadowAt(worldPixel)` (1 where a shadow covers that art pixel, 0 outside the mask or while the sun casts nothing, by the ground there, Lighting.md's Phase 11: level ground its red and, near raised ground, the tops' shadows one drop up the screen unless that lies on a top; a top its green and the level casters' high parts' blue one drop down the screen; a cliff face its alpha, the clouds) and `SunShadowUnder(feet)` (read two art pixels toward the sun from a caster's feet, past where its own shadow starts, for a prop). `SunShadowFrom(worldPixel, props)` is `SunShadowAt` from the mask or, with props, from `_SunShadowProps`, the props' texture, which holds the props' and cliffs' shadows but neither the units' nor the clouds (`SunShadowTexelAt`); `SunShadowColumn(worldPosition)`, a unit's (LightingGaps.md's Phase 3), reads that on its feet' row under each of its columns, with the clouds from the mask's alpha, so a unit never darkens itself and a shadow's edge sweeps across it as it walks. |
| [SunShadowCaster.shader](../Assets/Shaders/Sprites/SunShadowCaster.shader), [SunShadowCaster.mat](../Assets/Materials/SunShadowCaster.mat) | `TopDownRPG/Sun Shadow Caster`: lays a sprite on the ground from its feet, its object's origin: a point as high above the feet as `h` lands at its own distance across plus `h` times the shadow vector, from the feet's height, so the silhouette is flipped at its base, stretched and leaned; the part below the feet, which lies on the ground already, is discarded rather than folded, so it is exact for any sprite mesh. Writes 1 where the sprite is opaque, blending by the maximum, by the ground under its feet (`SunShadowGroundAt`, read in its vertex stage; Lighting.md's Phase 11): into green for a caster on raised ground's top, else into red, and into blue too for its parts higher than a cliff near it. |
| [SunShadowCliffs.shader](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/SunShadowCliffs.shader) | `TopDownRPG/Terrain/Sun Shadow Cliffs`, drawn with a terrain chunk's own material: grows the chunk's quad toward where the cliffs' shadows fall (the cliff's height times the shadow vector) and marks a level art pixel where, looking toward the sun up to that far, it finds ground under raised ground's top (raised ground with raised ground the cliff's height above it), searched by cells every 4 pixels and then pixel by pixel; ramps cast none, and raised ground, its faces included, takes none from its own cliffs. Crisp shapes only. Its second pass (`SunShadowGround`, Lighting.md's Phase 11) draws the ground into the mask's ground, blending by the maximum, over the chunk's quad grown on every side by the tallest caster's shadow and a shadow's drop: raised ground's top where it lies more than the cliff's height above its foot (its ledge and outline included), its cliff face within that height but on a ramp's slope, which counts as level ground, and level ground elsewhere, with the cliff's height beside it. |
| [SunShadowClouds.shader](../Assets/Shaders/Lighting/SunShadowClouds.shader) | `Hidden/TopDownRPG/Sun Shadow Clouds` (Lighting.md's Phase 8), drawn by the pass as one triangle over the whole mask, blending by the maximum (`BlendOp Max`): at the centre of each texel's art pixel the clouds' field, four octaves of gradient noise over cells of the clouds' size, the lattice wrapped by a mask to the tile (a power of two), drifted; it writes the share of the sun a cloud takes away times how far the field lies above the level, eased over the edge's fade, into the mask's red, green and alpha, so the level ground, the tops and the cliff faces take it alike (Phase 11). |
| [SunClouds](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/SunClouds.cs) | Plain C#, the presenter's (Lighting.md's Phase 8): the clouds worked out from the world clock, so every machine of a session sees the same. `DriftAt(worldSeconds)` (against the wind, wrapped in doubles to the tile of `TileCells`, 16, cells of the clouds' size, `TileUnits`), `CoverAt(worldSeconds)` (the most cover times a level eased between those of the spells either side, each spell's hashed from its number), `LevelFor(cover)` (the field's level leaving that share of it above, from its values at 96 x 96 points of one tile, sorted, worked out once), `FieldAt(world, drift)` and `Field(point)`, the field as the shader works it out. |

- Lights that are not spells (Lighting.md's Phases 5 and 6): the light props, campfires, lamp posts, torches and braziers the prop baker draws (see World, Props, and Editor tools, Props/), each carrying a point Light 2D, off, and the ground of a glowing material (lava), whose terrain renderer draws its glow through a Sprite Light 2D (`TerrainBlendRenderer.GlowLight`, `TerrainGlowMap`, see World), burn by the night lights' share of the time of day: off by day, so the afternoon draws today's pixels, rising at sunset, full at night, and at full in an indoor world. The light props' flames, coals and glass glow and their flames flicker in the sprite shader (above), and a campfire's, torch's and brazier's flame moves through its six frames there at the flicker's steps, by day too, each prop from the frame its place picks (Phase 6); the charred trees and stumps glow steady with embers, with no light of their own. On blend style 0 and the `Default`, `Terrain` and `Entities` sorting layers, a prop light casting hard shadows at full that walls and blocking props stop (below), lava's glow none; a prop light stands on the ground under its flame or lantern. Every machine burns them from the chunks it has loaded and the shared time of day, flickering on its own clock as its water moves; nothing about them is sent and nothing in the game reads them.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PropLight](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/PropLight.cs) | On a light prop's `Light` child, which the prop writer makes: its Light 2D (`LightSource`, off as written), its full strength at night (`Intensity`) and how far its strength flickers either way (`Flicker`, a share); `Configure` for the writer and tests; `Reset` points it at the Light 2D beside it, and `OnValidate` warns without one. It runs nothing. |
| [LightWarmUp](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/LightWarmUp.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, `IDisposable`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): as the session starts, behind its load, makes far from any world a hidden camera, a point light casting hard shadows and a Shadow Caster 2D that leaves itself lit, as the prop lights and the props' casters are written; on its second tick, once both have built their meshes, draws them into a 32 x 32 texture through a render request (`Rendered`), and destroys everything on the next (`Running`), or when disposed. Mono compiles URP's 2D light and shadow code the first time it runs, which a development player otherwise did in the frames the night's lights first burnt near casters. |
| [PropLighting](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/PropLighting.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, `IDisposable`) over the `DayClock` (none: full, no flicker), `IClock` and `Worlds`. `Add(lights, ground, world)` takes a chunk's prop lights, its renderer's ground glow and its world (indoor ones burn at full, by `WorldLighting.Profile`), burning them at once; `Remove(lights)` lets them go. Each update it reads the clock's night lights' share and the flicker's step (`IClock.Time` times the settings' `FlickerSteps`) and writes only when the share moved by 1/512 or a step passed: each prop light on and at `StrengthAt(full, flicker, share, step, seed)` (its strength times the share times one plus its flicker share times a hash of the step and its seed, `SeedOf` its position's art pixel, between -1 and 1) while the share is above none, off otherwise, lights already off left alone; each renderer's `GlowLight` at its `GlowStrength` times the share; and at each step `_GlowFlicker` (the step, the settings' `GlowFlickerShare`) for the shaders, by day too, so moving flames step through their frames, zeroed when disposed; `FlameFrameAt(position, step, frames)` is the frame the sprite shader shows a moving flame standing there at that step (Lighting.md's Phase 6). Allocates nothing as it flickers. `Shown`, `GroupCount`. The lighting panel's `internal` overrides (`LightingOverride`), each writing every light at the next update when it changes: `PropLightsOverride` and `GroundGlowOverride` burn the prop lights and the ground's glow by the share, at full or not at all (each group remembers whether its lights and its glow were left on); `FlickerOverride` off holds the step at 0, the lights at their steady strength, no pixel flickering (`_GlowFlicker.y` 0) and every moving flame on its first frame; this machine's `UserSettings.ReduceFlashing` (DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), read at each update, stills the flicker the same way from the update it changes, without ever writing the override, which stays the panel's: the flicker stands still while either asks (`Flickers`, false while one does); `GlowingPixelsOverride` off sets `_GlowFlicker.z` to 1. Walls and props stopping the lights (Lighting.md's Phase 7): `Add(lights, casters, ground, world)` also takes the chunk's props' Shadow Caster 2Ds, written off; each prop light that casts (its Light 2D's `shadowsEnabled` as written) reaches the casters within its radius of where it stands, of every loaded chunk, found as chunks come and go, and while it burns and casts it holds them awake, a caster enabled while at least one light holds it (`AwakeCasters`) and disabled when the last lets go, its chunk is removed or the lights go out, so a caster's script runs only near a burning light. `LightShadowsOverride` off sets the casting lights' `shadowsEnabled` false and lets every caster sleep. |
| [ChunkPropLights](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/ChunkPropLights.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `IDisposable`): hands the chunk's `PropLight`s, its props' shadow casters (`CastersOf(props)`: the Shadow Caster 2D on each `PropFade`'s visuals, Lighting.md's Phase 7), its `TerrainBlendRenderer` and its `ChunkIdentity`'s world to `PropLighting` in `Start` and takes them back when disposed (see Composition / DI). |

- Light shadows (Lighting.md's Phase 7): walls and blocking props stop the prop lights through URP's Shadow Caster 2D, which takes a casting light's shadow out of what it draws into blend style 0's light texture, so every lit shader shows it with nothing more. A blocking prop's caster lies on its `Visuals` over its footprint (see World, Props), asleep until `PropLighting` wakes it, casting onto the `Default` and `Terrain` sorting layers only, so no sprite darkens by screen pixel in a prop's shadow; the walls' caster lies on their `CollisionTilemap`, shaped by its colliders, always awake and, since KnownGaps.md's Phase 7, casting onto those sorting layers only too, so units and props read the walls' shadows at their feet as they read the props' (Lamps' shadows on the casters, above). A light's own prop casts nothing, and spell lights, lava's glow and the global lights cast nothing (`WorldLighting` turns its global lights' shadows off as its world loads, since URP would still draw a shadow texture for one near a caster). Shadows are hard and stay on the art grid as the camera moves.

| Type / asset | Role |
|---|---|
| [LightShadowProjected.shader](../Assets/Shaders/Lighting/LightShadowProjected.shader) | `Hidden/TopDownRPG/Light Shadow Projected`, our copy of URP's projected shadow shader (`Shadow2D-Projected.shader`), which the 2D renderer draws each casting light's shadow into its shadow texture with: URP's projection of each caster edge away from the light, its hard shadow widened by an art pixel along its sides and toward the light, keeping a texel only when the centre of the art pixel it lies in is behind the edge seen from the light (the edge's ends from the triangle's provoking vertex, `nointerpolation`), so the shadow changes only at whole art pixels and holds still against them as the camera moves; a soft shadow's penumbra drawn as URP draws it. Its two passes write the shadow and the caster's own unshadowing as URP's do. |
| [LightShadowResources](../Assets/Editor/Lighting/LightShadowResources.cs) | Editor-only, static: the 2D renderer reads its projected shadow shader from URP's 2D renderer resources in `Assets/UniversalRenderPipelineGlobalSettings.asset`, internal and hidden from the inspector, which point at `LightShadowProjected.shader` (`ShaderPath`). `Current` reads it (by reflection); `Apply()`, `Tools/TopDownRPG/Lighting/Use Art-Grid Light Shadows`, points them at ours again after a URP upgrade resets them, and saves the settings (Known gaps). `[InitializeOnLoad]` (LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): once the editor has loaded its scripts (`EditorApplication.delayCall`), `RestoreIfLost()` applies ours with a warning whenever the resources hold another shader, so an upgrade fixes itself on its next load. |
| [NightPreview](../Assets/Editor/Lighting/NightPreview.cs) | Editor-only `ScriptableSingleton` (LightingGaps.md's Phase 6): a time of day previewed in the open scenes, `Show(phase)` and `Hide()` (`Shown`, `Phase`). Showing lights them at that phase of `Assets/Data/Lighting/DayNight.asset` as play does: an outdoor `WorldLighting`'s global lights take the sky and the sun (`ApplySky`), an indoor one keeps its own and burns its lights at full; every `PropLight`'s light burns at its strength times the night lights' share and every terrain renderer's glow light at its glow strength times it, without flicker; the sun's shadow vector and colour while it is up outdoors (`SunShadowGlobals.Set`), no clouds, and `_TerrainReflection` and `_LightRelief` as `DayNightPresenter` writes them. The props' casters stay asleep. It records each Light 2D's whole serialized state first (`EditorJsonUtility`) and puts it back when hidden, before any scene saves (showing again once it has), and before play starts (showing again after it), and lights a scene that opens; hidden, the shader globals go back to none. Its state outlives script reloads. |
| [TimeOfDayOverlay](../Assets/Editor/Lighting/TimeOfDayOverlay.cs) | The Scene view's Time of Day overlay (LightingGaps.md's Phase 6, shown by default): a Preview toggle, a Phase slider (0 to 1) and the phase with the night lights' share at it, driving `NightPreview`. |
| [PropShadowRedraw](../Assets/Editor/Lighting/PropShadowRedraw.cs) | Editor-only, `[InitializeOnLoad]` (LightingGaps.md's Phase 3): whenever the editor publishes changes to the open scenes (`ObjectChangeEvents.changesPublished`), marks the props' sun shadows out of date (`SunShadowGlobals.RedrawPropShadows`), so a prop moved, added or removed by hand in play mode casts from where it stands at once rather than when the sun or the view next moves enough. |
| [ShadowCasterSceneSaving](../Assets/Editor/Lighting/ShadowCasterSceneSaving.cs) | Editor-only, `[InitializeOnLoad]`: before a scene saves (`EditorSceneManager.sceneSaving`), marks the shadow meshes its Shadow Caster 2Ds made in the editor not to be saved (`KeepMeshesOut(scene)`), since saving wrote each as an unreferenced object of its own (Known gaps). |

- Region skies (Lighting.md's Phase 8): each region of a generated world has a sky of its own, a tint the day's sky and sun take and how much of the day's cloud cover it has, which the world generator writes into the chunks it reaches (see Editor tools, World generation) and each machine blends at its own local player's place, so the whole screen takes the sky of where its player stands. Nothing about it is sent and nothing in the game reads it. Other worlds have no regions and keep the day's own sky.

| Type / asset | Role |
|---|---|
| [RegionSky](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/RegionSky.cs) | ScriptableObject (create menu `TopDownRPG/Lighting/Region Sky`, `Assets/Data/Lighting/Skies/Sky_<region>.asset`): `Tint` (white for the day's own) and `CloudCover` (1 for the day's). The Village Forest's (0.96, 1, 0.88), the Lakeside's (0.93, 1, 1.05), the Snowfield's (0.90, 0.96, 1.10) at 1.3 cover, the Coast's (1.05, 1.02, 0.94) at 0.7, the Lava Fields' (1.04, 0.86, 0.74) at 1.3; the Clearing has none. `Create` for tests. |
| [ChunkSky](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/ChunkSky.cs) | On a generated chunk's grid, written by the world generator: the skies of the regions in or near the chunk (`Skies`) and each one's weight, 0 to 255, at every `Spacing` (4) cells of the chunk, its edges included (`Samples`, 17 a side), so two chunks share the samples along their border (`Weights`, a byte array, sky by sky and row by row). `WeightAt(sky, cell)` blends a sky's weight between the four samples around a place in cells from the chunk's corner. `Configure` for the writer and tests; `OnValidate` warns when the weights and the skies disagree. |
| [RegionSkies](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/RegionSkies.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton: the loaded chunks' `ChunkSky`s by world and coordinates (`Add`, `Remove`, `Count`); `At(world, localPosition)` blends the skies reaching a place into a `RegionSkyBlend` (`DayNightTypes.cs`: `Tint` and `CloudCover`; `None`, white and 1): each sky's tint and cover by its weight, the day's own by what is left, the weights scaled down where they add up to more than one; the day's own in a chunk not held or without skies. Allocates nothing. |
| [ChunkRegionSky](../Assets/Scripts/Lighting/ChunkRegionSky.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `IDisposable`): hands the chunk's `ChunkSky` with its `ChunkIdentity`'s world and coordinates to `RegionSkies` in `Start` and takes it back when disposed (see Composition / DI). |

- The dev panels move this machine's time of day, and the lighting panel forces parts of the lighting on or off, on this machine alone (see Dev tools); the Sandbox holds a lighting showcase (see Scenes).

## UI

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [MenuStack](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/MenuStack.cs) / [IMenuLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/IMenuLayer.cs) | Main-scope entry point (`ITickable`) and the only reader of `IMenuInput`: the open menus, newest on top (`Push`, `Remove`, `Top`, `Count`). Each tick it handles at most one press: Back or the Menu key goes to the top layer's `HandleBack`, and the Menu key with nothing open raises `MenuRequested`. Escape is both keys and still counts once, so no menu needs its own frame bookkeeping. |
| [UISelection](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/UISelection.cs) | Main-scope singleton over the scene's `EventSystem`: `Select(selectable)` (null clears). The menus move focus only through it, never through `EventSystem.current`. |
| [UiScale](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/UiScale.cs) / [UiScaleSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/UiScaleSettings.cs) | The UI zoom (DesignSystem.md's Phase 3): how many display pixels one UI px is drawn as. Static `ReadableZoom(height)` (the display height divided by `LinesPerZoom`, 390, rounded up: the smallest zoom at which Caption text has an 18 px body per 1080 display lines), `FittingZoom(width, height)` (the largest at which `MinimumLayout`, 480 x 270, fits), `StepsFor(width, height)` (the fitting zoom above the readable one, up to `MaxSizeStep`, 2) and `ZoomFor(width, height, step)` (the readable zoom lowered to fit, plus the step where it fits): x2 at 720p, x3 at 900p and 1080p (x4 Large), x4 at 1440p, x6 at 4K. A Main-scope entry point: each tick compares `Screen.width`, `Screen.height` and `UserSettings.UiSizeStep` with the last ones and applies them (`Apply`, internal for tests), raising `Changed` when `Factor` or `StepsThatFit` changes. Local, never replicated. The settings asset is `Assets/Data/UI/UiScale.asset`; `Create` for tests. |
| [UiPixelScale](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/UiPixelScale.cs) | On each UI canvas: `PlayerHudCanvas`, `DamagePopupCanvas` and `GameMenuCanvas` (the Gameplay scope's auto-inject objects) and `MainMenuCanvas` (the Menu scope's). Once injected with `UiScale` (`Construct`), and on every `Changed`, sets its `CanvasScaler` to a constant pixel size at 32 reference pixels per unit (`ReferencePixelsPerUnit`, so one sprite pixel is one UI px) and the zoom; logs an error from `Start` when never injected, keeping the scaler as saved (constant pixel size, 3, 32). The transition fader's canvas keeps its own constant pixel size. |
| [ScreenFader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/ScreenFader.cs) | `CanvasGroup` fade used by `GameFlow` (unscaled time); blocks raycasts while visible; starts opaque. |
| [MainMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/MainMenuController.cs) | Button handlers `ContinueGame` (`SessionSave.StartFrom` the latest save, `GameSave.TryLoadLatest`: a session's, or a joined client's copy of its place whose host is gone, which goes on as a session of its own (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), then an offline world session with the local player at `PlayerStarts`' place for this install's player id; the button is only interactable, and selected first, when `GameSave.HasSave`), `StartNewGame` (`SessionSave.StartNew`, then the starting world at its entry, offline), `HostGame` (start hosting, then from the latest save when there is one, else new, as Continue and New Game do; an error and a status line when `GameScenes` has no starting world), `JoinGame` (join the address in `joinAddressField`, prefilled with the default; once connected it empties the `SessionSave` and starts `GameFlow.StartJoinedGameAsync`, ignores repeat clicks while connecting, and shows the host's reason when it refuses the join) and `QuitGame`; the four start handlers do nothing while a `GameFlow` transition runs (the fader blocks clicks, but keyboard and gamepad Submit still reach the selected button, and a session started then would be shut down when the menu finishes fading in) or while a join is connecting; writes progress and failures to `statusText` (`Status`, internal), and on `Start` says the host is gone (`HostGoneStatus`) when a client's copy was just written with its host gone (`GameSave.TakeHostGoneNotice`) and a save is there to continue; selects Continue, or else `firstSelected`, for gamepad/keyboard navigation; logs an error in `Awake` when `continueButton` is unassigned. `OpenOptions` (the Options button) hides the `Buttons` panel and opens the options screen, unless a transition runs; closing it shows the buttons again with Options selected. Logs an error in `Awake` when the buttons panel or Options button is unassigned. |
| [OptionsMenuView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/OptionsMenuView.cs) / [KeyBindingRowView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/KeyBindingRowView.cs) | Passive options screen on the [OptionsMenu](../Assets/Prefabs/UI/OptionsMenu.prefab) prefab, built from the UI components (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5): a full-screen `Panel` (a `PanelRaised`, hidden by default) with the "Options" title, a scroll list (`List`, a `ScrollList`: a `ScrollRect` with a `RectMask2D` viewport, a scrollbar shown when the rows overflow, and its `Rows` content, a vertical layout sized by a `ContentSizeFitter`, between the title and the footer, so it fits 480 x 270 UI px; DesignSystem.md's Phase 3) filled from the `SectionHeader`, `Row`, `RowSwatch` and `RowToggle` prefabs (`AddSectionHeader`, `AddRow`, `AddSwatchRow`, `AddToggleRow`; a row: a label, a primary button showing the key or value and a secondary Reset, both buttons with a [ScrollIntoView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ScrollIntoView.cs); a swatch row also shows its swatch in a chip before the button, `SetSwatch`; a toggle row a 12 UI px box there, holding the skin's check mark while it is on, `SetToggle`, its button reading On or Off, DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), a `NoticeLine`, a secondary Reset all and a primary Back, a listening panel (a `ScrimModal` with a raised box) that blocks clicks, and a Reset all confirmation (a `Dialog`: Reset / Cancel). `Looks`: the catalog of looks the Character section offers (the prefab names `CharacterLooks.asset`; none hides the section). `Colours`: the colours its colour rows offer (the prefab names `CharacterColours.asset`; none shows no colour rows; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5). Logs an error in `Awake` for missing references. |
| [ScrollIntoView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ScrollIntoView.cs) | On a selectable in a `ScrollRect`'s content (the key-binding row's buttons): `OnSelect` (keyboard, gamepad, mouse or code) moves the content by the least that shows it whole in the viewport, top first when taller (`Reveal(list, target)`). Finds its list in `Awake`. |
| [OptionsMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/OptionsMenuController.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`) and `IMenuLayer`: in `Start` builds, when the view names a catalog of more than one look, a "Character" section with a "Look" row (`LookRow`) whose button shows the look `UserSettings` names (the first for an empty or unknown name) and keeps the next one (after the last the first), its Reset the first, following `CharacterLookChanged` (AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 2), then a part row per slot of more than one option of the first look that wears parts (`CharacterLook.Parts`; eight for the adventurer, labelled by the slot, PlayerCharacter.md's Phases 6 and 7, none for the weapon's slot, `CharacterPartSlot.FollowsWeapon`; `PartRowCount`, `GetPartRow`), its button showing the option `UserSettings` picks (the first for an empty or unknown name) and keeping the next (after the last the first, kept as no pick), its Reset the first, following `CharacterPartsChanged`, and when the view names `CharacterColours`, a colour row per choice whose dyes a look of the catalog carries or its parts do (`CharacterAnimationSet.Dyes`, `CharacterParts.Dyes`; fifteen for the adventurer, PlayerCharacter.md's Phases 5 to 7; `ColourRowCount`, `GetColourRow`), its button showing the swatch `UserSettings` picks (the choice's own for an empty or unknown name) and keeping the next of the choice's list (after the last the first, the choice's own kept as no pick), its Reset the choice's own, following `CharacterColoursChanged`; then a "Display" section with the "UI size" row (`UiSizeRow`, DesignSystem.md's Phase 3), its button showing the size the display shows (`UiSizeNames`: Normal, Large, Larger; the settings' `UiSizeStep` lowered to `UiScale.StepsThatFit`) and keeping the next that fits (after the largest Normal), its Reset Normal, following `UiSizeStepChanged` and `UiScale.Changed`, and the "Reduce flashing" and "Reduce motion" toggle rows (`ReduceFlashingRow`, `ReduceMotionRow`, DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), each button reading On or Off (`OnName`, `OffName`) with its box marked while on and turning its preference the other way, its Reset off, following `ReduceFlashingChanged` and `ReduceMotionChanged`, and the "Allies' damage numbers" row (`AllyPopupsRow`, UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), its button showing `Shown`, `Smaller` or `Hidden` (`AllyPopupsNames`) and keeping the next (after `Hidden` `Shown`), its Reset `Shown`, following `AllyPopupsChanged`; then a row per `InputBindings` action under its section header, and wires the buttons. `Open(closed)` clears the notice, selects the first row (the Look row, else the first part, colour, UI size, Reduce flashing or binding row) and pushes it on the `MenuStack`; `Close()` cancels a listening rebind, takes it off the stack and runs `closed` once. Selecting a binding starts `StartRebind` on the next tick (the click is never captured), shows the listening prompt and clears the selection; when it ends the row is reselected and a swap names the action that moved ("Class ability 1 moved to S."). A row's Reset resets that action (same notice on a swap); Reset all asks first. `HandleBack` closes the confirmation, or else the screen, and does nothing while a rebind is pending or listening (the rebind consumes Escape as its cancel). Rows follow `BindingsChanged`. |
| [GameMenuView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/GameMenuView.cs) | Passive in-game menu on the [GameMenuCanvas](../Assets/Prefabs/UI/GameMenuCanvas.prefab) prefab (which also holds an `OptionsMenu` instance), built from the UI components: a dimming full-screen `Panel` (a `Scrim`, hidden by default) with a `PanelRaised` box of four `Button`s, Resume, Options, Leave game and Quit, and a confirmation (a `Dialog`: question, a danger confirm button whose label it sets, a secondary Cancel). Logs an error in `Awake` for missing references. |
| [GameMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/GameMenuController.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`) and `IMenuLayer`. `MenuStack.MenuRequested` opens it (`Open()`: Resume selected, pushed on the stack, a `GameplayInputGate` block, and a `TimeFreeze` hold when `ISessionStatus.IsActive` is false, so solo play pauses and online play, a lone host included, never does). `HandleBack` closes the confirmation or else the menu; nothing opens or reacts during a transition or once leaving. `Close()` also closes the options screen, takes it off the stack and gives back input and time; `Dispose` does the same, so leaving or a session restart never strands them. Options opens the options screen on top of it, whose close shows the menu again with Options selected. Leave game runs `GameFlow.ShowMainMenuAsync` (`NetworkSessionLifecycle` then ends the session) and Quit runs `ApplicationExit.Quit`, each behind a confirmation for an online host ("Leaving ends the game for everyone in it."). `IsOpen`, `IsPaused`, `IsLeaving`. |
| [PlayerHudView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PlayerHudView.cs) | Passive view on `PlayerHudCanvas`, laid out in UI px and built from the UI components. Since UiRevamp.md's Phase 2 everything sits in one `CommandBar`, 378 x 60 UI px at the bottom centre, 8 above the bottom edge (DesignSystem.md's Screen regions): the ability row (Q W E R, an 8 px gap, the weapon slot, 1 and 2: seven 32 UI px slots 2 apart, 242 wide), the 242 x 13 health bar (a `Bar`; 12 until UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, which made its inside odd so the text centres) 2 above it, the `MessageLine` 1 above that, and the two status groups at its sides; one `Tooltip`, drawn last on the canvas. The slots keep the key at the top left, the cooldown seconds at the top right (swapped in UiRevamp.md's Phase 3) and the name at the bottom in Caption text, its two lines 10 UI px apart. It passes the health to its `HealthBarView` (`SetHealth(current, max, tickHealth)`, `ShowLoss`, `HideLoss`, `SetLow`, `ClearHealth`) and the weapon to its `WeaponSlotView` (`SetWeapon`). `HealthBar`, `HealthFill`, `HealthText`, `WeaponSlot` and `WeaponIcon` (internal) for tests. |
| [AbilityBarView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilityBarView.cs) / [AbilitySlotView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilitySlotView.cs) | Passive ability row of the command bar: six slots (four class, a gap, then, after the weapon slot that sits in the row since UiRevamp.md's Phase 2, two weapon), each labelled with its bound key (`SetKey`), and the message line where a failed press says why (`ShowMessage`, `HideMessage`, `MessageText` and `IsMessageShown` internal; a `MessageLine` over the health bar, hidden until a failure; Phase 1). Each slot (a `SlotAbility`) shows the ability's icon, or its name when it has none (all blanks today), or an empty state (its fill in `SurfaceEmpty` rather than `Surface`); a radial cooldown fill (from the top, counter-clockwise) with whole seconds remaining in its top right corner (its key in the top left), clear of the name (cached strings, no per-frame allocation); a failure flash in `Danger` at `flashOpacity` (0.6) at its strongest, its image enabled only while it shows; and the slot states of UiRevamp.md's Phase 1 (`SetCasting`, `SetQueued`, `SetDisabled`, read back as `IsCasting`, `IsQueued`, `IsDisabled`): its 1 px frame in `Accent` while its cast runs (`Border` otherwise), the skin's `SlotCorners` in `Accent` 1 UI px outside it while its cast waits in the buffer, and the skin's `Hatch` tiled in `ControlDisabled` inside the frame and under the name while crowd control blocks it; the brackets and the hatch are hidden images that never take the pointer. `SetAbility` resets the cooldown, the flash and the three states. The slot reads its five roles from its `UiTheme` when it wakes. Slot frames block clicks. As an `ITooltipSource` a slot gives its ability's name, numbers (`TooltipDetails`, built when it shows another ability) and description to its `TooltipTrigger`, raising `TooltipChanged` when it shows another. |
| [StatusBarView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusBarView.cs) / [StatusIconView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusIconView.cs) | Passive status groups of the command bar (UiRevamp.md's Phase 2): six `StatusIcon`s for buffs left of the abilities and six `StatusIconDebuff`s for debuffs right of them, each group a `GridLayoutGroup` of 3 x 2 20 UI px cells 2 apart (64 x 42), starting at the corner nearest the abilities, bottom row first, so the first icon of each kind sits beside the slots and they fill outwards (`Show(kind, index, definition, stacks)`, `ShowMore`, `SetElapsed`, `HideFrom(kind, index)`, `GroupCapacity(kind)`, `Icon(kind, index)` internal; the buffs' array was `icons`, `FormerlySerializedAs`). Each icon: a frame in the status colour, its icon sprite (or its two-letter abbreviation when it has none, as every test status does today), a radial fill darkening as it runs out, a frame in `Border` (in `Danger` with the skin's `Notch` in `Danger` in its top right corner on a debuff), and a stack count above 1; the letters and the count are white Caption text outlined in `Outline`, so they read on any status colour and on its darkened part. `ShowMore(count, names)` makes an icon "+n" on its theme's `Surface` (`More`), its tooltip "n more" with the names of the statuses that do not fit (cached labels and titles to 99). A shown icon's frame blocks clicks, as the ability bar's do (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5), and nothing else in it; unused icons are inactive and take no cell. As an `ITooltipSource` an icon gives its status's name and description to its `TooltipTrigger`. |
| [StatusBarPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusBarPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`): binds to the local player's `StatusEffects` through `LocalPlayerTracker` (rebinding when it changes, clearing both groups without one); on `Changed` shows each distinct active status in its kind's group in the order the statuses list them, with its stacks (independent instances counted together); a kind with more statuses than its group has icons shows all but the last that fit and "+n" on the last icon for the rest, naming them (UiRevamp.md's Phase 2; the names built only on a change). Each frame it pushes each status icon's elapsed fill (from its longest remaining instance) only when it moved. On a client the remaining times come from the mirror's countdown. |
| [WorldStatusIcons](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WorldStatusIcons.cs) | On the `WorldBar` component, the Weasel's `HealthBar`: four 5 px pips above the health bar, 1 px apart from its left end (`Overlay` layer, unlit, hidden until used), showing the unit's distinct debuffs in landing order, each in its status colour with its icon sprite or the skin's pip (each renderer a `ThemedGraphic` in `Icon` until a debuff colours it). Refreshed on `StatusEffects.Changed`, so it works on every machine. Players have no world bar, so no world icons. |
| [AbilityBarPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilityBarPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`): binds to the local player through `LocalPlayerTracker` (its `PlayerAbilities`, `PlayerController` and `StatusEffects`; rebinding when it changes, clearing the bar and hiding the message without one), refreshes slots on `SlotsChanged` (weapon changes), and pushes cooldown fill and seconds each frame only when they change. It answers every press (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1): `CastFailed` flashes the slot (fading over 0.35 s on `IClock`) and shows the outcome's words from `CastFailureMessages` on the message line for `HudSettings.MessageSeconds` (1.2 s; a newer failure restarts it), none for an empty slot, a dead player, or a buffered cast that expired or was dropped; `CastStarted` and `CastEnded` frame the slot whose cast runs; `PlayerController.BufferedCastChanged` moves the brackets to the buffered slot or takes them off; and `StatusEffects.Changed` hatches the slots the player's `Controls` block: every slot with an ability while stunned or silenced, those whose cast moves the caster (`CastMovement.Ability`) while rooted, never an empty one. Binding a player shows its running cast (`RunningSlot`), buffered slot (`BufferedSlot`) and controls at once. The orders and abilities run on the machine that owns the player, a joined client's own included, and its statuses are mirrored there, so all of it works on a client with nothing sent; the host's simulation reads none of it. Labels the slots from `IInputBindingLabels` at start and on `BindingsChanged`. `Tick` allocates nothing. |
| [PointerFeedbackPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PointerFeedbackPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`ITickable`), local only: each frame picks under the pointer with `PointerTargetPicker` and, over a living enemy, shows the hover marker under it and the attack cursor; a move press that is not on an enemy drops a move marker at the cursor, and an attack-move press drops an attack-move marker there, over an enemy too. Nothing shows while gameplay input is off, the pointer is over UI, or the local player is missing or dead. |
| [PointerFeedbackLayer](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PointerFeedbackLayer.cs) | On `PointerFeedback` in the Gameplay scene: an `ObjectPool<MoveMarker>` per marker prefab, `MarkerMove` and `MarkerAttackMove` (each prewarmed with 4; `SpawnMarker(position, attackMove)` takes from the kind's pool and `Tick` returns a finished one to it), the `HoverMarker` (a `MarkerHover` instance, its renderer off until shown) and the attack cursor (`AttackCursor.png`, hotspot at its centre, set with `Cursor.SetCursor` only when it changes and reset on disable). |
| [WorldHealthBar](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WorldHealthBar.cs) | Thin world-space bar above a unit, like League of Legends minion bars, on the [WorldBar](../Assets/Prefabs/UI/Components/WorldBar.prefab) component (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5): the skin's bar frame, 22 x 4 art px, and its bar fill, 20 x 2 inside it, which empties from the right, `ThemedGraphic`s in `HealthTrack` and `Health`. Both are 9-sliced `SpriteRenderer`s on the `Overlay` sorting layer with URP's `Sprite-Unlit-Default` material, because the areas' 2D lights do not light `Overlay` and a lit material would draw black. The fill is resized through `SpriteRenderer.size`, so its corners keep. The Weasel's bar sits 44 art px above its feet, on whole art px. Polls `Health.CurrentHealth` in `LateUpdate` with one integer compare (network syncs set health silently, so events would miss them) and hides while the unit is dead. On the Weasel prefab (and its stress-test variant), so it shows on every machine. |
| [MoveMarker](../Assets/Scripts/UI/MoveMarker.cs) | On the pooled marker prefabs (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5): `MarkerMove`, four chevrons pointing in, in `Positive`, and its variant `MarkerAttackMove`, the ring crossed by four ticks, in `Danger` (the skin's sprites on `Terrain` order 10, variants of `Marker`, as `MarkerHover` is). `Show(position)` restarts it: it shrinks and fades over 0.45 s in its own role (`Role`), read from its `UiTheme` when it wakes, or on its first show for a copy that never woke. |
| [PlayerHudPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PlayerHudPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, since UiRevamp.md's Phase 2); binds to whichever player `LocalPlayerTracker` holds (rebinding when it changes) and listens to its `Health.Damaged`/`Healed`/`Died`/`Restored` and `PlayerWeaponController.EquippedWeaponChanged`, pushing the health and the equipped weapon into the view, and each tick compares the health with what it showed (two integer compares), so a change a network sync made silently (`Health.SyncTo`) shows too. With `HudSettings`: a tick every `TickHealth` HP (0 since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3: none); a drop shows the part just lost from the health before it, for `RecentLossSeconds` (0.4), a second drop within that keeping the highest start and restarting the time, and a rise past it hiding it; at or under `LowHealthShare` (30%) the frame is `Danger` at a strength easing from full to `LowPulseFloor` (35%) and back every `LowPulseSeconds` (1 s), held full while `UserSettings.ReduceFlashing` is on. Clears the health display while there is no local player. On this machine only; `Tick` allocates nothing. |
