| [HealthBarView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/HealthBarView.cs) | On the `Bar` component (UiRevamp.md's Phase 2): the fill (`HealthPlayer`), the part just lost under it (`Lost`, `HealthLost`, hidden until shown), the frame (`Border`, or `Danger` at a given strength while low), the text ("72 / 100", outlined, drawn smaller by `PixelTextScale` since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, built only when the numbers change; "-- / --" cleared) and the ticks, copies of a hidden `TickTemplate` (the skin's `BarTick` in `HealthTrack`, 1 x 3 UI px from the top of the fill) anchored at each multiple of the tick spacing below the maximum (none for a spacing of 0, the command bar's since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), laid out only when the maximum or the spacing changes and kept for reuse. Reads `Border` and `Danger` from its `UiTheme` when it wakes. `Fill`, `Lost`, `Frame`, `Text`, `TickCount`, `Tick(i)` and `IsLow` (internal) for tests. |
| [WeaponSlotView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WeaponSlotView.cs) | The HUD's weapon slot (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1): the equipped weapon's icon in the theme's `Icon` role, or `IconMissing` without a weapon or icon (both read from its `UiTheme` when it wakes), moved here from `PlayerHudView`; as an `ITooltipSource` it gives the weapon's name and its damage, type and range (`TooltipDetails`), with no description, to the `TooltipTrigger` beside it on `WeaponSlot`, whose image now takes the pointer. |
| [HudSettings](../Assets/Scripts/UI/HudSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/UI/HudSettings.asset`, UiRevamp.md's Phase 1): how long a failed press's reason shows on the message line (`MessageSeconds`, 1.2 s), and since Phase 2 the health bar's tick (`TickHealth`: 10 HP, one Weasel hit, until Phase 3 set it to 0, which draws none), recent loss (`RecentLossSeconds`, 0.4) and low state (`LowHealthShare`, 0.3; `LowPulseSeconds`, 1; `LowPulseFloor`, 0.35), and since Phase 3 how long a notice shows on the notice line (`NoticeSeconds`, 3 s of game time). Getters only; `Create` for tests. |
| [CastFailureMessages](../Assets/Scripts/UI/CastFailureMessages.cs) | Static, the one table of failure words keyed by `CastOutcome` (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1): `For(outcome)` gives "On cooldown", "Already casting", "No target", "Stunned", "Silenced" or "Rooted", constants so showing one never allocates, and null for `EmptySlot`, `Dead`, `Expired` and `Cancelled`. |
| [TooltipDetails](../Assets/Scripts/UI/TooltipDetails.cs) | Static (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1): the numbers a tooltip shows under its title, read from the definitions. `For(ability)`: "Cooldown 2 s · Cast 0.25 s" ("Instant" for no cast time), and "Range 6" on a second line for an ability aimed at a point, a unit, or along a line of some length (not for `None` targeting or a 0-long line). `For(weapon)`: "20 Physical · Melee", or "24 Physical · Range 5" (the attack range) for a weapon that fires a projectile. Invariant culture, up to two decimals. |
| [NoticeView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/NoticeView.cs) | The HUD's notice line (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3): `Notice` on `PlayerHudCanvas`, a `Panel` (its image never taking the pointer) anchored at the top centre, 8 UI px below the top edge, in the notice band, holding a `NoticeLine` in `Accent` read against the panel's `Surface`, centred, wrapping, ending in an ellipsis past what fits; drawn before the tooltip, hidden until shown. `Show(notice)` lays the text out at the widest panel (`MaxWidth`, 240 UI px, less 4 UI px of side padding), then shrinks the panel to its widest line, an even number of UI px so it stays centred, and to its lines (at most `MaxLines`, 2) at the font's line height (11 UI px for Galmuri7) plus 2 UI px above and below, at most `MaxHeight` (24, the band's height, which two lines take whole): 15 UI px tall for one line. Showing the text it already shows (the same string) does nothing; `Hide()`. `IsShown`, `Text`, `Label` (internal) for tests. |
| [NoticePresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/NoticePresenter.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, + self; UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), on this machine only, the HUD's notice queue on its `NoticeView`: `Show(notice)` shows a notice at once when none shows, restarts its time when it already shows, and queues it once otherwise; each shows for `HudSettings.NoticeSeconds` of game time (`IClock`), then the next, and the time stands still while `GameFlow.IsTransitioning`, behind the fade. From state this machine already has, nothing sent: `GameplaySettings.WaystoneDiscoveredText` ("Waystone discovered") on `WaystoneNotices.Discovered` for the local player, as the waystone's stone wakes (`WaystoneMarkers`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 3); "Entering the" and the world's display name (`EnteringFormat`, the asset's name without one, built once per world) when `PlayerWorlds` puts the local player in another world than the one announced last (a session's start, a join, a trip to another world; a trip within the world, a respawn's included, announces nothing), or binds it already in one; and while the local player is dead, from its `Health.Died` on this machine (a client's from the host's replicated hit), "You fell. Respawning in 3" (`FellFormat`) counting whole seconds of game time down from `GameplaySettings.RespawnDelaySeconds` since that death, then "Respawning..." (`Respawning`; at once for a player bound dead) until `Health.Restored`; the death notice holds the line, the shown notice's time standing still and the queue waiting behind it. The countdown's texts are built once at start, so `Tick` allocates nothing. `Shown`, `WaitingCount`, `Holds(notice)` and `Announce(world)` (internal) for tests. |

| [UiTheme](../Assets/Scripts/UI/UiTheme.cs) / [UiColour](../Assets/Scripts/UI/UiColour.cs) / [UiTextSize](../Assets/Scripts/UI/UiTextSize.cs) | The UI theme (DesignSystem.md's Phase 4), a ScriptableObject in Gameplay (`Assets/Data/UI/UiTheme.asset`, getters only): a colour per `UiColour` role (`Colours`, each role once; `Colour(role)`) and, per `UiTextSize`, a `TextStyle` (`Style(size)`): its Galmuri font asset, its size in whole UI px and that font's outlined material. Both enums are append-only, since prefabs store them by value. A missing role or size logs an error and gives magenta or an empty style. `Create(roles, sizes)` for tests. [UiTheme.md](Generated/UiTheme.md) has the values. |
| [ThemedText](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ThemedText.cs) | On every UI text: its theme, its colour role, the role it is read against (`background`, for contrast; `Outline` outlines it) and its size. When it wakes it sets the text's font, size, colour and material (the size's outlined preset when the background is `Outline`, so outlines share materials rather than make one per text). `SetSize(size)` and `ResetSize()` for views that change it (the periodic popups). Each time TMP builds the text's mesh (`OnPreRenderText`, subscribed while enabled), `SnapToPixels` moves each glyph by what is left of its origin under a whole UI px counted from the root canvas's corner, so the pixel font's pixels cover whole display pixels wherever the rect lands; a text drawn at another scale than its canvas (`PixelTextScale`) snaps to whole display pixels instead (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3). No `Update`. |
| [PixelTextScale](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PixelTextScale.cs) | UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, on the `Bar` component's text (the command bar's health numbers): draws its text smaller than the zoom while its pixel font stays crisp. Each font pixel covers the whole number of display pixels nearest `TargetScale` (0.75) of the zoom's, rounded half up, at least two unless the zoom is smaller and at most the zoom's (`ScaleFor(zoom, target)`): its scale is 2/3 at x3, 3/4 at x4, 4/5 at x5, 5/6 at x6 and 1 at x2. Injected with `UiScale` through the Gameplay scope's auto-inject objects (`PlayerHudCanvas`), it follows `Changed` while enabled and rebuilds its text, whose `ThemedText` then snaps to whole display pixels; without a zoom it keeps its saved scale. `Apply(zoom)` (internal) for tests. |
| [ThemedGraphic](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ThemedGraphic.cs) | On every UI Image and RawImage that is not a control's target, and on the world UI's sprites (the world bar, its status pips, the markers): its theme and role; when it wakes it sets the colour of its Image, RawImage or SpriteRenderer. Views that recolour at runtime (the status frames, the slot frames) do so after. A bar's fill names its `Track`, the themed graphic it is read against (the `Bar` component's fill its root, the `WorldBar`'s fill its frame), so `UiThemeUsage` measures the pair (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1); empty for every other graphic. |
| [ThemedSelectable](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ThemedSelectable.cs) | On every Selectable: when it wakes it sets its colour block from the control roles: its own normal role (`Normal`: `ControlNormal` for a primary button, `ControlSecondary` for a secondary one, `Danger` for a danger one; DesignSystem.md's Phase 5), and the shared `ControlHighlighted`, `ControlPressed`, `ControlDisabled`, and `Accent` for selected (`ControlColours(theme, block, normal)`), its target graphic left white so the block's colours show as they are. |
| [HudTooltip](../Assets/Scripts/UI/HudTooltip.cs) / [TooltipTrigger](../Assets/Scripts/UI/TooltipTrigger.cs) / [ITooltipSource](../Assets/Scripts/UI/ITooltipSource.cs) | The HUD's tooltips (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5), local to this machine. `HudTooltip`, on the `Tooltip` component (one on `PlayerHudCanvas`, drawn last, hidden until shown): a raised panel with a title (`Accent`), a line of numbers (`Text`, since UiRevamp.md's Phase 1) and a description (`TextMuted`), the last two wrapped at 150 UI px and hidden when empty, laid out at its content's size; `Show(target, title, details, description)` puts it 2 UI px above the middle of the target's top edge, and since UiRevamp.md's Phase 2 above the top of the rect its trigger keeps clear (`clearOf`: the `CommandBar`, for every trigger in it, so the health bar stays in view), or below the target's bottom edge when there is no room above, moved inside its canvas, on whole UI px; `Hide(target)` hides it only when it shows that target's. None of its graphics is a raycast target. `TooltipTrigger`, on the `SlotAbility` and `StatusIcon` components (`StatusIconDebuff` included) and the HUD's weapon slot (the HUD's instances name its tooltip): shows the tooltip for the `ITooltipSource` on its object (`AbilitySlotView`, `WeaponSlotView`, `StatusIconView`: `TryGetTooltip(out title, out details, out description)`, false with nothing to show; a status has no numbers) while the pointer is over it (`IPointerEnterHandler`, `IPointerExitHandler`) or it is selected (`ISelectHandler`, `IDeselectHandler`; nothing selects a HUD element yet), follows the source's `TooltipChanged` while enabled, and hides it when disabled. |

The themed components and the views reference the theme asset directly, so pooled prefabs that are never injected work too, and read it once when they wake; nothing reads it per frame and no tool writes it into prefabs or scenes, which keep the values they were converted with for the editor's sake. The transition fader in `Main` is not themed: it fades to black, in Core. Every UI text is in Galmuri, a pixel font (SIL OFL 1.1, Lee Minseo): `Assets/Fonts` holds Latin subsets of its 7, 9, 11 and 14 sizes (printable ASCII, Latin-1, dashes, curly quotes, the bullet and the ellipsis, made with fontTools from Galmuri 2.40.4, with a zero-width space added for TMP's empty input field; `Galmuri - OFL.txt` beside them), a static SDF font asset of each (`<name> SDF.asset`) and an outlined material preset of each (`<name> SDF Outline.mat`), all written by `UiFonts` (Editor tools). `LiberationSans SDF` stays TMP's default for new text and is no longer used by the UI; a character outside the Galmuri atlases falls back through TMP's settings to LiberationSans and its dynamic fallback, which then gains glyphs (and a git diff); nothing should reference the fallback directly.

The UI's art is the skin `UiSkinRecipe` writes into `Assets/Sprites/UI/Skin` (Editor tools), and its screens are built from the component prefabs in `Assets/Prefabs/UI/Components` (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5; its Components table lists them): `Panel`, `PanelRaised`, `Scrim` and `ScrimModal`; `Button`, `ButtonSecondary`, `ButtonDanger` and `ButtonLarge`; `Dialog`; `SectionHeader`; `Row`, `RowSwatch` and `RowToggle` (Phase 6); `ScrollList`; `NoticeLine` (wrapping since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3) and its variant `MessageLine` (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1); `Slot` and `SlotAbility`; `StatusIcon` and `StatusIconDebuff` (UiRevamp.md's Phase 2); `Tooltip`; `Bar` and `WorldBar`; `PopupText`; `Marker`, `MarkerMove`, `MarkerAttackMove` and `MarkerHover`. A component carries its themed components, text size and font, so one change to it reaches every use.

Damage popups are under Combat.

## Player

Prefab: [Player.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Player/Player.prefab) (`PlayerController` with `PlayerControlSettings`, `PlayerMotor2D`, `PlayerWeaponController` (the `WeaponCatalog`, starting with the sword), `PlayerAbilities` with `Class_Placeholder`, `PlayerCastVisuals` (its casts on its visuals), `PlayerLook` (`CharacterLooks.asset`, whose adventurer wears its parts, and `CharacterColours.asset` over its visuals and palette, showing the gear of its `PlayerWeaponController`'s weapon), `Health` (100 HP), `DamageReceiver` (`Profile_Player`, popups 1.56 above the feet), `DisableOnDeath`, `StatusEffects`, `StatusVisuals`, `NetworkObject`, `NetworkTransform` with owner authority syncing x/y position only (`LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas, see Transform smoothing), `PlayerNetworkSync`, `NetworkHealth`, `NetworkStatusEffects`, `PlayerWorldSync`, `HitFeedback` (height 0.62, the adventurer's middle, holding through its character visuals), and a `Visuals` child on the feet holding the `SpriteRenderer` (`SpritePixelLit.mat`; nothing in it is scaled, so its pixels are the terrain's), its `CharacterPalette` (the player's colours), and `CharacterVisuals` with the adventurer's set and `Locomotion_Player`, which the motor and the weapon controller feed, see Character animation, with a `CastLight` child, the Light 2D of its casts' glow, and no lantern renderer or light since the player's lantern was removed; no Mecanim animator), referenced by `GameplayLifetimeScope`, spawned at runtime by `PlayerSpawner`, and listed in `Assets/DefaultNetworkPrefabs.asset`. Offline the networking components stay unspawned and do nothing.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayerController](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerController.cs) | Wires the command source, `PlayerOrders`, `PlayerMotor2D` and the weapon. Each `Update` (internal `Tick`): read a `PlayerCommand`, tick the orders (which start casts on its `PlayerAbilities`) with the motor's state, the clock, the equipped weapon's `AttackRange` and the radius its attack sweeps toward a target (`AttackSightRadius`: its projectile's for a ranged weapon, 0 for a melee one), then apply the output (`MoveTo`/`Stop` on the motor, `SetAim`) and drive the weapon's attack: a stun, a running cast or a cast that started on the tick (`PlayerOrderOutput.CastStarted`: an instant cast is already over by then) cancels it (`CancelAttack`); otherwise the order the command gave (`PlayerOrderOutput.Issued`), or the orders asking the motor to move (a chase), reaches it as `Order(move)`, a move order or Stop counting as a move; a swing request calls `TryAttack(aim, the order's target)`; and `Tick` moves the attack on, after the swing so a hit can land on the tick it starts. It passes its `StatusEffects` controls into the order context, and after the tick their `MovementScale` (slow; 0 while stunned or rooted) to the motor's `SetSpeedScale`, or 0 while an attack runs, so the motor holds still without stalling (as under a root) and a move or chase given meanwhile waits for the attack to end; on a client the statuses are the owner's mirror of the host's. Remote copies (`SetSimulatesMovement(false)`) do nothing in `Tick` and only show replicated movement. Death (`OnDisable`, through `DisableOnDeath`) and `Teleport` clear the orders and cancel the attack (`ClearOrders`), so a respawn starts Idle. No source on a simulated player = idle, with an error logged in `Start`. Keeps `SetCommandSource`, `CurrentMove`, `FacingDirection`, `SimulatesMovement`, `Face`, `Teleport`; adds `ShowRemoteMovement`, `ClearOrders`, `CurrentOrder`, `ControlSettings`, and for the HUD `BufferedSlot` and `BufferedCastChanged`, which subscribes to the orders' event (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1). Gizmos (Debug toggle) for the ability side of its orders: a line and box to the target a CastWhenInRange is approaching, labelled with the slot number and ability, and a "buffered" label under the player. |
| [PlayerOrders](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrders.cs) | Plain class: the order state machine (Idle, Move, Attack, AttackMove, CastWhenInRange, Casting, plus a paused order and a buffered cast), see Player orders below. [PlayerOrderTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrderTypes.cs) holds `PlayerOrderKind`, `PlayerMotorRequest`, `PlayerOrderIssue`, the `PlayerOrderContext` input and the `PlayerOrderOutput` (motor request + destination, aim, swing, the order the command gave, and whether a cast started on the tick). |
| [PlayerMotor2D](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerMotor2D.cs) | Owns a `PathFollower2D` (configured from `PlayerControlSettings`, straight-line shortcut on). `MoveTo(point)` paths on its own navigation, `PlayerNavigation.Of(transform)` (its world's `WorldNavigation`; the follower is reconfigured when that changes), with partial paths allowed (an unwalkable point ends at the nearest walkable cell; a point beyond the loaded chunks ends at the loaded edge; on a standalone grid, as in tests, a point with no walkable cell within the grid's own nearest-cell search radius is walked back along the line toward the player until a walkable cell); without navigation (a player in no world) it goes in a straight line. `FixedUpdate` sets `Rigidbody2D.linearVelocity` from the follower (`Time.fixedDeltaTime`) at the settings' move speed times a speed scale (`SetSpeedScale`; the follower gets the scaled speed, so at 0 the body holds still, stall time stays 0 and the path is kept), facing follows movement. `Update`, and `Stop` (so a stopped or dead player stands), feed its `CharacterVisuals` the velocity (`Velocity`, units per second) and facing. `Stop`, `Face`, `SetAim(aiming, direction)` (while aiming the player faces the aim and movement does not turn it), `Teleport`, `ReachedDestination`, `StalledTime`, `CurrentMove` (the move as a share of the settings' move speed: the actual speed over the base speed, 0.6 when chilled), `Velocity`, `FacingDirection`; `ShowRemoteMovement(move, facing)` for remote copies, whose body it never moves. Sets up the body (no gravity, frozen rotation, interpolation) in `Awake`. |
| [PlayerControlSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerControlSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/PlayerControlSettings.asset`): move speed 5, waypoint reach and arrival 0.1, terrain profile `TerrainMovement_Default`, stuck timeout 0.75 s, hold re-evaluation 0.15 s, chase repath 0.5 s or when the target moved 0.5, cast buffer window 0.4 s, attack-move acquire range 3, pick radius 0.35, enemy layers `Enemy`, ally layers `Player`, obstacle layers `Obstacles` (the walls, which block an attack's line of sight; a test checks every asset keeps them). `FollowerSettings` builds the path follower settings. |
| [PlayerCommand](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerCommand.cs) / [IPlayerCommandSource](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IPlayerCommandSource.cs) | One frame of intent in world space (`PointerWorld`, `Target` under the pointer for the move button, `AbilityTarget` under it for the ability pressed (picked with that ability's unit filter; `Target` when none is given), `MovePressed`, `MoveHeld`, `StopPressed`, `HasAbility`/`AbilitySlot` (the lowest slot pressed this frame), `AttackMovePressed`; `default` is "nothing") and where the local player gets it from. Only the local player reads commands. |
| [LocalPlayerCommandSource](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayerCommandSource.cs) | Builds commands from `IPlayerInput` with the Main-scope `Camera` (screen to world) and `PointerTargetPicker`: while the move button is pressed or held, the closest unit of either team as the `Target`; on an ability press, the closest unit the unit filter of the local player's ability in that slot accepts (`LocalPlayerTracker`, its `Abilities`; either team for an ability not aimed at a unit, an empty slot or no local player) as the `AbilityTarget`, so an ally-only cast picks the ally under the cursor over a closer enemy. A press over UI is dropped along with the hold that follows it; a hold that started on the world keeps steering over UI. The attack-move key, like the ability keys, are passed on wherever the pointer is. Nothing while gameplay input is disabled. `PlayerBinder` gives it to the local player only. |
| [PointerTargetPicker](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PointerTargetPicker.cs) | `Pick(worldPoint, filter)`: non-allocating `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` within the pick radius on the layers of the teams the `AbilityUnitFilter` accepts (`Any`, the default: the enemy and ally layers; `Enemy` or `Ally`: that team's), returning the living unit of those teams whose collider centre is closest. Enemies need a `DamageReceiver` (alive `Health`); allies must be registered, living players. No line-of-sight test. The collider to `Health` lookup for enemies is cached by an [EnemyHealthLookup](../Assets/Scripts/Player/EnemyHealthLookup.cs) (up to 64 colliders, cleared when full; destroyed entries are looked up again), so the hover pick every frame does no component lookups once warm. |
| [UnitTarget](../Assets/Scripts/Player/UnitTarget.cs) | A picked or ordered unit: `Health` (identity), `Collider`, `UnitTeam` (`None`, `Enemy`, `Ally`). |
| [IUnitQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IUnitQueries.cs) / [PlayerUnitQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerUnitQueries.cs) | What orders ask about a target: alive, position, and collider-to-collider distance from the player (`Collider2D.Distance`, zero when overlapping or when the target is the player's own collider, as for an ally cast on oneself, which Unity refuses to measure; centre distance when a collider is missing or disabled); `InSight(target, radius)`, whether an attack reaches the target past the walls: a `Physics2D.CircleCast` of the radius (a ray for 0) on the settings' obstacle layers from the player's feet toward the middle of the target's collider, as an arrow flies, up to where it first touches that collider (true without obstacle layers, for the player's own body and for a target it touches); and `NearestEnemyInRange(point, range)` for attack moves: a non-allocating `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` around the player's collider on the settings' enemy layers (no triggers), keeping living enemies (through its own `EnemyHealthLookup`) within `range` by collider distance, and returning the one whose position is closest to `point`. |
| [PlayerWeaponController](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerWeaponController.cs) | Holds the equipped `PlayerWeapon`, the `WeaponCatalog` that names weapons on the wire (`Catalog`, `WeaponIndex`: the equipped weapon's index, -1 outside it; `WeaponFor(index)`: the catalog's weapon, or the equipped one for an index outside it; `StartingWeapon`, which must be in the catalog: an error in `Awake` otherwise), its `AttackState` on the injected `IClock` (internal `State`), the injected `IRandom` that picks its next attack, and the character's `CharacterVisuals` (`visuals`, found under the player when unassigned). `Equip(weapon)` ends any attack (and any attack a copy plays), looks the clips of the weapon's attacks up in the visuals' set (an error when the set lacks the first attack's; an attack whose clip the set lacks plays the first's, as the Bandit's set holds each weapon's first attack only; the equipped and the catalog weapons' clips are looked up once and kept) and starts the weapon's attacks over from its first; the weapon is in hand only in those clips, and the player's `PlayerLook` shows its gear otherwise (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7). `EquipOnCopy(weapon)`, the owner's switch reaching a copy, does the same but leaves the attack the copy shows alone: the owner already sent the cancel of the attack its switch ended, and an attack it made after the switch, whose message names its weapon, can arrive before the switch. Internal `EquipFromCatalog(index)` (tests). `TryAttack(direction, target)`, a swing request toward the direction (the facing when it is zero) at an optional target unit (`AttackTarget` while the attack runs), starts the weapon's next attack when the state allows: its first after it was equipped, then one of the attacks other than the last at random (the next in order without a random source), never the same twice in a row, so a weapon's two attacks alternate (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 4). The attack runs on the weapon's timeline and cooldown divided by `AttackSpeed` (1 until statuses scale it), plays its clip on the visuals and raises `AttackStarted(attack, direction, attackSpeed)` with its index. `Tick`, which `PlayerController` drives after the orders, moves the attack on: at contact a melee weapon's hit resolves through `AttackService.Resolve` around where the player stands, and a ranged weapon's projectile leaves through `ProjectileService.Launch` toward the middle of a living target's collider (along the attack without one); on a client a melee weapon's hit is also predicted there (`AttackService.Predict`), so the owner's contact frame holds at once (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4); `AttackContacted(attack, direction, position, target)` is raised with that direction and the attack's target (even when the same update also ended the attack, when `AttackTarget` is already none); a recovery an earlier order cut short stops showing. `Order(move)` passes an order to the cancel rules and `CancelAttack()` ends the attack (a stun, a cast, a teleport, death, another weapon); either, when it ends one early, stops the visuals and raises `AttackCancelled`, which a chained attack does not. `OnDisable` (death) cancels and stops the visuals' attack. It only ever stops its attack (`StopAction(CharacterActionKind.Attack)`), so a cast showing on the same visuals plays on. On other machines: `PlayRemoteAttack(weaponIndex, attack, direction, speed)` plays the clip of the attack the owner made with that weapon (nothing for an index past its attacks), its windup held until contact, at most the character's contact wait past it (`CharacterLocomotionSettings.ContactWaitSeconds`), after which the copy goes back to its locomotion and a later contact still lands its hit, `PlayRemoteContact`, `StopRemoteAttack` (visual only: no state, cooldown, hit or event), and `ResolveRemoteContact(weaponIndex, attack, direction, position, target)` lands the contact around where the owner stood: a melee hit resolves only on the host (`AttackService` checks authority), and a projectile is flown on every machine, hitting only on the host, every attack of a weapon landing alike; ignored for an attack the weapon lacks and while this machine sees the player dead. `IsAttacking`, `AttackPhase`, `AttackSpeed`, event `EquippedWeaponChanged`. |
| [PlayerHandle](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerHandle.cs) | One player as other systems see it: `Transform`, `Health`, `IsAlive`. |
| [IPlayerRegistry](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IPlayerRegistry.cs) / [PlayerRegistry](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerRegistry.cs) | Every player in the session: `Players`, `Contains`, `AnyAlive`, events `PlayerAdded`/`PlayerRemoved`. `Add`/`Remove` (ignore null and duplicates) are on the concrete class only. Used by mobs and death handling. |
| [PlayerSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerSpawner.cs) | Instantiates the player prefab through `IObjectResolver` (injecting its components) and moves it into the Gameplay scene so it survives trips. `SpawnLocalPlayer`: offline binds it as the local player directly; when hosting, spawns it as the host's network player object. `SpawnRemotePlayer(clientId, world, position, savedWeapon, discovered)` (host only): places a player owned by that client on a free entry slot at the position (`EntrySlots`, so players joining at one entry stand apart), gives its `PlayerWorldSync` the world to start in and the waystones its player discovered (`SetSpawnWaystones`), has it equip its saved weapon and spawns it, unless the client already has one. `PlayerNetworkPrefab`, `PlayersScene`. |
| [GameplayPlayers](../Assets/Scripts/Player/GameplayPlayers.cs) | Gameplay entry point. Offline: spawns the local player. Online: `RegisterNetworkPrefabs` (build callback) registers the player prefab with the session so client copies are injected from this scope and placed in the Gameplay scene; in `Start` the host spawns its own player, one for every joined client and then each newly joined client, where `PlayerStarts` places it by its player id, with the weapon and the waystones the `SessionSave` has for that id, once the world session has loaded that world around the place (`IWorldSession.PrepareAsync`, at once when it already is; one wait per client, dropped when the client leaves or the scope goes); a client sends joined (`NetworkSession.NotifyJoined`). Unregisters on dispose. |
| [PlayerBinder](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerBinder.cs) | `BindLocal(controller)`: local command source, movement on, its `PlayerLook` following the local settings (offline and online alike), registers, assigns it to `LocalPlayerTracker`. `BindRemote(controller)`: no command source, movement off, registers. `Unbind(handle)`. It places nobody: the world session does (its start, trips, respawns). |
| [LocalPlayerTracker](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayerTracker.cs) | The current `LocalPlayer` (null until this machine's player exists) and a `Changed` event. |
| [PlayerNetworkSync](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerNetworkSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on the player. On spawn every machine seeds the player's `CharacterVisuals` idle animations with its network id (`SeedIdleAnimations`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3: the same picks everywhere, nothing on the wire), and the owner binds as local and forwards its weapon's attacks (Animation.md's Networking; owner-invoked, run on everyone else), each naming the weapon it was made with by its catalog index, so a message that overtakes a weapon switch still plays and lands with it: `AttackStarted` as `AttackStartedRpc(weapon, attack, direction, attackSpeed)` (the copy plays the windup, held until contact), `AttackContacted` as `AttackContactRpc(weapon, attack, direction, attackerPosition, target, hasTarget)` (the target the contact event names; the copy plays the contact and lands it with `ResolveRemoteContact` around where the owner stood, as casts do: the host resolves a melee hit, and every machine flies a projectile toward the target, a `NetworkObjectReference` of the attack's target, hitting only on the host; so only the host resolves hits, each attack at most once, and copies' attacks are visual only; the host's own player resolves locally) and `AttackCancelled` as `AttackCancelledRpc` (the copy stops). The owner also writes its equipped weapon's catalog index to an owner-written `NetworkVariable<int>` when it switches (nothing while it still holds its starting weapon, which the unwritten -1 stands for, and nothing for a weapon outside the catalog); every other machine equips that weapon on its copy (`EquipOnCopy`, which leaves the attack the copy shows alone: the variable goes out on the next network tick while the attack messages go at once, so an attack made on the frame of a switch can arrive first), at spawn too, so a late joiner sees the current weapon, and the copy's abilities follow; the host writes the weapon a client's player holds in the save (`SetSavedWeapon`, given before the spawn) to a server-written `NetworkVariable<int>` as it spawns, which the owner equips (at its spawn, or when the value arrives) and every copy shows until the owner writes one of its own; likewise the owner writes the catalog index of the look its `PlayerLook` shows (`PlayerLook.Chosen`; the unwritten -1 keeps a copy's own) to an owner-written `NetworkVariable<int>`, which every other machine shows on its copy (`PlayerLook.Show`), at spawn too, and its colours (`PlayerLook.ColoursChosen`, a `CharacterColourPicks`; the unwritten default is the look's own colours) to an owner-written `NetworkVariable<CharacterColourPicks>`, which every other machine shows on its copy (`PlayerLook.ShowColours`), at spawn too, so a late joiner sees them (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5), and its parts (`PlayerLook.PartsChosen`, a `CharacterPartPicks`; the unwritten default is the look's own parts) to an owner-written `NetworkVariable<CharacterPartPicks>`, which every other machine shows on its copy (`PlayerLook.ShowParts`, before the look), at spawn too (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6); its weapon's gear needs nothing of its own on the wire: every machine's `PlayerLook` shows the gear of the weapon its copy equips (Phase 7), the picks' weapon slot left alone; other machines bind it as remote, make its body kinematic without interpolation, and every frame pass the owner-written `move`/`facing` `NetworkVariable`s to `PlayerController.ShowRemoteMovement`, which the copy's `CharacterVisuals` animate (owner updates them when they change by more than 0.01; `move` is the motor's `CurrentMove`, the actual speed over the base speed, so a slowed or hasted copy picks the owner's gait and playback rate). The owner also forwards `PlayerAbilities.CastStarted` as `CastRpc(slot, weapon, point, direction, target, hasTarget)` (owner-invoked, runs on everyone else; the target is a `NetworkObjectReference` resolved with this object's `NetworkManager`, an ally when it has a `PlayerController`, otherwise an enemy). Receivers call `PlayerAbilities.PlayRemoteCast` with the weapon the cast was made with, which only the host's `AbilityService` applies. When the owner's cast completes, it sends `CastCompletedRpc(slot, weapon, point, direction, target, hasTarget, casterPosition)` to every other machine (owner-invoked; KnownGaps.md's Phase 4, where a client's went to the host alone), with the weapon the cast started with (a running cast keeps its ability across a weapon switch) and the owner's own position at completion: each copy shows the cast's release on the update it arrives (`PlayerAbilities.CompleteRemoteCast`), and then the host calls `PlayerAbilities.ResolveRemoteCast` with that weapon, so its `AbilityService` resolves the effect around where the owner was rather than around the host's lagging copy, and the release shows on the update the cast lands; the host's own player resolves directly and sends the completion for the clients' copies. An interrupted cast (its `CastEnded` not completed: a stun, a silence, death, a teleport) sends `CastCancelledRpc` to everyone else instead, whose copy stops it (`PlayerAbilities.StopRemoteCast`); it never lands. The copy's facing comes from the replicated `facing` (the owner aims during the cast), as it does for attacks. The owner's teleports (`PlayerController.Teleported`: arriving at an entry, a session's start) go through `NetworkTransform.Teleport`, so the other machines snap the copy there instead of interpolating, which across worlds would slide it thousands of units. `Handle` (internal): the player's registry handle once bound. Unbinds on despawn. |
| [PlayerWorldSync](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerWorldSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on the player, its `ITripLink` and `IWaystoneLink`. In `OnNetworkPostSpawn` (once `PlayerNetworkSync` bound the player): the host puts the player in the world `SetSpawnWorld` gave it (a client's player) and writes a server-only `NetworkVariable<PlayerWorldState>` (world id, trip count, trip entry) from `PlayerWorlds` now and on every change, telling `IClientReadiness.BeginTrip` about a client's player with the trip's world; every client applies it to its own `PlayerWorlds` (`Apply`; an unlisted world id logs an error), and the owner hands a new trip to `NetworkTripAuthority.Deliver` with the entry's position. `SendTripRequest(exitId, position)` (owner) sends `RequestTripRpc` to the host, which checks it through `NetworkTripAuthority.Approve`. `SendTripArrived(trip)` (owner, once it faded in at the end of a trip) sends `TripArrivedRpc(trip)` to the host, which ends the trip's protection (`NetworkTripAuthority.ReportArrived`). `SendWaystoneDiscovered(worldId, entry)` (host, for a client's player) adds the waystone to the player's discoveries and sends `WaystoneDiscoveredRpc` to the owner, which raises it on its `WaystoneNotices` for the popup. The discoveries (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3) are a `NetworkList<NetworkWaystone>` read by the owner only and written by the host: filled in `OnNetworkSpawn` from what `SetSpawnWaystones` gave it (the save's, before the spawn), each waystone by its world's place in Game Scenes and its entry's in that world, a world or entry the build lacks left out; the owner, a client, hands it to its `KnownWaystones` (`Replicate`, as world ids and names) at its spawn and on every change. A respawn reaches the owner as a trip like any other. `State`, `ReplicatedWaystoneCount` (internal). |
| [LocalPlayer](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayer.cs) | The player this machine controls: `Controller`, `Weapon`, `Abilities`, `Handle`, `Transform`. Reached through `LocalPlayerTracker` by the camera, HUD, the stress spawner and the local command source. |
| [PlayerDeathHandler](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerDeathHandler.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`); does nothing on a client (`IGameAuthority`). Listens to `Health.Died` of every registered player (follows `PlayerAdded`/`PlayerRemoved`) and runs each dead player's respawn on its own (WorldStreaming.md's Death and respawn): `GameplaySettings.RespawnDelaySeconds` of game time (`IClock`) after the death, and once a trip the player was on has arrived, `PlayerRespawner` sends it to its waystone; while it travels there it stays dead, and once it stands there it is restored. A player in no world, or in a world without entries, is restored where it stands. No rule looks at the other players: nothing restarts when every player is dead at once. A player leaving the registry, or alive again, is forgotten; `PendingCount` and `IsTravellingToWaystone` (internal) for tests. |
| [PlayerRespawner](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerRespawner.cs) | What a respawn does, where game state is decided. `TryFindWaystone(player)`: the player's world and the waystone it respawns at (`WaystoneRules.TryFindNearest` from where it stands, among the waystones its player id discovered in the `SessionSave`, `PlayerIds`). `TrySendToWaystone(player, out respawn)`: a trip within its world to that waystone through `NetworkTripAuthority.Send` (the arrival held, so this machine streams the waystone's chunks at once in whatever world, the player put in the world for one more trip, `Approved` raised: the host's own player travels through `LocalTripTraveller`, a client's hears of it through its networked world and fades, moves itself, waits for the chunks and reports them with the new trip count). `HasArrived(player, respawn)`: in that world within two cells of the entry (`TripArrivals.HasArrived`), the entry's chunk loaded here and, for a client's player, reported by its client during that trip (`IClientReadiness.IsClientReady(owner, chunk)`: a chunk it held before the trip counts only once reported again, KnownGaps.md's Phase 4). `TryRestore(player)`: `Health.Restore()` for a dead registered player, which re-enables it through `DisableOnDeath` and reaches every machine through `NetworkHealth`; it moves nobody. `IsTravelling(player)`: a trip under way (`TripArrivals.IsHeld`). |

Config: [GameplaySettings](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/GameplaySettings.cs) (`Assets/Data/GameplaySettings.asset`): respawn delay (3 s of game time), entry slot spacing (0.6, `EntrySlots`), autosave interval (60 s of game time, `GameSaveRecorder`), under Trips the trip protection's time limit (10 s of game time, `TripProtection`, also how long a respawn's arrival waits for the player to come back before its fade in), under Waystones the discovery reach (2 cells along each axis, `WaystoneDiscovery`) and the discovery notice's text ("Waystone discovered", `NoticePresenter`; its popup colour went with the popup in UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), and under Outfits the composed outfits nobody wears that `CharacterOutfits` keeps (2) and the outfit sprites it makes a frame (500; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7).

### Player orders

[PlayerOrders](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrders.cs) runs on the machine that moves the player (the owner). It holds one current order, at most one paused order while a cast runs, and at most one buffered cast. Each tick: Stop; a move press (or a held button every `HoldReevaluateInterval`) issues an order; an attack-move press issues an attack move (after a move press on the same tick, so it wins); an ability press is handled; a buffered cast older than `CastBufferWindow` is dropped; then the current order runs. A held button only keeps steering a move or attack: unlike a press, it never replaces a CastWhenInRange approach or drops a buffered cast (the camera follows the player, so the cursor's world point changes at every re-evaluation). One `fresh` flag marks the order that has not started yet (so a move ignores the motor's stale arrival); it is set for new orders, for the order resumed after a cast, and for the paused order when a buffered cast fires, so a buffered `Continue` cast restarts the paused move instead of reading the stopped motor as arrived. The output's aim is zero whenever there is no aim, so a swing at a target on top of the player falls back to the facing. The output also reports the order the tick's command gave (`Issued`), which the weapon's cancel rules read: `Move` for a move order (a press, or a held button steering to a new point) or Stop, `Other` for an attack order on another unit, an attack move or an ability press that starts a cast or an approach, and `None` for anything else (clicking or holding over the unit already attacked, an empty command, a failed press). The orders keep asking for swings; the weapon's attack state decides whether one starts (see Player weapon and attacks).

| Order | Issued by | Runs | Ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| Idle | Stop, or an order ending | Nothing | Any new order |
| Move(point) | Press (or hold) on the ground, an ally, or a dead enemy | `MoveTo(point)` once; the arrival check is skipped on the tick it is issued, because the motor still reports the previous path | Motor reached its destination, or stalled for `StuckTimeout` |
| Attack(target) | Press (or hold) on a living enemy; re-issuing the same target keeps the chase as it is | In `AttackRange` (collider distance) and in sight (`IUnitQueries.InSight` with the attack's sweep radius: no wall between): `Stop` once, aim at the target, request a swing every tick (the weapon cooldown decides). Out of range or behind a wall: `MoveTo(target)` when the chase starts, every `RepathInterval`, or once the target moved `TargetMoveRepathDistance` from the last goal, so a bow walks round a wall before it shoots. Walls and distance never cancel it. | The target dies or is destroyed |
| AttackMove(point) | The attack-move key, at the cursor's point | Without a target, every tick it takes the living enemy within `AttackMoveAcquireRange` of the player, or the weapon's `AttackRange` when that is longer (a bow's 5; collider distance), that is closest to the point (`IUnitQueries.NearestEnemyInRange`); with none, it walks to the point like a Move. With a target it engages it exactly like Attack (chase, stop once in range, aim, swing). Taking a target or losing it restarts the movement (the order is fresh again), so after a kill it walks on at once, ignoring the motor's stale arrival, or takes the next enemy in range. Paused during a `Continue` cast it walks, takes targets and chases, but never swings. | Arrived at the point, or stalled for `StuckTimeout`, with nothing in range |
| CastWhenInRange(slot, target) | A `Unit` cast whose target is beyond the ability's range | Chases the target like Attack | In range: the cast starts (and this order is gone, so the cast resumes into Idle). Target lost: Idle, reported as `NoTarget`. Another order replaces it. |
| Casting(slot, aim) | A cast that started | Faces the aim (unless the ability targets `None`). `Stop`/`Ability` movement: the motor is stopped at the start. `Continue`: the paused order keeps running its movement (a paused attack chases but never swings). | After `CastTime` (instant casts end on the tick they start): a buffered cast fires if it is still valid, keeping the paused order paused; otherwise the paused order resumes as a fresh order (a move repaths, an attack re-chases). |

Ability presses (`PressAbility`):
1. The caster's `CanCast` fails (`EmptySlot`, `OnCooldown`, `Dead`), or crowd control blocks the ability (`Silenced`; `Rooted` for `CastMovement.Ability`): reported, nothing else happens. The same control check runs again whenever a cast is about to start (an approach in range, a buffered cast).
2. The aim is built from the command: `None` ignores it; `Direction` aims at the cursor; `Point` clamps the cursor to the range; `Unit` needs the command's `AbilityTarget`, the unit under the cursor picked with the ability's unit filter, to be alive and accepted by that filter (`Enemy`, `Ally` (registered players, the caster included) or `Any`), otherwise `NoTarget`.
3. During a cast: a non-bufferable ability fails with `CastInProgress`; a bufferable one becomes the buffered cast with that aim, replacing (and reporting as `Cancelled`) an older one.
4. Otherwise the cast starts at once (`IAbilityCaster.TryCast`), pausing the current order, or turns into CastWhenInRange when a unit is out of range.

`BufferedCastChanged` (UiRevamp.md's Phase 1) is raised with the slot when a cast is buffered and with -1 whenever the buffer clears: replaced by a newer press (-1, then the new slot), dropped, expired, or fired when the running cast ends; the ability bar draws its brackets from it.

Rules: a new order replaces the current one, except during a cast, when it replaces the paused order instead; a click (not a hold) drops the buffered cast (`Cancelled`). Stop drops the buffered cast and, during a cast, the paused order; the started cast itself runs to its end, then the player is Idle. A buffered cast is re-checked when it fires (cooldown, a dead unit target: reported and dropped, and the paused order resumes); a buffered unit cast whose target moved out of range becomes CastWhenInRange, replacing the paused order. An empty command (gameplay input disabled during transitions) makes no new order and lets the current one run on. Crowd control arrives in `PlayerOrderContext.Controls`: when a stun starts the orders `Clear` and ask the motor to stop once, and while it lasts commands are ignored, ability presses report `Stunned` and nothing swings (the player is Idle afterwards); when a silence starts a running cast is cancelled (its cooldown stays spent, its effect never lands), the buffered cast is dropped (`Cancelled`) and the paused order resumes; a root changes nothing in the orders, since the motor holds still without stalling, so a move or chase waits for it to end. A cast whose cast time runs out completes (`IAbilityCaster.CompleteCast`); one that is stopped early is cancelled (`CancelCast`). `Clear` (death, teleports, becoming a remote copy) cancels a running cast, drops the buffered one and every order without a motor request; the controller stops the motor itself.

## Character animation

The player is drawn by the adventurer's baked clips (PlayerCharacter.md's base body, the default look since its Phase 1), in the colours and parts its player picks in the options (its Phases 5 and 6), carrying the gear of the weapon it has equipped (its Phase 7), or by another look its player picks there (the Bandit, a stand-in for a character drawn by hand in Aseprite, AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 2), and the Weasel by the Weasel's ([Animation.md](Animation.md)'s Runtime; Phase 2 built the locomotion, Phase 3 the action layer that plays attacks, Phase 5 the casts, Phase 7 the mobs). Animation is never replicated and nothing in gameplay reads it: each machine animates every player from its motor's velocity and facing, simulated on the owner and replicated on a remote copy (`PlayerNetworkSync`), and plays its attacks from the weapon's attack state on the owner and from the owner's attack messages on a copy, and its casts from its abilities' cast events on the owner and the owner's cast messages on a copy (`PlayerCastVisuals`). A mob is animated from its motor's velocity and facing on the host and the replicated move and facing on a client, and plays its attacks from its damage dealer's attack state on the host and the host's attack messages on a client (`MobNetworkSync`). The code is under `Assets/Scripts/Characters`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [CharacterAnimationSet](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterAnimationSet.cs) | ScriptableObject the character baker writes beside the sheets (`Assets/Sprites/Characters/Adventurer/Adventurer_AnimationSet.asset`, `Assets/Sprites/Characters/Rogue/Rogue_AnimationSet.asset`, `Assets/Sprites/Characters/Weasel/Weasel_AnimationSet.asset`, see Editor tools), or an Aseprite character recipe for a character drawn by hand (AsepriteCharacters.md; none in the game yet); runtime code only reads it and cannot tell the two apart. `Clips`: every clip of the recipe in order. `Locomotion(gait)`: the first clip of that gait drawn without a weapon. `Find(name)`: the first clip of that name (a weapon's attack, looked up when it is equipped, an ability's cast clip, on its first cast, or a mob's attack, when its motor initialises). Its idle animations are the clips flagged `IsIdleAnimation` (the adventurer's five, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3). `Dyes`: the dyes its sheets' palette maps name, a bit per `CharacterDye` (the adventurer's: the skin to the buckle; 0 for a set without maps, the rogue's, the Weasel's and the Bandit's), `Carries(dye)` (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5); an outfit's set, composed at runtime (`CharacterComposer.Build`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6), names its parts' dyes too. `SameClipsAs(other)`: the other holds these clips drawn by other sprites, in order (an outfit of the same look, Phase 7), and `Matching(clip, other)` and `SpriteMatching(sprite, other)` find a clip's and a sprite's counterpart there. `Create(clips, dyes)` for the baker, the composer and tests. |
| [CharacterAnimationClip](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterAnimationClip.cs) | One baked clip: `Name`, `Gait` ([CharacterGait](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterGait.cs): `None` for an attack, `Idle`, `Walk`, `Run`, `Sprint`), `Armed` (drawn with a weapon), `FrameCount`, `FrameSeconds(frame)` at natural speed, `Duration`, `NaturalSpeed` (how fast a gait's planted feet travel over the ground at its natural rate, east or west, in units per second; 0 for the others), `ContactFrame` (the frame an attack lands on or a cast releases on, from its keyed motion; -1 for the others) with `HasContact`, `WindupSeconds` and `RecoverySeconds` (the natural length of the frames before and after it), `SpriteAt(direction, frame)` (a row of frames per direction), `GaitFrames` (how many gait frames a cast drawn over a gait has under each of its frames; 0 for the others) and `SpriteAt(direction, frame, gaitFrame)`, `IsComplete`. A cast whose frames glow (Lighting.md's Phase 2) has `LightColour` and a light per sprite, `LightAt(direction, frame, gaitFrame)` found as its sprite is (none without, `HasLights`). A clip of a character carrying a lantern (Lighting.md's Phase 9) has, per sprite, the lantern's layer, `LanternAt(direction, frame, gaitFrame)` (null where the body hides the whole lantern), and its light, `LanternLightAt`, found as the sprite is (`HasLantern`). `IsIdleAnimation`: an idle animation, played once at its natural pace after the character has stood still a while (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3), from its keyed motion. `WithSprites(sprites)`: a copy drawn by other sprites in the same order, keeping its timing, contact, gait frames and lights (an outfit's clips, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6); `SameFramesAs(other)` tells such a copy (the same name, frames, timing, contact and gait frames, a sprite for each), and `SpriteMatching(sprite, other)` its sprite where this clip draws one (Phase 7). `Create` for the baker and tests. |
| [CharacterFrameLight](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterFrameLight.cs) | Serializable struct, the light a frame's glow casts (Lighting.md's Phase 2), or its carried lantern (Phase 9): `Offset`, where it shines from in units from the feet on the screen, and `Strength`, 0 (none, `IsLit` false) to 1. The character baker writes it. |
| [CharacterDirections](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterDirections.cs) | The eight directions, counterclockwise from east (`East` 0, `North` 2, `West` 4, `South` 6): `Angle`, `Name` (E, NE, ..., the slices' names), `Nearest(facing)` (south for none), `Keep(current, facing, margin)`: the current direction while the facing lies within 22.5 degrees plus the margin of it, else the nearest. The baker uses it too. |
| [CharacterLocomotionSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLocomotionSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject: the speeds from which the character walks, runs and sprints, the hysteresis share around each boundary, the range a gait's playback rate is clamped to, the direction margin, and under Copies the contact wait (`ContactWaitSeconds`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 4): how long past its windup an action played until its contact (a copy of another machine's attack or cast) holds its last windup frame waiting for the owner's contact or completion before it ends and the character goes back to its locomotion, and under Idle animations how long the character stands still before its first (`IdleAnimationAfter`) and the fewest and most seconds from the end of one to the start of the next (`IdleAnimationGapMin`, `IdleAnimationGapMax`; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3). `Assets/Data/Characters/Locomotion_Player.asset`: walk from 0.1, run from 1.9, sprint from 5.6 units per second, 8%, 0.45 to 1.9, 8 degrees, a 0.25 s wait, the first idle animation after 8 s and the next 10 to 16 s after one ends; `Locomotion_Weasel.asset`: walk from 0.1, run from 1.6, sprint from 100 (never: the Weasel has no sprint), 8%, 0.5 to 1.5, 8 degrees, 0.25 s. `From(gait)`; `Create` for tests. |
| [CharacterLocomotionLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLocomotionLayer.cs) | Plain class over a set's unarmed idle and gait clips (complete ones; a missing gait shows the nearest slower clip, else the nearest faster). `Advance(speed, deltaTime)`: picks the gait (`Pick`: a boundary below the current gait holds until the speed drops the hysteresis share under it, one above is crossed only a share over it, and standing still is idle), sets the playback rate (speed over the clip's natural speed, clamped; idle plays at 1) and moves the normalised phase on. A switch between gaits keeps the phase, so the stride goes on where it was (every gait starts with the left foot planted in front); starting to move or stopping starts the new clip at its first frame. `WouldMove(speed)` (whether the speed picks a gait from the current one: the character moves). `Gait`, `Phase`, `PlaybackRate`, `Frame`, `Clip`, `SpriteFacing(direction)`. Allocates nothing once built. |
| [CharacterActionLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterActionLayer.cs) | Plain class, the action layer: an attack's or a cast's clip on its timeline (an `AttackTimeline`; a cast's gather is its windup, its release its contact, its follow-through its recovery) from a start time, in one direction, its `Kind` a [CharacterActionKind](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterActionKind.cs) (`Attack` or `Cast`). Each phase plays its frames stretched to that phase's duration in their natural proportions (the frames before the contact frame over the windup, the contact frame for the contact, the frames after it over the recovery), reading phases with `AttackTimeline.PhaseAt` from the same start time as the attack state, so the contact frame shows on exactly the update the hit resolves, at any attack speed; the first update past the windup always shows the contact frame, even one that jumps past the whole contact. `Play(clip, direction, timeline, now, untilContact)` for an attack (refused for a clip without a contact frame); `PlayCast(clip, movingClip, direction, followsFacing, timeline, now, beat, untilRelease)` for a cast: the moving clip (the cast drawn over a gait) kept when it is complete with the clip's frames and release frame, `FollowsFacing` for a cast aimed nowhere (`Turn` sets its direction), and with a positive beat a cast longer than its clip's natural gather channels (the gather at its natural pace, then its last two gather frames in turn, a beat each, until the release). `PhaseAt(now)`, the phase its next advance shows; `ContactEndsAt`, when the contact (a cast's release) stops showing; played until contact (a copy of another machine's attack or cast, or the owner's cast) the windup holds its last frame past its end until `ReachContact(now)`, at most the `wait` that `Play` and `PlayCast` take past the windup, after which the action ends (`PhaseAt` none, `Advance` false) and a later contact shows nothing (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4), which plays the contact and the recovery from that moment, timed from it rather than from a start moved back by the windup (late in a session that subtraction rounds and could leave the action a hair short of its contact); an early one cuts the windup short; once reached it changes nothing. `Stop`, `Advance(now)` (false once the recovery is over), `IsPlaying`, `Clip`, `MovingClip`, `ContactShown`, `Direction`, `Frame`, `Phase`, `Sprite`, `MovingSpriteAt(direction, frame, gaitFrame)`, and the lights of those frames, `Light` and `MovingLightAt(direction, frame, gaitFrame)`. Allocates nothing. |
| [CharacterIdleAnimations](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterIdleAnimations.cs) | Plain class, a `CharacterAnimator`'s idle animations (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3): the set's complete clips flagged `IsIdleAnimation`, timed by the locomotion settings and picked by an `IRandom`. `Advance(now)` for a character standing still: the first call since a stop starts the wait; once it is over, an idle animation plays once from its first frame at its frames' natural durations (`IsPlaying`, `Clip`, `Frame`); while the character keeps standing, the next starts a gap picked between the settings' fewest and most seconds after it ended, never the one before. `Stop` cuts the one playing and starts the wait over. A set without idle animations, or no source, plays none (`Count`). No allocation once built. |
| [CharacterAnimator](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterAnimator.cs) | Plain class on an injected `IClock`. `Update(velocity, facing)`, on the clock's time since the last update: while an action plays, the action layer's sprite in its direction (a cast aimed nowhere turning with the facing, `CharacterDirections.Keep`), the locomotion layer waiting where it was; otherwise the direction kept or changed (the first update snaps to the nearest) and the locomotion layer advanced at the velocity's speed, by nothing on the first update after a standing action, so it resumes where it was. A cast with a moving clip shows it while the character moves (`CharacterLocomotionLayer.WouldMove`): the locomotion layer advances underneath and picks the gait frame from its phase, so the legs keep their gait and stride (`ShowsMovingCast`); a cast without one ends as soon as the character moves after its release frame showed, cutting the follow-through. `PlayAction(clip, direction, timeline, holdUntilContact)` (nearest of eight to the direction; held until contact at most the settings' contact wait past the windup, as is a cast played until its release), `PlayCast(clip, movingClip, direction, followsFacing, timeline, channelBeat, untilRelease)` (the current direction when the direction is zero), `ReachContact` (a copy's contact, or the owner's cast completing), `StopAction(kind)` (only an action of that kind, so ending an attack never stops a cast). A cast started while another cast's release is due or showing (a buffered cast, which fires as the running one completes) waits (`HasQueuedCast`) until that release has shown for its own time (KnownGaps.md's Phase 7), a hit-stop holding it on, then starts with its gather shortened by the wait, so its release still shows on the update it completes; one completed meanwhile (an instant cast) shows its release as it starts; moving while it waits starts it at once, as moving would cut the release; a newer cast replaces it, and a stop or an attack drops it. The hit-stop: `Hold(seconds)` keeps the sprite its next update shows on screen for that long on the clock (longer when a hold already runs that long) while the action keeps its time underneath and the locomotion layer stands still (under a moving cast the held frame stays over legs that run on); when it ends the action shows the frame for the real time. Reaching the action's contact ends a hold early, so a unit hit in its own windup still shows its contact frame on the update its hit lands, and playing or stopping an action, or moving cutting a cast, ends it. `HoldContact(seconds)` holds only when the action shows its contact (a cast's release) on the next update (an attacker or caster whose hit just landed; `CharacterActionLayer.PhaseAt`); a hold already running (the unit was struck in its windup) then takes the contact frame on that update and holds it, rather than ending as the action reaches contact. Standing still with no action or hold showing, the idle animations (`CharacterIdleAnimations`, built from the set, the settings and the random source the constructor takes, none without one) show in the direction faced (`ShowsIdleAnimation`); moving, playing an action or a hold stops one and starts the wait over, and a hold shows the character standing. `Direction`, `Gait`, `Phase`, `PlaybackRate`, `IsActing`, `ActionKind`, `ActionPhase`, `IsHolding`, `Frame` and `Clip` (the action's while it plays, its moving clip while it shows that, an idle animation's while one shows), `Sprite`, and `Light` and `LightColour`, the light of the sprite the last update showed and its clip's light colour (none for locomotion), and `Lantern` and `LanternLight`, its lantern layer and the lantern's light, all set with the sprite and so held with it. No allocation per update. `Rebind(from, to)` goes on with a set of the same clips drawn by other sprites (another outfit of the same look, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7): each layer takes the new set's clips where it held the old one's (`CharacterLocomotionLayer.Rebind`, `CharacterActionLayer.Rebind`, `CharacterIdleAnimations.Rebind`, a cast waiting too), so the action, the gait and its phase, an idle animation and a hold carry on, the held sprite taking the new set's; false, changing nothing, for a set of other clips. |
| [CharacterVisuals](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterVisuals.cs) | On the player's and the Weasel's `Visuals` child beside its `SpriteRenderer`: the set and the locomotion settings (an error in `Awake` when either is missing), swapped by `SetLook(set, locomotion)` (a player's look: a set of the same clips at the same settings, another outfit of the same look, goes on with the animator (`CharacterAnimator.Rebind`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7), the action, the gait and a hold carrying on in the new sprites; any other set builds the animator again on its next use, so an action showing stops; the weapon and the cast visuals notice the new set and look their clips up again, a cast whose clip over the run the set lacks playing standing). `SetLocomotion(velocity, facing)` from the motor; `PlayAction`, `ReachContact` and `StopAction(kind)` from the weapon (a mob's from its motor); `PlayCast`, `ReachContact` and `StopAction(kind)` from `PlayerCastVisuals`; `Hold` and `HoldContact` from its unit's `HitFeedback`; in `LateUpdate` (internal `Tick`) it asks its animator, built on first use on the injected clock with a `SystemRandom` seeded by `SeedIdleAnimations(seed)` (0 until then; a new seed builds the animator again) for its idle animations, for the sprite and sets it on the renderer when it changed. `PlayerNetworkSync` seeds a player's with its network id on every machine, so a standing player's copies pick the idle animations its owner does for the same stops. With a cast light (`CastLight`, a Light 2D on a child, and its intensity at full strength, Lighting.md's Phase 2), each `Tick` shows the animator's light on it: at the frame light's offset, its strength times that intensity, in the clip's light colour, and off for a frame without one; it writes only what changed and turns the light off in `OnDisable`. The Weasel's has none. A character carrying a lantern (Lighting.md's Phase 9) has a lantern renderer (`LanternRenderer`, on a child, drawn over the body at order 1 inside the character's sorting group) and light (`LanternLight`, on a child): while `ShowLantern(lit, intensity)` has it burning, each `Tick` puts the animator's lantern layer on the renderer and its light at the frame's offset, at that intensity; out, the renderer shows no sprite and the light is off, as it is in `OnDisable` (`LanternLit`). `AnimationSet`, `Locomotion`; `Animator` (internal), `Configure(light, intensity)` and `ConfigureLantern(renderer, light)` for tests. |
| [CharacterLookCatalog](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLookCatalog.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Characters/CharacterLooks.asset`): every look a player can be drawn with, in a fixed order, the first the default (a look's index goes on the wire, safe since join approval refuses other builds; the local settings name it, a removed look's name falling back to the first): each a [CharacterLook](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLookCatalog.cs) (`Name`, kept in the local settings; `AnimationSet`, baked or imported from Aseprite; `Locomotion`; `Parts`, the `CharacterParts` its character can wear, none for a look whose parts are its own, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6). The adventurer (`Adventurer_AnimationSet` on `Locomotion_Player`, wearing `Adventurer_Parts`) first, then the Bandit (`Bandit_AnimationSet`, the hand-drawn stand-in, AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 2, on `Locomotion_Bandit`). The rogue was a look until PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 1 retired it; its recipe and sheets stay as the baker's test bed. `Get(index)`, `IndexOf(name)`, `Count`; `Create` for tests. |
| [PlayerLook](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerLook.cs) | On the player's root: the look catalog and the character visuals (an error in `Awake` when either is missing), the colours and the palette (found under it when unassigned), `UserSettings` and `CharacterOutfits` injected. `Show(index)` puts a look on the visuals in the parts shown (`Index`; -1 while the prefab's own shows; false for an index the catalog lacks). `ShowParts(picks)` puts a player's parts on (`ShownParts`; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6), its weapon's slot showing the equipped weapon's gear whatever the picks say (`WornParts`): the look's own set at once for its own parts (`CharacterParts.IsOwn`), a look without parts or no outfits, else the outfit `CharacterOutfits.Wear` composes, the set shown before staying until it is ready (the look's own when the look changed); it lets go of the outfit worn before, and of its own when destroyed. `Parts`: the shown look's (the first while the prefab's own shows). `LocalParts()`: per slot of those parts the option the settings name, the first for an empty or unknown name, the weapon's slot left to the weapon. `ShowWeapon(weapon)` shows that weapon's gear in the weapon's slot (`PlayerWeapon.Gear`, `CharacterPartSlot.GearOption`: the option it names, else the one drawing nothing; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7), wearing the outfit again only when the gear changed; the `PlayerWeaponController` on the root (`weapon`, found there when unassigned) calls it through `EquippedWeaponChanged`, subscribed while enabled, on every machine alike, the owner's `Equip` and a copy's `EquipOnCopy`, and once as it is enabled. `ShowColours(picks)` puts a player's colours on the palette (`ShownColours`). `LocalColours()`: the picks the settings name, a choice's own for an empty or unknown swatch. `FollowLocalChoice()` (the local player, from `PlayerBinder.BindLocal`): shows the look the settings name, the first for an empty or unknown name or without settings, raising `Chosen(index)`, then the parts they pick, raising `PartsChosen(picks)`, and the colours they pick, raising `ColoursChosen(picks)`, and again on every `CharacterLookChanged`, `CharacterPartsChanged` or `CharacterColoursChanged` while enabled, until `StopFollowing()`. `Configure` for tests, the weapon too. Visual only. |
| [ColourRamp](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/ColourRamp.cs) | The four colours a material is drawn in, darkest first: `Outline`, `Shadow`, `Middle`, `Light`, `Tone(index)` (0 the outline to 3 the light), value equality. The editor's recipes draw with them (it moved to the runtime assembly in PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5), and a player's colours are made of them. |
| [CharacterDye](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterDye.cs) | A ramp a player can colour, with fixed values a palette map names (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5): `None` (the sheet's colour stays), `Skin`, `Mouth`, `Blush`, `Hair`, `Brows`, `Eyes`, `EyeGlint`, `Tunic`, `Shirt`, `Trousers`, `Boots`, `Belt`, `Buckle`, `Gloves`, `Strap`, `Pouch`, and the parts' `Headwear`, `Cape` and `CapeLining` (Phase 6). |
| [CharacterPaletteMap](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterPaletteMap.cs) | How a sheet names the colour of each pixel a player colours: its palette map (`MapTextureName`, `_PaletteMap`, the sheet's secondary texture) holds a byte a pixel, the dye times four plus the tone (`Code`, `DyeOf`, `ToneOf`), 0 where the sheet's colour stays, which is also the pixel's index in a palette (`PaletteTextureName`, `_PaletteTex`: 4 tones a row by `Rows` 32 dyes, `PaletteSize`). |
| [CharacterColours](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterColours.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Characters/CharacterColours.asset`), the colours a player can give its character (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5): swatch lists ([CharacterSwatchList](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterColours.cs), a name and its [CharacterSwatch](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterColours.cs)es, each a name and a ramp a dye) and the choices a player makes, in a fixed order (a choice's index goes on the wire, safe since join approval refuses other builds; the local settings name its swatch): each a [CharacterColourChoice](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterColours.cs) (`Name`, the options row's label and the settings' key; `List`; `Own`, the swatch the look was baked in; `Dyes`, which the swatch's ramps colour in order). `Count`, `Get(index)`, `IndexOf(name)`, `Swatches(choice)`, `OwnIndex(choice)`, `IndexOfSwatch(choice, name)`, `Pick(picks, choice, name)` (none for the choice's own or an unknown name, so picks of the look's own colours are the default), `Shown(picks, choice)`, `Colours(choice, dyes)` (whether it colours a dye of a set's mask), `WritePalette(picks, pixels)` (each choice's dyes in the ramps of the swatch it shows, opaque; a dye no choice colours clear). `OnValidate` warns about more choices than picks carry, a choice whose list lacks its own swatch, a swatch with too few ramps and a dye two choices colour. `Create` for tests. |
| [CharacterColourPicks](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterColourPicks.cs) | A player's colours as every machine names them: a byte per choice (up to `MaxChoices` 16), the swatch picked or none for the choice's own (`Get(choice)` -1, `With(choice, swatch)`, `IsDefault`), value equality, `INetworkSerializeByMemcpy` so a network variable carries it as it is. |
| [CharacterSlot](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterSlot.cs) | The slots a player's parts fill (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): `Hair`, `Headwear`, `Top`, `Cape`, `Legs`, `Boots`, and since its Phase 7 `Hands`, `Bag` and `Weapon`, the last the gear of the weapon equipped rather than a player's pick. |
| [CharacterPartPicks](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterPartPicks.cs) | A player's parts as every machine names them: a byte per slot (up to `MaxSlots` 16 in two 64-bit words, raised from 8 by PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7; `MaxOptions` 256), the option shown, 0 the slot's first, the look's own (`Get(slot)`, `With(slot, option)`, `IsDefault`), value equality, `INetworkSerializeByMemcpy`. |
| [CharacterParts](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterParts.cs) | ScriptableObject the character baker writes beside a look's sheets (`Assets/Sprites/Characters/Adventurer/Parts/Adventurer_Parts.asset`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): the parts a look's character can wear. `Look`, the set it was baked with (its own parts); `Body`, the body's layer file; `InnerLineGap`, the recipe's; `Slots`, each a [CharacterPartSlot](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterParts.cs) (`Slot`; `VariesWith`, the slot whose option its layers are drawn under, as the hair under the headwear, -1 for none; `Options`, the first the look's own, `IndexOf(name)`), each option a [CharacterPartOption](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterParts.cs) (`Name`, kept in the local settings; `Dyes` its layers carry; `Layers`, its layer files, one per option of the slot it varies with, null for an option drawing nothing). `Get(slot)`, `Shown(picks, slot)` (an option the slot lacks shows its first), `IsOwn(picks)` (every slot showing its first), `LayerOf(picks, slot)`, `DyesOf(picks)`, `Dyes` (every option's). A slot's `FollowsWeapon` (the weapon's: no options row, nothing in the settings or a pick) and `GearOption(gear)` (the option a weapon's gear names, else the first whose `DrawsNothing`, else the first; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7). `Create` for the writer and tests. |
| [CharacterLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLayer.cs) | One layer of a character (its body, or one option of a slot) for every frame of every clip, before shading (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): `Clips`, each a [CharacterLayerClip](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLayer.cs) (`FrameSize`, `Feet`, `Frames` direction by direction and within each frame by frame, and `Materials`, a [CharacterLayerMaterial](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLayer.cs) per material id: `Outlined`, `Dye`, `Ramp`; every clip of the body's layer has one, and a weapon's gear one for each clip it draws its weapon stowed in, giving the weapon's materials its ramps, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7; null otherwise), each frame a [CharacterLayerFrame](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterLayer.cs) (`Box`, around what it draws, and per pixel of the box `Material` (`Empty` 255 for none), `Part`, `Tone` before any line, `Step`, the drawing step that drew it, and `Depth`). `Write()` gzips it (version 4: a table, or none, per clip), each frame's bytes by plane and its depths as 16-bit steps of 1/`DepthSteps` (512) of a pixel past the frame's nearest whole pixel; `Read(bytes)` reads it back, refusing other bytes (`InvalidDataException`); `Quantize(material, depth)` puts depths on those steps, as the baker does before writing, so a file holds them exactly. |
| [CharacterComposer](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs) | Plain class, thread-safe per instance, that composes an outfit from its layers as one canvas drawn with them all would (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): `ComposeFrame(width, height, layers, materials, gap, pixels, codes)` keeps per pixel the nearest layer's (a tie in depth to the earlier drawing step), colours it in its material's ramp, draws the inner lines where another outlined part lies nearer by more than the gap and the outline beside each empty pixel, as `ArtCanvas.Shade` does, and names each dyed pixel's palette code; `MaterialsOf(layers)`: the table a clip is coloured with, the last of its layers' that has one, else the body's (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7); `ComposeAll(body, parts, gap)` composes every frame of every clip with it, cropped ([CharacterOutfitFrames](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs), [CharacterOutfitFrame](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs)), off the main thread; `Build(frames, look, dyes, name)`, on the main thread, packs them into one RGBA32 sheet `AtlasWidth` (2048) wide and its R8 palette map, point filtered and no longer readable, with a sprite per frame on the feet at 32 px per unit holding the map as its `_PaletteMap`, and the look's clips over them (`WithSprites`) in a new set naming the look's and the parts' dyes: a [CharacterOutfit](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs) (`Set`, `Sheet`, `PaletteMap`; `Destroy()` destroys them all). Since Phase 7 `Build` is `Pack(frames)`, which lays the frames out and copies them into an atlas off the main thread ([CharacterOutfitAtlas](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs)), then `BeginBuild(atlas, look, dyes, name)`, which makes the textures, and a [CharacterOutfitBuild](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterComposer.cs)'s `Step(count)`, which makes that many more sprites (`IsDone`), `Finish()`, the outfit, and `Abort()`, which destroys what it made. |
| [CharacterOutfits](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterOutfits.cs) | Plain class, Gameplay scope (`IDisposable`), PlayerCharacter.md's Phases 6 and 7, made with how many outfits nobody wears it keeps and how many sprites it makes a frame (`GameplaySettings`' Outfits): the outfits players wear, one per look's parts and picks. `Wear(parts, picks, ready)` gives a [CharacterOutfitLease](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterOutfits.cs): for the look's own parts its set at once, no outfit made; else the outfit worn by another lease at once, or composed the first time: the layer files' bytes read on the main thread, read and composed on a worker thread (`Awaitable.BackgroundThreadAsync`), built back on the main thread, then handed to every lease waiting (`ready`, `Set`). `Release()` lets go once; when the last wearer lets go, a composed outfit becomes the newest spare (`SpareCount`), worn again at once by the next `Wear` of its picks, and the oldest spare past the most kept is destroyed with its textures, sprites and set; a composition under way is dropped. In play a composition starts on the frame after the `Wear`, only if it is still worn then, packs its atlas off the main thread too, and builds its sprites a frame's worth at a time (Phase 7). A failed composition logs its exception and hands nothing. `Dispose` destroys every outfit; a composition finishing after it builds nothing. |
