| [CharacterPalette](../Assets/Scripts/Characters/CharacterPalette.cs) | On the player's `Visuals` beside its body's `SpriteRenderer` (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5): `Show(colours, picks)` writes the picks' palette (`CharacterColours.WritePalette`) into a 4 x 32 sRGB, point-filtered texture of its own and draws the renderer with a copy of its own material holding it as `_PaletteTex`, both made on first use and kept (a property block would take the renderer out of the SRP Batcher, and `HitFeedback` clears its block when a flash ends); the default picks, or no colours, put the renderer's own material back (`Clear`). `IsShown`. Both are destroyed with it. A look whose sheets carry no palette map shows its own colours under it. |

- Layout: the sprites pivot on the feet, so the player's `Visuals` sits on its root, where the old centred sheet had it half a unit up. The feet collider, the camera (which follows the body) and the sorting (the root's group, at the feet) line up with where the player stands; the drawn body's middle is now about 0.7 above the feet instead of 0.5. The 80 px attack frames pivot on the feet like the 64 px locomotion frames ((40, 22) and (32, 12)), so they line up on the same `Visuals`: in every direction the first attack frame puts the hood's top at the idle's height and the boots within a pixel (`CharacterPrefabTests`).
- Attacks: each weapon's attack shows its baked clip, the sword's `SwordSlash` and the greatsword's `GreatswordCleave` with their smears drawn into their frames, the longbow's `BowShot` with the release's flash; nothing else is drawn for a swing (the old rotated slash sprite is gone), and the bow's arrow flies as a projectile from its contact (see Projectiles). A weapon is in hand only in its attack, appearing there on the attack's first windup frame (Animation.md's Phase 3 notes); standing, walking, running and sprinting the adventurer carries it stowed, its gear (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7: the sword in its scabbard at the left hip, the greatsword and the longbow with its quiver on the back), which its own attacks and casts keep without the weapon (an empty scabbard, the sling, the quiver); the Bandit shows nothing outside its attacks. A copy plays the clip of the weapon the owner attacked with.
- Casts (Animation.md's Phase 5): each ability names its clip (`AbilityDefinition.CastClip`), eight frames with the release on frame 4 (the instant Leap's five, the release first), played on the ability's cast timeline: the gather as long as the cast time, the release for 60 ms from the moment the cast completes, a 240 ms follow-through (Leap's 300 ms), Smite channelling. The class casts (`CastBolt`, `CastLeap`, `CastMend`, `CastSmite`) are unarmed, Bolt, Mend and Smite with a light baked at the casting hand; the weapon casts (`CastWhirl`, `CastLunge`, `CastSweep`, `CastCharge`, `CastVolley`, `CastRoll`) show their weapon. The Continue casts also name a clip drawn over the run (`CastWhirlRun`, `CastSmiteRun`), shown while the player moves; moving cuts other casts' follow-through once their release has shown. A buffered cast, which fires as the running one completes, waits for that cast's release to show its 60 ms before its own gather, which fills what is left of its cast time, so neither release is lost and no hit moves (KnownGaps.md's Phase 7). A copy plays the owner's casts from `CastRpc`, its gather held until the owner's completion (`CastCompletedRpc`) shows its release, as the host resolves it (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4; at most the contact wait past the cast time), and stops them on `CastCancelledRpc`.
- Parts (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): a player's outfit is its look's set for the look's own parts, and otherwise a set composed at runtime from the parts' layer files, one sheet of 2048 x 1559 and its palette map (15.2 MiB together, 2616 sprites) for the adventurer, about a second the first time its picks are worn and shared by every player wearing them. Its pixels are what the editor bakes for the same picks, and its palette map takes the player's colours as the baked sheets' do. Since PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7 the hands (bare or gloved) and a bag (the satchel) are parts too, and the weapon's slot shows the equipped weapon's gear, the sword's the look's own, so a player with the sword in its own parts composes nothing; an outfit is 14.5 to 16.7 MiB, built off the main thread but for its textures and its sprites, 500 a frame; the two most recent nobody wears are kept; and putting an outfit on keeps the action showing.
- The lantern (Lighting.md's Phase 9): removed from the player on 2026-11-05, so no set the game ships carries one (the rogue's recipe names none, and the Bandit's files lost their lantern layers). A character carrying one would have each clip's lantern layer and light per frame, from the baker or its Aseprite files' lantern layers (AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 4), and the visuals draw them while `ShowLantern` has it burning.
- Speeds: at the base move speed (5 units per second) the player runs at 1.33 times the adventurer's run's rate, and chilled (3) at 0.80, so its feet keep up going east or west; nothing in play reaches the walk or the sprint yet (see Known gaps). Animation.md's Phase 2 notes have the measurements behind the bands, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 2 notes the adventurer's.
- The Weasel (Animation.md's Phase 7): its idle, walk (natural speed 1.20), run (2.00, its move speed, played at its natural rate) and `DaggerSlash` (8 frames: the windup on frames 0 to 3, the contact on 4, the recovery on 5 to 7; the dagger only in it), all 64 px frames on the feet at (32, 12). Chilled (1.2) it walks at its natural rate. Its attack plays on its config's timeline (0.45, 0.05 and 0.25 s); a copy plays it held on the windup's last frame until the host's contact arrives, at most the 0.25 s contact wait past its windup.

## Abilities

Abilities that go through the whole pipeline (cooldown, cast time, movement during the cast, buffering, networking, animation). Six have real effects, which land when their cast completes (see below); the four movement abilities are still blanks. Cooldowns, orders and cast state live on the owner only and are never replicated. The class is the prefab default on every machine and a cast's messages name the weapon it was cast with (its catalog index), so a slot index and that weapon identify an ability on the wire, even before a weapon switch reaches a machine.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [AbilityDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject: display name, icon, description (what its HUD tooltip shows; DesignSystem.md's Phase 5), cooldown (from cast start), cast time (0 = instant), `AbilityTargeting` (`None`, `Direction`, `Point`, `Unit`), `AbilityUnitFilter` (`Enemy`, `Ally`, `Any`; `Accepts(team)`), range, `CastMovement` (`Stop`, `Continue`, `Ability`, which stands still until abilities can move the caster), bufferable, and an effect: a `Hit` (damage, type, statuses), a heal amount (`HasEffect`) and an area radius (`None`: around the caster, 0 = the caster only; `Point`: around the point; `Direction`: half-width of a line `range` long; unused for `Unit`). And how its cast looks (Animation.md's Phase 5): `CastClip` and `MovingCastClip` (clip names in the character's set, standing and drawn over the run), the release and follow-through lengths (0.06 and 0.24 s by default) and `ChannelBeat` (0: the gather stretches over the cast time), with `CastTimeline` (the gather as long as the cast time, the release, the follow-through). `Create(...)` for tests. |
| [AbilityTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityTypes.cs) | The enums above, `CastOutcome` (`Started`, `EmptySlot`, `OnCooldown`, `CastInProgress`, `NoTarget`, `Dead`, `Expired`, `Cancelled`, `Stunned`, `Silenced`, `Rooted`), `CastAim` (point, direction, optional `UnitTarget`) and `AbilityCast` (slot, ability, aim). |
| [PlayerClass](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/PlayerClass.cs) | ScriptableObject: exactly four abilities (slots 1–4). [AbilitySlotArrays](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilitySlotArrays.cs) keeps class and weapon lists at their fixed size. |
| [AbilityCooldowns](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityCooldowns.cs) | Plain class: per-slot ready times on the clock, `IsReady`, `Remaining`, `Start`. |
| [IAbilityCaster](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/IAbilityCaster.cs) | What orders need: `GetAbility`, `CanCast` (empty, cooldown, dead), `TryCast`, `CompleteCast` (the cast time ran out), `CancelCast` (stopped early), `ReportFailure`. Whether a cast is already running is decided by the orders. |
| [PlayerAbilities](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/PlayerAbilities.cs) | Component (implements `IAbilityCaster`): builds the six slots from its `PlayerClass` and the equipped weapon's two abilities (`AbilityFor(slot, weapon)`), rebuilding on `EquippedWeaponChanged` and in `OnEnable`; a weapon ability keeps its own cooldown across weapon switches (the weapon slots' ready times are kept per ability and restored when it comes back), so a switch never readies an ability early nor holds the new weapon's. `TryCast(slot, aim)` fails without side effects (raising `CastFailed`) when the slot is empty, on cooldown, dead or another cast runs; otherwise starts the cooldown, raises `CastStarted` and hands the cast to `AbilityService.Apply`. `CompleteCast` raises `CastEnded(slot, true)` and `CastCompleted` (with the cast as started) and has `AbilityService.Resolve` apply its effect; `CancelCast` only raises `CastEnded(slot, false)`. `ResolveRemoteCast(slot, aim, casterPosition, weapon)` resolves a cast a client completed, under the `PlayRemoteCast` rules. `CompleteRemoteCast()` (the owner's completion) raises `RemoteCastCompleted`; `StopRemoteCast()` (the owner's cancel) raises `RemoteCastCancelled`. `RemainingCooldown(slot)` feeds the HUD, `SlotsChanged`. `PlayRemoteCast(slot, aim, weapon)` (the weapon the owner cast with; without one, the equipped weapon) replays another machine's cast into `AbilityService` with no cooldown or cast events, first raising `RemoteCastStarted` for its visuals (whatever its unit target; not for a caster seen as dead), ignoring it when this machine sees the caster as dead or a `Unit` cast's target as missing (it despawned or did not resolve) or dead. Logs an error in `Start` without an injected service. Gizmos (Debug toggles): the last 8 casts, local and remote, are kept in a fixed ring; each shows its aim while its cast time runs and fades over `castGizmoSeconds` (1 s) after, in a per-slot colour (`None`: a circle round the caster; `Direction`: a 2-unit ray; `Point`: a line to the point; `Unit`: a line to the target), dimmer for remote casts, with a label stack above the head (slot number 1–6, name, cast time left, "(remote)"). When selected, the range of every `Point` and `Unit` ability is a circle labelled with its slot number on its right edge. |
| [PlayerCastVisuals](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/PlayerCastVisuals.cs) | Component on the player (Animation.md's Phase 5), on every machine: plays casts on the player's `CharacterVisuals` from its `PlayerAbilities`' events, the owner's (`CastStarted`, its release waiting for `CastEnded` with completed, which reaches the release on that update; a cancel stops the clip) and a copy's (`RemoteCastStarted`, its release waiting for `RemoteCastCompleted`, the owner's completion arriving, which reaches the release on that update, at most the contact wait past the cast time; `RemoteCastCancelled` stops it). A cast plays its ability's `CastClip` and `MovingCastClip` on its `CastTimeline` with its `ChannelBeat`, toward its aim, or turning with the facing for an ability aimed nowhere; nothing for an ability without a clip. Clips are looked up by name in the set on an ability's first cast and kept (an error for a missing one). `OnDisable` (death, despawn) stops any cast showing. Visual only. Internal `Configure` for tests. |
| [AbilityService](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton mirroring `DamageService`: `Apply(caster, cast)` (cast started) and `Resolve(caster, cast, casterPosition)` (cast completed; the position is where circles around the caster and lines from it start) act only where `IGameAuthority.IsAuthoritative`. `Apply` raises the internal `CastApplied` (tests) and nothing else. `Resolve` picks the affected units by targeting: the `Unit` target; the caster (`None` with radius 0) or every unit in a circle around it; every unit in a circle around the aimed `Point`; or every unit in a capsule from the caster along the aim `Direction`, `range` long (none without a direction). Areas use non-allocating `Physics2D` overlaps on `CombatSettings.UnitLayers` (no triggers), each unit counted once. Each unit is healed through `DamageService.ApplyHeal` and hit through `HitService`, with the caster as the source and the caster position as the hit's origin, so damage and debuffs only land on enemies and heals and buffs on allies. Then it raises the internal `CastResolved` (tests). Offline and host-owned casts reach both from `PlayerAbilities`; a client's cast reaches `Apply` through `CastRpc` and `Resolve` through `CastCompletedRpc` (which every other machine's copy also releases on), so each cast is applied once and resolved at most once, on the host. |

Blanks (`Assets/Data/Abilities`, used by `Assets/Data/Classes/Class_Placeholder.asset` and the weapons): [Abilities.md](Generated/Abilities.md) lists each with its slot, targeting, range, area, timings, movement, effect and cast clips (DesignSystem.md's Phase 1). Blank Bolt, Blank Whirl, Blank Sweep, Blank Volley, Blank Smite and Blank Mend have real effects, which land when their cast completes; Blank Leap, Blank Lunge, Blank Charge and Blank Roll stay blank until abilities can move the caster. The asset and display names still say "Blank". Leap's clip is a 0.3 s flourish, and Smite's channels every 0.1 s (Animation.md's Phase 5).

## Combat

### Health and damage

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [Health](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Health.cs) | Int HP; `ApplyDamage(amount, source, type, flags, origin)` clamps, returns applied amount; events `Damaged(Health.DamageEvent)` (amount, source, HP before and after, type, flags, and `Origin`, the point the hit came from as the machine that resolved it saw it, null for none; KnownGaps.md's Phase 4) and `Died(Health)` (once per death). `Heal(amount)` adds up to max HP, never revives a dead unit, and raises `Healed(Health, added)`. `Restore()` refills to max and raises `Restored(Health)` (no-op at full health); a restored `Health` can die again. `SyncTo(value)` sets HP (clamped) for network sync without `Damaged`, but raises `Died` when it takes a living unit to 0, so death reactions (`DisableOnDeath`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`) also run on machines that only learn the result. Coming back to life always goes through `Restore`. |
| [NetworkHealth](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/NetworkHealth.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on players and mobs. Host: mirrors HP into a server-written `NetworkVariable` and sends `HitRpc(amount, type, flags, healthAfter, source, hasSource, origin, hasOrigin)` on `Health.Damaged`, naming the hit's source unit (a `NetworkObjectReference` of the spawned network object of its `DamageReceiver`, when it has one) and the point it came from (when it has one), and on `DamageReceiver.ImmuneHit` (amount 0, its source unit when it has one, no point), `HealedRpc(amount, healthAfter, source, hasSource)` on `Health.Healed`, naming the heal's source unit when it has one (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, so a client's popups tell its own heals and immune hits apart) and `RestoredRpc` on `Restored`, all three server-invoked only (`InvokePermission`), so a client cannot fake a hit to the others through the host's relay. It forwards `Health.Damaged` rather than a service event because that fires before `Died`, so the RPC leaves before a dying mob despawns. Clients: sync HP from the variable on spawn (a unit that arrives dead, e.g. a teammate awaiting respawn when someone joins, dies there through `SyncTo`), replay hits and heals through `DamageService.ApplyReplicatedHit`/`ApplyReplicatedHeal` (so `Damaged`/`Died`, HUD, popups and hit feedback work as on the host; a hit carries this client's copy of its source, or none when it does not have it, and the host's point) and correct to `healthAfter`, and `Restore` on `RestoredRpc`. |
| [DamageReceiver](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageReceiver.cs) | Marks something as hittable: `FindFor(transform)` walks up parents; exposes its `Health`, `PopupWorldPosition`, its `CombatProfile`, `Faction` (`None` without a profile), `StatusImmunities`, `Statuses` (its `StatusEffects`, or null), `GetDefense(type)` (profile resistance and immunity combined with its statuses' resistance deltas, granted immunities and damage-taken multiplier) and `DamageDealtMultiplier` (from its statuses). Event `ImmuneHit(type, flags)`, raised by `DamageService` on the authority for hits it was immune to. Applies nothing itself. |
| [DamageService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton (with `CombatSettings`) and the only gameplay path that changes HP. `ApplyDamage(receiver, amount, type, source, flags, origin)` returns a `DamageResult`; it rejects (`default`) null or dead targets, non-positive amounts, every hit on a machine without `IGameAuthority` (a client) and a source of the target's own faction (found with `GetComponentInParent<DamageReceiver>` on the source; no faction on either side means no rule). Otherwise it resolves through `DamageMath` with the source unit's damage-dealt multiplier and the target's defence, raises `ImmuneHit` for immune hits (with the hit's source), applies to `Health` and publishes a `DamageReport` (immune hits included, with amount 0). The result's amount is the HP actually lost. `ApplyPeriodicDamage(receiver, amount, type, source, dealtMultiplier)` does the same for a damage-over-time tick, with the multiplier captured when its status landed and the `Periodic` flag. `ApplyHeal(receiver, amount, source)` heals a living target unless the source is of another faction and publishes a `HealReport`. Attacks play on every machine but only the host (or the offline game) resolves their hits (`AttackService`). `ApplyReplicatedHit(receiver, amount, type, flags, source, origin)`/`ApplyReplicatedHeal` apply and publish what the host already decided, regardless of authority, a hit or a heal with this machine's copy of its source (the hit with the host's point) (used by `NetworkHealth` on clients). A hit's `origin` goes into `Health.Damaged` and the `DamageReport`; a tick has none. Tests still call `Health.ApplyDamage` directly to set up state. |
| [DamageReport](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageReport.cs) | Target, amount (HP lost), type, flags, source (on a client its copy of the source the host named; none for an immune hit), popup world position, `Origin` (where the hit came from as the host resolved it: an arrow's shot, where an attacker or caster stood; null for a tick, an immune hit or a hit applied directly); `IsImmune`. [HealReport](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/HealReport.cs): target, amount added, source, popup world position. |
| [CombatEvents](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatEvents.cs) | Gameplay-scope event bus: `DamageApplied(DamageReport)`, `HealApplied(HealReport)`, `StatusBlocked(StatusReport)` (`PublishBlocked`), and `HitPredicted(attacker)` (`PublishPredicted`), a client's own melee hit it predicts at its contact, for its hit feedback (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4). |
| [Hit](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Hit.cs) | Serializable struct: amount, damage type and the statuses it applies (`StatusCount`, `GetStatus`). |
| [IUnitProtection](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/IUnitProtection.cs) | `IsProtected(receiver)`: which units are out of play for a while, their hits ignored and their statuses held; `TripProtection` in play, fakes in tests. |
| [HitService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/HitService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton: `ApplyHit(receiver, hit, source, origin)` (its damage coming from `origin`, the point it came from) ignores a hit on a unit out of play (`IUnitProtection`: a travelling player), returning false; otherwise it applies the damage through `DamageService` and then, if the target survived, each status through `StatusEffectService`, each under its own faction rule (so a damaging hit's buffs still land on allies whose damage is refused, and its debuffs only on enemies). Returns whether the hit reached the target: its damage resolved (an immune hit included) or a status landed, stacked, refreshed or was blocked by an immunity. Used by `AttackService` (players' weapons' and mobs' melee hits) and `AbilityService`; it keeps the two services independent. |
| [CombatFactions](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatFactions.cs) | Static: `UnitOf(source)` (the `DamageReceiver` on it or a parent), `SameFaction` (blocks hostile effects; also from a faction captured earlier, a projectile's shooter's), `OtherFaction` (blocks helpful ones); a side without a faction is exempt. |
| [DamageTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageTypes.cs) | `DamageType` (`Physical`, `Fire`, `Frost`, `Lightning`, `Poison`, `True`; serialized, append-only), `DamageTypeMask` (flags, `Includes(type)`), `DamageFlags` (`Immune`, `Resisted`, `Weakness`, `Periodic`), `Faction` (`None`, `Players`, `Mobs`). |
| [DamageMath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageMath.cs) | Static, pure: `Resolve(raw, type, dealt, DefenseSnapshot, CombatSettings, flags)`. Immune: 0 with `Immune`. Otherwise resistance clamped to the settings' floor and cap (0 for `True`), `raw x dealt x (1 - resistance/100) x taken`, rounded half away from zero, at least the minimum damage; `Resisted`/`Weakness` by the resistance's sign. See [DamageAndStatusEffects.md](DamageAndStatusEffects.md#resolving-a-hit). |
| [DefenseSnapshot](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DefenseSnapshot.cs) / [DamageResult](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageResult.cs) | A unit's defence against one type (resistance, immunity, damage-taken multiplier); a resolved hit (type, raw amount, amount, flags, `IsImmune`, `Resolved` = dealt damage or immune; `default` = rejected). |
| [CombatProfile](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatProfile.cs) | ScriptableObject: faction, [DamageResistance](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageResistance.cs) entries (type, percent in [-100, 100]; entries for one type add up), damage immunities, status immunity tags. `Create(...)` for tests. Assets `Assets/Data/Combat/Profile_Player.asset` (`Players`) and `Profile_Weasel.asset` (`Mobs`), no resistances yet. |
| [CombatSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Combat/CombatSettings.asset`): the resistance floor and cap, the minimum damage, the unit layers area effects search (`Player` and `Enemy`), the layers whose colliders stop projectiles (`ProjectileWallLayers`: `Obstacles`), the `StatusEffectCatalog`, the text size of periodic popups (`PeriodicPopupSize`, Caption) and of allies' popups shown smaller (`AllyPopupSize`, Caption; UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), a popup colour per damage type, heal and immune colours, the immune text. [DamageTypes.md](Generated/DamageTypes.md) has the values. |
| [MeleeDamageDealer](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/MeleeDamageDealer.cs) | A mob's melee attack on a timeline (Animation.md's Phase 7), an `AttackState` on the injected `IClock`: damage, interval, timeline, area, damage type and statuses (`AttackDamageType`, `AttackStatuses`; none on `Mob_Default`) taken from `MobConfig` (the statuses copied into the component's own array); the component serializes none of them. `TryStartAttack(target)`: when the cooldown allows and no attack runs, an attack on the unit the target belongs to (none without damage or a receiver; an error without an `AttackService`), its direction locked toward the target's feet, the cooldown (the interval) from now; raises `AttackStarted(direction)`. `Tick()` (the brain's, each frame): at contact the `Hit` lands through `AttackService` on every unit then in the area (`AreaCentre(direction)`: from the middle of the mob's collider past its edge along the direction by half the area's depth; the area's width across, depth along), the mob the source and where it stands the hit's origin, so the faction rules spare its allies; raises `AttackContacted`. A dead mob's attack is cancelled on its next tick or as it dies. `CancelAttack()` (a stun, death, a rebuild): before contact nothing lands and the cooldown is given back; raises `AttackCancelled`. `IsAttacking`, `Phase`, `AttackDirection`, `ReadyTime`, `Timeline`, `Area`; internal `State` and `Clock` for tests. Allocates nothing once built. |

### Player weapon and attacks

An auto attack is a gameplay timeline that the animation follows ([Animation.md](Animation.md)'s Attack timeline, Phase 3): a windup, a contact, when the hit resolves, and a recovery, their durations on the weapon divided by the attack speed, with cancel rules as data on the weapon. The cooldown runs from the attack's start. The owner's `PlayerWeaponController` (see Player) runs it; the host resolves every hit. A melee weapon's hit searches its area at contact; a ranged weapon's leaves as a projectile then (Animation.md's Phase 4, see Projectiles below). Weapons: the sword, the greatsword and the longbow, in the `WeaponCatalog`; the dev panel switches the local player's (see Dev tools).

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayerWeapon](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/PlayerWeapon.cs) | ScriptableObject: its id in saves (`WeaponId`, the asset's GUID, which `OnValidate` keeps in the editor as `WorldDefinition` keeps its world id; `test-` and the name for `Create`), name, damage, damage type, on-hit statuses (none yet) and `Hit` built from them. Attack: the cooldown at attack speed 1, the attack range for auto attacks (collider-to-collider), the windup, contact (at least 0.01 s) and recovery at attack speed 1 (`Timeline`) and the cancel rules `WindupCancelledByMove` (a move order or Stop during the windup aborts the attack: nothing lands and the cooldown is given back) and `RecoveryCancellable` (any order during the recovery ends it at once, and the next attack may start in it), both on for every weapon, as in League of Legends. Hit: `Delivery` ([AttackDelivery](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/PlayerWeapon.cs): `Melee` or `Projectile`, `FiresProjectile`). A melee hit's area: `HitDistance`, per-direction offsets from the feet (Down, Up, Left, Right: (0, 0.4), (0, 0.5), (-0.1, 0.5), (0.1, 0.5) on every weapon, half a unit above the feet; `HitOffset(direction)`) and `HitSize`, the area's width across the attack and depth along it: `HitCentre(position, direction)` is the position plus the direction's offset plus the distance along it, where the old slash's hitbox was. The hit fields kept their old serialized names (`slashSpawnDistance`, `slashSpawnOffsets`) through `FormerlySerializedAs`. A projectile (`Projectile`, a `ProjectileSpec`): speed, range from the feet, the radius it sweeps, how far ahead of the feet it leaves, whether it homes on the attack's target, and its `ProjectileVisual` prefab. Animation: the names of its attacks' clips in the character's set, the only frames that draw it in hand: the first attack's (`AttackClip`) and the others' in order (`otherAttackClips`, none by default; `AttackCount`; `AttackClipOf(attack)`, the first's for an index outside them), and the gear it is carried in outside them (`Gear`, an option of a look's weapon part slot: Sword, Greatsword and Longbow for the three; none by default, which shows no gear; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7). HUD icon, exactly two abilities (the A and S slots). `Create(...)` for tests (with a `ProjectileSpec` it fires that projectile, with other attack clips it has those attacks, and with a gear name that gear). Assets under `Assets/Data/Weapons`, their numbers in [Weapons.md](Generated/Weapons.md) (Animation.md's Phase 3 and 4 notes have how each was chosen): `Sword.asset` (`SwordSlash` then `SwordBackhand`), `Greatsword.asset` (`GreatswordCleave` then `GreatswordSweep`) and `Longbow.asset` (a homing arrow leaving 0.5 ahead of the feet, where the bow is at the release, drawn by `Arrow.prefab`; `BowShot` then `BowCantedShot`); each with its generated icon (`Icon_Sword`, `Icon_Greatsword`, `Icon_Longbow`). |
| [WeaponCatalog](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/WeaponCatalog.cs) | ScriptableObject: every weapon a player can equip, in a fixed order; a weapon's index is its id on the wire (the equipped weapon, and the weapon an attack or a cast was made with), safe since join approval refuses other build versions. `Count`, `Get(index)` (null out of range), `IndexOf(weapon)` (-1 when it does not list it), `Next(weapon)` (wrapping round; the first for a weapon it does not list), `Create` for tests. A save names a weapon by its id instead (`PlayerWeapon.WeaponId`, its asset's GUID), which survives a reordered catalog and a renamed or moved asset: `IdOf(weapon)` (empty for one it does not list) and `FindById(id)` (null for an unknown id, a removed weapon's: the player keeps its starting weapon). `Assets/Data/Weapons/WeaponCatalog.asset`: Sword 0, Greatsword 1, Longbow 2; append only. Referenced by the player prefab's `PlayerWeaponController`. |
| [AttackTimeline](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/AttackTimeline.cs) | `AttackPhase` (`None`, `Windup`, `Contact`, `Recovery`) and the timeline: the windup, contact and recovery durations, `Duration`, `At(attackSpeed)` (each divided by it; a speed that is not positive keeps them) and `PhaseAt(elapsed)` (the windup before contact, then the contact and the recovery for their durations, then none). The attack state and the animator's action layer both read phases with it. |
| [AttackState](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/AttackState.cs) | Plain class on an injected `IClock`, owned by the weapon controller. `TryStart(attack, direction, timeline, cooldown, windupCancelledByMove, recoveryCancellable)` when `CanStart`: the cooldown is over and no attack runs, or the running one is in a recovery that orders may cut short, which the next attack then ends (attacks chain). `Advance()` returns the [AttackStep](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/AttackState.cs) flags it passed: `Contact` once, on the call that reaches the end of the windup (before any end in the same call), `Ended` after the recovery, with `CutShort` when an order given earlier in the attack ends a recovery that may be cut short as it begins. `Order(move)`: during the windup a move aborts the attack under `windupCancelledByMove`; during a recovery that may be cut short any order ends it at once; otherwise the order is remembered for that recovery (the contact always plays out). `Cancel()` ends it in any phase. An attack that ends before contact gives its cooldown back. `Phase`, `IsBusy`, `Attack`, `Direction`, `Timeline`, `StartTime`, `ReadyTime`, `IsReady`. Allocates nothing. |
| [AttackService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/AttackService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton mirroring `AbilityService`, acting only where `IGameAuthority.IsAuthoritative`: `Resolve(attacker, weapon, direction, attackerPosition)` searches the weapon's hit area once, a box at `HitCentre` turned to the direction (its depth along it), with a non-allocating `Physics2D.OverlapBox` on `CombatSettings.UnitLayers` (no triggers), and hits every unit in it but the attacker (and its children) once through `HitService` with the attacker as the source and the attacker's position as the hit's origin, so the faction rules decide who is affected. Returns how many units the hit reached (0 off the authority). Offline and on the host's own player it runs at the owner's contact; a client's contact reaches it on the host through `AttackContactRpc`. Only melee weapons go through it; a ranged weapon's contact launches a projectile. `Resolve(attacker, hit, centre, size, direction, origin)` searches any such box with any hit, the hit coming from `origin`: the weapons' goes through it (from the attacker's position), and a mob's attack at its contact (`MeleeDamageDealer`, from where the mob stands). `Predict(attacker, weapon, direction, attackerPosition)` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4), off the authority only and for a weapon that fires no projectile, searches the same area as this machine shows the units, applying nothing: a living unit in it that is not the attacker, not of its faction and not immune to the weapon's damage type raises `CombatEvents.HitPredicted` with the attacker and returns true, so a client's own contact frame holds without waiting a round trip for the host's hit; the owner's `PlayerWeaponController.Tick` calls it at contact. |

### Projectiles

A ranged weapon's hit is a projectile that leaves at contact (Animation.md's Phase 4): flown on every machine, from the owner's contact there and from the owner's contact message elsewhere, and resolved only by the host when it reaches a unit. Projectiles are never networked objects.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [ProjectileSpec](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/ProjectileSpec.cs) | How a projectile flies: speed, range (from the shooter's feet), the radius it sweeps, the launch distance ahead of the feet (clamped to the range), whether it homes, and its `ProjectileVisual` prefab (none in tests). Built by `PlayerWeapon.Projectile`. |
| [ProjectileFlight](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/ProjectileFlight.cs) | Plain class, one projectile in flight, free of engine lookups: `Launch(source, sourceUnit, spec, hit, origin, direction, target)` and `Advance(distance, queries, contacts)`, which turns a homing projectile toward the middle of its target's collider and steps it along its direction, stopping it at the nearest wall or unit it can hit within the step (`ProjectileOutcome`: `Flying`, `Wall`, `Unit` with `Struck`, `Expired` once it has flown its range). It never hits its shooter or a part of it, a dead unit, or a unit of the faction the shooter had at launch (it flies through teammates, even once its shooter is gone, a player who left); while it homes on a living, enabled target it hits only that target; without a target, or once its target dies or is gone, it flies straight and hits the first unit it can. `Position`, `Origin` (the point it was shot from, its shooter's feet at the shot, which its hit comes from), `Direction`, `Flown`, `IsHoming`, `Visual`; `Clear` for its pool. |
| [IProjectileQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/IProjectileQueries.cs) / [PhysicsProjectileQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/PhysicsProjectileQueries.cs) | What a flight asks of the world: `Cast(origin, direction, distance, radius, results)` fills [ProjectileContact](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/IProjectileQueries.cs)s (the distance along the step and the unit, none for a wall), in no particular order. The physics one is a non-allocating `Physics2D.CircleCast` against the unit and wall layers (no triggers): a collider on a wall layer is a wall, any other the unit of its `DamageReceiver`, looked up once per collider (up to 128, cleared when full) and left out without one. EditMode tests stand fakes in. |
| [ProjectileService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/ProjectileService.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`ITickable`, `IDisposable`) over `IGameAuthority`, `HitService`, the physics queries on `CombatSettings`' unit and projectile wall layers, `IClock` and the `ProjectileVisualPool`. `Launch(shooter, spec, hit, origin, direction, target)` takes a pooled flight, covers the launch distance at once (a wall or unit within it stops the projectile there) and shows it through a pooled visual; `Tick` flies every projectile by its speed over the clock's time since the last frame (internal `Advance(deltaTime)`), moving its visual; a projectile that stops at a unit hits it through `HitService` with the shooter as the source and the point it was shot from as the hit's origin, so every machine turns the hit's feedback from the shot (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4), only where `IsAuthoritative` (offline or the host), and raises the internal `UnitStruck(unit, resolved, shooter)` (tests). `FlyingCount`, internal `Flying(index)`. Flights and visuals are pooled: launching, flying and landing allocate nothing once warm. `Dispose` releases every visual. |
| [ProjectileVisual](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/ProjectileVisual.cs) | On a projectile's prefab: its root stands where the projectile flies on the ground (so it sorts by that point) and its sprite child sits at the height it flies at. `Show(position, direction)`, `Move` (the sprite of the direction nearest its flight from its `DirectionalSprites`, set only when that direction changes; nothing rotates or scales it), `Hide`. Logs an error in `Awake` without its sprites or renderer. Prefab: [Arrow.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Combat/Arrow.prefab) (`Arrow_Directions`; a `Sprite` child 0.76 up, the bow's height at the release, with `SpritePixelLit` on the `Entities` sorting layer; a `SortingGroup` on the root sorts it there; unscaled, like the player). |
| [ProjectileVisualPool](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/ProjectileVisualPool.cs) | Gameplay-scope pool of projectile visuals, one free list per prefab, as `StatusVisualPool` is for status visuals; new instances move into the Gameplay scene; all destroyed on dispose. |
| [DirectionalSprites](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Projectiles/DirectionalSprites.cs) | ScriptableObject the weapon sprite writer writes (`WeaponSpriteWriter.WriteDirections`), from a weapon sprite recipe's baked arrow or an Aseprite projectile recipe's file: a sprite per direction, counterclockwise from east, evenly spaced; `IndexOf(direction)` (the nearest; east for none), `Get`, `Facing`, `Count`, `Create`. `Assets/Sprites/Weapons/Arrow_Directions.asset`: the arrow in 32 directions. |

### Death

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [DisableOnDeath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DisableOnDeath.cs) | Player: on death disables every other enabled MonoBehaviour except `Health`/`DamageReceiver` and networking components, then `DeathPhysics.Disable`; on `Health.Restored` re-enables exactly the behaviours and colliders it turned off and restores the Rigidbody2D's `simulated` flag. |
| [DestroyMobOnDeath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DestroyMobOnDeath.cs) | Mobs: disables physics and plays its death effect (`DeathEffect`, an `EffectPlayer` prefab: the Weasel's `DeathPuff.prefab`) from the `EffectPool` on every machine, its feet where the mob stood and sorted there. With authority it despawns the mob (destroying it everywhere) or, offline, destroys it; on a client it only hides the mob's renderers until the host's despawn arrives. |
| [DeathPhysics](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DeathPhysics.cs) | Static helper: disable colliders, zero and unsimulate the Rigidbody2D. |

### Damage popups

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [DamagePopupPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamagePopupPresenter.cs) | Entry point: `CombatEvents.DamageApplied` -> `DamagePopupLayer.Spawn` with the amount in the type's colour, or the immune text in the immune colour; `HealApplied` -> "+amount" in the heal colour; damage-over-time hits at `CombatSettings.PeriodicPopupSize`, the others at the popup's own size; `StatusBlocked` -> the immune text (colours from `CombatSettings`); `Tick` advances popups, `PostLateTick` re-projects them after the camera moved. Allies' popups (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3): a hit, tick, heal or block that involves another registered player but not the local one (`IsAllies(target, source)`: neither the target nor the source's unit is the local player, and one of them is a player of the `IPlayerRegistry`) shows as this machine's `UserSettings.AllyPopups` asks: like the others (`Shown`, the default), at `CombatSettings.AllyPopupSize` (`Smaller`; Galmuri has nothing under Caption, so it is the ticks' size), or not at all (`Hidden`); the local player's own (dealt or taken, a heal it gave an ally included) and those no player is part of always show. A client tells them apart with nothing new decided there: hits, immune hits, heals and blocks name their source unit on the wire. On this machine only. |
| [DamagePopupLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamagePopupLayer.cs) | On `DamagePopupCanvas`; `ObjectPool<FloatingDamageText>` with prewarm; `Spawn(message, colour, position, camera, size)` (a text size, or null for the popup's own), the popup rising unless this machine's `UserSettings.ReduceMotion` is on as it spawns (`[Inject] Construct(UserSettings)`, through the Gameplay scope's `RegisterComponent`; without settings popups rise; DesignSystem.md's Phase 6); releases popups past their lifetime. |
| [FloatingDamageText](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/FloatingDamageText.cs) | TMP text (message and colour given by the caller) that rises and fades, or fades in place when shown without rising (`Rises`); world-to-canvas projection, placed on the nearest whole UI px from the canvas's corner so its pixel font stays crisp. Its `ThemedText` (Heading, read against `Outline`, so outlined) takes the size each show asks for, or its own. Prefab: the `PopupText` component, `Assets/Prefabs/UI/Components/PopupText.prefab`. |

## Status effects

Specified in [DamageAndStatusEffects.md](DamageAndStatusEffects.md). The host applies, ticks, expires and removes statuses; clients mirror them. Nothing applies statuses in play yet (Phase 5 adds a dev tool and the first ability effects). Crowd control reaches players through `PlayerController` (orders and motor speed scale) and mobs through `MobController`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [StatusTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusTypes.cs) | `StatusTags` (flags: `Stun`, `Root`, `Silence`, `Slow`, `Burn`, `Chill`, `Poison`, `Bleed`; append-only), `StatusControls` (flags: `Stun`, `Root`, `Silence`), `StatusKind` (`Buff`, `Debuff`), `StatusStacking` (`Refresh`, `AddStack`, `Independent`), `StatusPeriodic` (`None`, `Damage`, `Heal`), `StatusApplyOutcome` (`Landed`, `Refreshed`, `Stacked`, `Immune`, `WrongFaction`, `TargetDead`, `NoAuthority`, `Invalid`). |
| [StatusEffectDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject: display name, icon, description, colour (HUD frame, world icon and visual tint), optional `StatusVisual` prefab, `Abbreviation` (first two letters of the name, cached), kind, tags, duration, stacking and max stacks, periodic effect (amount per tick and stack, damage type, tick interval), move speed, damage dealt and taken multipliers, controls, resistance deltas, granted damage immunities and status immunities. `Create(...)` for tests. |
| [StatusEffectCatalog](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectCatalog.cs) | ScriptableObject listing every definition; `Get(index)`, `IndexOf(definition)`. The index is a status's id on the wire. Referenced by `CombatSettings`. |
| [StatusEffectSet](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectSet.cs) | Plain class: one unit's instances (definition, id, stacks, remaining, tick timer, source, captured damage-dealt multiplier). `Apply` (blocked by base or granted status immunity; refresh, add a stack, or add an independent instance; at the cap the instance closest to expiring is renewed, keeping its tick phase so spamming cannot stall ticks; a granted status immunity removes the active statuses it now blocks), `Remove`, `Clear`, `Tick(deltaTime, dueTicks)` (first tick one interval after landing, a tick on the expiry instant, refreshing keeps the tick phase), `SyncFrom(snapshots)` and `CountDown(deltaTime)` (runs remaining times down without ticking or expiring) for mirrors, `GetRemaining(definition)` (longest instance), `GetSnapshot`, `GetStacks`, and combined values recomputed only on change: move speed, damage dealt and taken (products, multipliers raised to the stack count), controls (OR), resistance deltas (times stacks), granted immunities. Event `Changed`. No allocation once warm. [StatusTick](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusTick.cs) and [StatusSnapshot](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusSnapshot.cs) are its outputs. |
| [StatusEffects](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffects.cs) | Component on players and mobs: owns the set; `Changed`, `Blocked`, `Count`, `GetSnapshot`, `GetStacks`, `GetRemaining` (longest instance), damage and move speed multipliers, `Controls`, `MovementScale` (0 while stunned or rooted, otherwise the move speed multiplier), `IsHeld` (from an [IStatusHold](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/IStatusHold.cs)). No injection. |
| [StatusEffectService](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectService.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`ITickable`), authority only. `Apply(target, definition, source)`: rejects without authority, unknown or unit-less targets, dead targets, and the wrong faction (debuffs only across factions, buffs only within one); captures the source's damage-dealt multiplier; an immune target raises `StatusEffects.Blocked` and publishes `CombatEvents.StatusBlocked`. Tracks every unit a status landed on (subscribing to its `Health.Died` to clear its statuses on death) and drops it once its set is empty or it is destroyed. `Tick` advances every tracked unit neither held nor out of play (`IUnitProtection`; `IsHeld(receiver)` tells either) by the `IClock` delta, then turns the due ticks into `DamageService.ApplyPeriodicDamage` or `ApplyHeal`. `Remove`, `ClearAll`, `PublishReplicatedBlock` (clients). |
| [NetworkStatusEffects](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/NetworkStatusEffects.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on players and mobs. Host: rewrites a server-written `NetworkList<StatusEntry>` whenever the set changes and on the frame its statuses run again after a hold (`StatusEffectService.IsHeld`, the clients having counted them down through it), sends `BlockedRpc(catalogIndex, source, hasSource)` for blocked statuses, naming the status's source unit when it is spawned (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3; server-invoked only), and is the unit's `IStatusHold` (held while the owner is a client that `IClientReadiness` says is not ready: joined but with no chunk reported, or travelling until it reports a chunk for its trip). Clients: rebuild their mirror with `SyncFrom` on spawn and once per frame after list changes, subtracting the server time elapsed since each write from its remaining time. Logs an error when spawned without a catalog, and for a status missing from it. [StatusEntry](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEntry.cs): instance id, catalog index, stacks, remaining, server write time. |
| [StatusVisual](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisual.cs) | On a visual prefab: tints its sprite with the status colour and pulses its alpha (`Time.deltaTime`, visual only); `SetLayer(i)` draws the i-th visual on a unit 20% larger per layer so stacked rings stay visible; `Follow`, `Show`, `Hide`. The only prefab is [StatusAura.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Effects/StatusAura.prefab): the `GroundRing` sprite under the unit (`Terrain` layer, order 9, just below the markers), scale 0.9. |
| [StatusVisualPool](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisualPool.cs) | Gameplay-scope pool, one free list per visual prefab; new instances move into the Gameplay scene; all destroyed on dispose. |
| [StatusVisuals](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisuals.cs) | Component on players and mobs, injected with the pool (logs an error in `Start` without one): on `Changed` it returns the visuals of ended statuses and shows one for each active status with a visual, re-layering them; keeps them at the unit's feet in `LateUpdate`; returns them all in `OnDisable` (death through `DisableOnDeath`, despawn). Runs on every machine. |
| [StatusReport](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusReport.cs) | A blocked status: target, definition, source, popup position. |

Statuses survive trips between worlds, since players live in the Gameplay scene, and stand still during them though trips never stop time: the host holds a traveller's statuses until its machine has faded in (`TripProtection`). Test statuses in `Assets/Data/StatusEffects`, all in `StatusEffectCatalog.asset`: [StatusEffects.md](Generated/StatusEffects.md) lists each with its kind, tags, duration, stacking, periodic effect and totals, modifiers, controls, immunities and description (DesignSystem.md's Phase 1).

## Effects

Hit feedback and one-shot effects (Animation.md's Phase 6). Every machine shows the hits it learns of, the host its own and a client the host's through `NetworkHealth`; nothing here is networked, and nothing in gameplay reads it. The code is under `Assets/Scripts/Effects`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [HitFeedbackPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/HitFeedbackPresenter.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point on `CombatEvents.DamageApplied`. A direct hit: the struck unit's `HitFeedback` flashes white (50 ms), holds (80 ms) and is nudged away from the source; the source unit (its `DamageReceiver`'s object) holds its contact frame when the hit lands as it shows (`HoldContact`); the local player's camera kicks along the hit (`ICameraKick`) when it took the hit or is its source; then the impact of the hit's damage type plays from the `EffectPool`, facing along the hit, where the line from the source to the middle of the struck unit's collider enters it (`HitFeedback.ImpactGround`), raised to its impact height and sorted with its feet. An immune hit, an immune tick included, only flashes the unit in `CombatSettings.ImmuneColor` (50 ms), as its popup reads the immune text; a damage-over-time tick only washes it 40% toward its type's `CombatSettings.GetColor` (80 ms). Directions come from the point the hit came from as the host resolved it (`DamageReport.Origin`: where an arrow was shot from, where an attacker or caster stood; KnownGaps.md's Phase 4), the same on every machine, to where this machine shows the target; a hit without one comes from where this machine shows its source, and a hit with neither does not nudge or kick. A client's own melee hit it predicts at its contact (`CombatEvents.HitPredicted`, from `AttackService.Predict`) holds its attacker's contact frame there and then, as a landed hit does (internal `ShowPredicted`); the flash, nudge, impact and kick wait for the host's hit, which a round trip later holds nothing more. This machine's display preferences (`UserSettings`, DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), read at each hit: with `ReduceFlashing` every flash (a direct hit's, an immune hit's and a tick's wash) shows at the settings' `ReducedFlashShare` of its strength, and with `ReduceMotion` the camera does not kick; the hold, the nudge and the impact stay. Internal `Show(report)` for tests. Allocates nothing once its effects are warm. |
| [HitFeedback](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/HitFeedback.cs) | On a unit's root (the player and the Weasel): its body renderer, the transform holding it, its `CharacterVisuals`, its body collider and impact height. `Flash(colour, amount, seconds)`: a property block mixing the lit sprite that far toward the colour after lighting (the `TopDownRPG/Sprite Pixel Lit` shader's `_FlashColor`/`_FlashAmount`), cleared when it ends so unflashed sprites keep batching. `Hold(seconds)` and `HoldContact(seconds)`: through the character visuals. `Nudge(direction, stepPixels, stepSeconds, pixelsPerUnit)`: the sprite's transform off its rest place by each step's whole pixels in turn (each axis rounded), then back; the root, the body and its colliders never move. `ImpactGround(source, hasSource)`. Timed on `Time.deltaTime` in `LateUpdate` (internal `Tick`), each whole on the frame it starts; nothing shows while it is disabled (a dead player), and disabling puts the sprite, its place and its material back. Logs an error in `Awake` without its renderer, transform or character visuals, or when the body's material cannot flash. Internal counters (`FlashCount`, `HoldCount`, `NudgeCount`, `LastNudgeDirection`, `NudgeOffset`, `IsFlashing`, `IsHolding`) and `Configure` for tests. |
| [HitFeedbackSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/HitFeedbackSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Combat/HitFeedbackSettings.asset`): the hit flash's colour and length (white, 0.05 s), the immune flash's length (0.05 s), a tick's wash (0.4 for 0.08 s), the hold (0.08 s, the attacker too), the nudge's steps (2 px then 1 px, 0.04 s each, at 32 px per unit), the impact effect (`Impact.prefab`), the camera kick (2 px over 0.12 s) and `ReducedFlashShare`, how much of its strength each flash keeps when the player reduces flashing (a third; DesignSystem.md's Phase 6). `Create` for tests. |
| [EffectSprites](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/EffectSprites.cs) | ScriptableObject the effect writer writes (`EffectSheetWriter.Write`), from a baked effect recipe or an Aseprite effect recipe's files: the frames of a one-shot effect per variant (an impact's damage type, in `DamageType`'s order) and direction (evenly spaced counterclockwise from east), each shown `FrameSeconds`, and each variant's light colour (`LightColour(variant)`, clear for one that casts no light, Lighting.md's Phase 2); `Get(variant, direction, frame)`, `DirectionOf(direction)` (the nearest), `Duration`, `Create`. Runtime code only reads it. |
| [EffectPlayer](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/EffectPlayer.cs) | On an effect prefab's root, with a `SortingGroup` (`[RequireComponent]`) and its sprite renderer on a `Sprite` child: `Play(variant, direction, position, sortingY, pool)` stands the root at `sortingY` less its `sortNudge` (toward the camera), where the group sorts it, moves the child to `position`, shows the first frame there, then each frame on `Time.deltaTime` (internal `Tick`), and goes back to its pool after the last. With a light (`EffectLight`, on its sprite child, and its `lightIntensity`, Lighting.md's Phase 2), a variant its frames give a light colour turns it on at that intensity in that colour as it starts, fading it linearly to nothing over its frames; other variants leave it off. A pooled effect stays active with its renderer and light off (`Hide`). `IsPlaying`, `Variant`, `Direction`, `Frame`, `Sprites`. Logs an error in `Awake` without its frames or renderer, or with the renderer not on a child. Nothing rotates or scales it. |
| [EffectPool](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/EffectPool.cs) | Gameplay-scope pool of `EffectPlayer`s, one free list per prefab, as `ProjectileVisualPool` is for projectiles: `Play(prefab, variant, direction, position, sortingY)` on a free instance or a new one moved into the Gameplay scene, `Release`; every instance is destroyed on dispose. Internal `Played` event and `CreatedCount` for tests. Playing allocates nothing once warm, but for turning an effect's Light 2D on and off in the editor (its sorting layer callbacks, under `UNITY_EDITOR`). Users: the presenter's impacts and `DestroyMobOnDeath`. |

Prefabs: [Impact.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Effects/Impact.prefab) (`Impact_Spark_Sprites`, URP's `Sprite-Unlit-Default`, so it shows bright whatever the light, on the `Entities` layer, sorting 0.05 in front of what stands at its sorting height; a `Light` under its `Sprite`, a point light of radius 2 at 1.2, see Lighting) and [DeathPuff.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Effects/DeathPuff.prefab) (`DeathPuff_Smoke_Sprites`, `SpritePixelLit`, `Entities`, no nudge), both unscaled. The flash is the pixel-lit sprite shader's, mixed in after lighting in its lit and unlit passes (see Lighting). In the editor it compiles on first use, so the first sprites drawing with it show nothing for a frame or two there.

## Mob AI: components

Prefab: [Weasel.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Mobs/Weasel.prefab): `MobController`, `MobMotor2D`, `MobPerception2D`, `MobPathAgent2D`, `MobPatrolAnchor`, `MeleeDamageDealer`, `Health` (its health in [Units.md](Generated/Units.md)), `DamageReceiver` (`Profile_Weasel`), `StatusEffects`, `StatusVisuals`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`, `NetworkObject`, `NetworkTransform` (server authority, x/y only, `LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas), `NetworkHealth`, `NetworkStatusEffects`, `MobNetworkSync`, `HitFeedback` (height 0.65, holding through its character visuals), `CharacterVisuals` (the Weasel's set and `Locomotion_Weasel`) on `Visuals` (on the root, the feet; its renderer on `SpritePixelLit.mat`), a `SortingGroup` on the root sorting it at its feet, `WorldHealthBar` and `WorldStatusIcons` (on the `HealthBar` child, 1.39 above the feet, clear of the ears, with an `Overlay` sorting group of its own sorting at the root); config `Mob_Default`, death effect `DeathPuff.prefab`. No Mecanim animator.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [MobController](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobController.cs) | The brain. Initializes all mob components from `MobConfig` and its `INavigation2D` (its world's `WorldNavigation`, or a standalone `NavigationGrid2D` in tests), builds a standalone grid if needed, prewarms regions, creates the six states and `MobSeparation2D`. `Update` -> `TickStateMachine(dt)`; `FixedUpdate` -> `FixedTickStateMachine()`. While enabled it plays its `MeleeDamageDealer`'s attacks on the motor (`AttackStarted` -> `MobMotor2D.PlayAttack` on the dealer's timeline, `AttackCancelled` -> `StopAttack`). Exposes components to states, `ChangeState`, attack-range checks (collider distance), gizmos + state label. `Configure(...)` is a non-DI setup path used by tests. `SetHome(rect)` gives a mob of a world its home chunk's area (`ChunkMobSpawner` calls it; see `MobPatrolAnchor`). Logs an error in `Start` if it was never given its grid, players and random source. Reads its `StatusEffects` (when it has one): while stunned (`IsStunned`) perception still ticks but a running attack is cancelled (its cooldown given back), the state neither ticks nor fixed-ticks and the motor is stopped, so the mob resumes in the same state; every fixed tick it sets the motor's speed scale to 0 while an attack runs (the mob holds still through it), else to the statuses' `MovementScale` (0 while stunned or rooted, so a rooted mob keeps deciding and attacking in range but cannot move). |
| [MobMotor2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobMotor2D.cs) | Rigidbody2D movement: `desiredVelocity` (path) + `steeringVelocity` (separation), clamped to `CurrentMoveSpeed` (move speed times the speed scale set by `SetSpeedScale`), accelerated with `MoveTowards` in `FixedTick`; a speed scale of 0 stops the body at once. The move speed and acceleration are the config's, taken in `Initialize`; the component serializes neither. Faces the intended direction, not drift. Feeds the mob's `CharacterVisuals` (Animation.md's Phase 7; `Visuals`, found in the children when empty): every fixed tick `SetLocomotion` with the velocity it moves at on purpose (zero while only separation drifts it) and its facing; `PlayAttack(direction, timeline, untilContact)` plays its config's attack clip (`MobConfig.AttackClip`, looked up in `Initialize`, an error when the set lacks it) toward the direction, `ShowAttackContact` and `StopAttack`. `IsMoving`, `FacingDirection`, `CurrentMove` (its actual velocity over its move speed while it moves on purpose, zero standing). `ShowRemoteMovement(move, facing)` (a client) shows the replicated move at the copy's own move speed instead of its body. |
| [MobNetworkSync](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobNetworkSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on mobs (Animation.md's Phase 7). Host: writes the motor's `CurrentMove` and `FacingDirection` into server-written `NetworkVariable`s when either changed past 0.01, and relays its `MeleeDamageDealer`'s attacks to the clients: `AttackStartedRpc(attack, direction)` (attack 0, the mob's only one), `AttackContactRpc` and `AttackEndedRpc` (an early end: a stun, death), which only the server may invoke (`InvokePermission`; Netcode's default would let any client make every other client's copies swing). Clients: disable the `MobController` (no AI), make the body kinematic without interpolation, feed the replicated move and facing into `MobMotor2D.ShowRemoteMovement`, and play each attack on the copy (`MobMotor2D.PlayAttack` on the copy's own config's timeline, held on the windup's last frame until the contact arrives, `ShowAttackContact`, `StopAttack`). Only the host resolves hits. A late joiner sees attacks from their next start; a contact or end without a start does nothing. |
| [MobPerception2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPerception2D.cs) | Picks its target from its `IPlayerRegistry`: its world's `WorldPlayers`, so a mob of a world considers only the players in it and drops a target that leaves it. Range hysteresis (`detectionRadius` to acquire, `loseTargetDistance` to keep), throttled `Physics2D.Linecast` against `obstacleLayerMask`, logging an error from `Initialize` when the config sets no obstacle layers (it would see through walls). Keeps its current target while it stays detected; while it is not detected (hidden or outside detection range) it switches to the nearest other alive player inside `detectionRadius` with line of sight. Drops the target when it dies, leaves the registry or goes beyond `loseTargetDistance`. Exposes `CurrentTarget`, `CurrentTargetPlayer`, `HasDetectedTarget`, `HasLineOfSight`, `LastKnownTargetPosition`, `IsTargetHiddenInRange`. |
| [MobPathAgent2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPathAgent2D.cs) | Mob wrapper around `PathFollower2D`: configures it from `MobConfig` (movement profile, `waypointReachDistance`, `arrivalDistance`; the nearest-cell search radius is its navigation's; shortcut off), builds from `MobMotor2D.Position`, ticks the follower at `MobMotor2D.CurrentMoveSpeed` (so a rooted mob does not stall) and feeds the returned velocity to the motor (`Stop` when there is no path). Public API: `BuildPathToWorld`, `CanReachWorldTarget`, `FixedTick`, `ClearPath`, path flags, `StalledTime`, `Navigation`. Keeps the `MobPathAgent2D.BuildPathToWorld` profiler marker and the path gizmos. See Navigation. |
| [MobPatrolAnchor](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPatrolAnchor.cs) | Remembers spawn position; samples reachable roam destinations within `patrolRoamRadius` and idle durations from the injected `IRandom` (no roaming without one). A mob of a world has a home (`SetHome`, `HasHome`, `HomeArea`: its home chunk's 64 x 64 cells in world units, kept across rebuilds): roam destinations lie inside it (`IsInHome`), and `IsWithinLeash(position)` and `IsPathWithinLeash(waypoints)` tell whether points lie within `MobConfig.LeashCells` of it, always true without a home. `IsLeashed` (`SetLeashed`) records that the mob gave up at its leash and is going home; `Initialize` clears it. An area's mob has no home and no leash. |
| [MobSeparation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobSeparation2D.cs) | Plain class for crowd steering. See Crowd separation. |
| [IMobState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/IMobState.cs), [MobStateId](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobStateId.cs) | State contract (`Enter`/`Tick`/`FixedTick`/`Exit`) and ids. |
| [MobConfig](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobConfig.cs) | ScriptableObject with movement, perception, search, combat, crowd, patrol, leash (`LeashCells`, 8: how far beyond its home chunk's edge a mob of a world chases; see Worlds and streaming for the rule it must keep) and navigation tuning, read-only at runtime: private serialized fields behind PascalCase properties (`MoveSpeed`, `DetectionRadius`, `AttackStatuses` as a read-only list, ...), with the design notes as inspector tooltips. Its obstacle mask has no default in code: each asset sets it in the inspector (the `Obstacles` layer). The Combat header holds the attack (Animation.md's Phase 7): its reach to start (`AttackStopDistance`, collider to collider) and the exit buffer, damage, type and statuses, the interval (the cooldown from the start), the timeline (`AttackTimeline` from the windup, contact (at least 0.01) and recovery), its area (`AttackArea`: width across, depth along, from the collider's edge) and its clip in the mob's animation set (`AttackClip`). Tests build theirs with `MobConfig.Create(...)`, whose named values left out keep the defaults, but for the obstacle mask, which defaults to the `Obstacles` layer. Assets: `Assets/Settings/AI/Mob_Default.asset` (10 Physical, interval 0.75, 0.45/0.05/0.25 s, a 1 by 0.75 area, `DaggerSlash`), `Assets/Dev/StressTest/Mob_StressTest.asset` (the same with no damage, so it never attacks). |

Per-frame order: `Update`: perception tick, then the damage dealer's `Tick` (the running attack moves on, its hit landing at contact; while stunned a running attack is cancelled instead), then state `Tick` (decisions, repath, starting attacks; skipped while stunned). `LateUpdate`: the character visuals show the frame. `FixedUpdate`: motor speed scale (0 while an attack runs, else from statuses), separation sense, state `FixedTick` (path following sets desired velocity; skipped and the motor stopped while stunned), motor applies desired + steering.

## Mob AI: state machine

States are plain classes deriving from [MobStateBase](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/MobStateBase.cs), which exposes the brain's components and config. "Spots target" below (`SpotsTarget`) means the mob is not leashed, `HasDetectedTarget`, the target and the mob both lie within the leash (`IsWithinLeash`), and `PathAgent.CanReachWorldTarget(target)`. "Gives up at the leash" (`GiveUpAtLeash`) marks the mob leashed and goes to Return; while leashed it spots no target, until Idle's timer runs out. Only a mob of a world (with a home chunk) has a leash; for an area's mob every leash check passes.

| State | Enter | Tick transitions | FixedTick |
|---|---|---|---|
| [IdleState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/IdleState.cs) | Clear path, stop, roll idle duration | Spots target -> Chase; timer done -> no longer leashed, Patrol | Stop |
| [PatrolRoamState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/PatrolRoamState.cs) | Pick reachable roam point (inside the home chunk for a mob of a world), build path; fail -> Idle | Spots target -> Chase; arrived or stalled `stuckTimeout` -> Idle | Follow path |
| [ChaseState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/ChaseState.cs) | Clear path | Lost target -> Search if hidden within lose range, else Return; target or mob beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; in attack range -> AttackRange; every `repathInterval` repath, and unreachable / partial / goal-adjusted -> Return, a waypoint beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash | Wait (stop, face target) if an ally is directly ahead near the target (with release hysteresis), else follow path |
| [AttackRangeState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/AttackRangeState.cs) | Clear path, stop, attack | While an attack runs, nothing (it plays out, the mob holding still); then lost target -> Search/Return; target or mob beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; beyond `attackStopDistance + attackExitBuffer` -> Chase; else face + `TryStartAttack` | Stop; `Exit` cancels an attack still running (a rebuild) |
| [SearchState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/SearchState.cs) | Last known position beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; strict path to it; fail -> Return | Spots target -> Chase; once arrived or stalled, count down `searchDuration` -> Return | Follow path |
| [ReturnToSpawnState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/ReturnToSpawnState.cs) | Resolve spawn cell (or nearest walkable), build path; fail -> Idle | Spots target -> Chase; in spawn cell, arrived or stalled -> Idle | Follow path |

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    Idle --> Patrol: idle timer
    Idle --> Chase: spots target
    Patrol --> Idle: arrived / stuck
    Patrol --> Chase: spots target
    Chase --> AttackRange: in range
    Chase --> Search: lost, hidden in range
    Chase --> Return: lost / unreachable / leash
    AttackRange --> Chase: out of range
    AttackRange --> Search: lost, hidden in range
    AttackRange --> Return: lost / leash
    Search --> Chase: spots target
    Search --> Return: search time over / no path / leash
    Return --> Chase: spots target
    Return --> Idle: home / stuck
```

## Crowd separation

[MobSeparation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobSeparation2D.cs), owned by each `MobController`:

- `Sense(position)` each fixed tick: `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` (non-allocating, `ContactFilter2D` on the mob's own layer, no triggers) within `separationRadius * SenseRadiusFactor`; push away from neighbours closer than `separationRadius`, weighted by overlap; exactly coincident mobs get opposite deterministic directions from instance ids.
- Velocity = push * `separationStrength`, clamped per axis so it never points into an unwalkable cell or across the cliff of raised ground (look-ahead on the mob's navigation, `CrossesCliff`).
- `HasNeighborToward(target, maxDistance)`: used by `ChaseState` to wait behind allies within `crowdWaitDistance` of the target instead of pushing into them.
- The motor adds this steering velocity to the path velocity.

Config: `MobConfig` Crowd header (`separationRadius`, `separationStrength`, `crowdWaitDistance`).

## Navigation and pathfinding

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [NavigationGrid2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationGrid2D.cs) | MonoBehaviour on each chunk's grid, and on a standalone grid (a Scene Generator scratch scene, tests): what a navigation page reads, and a standalone grid's navigation (`INavigation2D`). On a standalone grid, `BuildGrid` (in `Awake`) collects cells from each terrain source's tilemap (row z = 0), tagged with the source's `TerrainType2D`, then removes every cell that has a tile on one of its `blockingTilemaps` (the collision tilemap), so a wall blocks all terrain layers, and every cell that a collider on its `obstacleLayers` overlaps (`Obstacles` on every grid: the footprints of blocking props). Those come from one `Physics2D.OverlapArea` over the collected cells at build time, each collider's bounds shrunk by 0.01 so one ending on a cell edge leaves the next cell free; triggers and colliders on tilemaps are skipped, since blocking tilemaps already cover theirs. The obstacle cells are kept for the cell refreshes below, so a tile edit never unblocks a prop's cell; a prop moved, added or removed at runtime needs a `BuildGrid` (`ConfigureObstacleLayers` sets the mask in code). Sources on a dual-grid render tilemap are skipped with an error, and so are blocking tilemaps that are dual-grid render tilemaps. The cells are then baked into a `NavigationPage` over `WalkableBounds` (the smallest rectangle holding every walkable cell; `GetCell`), in a `NavigationMap2D` of that one page and a pool of one slot of its own, made again on every `BuildGrid`; the grid passes every query to that map. On a chunk's grid (one with a `WorldChunk`) `Awake` builds nothing: `WorldNavigation.AddChunk` builds its page over the chunk's 64 x 64 cells into the world's map (`PlaceIn`), `RemoveChunk` takes it out (`Unbuild`), and outside Play Mode `BuildGrid` builds that page on its own for the gizmos. Each `TerrainMovementProfile2D` (and the null profile) gets a cost field on first use (`NavigationCostField`, see `NavigationPagePool`): per-cell traversal cost (0 = blocked; where terrain layers overlap, the highest `NavigationTerrainSource2D.Priority` decides and equal priorities combine as most restrictive walkability, highest cost; plus the profile's `EdgeCost` on cells touching a blocked one), diagonal cost, and 4-connected region, rebaked lazily, page by page, when a page changes or the profile's `Version` does. The grid's `Version` changes on every build and every change to its page (its own cells, or a neighbouring chunk's page loading or unloading). Live updates: while enabled the grid listens to `Tilemap.tilemapPositionsChanged` and, for its source and blocking tilemaps, re-reads just the changed cells (`RefreshCells`, also public) and bumps `Version`; a changed cell outside a standalone grid's `WalkableBounds` that gained terrain triggers a full `BuildGrid` (a chunk ignores cells outside its chunk). World/cell conversion, octile heuristic, Bresenham line cost, nearest-walkable search, O(1) `AreCellsConnected` and the `GridAStarPathfinder2D` are its map's. With a raised tilemap (`ConfigureRaisedTilemap`, `RaisedTilemap`; the scene writer and Convert Ground set the grid's) it also bakes the cliff of raised ground, a line along the sides between raised cells (ramps included) and level ones that blocks no cell (`RaisedGround.CanCross`; TerrainTypes.md's Phase 13): a byte per cell with a bit per side (`LeftSide`, `RightSide`, `DownSide`, `UpSide`; `GetCliffSides`), kept in the pool, which A* (a straight step across a cliff side, a diagonal one when either way round its corner crosses one), `TryGetLineCost`, `MovementCost` (a blocked step's cost), the region flood fill and `CrossesCliff(from, to)` (public, along the Bresenham line, for movement off the paths) respect, so units cross between raised and level ground only at a ramp's foot. The profile's edge cost counts a cell with a cliff side, on either side of the cliff. A changed cell of that tilemap re-reads the sides of the cell and its 4 neighbours. The cliff face rises the style's cliff height over `TerrainStyle.PixelsPerCell` (read at each build, before blocked cells are removed, from the `TerrainBlendRenderer` on the grid's object that draws the same raised tilemap; none without one) from the bottom of the lowest raised cell that is no ramp with level ground below, through the raised cells above it. The rows it covers whole (`RaisedGround.RowsCovered`: two at the project's 64 px, none for a face under a cell) are removed like walls (`RaisedGround.IsOnFace`, over the raised ground of every loaded page through `RaisedGround.IKinds`), except above a ramp, where the ramp's slope rises instead (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 14), and a changed cell of the raised tilemap also re-reads the cells above it up to that many rows (`RefreshFaceColumn`). `MoveOffCliffFace(point)` moves a point on the face, a covered cell included, straight up to its top (`RaisedGround.OffTheFace`); `PathFollower2D.BuildPath` applies it to every goal. `GetBlocker(cell, profile)` (internal, for `MovementBlockerGizmo`) tells what keeps a profile off a cell (`Blocker`: `None`, `NoGround` (no terrain, also outside the bounds), `Terrain` the profile cannot walk, `Wall` (a blocking tilemap), `Obstacle`, `CliffFace` (a cell the cliff face covers whole); the cliff's line blocks no cell). Outside Play Mode its gizmos build it on demand (`BuildGizmoGridIfStale`: when stale, or when unbuilt unless the last such build failed, so a broken setup logs its error once rather than on every repaint). |
| [NavigationTerrainSource2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationTerrainSource2D.cs) | Sits on a tilemap (`[RequireComponent(typeof(Tilemap))]`) and marks its cells as one `TerrainType2D`, with a `Priority` for layers stacked over others (a road or bridge). On dual-grid terrain it goes on the data tilemap; `IsDualGridRenderTilemap` flags a misplaced one. On a ground layer of a `TerrainBlendRenderer` (`TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing`) the priority is the layer's index there, its draw order (see World): `Reset` gives a source added there that priority, its inspector (`NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor`) shows it read-only, and in the editor `OnValidate` tells that renderer, which rebuilds and checks it, so sources added after the renderer built are followed too. |
| [INavigation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/INavigation2D.cs) | What paths, players and mobs use: `IsBuilt`, `Version`, `Pathfinder`, world/cell conversion, `IsCellWalkable`, `TryGetNearestWalkableCell`, `MovementCost`, `HeuristicCost`, `AreCellsConnected`, `RunsBeyondLoadedPages` (whether a cell's region runs on into ground the navigation could load but has not, so what it connects to is not all known; KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), `TryGetLineCost`, `CrossesCliff`, `MoveOffCliffFace`, `PrewarmRegions`. A world's `WorldNavigation` and a standalone `NavigationGrid2D` both provide it. |
| [NavigationPage](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationPage.cs) | Internal. One rectangle of cells read from a `NavigationGrid2D`'s configuration (its terrain layers, at most 64, usable blocking tilemaps, raised tilemap, obstacle cells and cliff face height): per cell, in flat arrays allocated once, the terrain layer bits of the highest priority there (0 = blocked or no ground; the cost bake reads a layer's `TerrainType2D` by its bit) and the kind of raised ground. `ReadTerrainBlock` reads each layer and blocking tilemap in one `GetTilesBlockNonAlloc`. Its `Slot`, `Base` (its first index in the pool), `Around` (the 3 x 3 pages around it in its map, itself in the middle), `IndexAt(x, y)` (a local cell, or one a step beyond its edge in a neighbouring page), and a `Stamp` renewed on every change, its own or a neighbour's. |
| [NavigationPagePool](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationPagePool.cs) | Slots for pages, shared by every map placed in it: a cell's index is `slot * SlotSize + local index`. Holds the cliff sides, each profile's `NavigationCostField` and the A* `SearchState`, all flat arrays sized to the pool and reused. The Gameplay scope's pool (24 slots of 4096 cells: the host holds at most 16 chunks in interest, plus those waiting out their hysteresis) is shared by every loaded world; a standalone grid has one of a single slot. A page placed when every slot is taken grows it (doubling, with a warning). `GetCostField(profile)` bakes again only the pages whose stamp changed (traversal cost from the layer bits, the edge cost, diagonal costs, and the page's own 4-connected regions, labelled from its base), then, after any change, joins the regions meeting along the right and upper borders of loaded neighbouring pages where no cliff runs between them (a union-find over the labels, flattened so `AreCellsConnected` is one read). A cell beside one where no page is loaded counts as beside a blocked cell for the edge cost. `UsedSlots`, `SlotCount`. |
| [NavigationCostField](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationCostField.cs) | Internal. One profile's view of a pool: `Costs` (0 = blocked), `DiagonalCosts`, `Regions` (-1 = blocked), `Roots` (each region's joined root), the stamp each slot was baked at and its region count. |
| [NavigationMap2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationMap2D.cs) | `INavigation2D` over pages of one size on a page grid, in a pool: the queries above, reading across the borders of loaded neighbouring pages, a cell where no page is loaded blocked. `AddPage` places a page, links it with its loaded neighbours, reads its cells (the cliff face's rows reading the page below) and then reads again what its neighbours read across their shared borders (their border cliff sides, and the face rows of the page above), stamping them; `RemovePage` does the same the other way. `RefreshCell` and `RefreshRaisedCell` follow tile edits in whichever pages the cells touch, stamping a neighbour when the cell is on its border. Every change bumps `Version`. `RunsBeyondLoadedPages(cell, profile)`: whether the cell's region, joined across the loaded pages, holds a border cell (no cliff across it) of a loaded page whose neighbour across that border is a page the map could load but has not (`CouldLoadPage`, every place of the page grid here; a world's navigation only its world's chunks); a single area's region never does. It walks the loaded pages' borders, so it is for occasional checks (a saved place), not paths. `MaxSearchCells` (4096) and `NearestCellSearchRadius` (8) by default (`WorldDefinition.DefaultMaxSearchCells` and `DefaultNearestCellSearchRadius`, the one source); a standalone grid sets its own, a world's navigation its world's. The radius is the only one: mobs pass none, so they search as far as their navigation does. A single-grid map (a standalone grid's) also reads its tilemaps outside its page, as a standalone grid always did; a world's map knows nothing outside its pages. |
