| [WorldNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/WorldNavigation.cs) | A world's navigation, in the world's scope: a `NavigationMap2D` with a page per loaded chunk on a 16 x 16 grid of 64-cell pages from the world's cell (0, 0), its pages in the Gameplay scope's pool, with its world's search limits (`WorldDefinition.MaxSearchCells`, `NearestCellSearchRadius`; the defaults with no world, as the edit-mode navigation of tests has). A chunk the world lacks is no page it could load, so a region at the world's edge runs on nowhere (`CouldLoadPage`). `AddChunk(grid, coords)` builds the chunk grid's page over the chunk's cells and joins it (false, with an error, for a chunk outside the world or added twice), `RemoveChunk(coords)`, `HasChunk`; `Dispose` takes every page out. A Sandbox chunk's page builds in 1 to 3 ms in the editor (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 3 notes). |
| [ChunkNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkNavigation.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point: adds the chunk's page to its world's `WorldNavigation` in `Start` (once every collider of the scene is in the physics world) and takes it out when the chunk's scope is disposed. |
| [IPathfinder2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/IPathfinder2D.cs) | `FindPath(request[, cellsBuffer])`. |
| [GridAStarPathfinder2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/GridAStarPathfinder2D.cs) | A* over a `NavigationMap2D`'s cost field: 8 neighbours without corner cutting or a step across the cliff of raised ground (not checked on a map without raised ground, `HasCliffSides`), a step off a page's edge reading the loaded neighbouring page directly (`NavigationPage.IndexAt`), binary heap, search state (`SearchState`) in flat arrays indexed like the pool, shared by every map in it and stamped per search (no clearing and no allocations once warm). Expands exactly the cells the earlier dictionary-based version did, about 7x faster in `PathfindingBenchmarkTests`. Strict requests fail fast when the goal is unwalkable or in another region. Partial requests adjust an unwalkable goal to the nearest walkable cell, and toward an unreachable goal return the path to the closest expanded cell, capped by `MaxSearchCells`; on a world's map a partial request toward a goal where no page is loaded, with no walkable cell near it, heads for it the same way, so the path ends at the loaded edge. `ProfilerMarker` `GridAStarPathfinder2D.FindPath`. |
| [PathTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathTypes.cs) | `PathRequest` (start, goal, allowPartial, profile), `PathResult` (success, partial, goal adjusted, reached resolved goal, cells). |
| [TerrainType2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/TerrainType2D.cs) | Terrain tag asset (`Assets/Settings/AI/Terrain_Grass.asset`, `Terrain_DryGrass.asset`, `Terrain_Weeds.asset`, `Terrain_Meadow.asset`, `Terrain_TallGrass.asset`, `Terrain_Snow.asset`, `Terrain_Dirt.asset`, `Terrain_Sand.asset`, `Terrain_Mud.asset`, `Terrain_Ice.asset`, `Terrain_Gravel.asset`, `Terrain_Plaza.asset`, `Terrain_Stone.asset`, `Terrain_Shallows.asset`, `Terrain_Water.asset`, `Terrain_Ocean.asset`, `Terrain_Lava.asset`, `Terrain_Planks.asset`): the asset itself is the tag, holding no data; tests build theirs with `Create(name)`. |
| [TerrainMovementProfile2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/TerrainMovementProfile2D.cs) | Per-mob rules: default walkable/cost + per-terrain `TerrainRule`s (read-only properties) + `EdgeCost`, extra cost for entering a walkable cell whose 8 neighbours include one the profile cannot walk (keeps paths off ground edges and wall corners; 0 = off), read-only at runtime; `Version` bumps when the asset is edited (`OnValidate`) so cost fields rebake. Tests build theirs with `Create(defaultWalkable, defaultCost, [edgeCost,] rules)`. Asset: `Assets/Settings/AI/TerrainMovement_Default.asset`. |
| [PathFollower2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollower2D.cs) | Plain class shared by mobs (through `MobPathAgent2D`) and, from Phase 2 of [PlayerControls.md](PlayerControls.md), players. `Configure(navigation, profile, settings)` (any `INavigation2D`), `BuildPath(start, goal, allowPartial)`, `CanReach(start, goal)`, `Tick(position, moveSpeed, deltaTime)` returning the desired velocity (zero once there is no path; after the navigation changes, a path that can no longer be walked is rebuilt, or dropped when it cannot be, no route or no navigation left), `Clear`, path flags, `StalledTime`, `Waypoints`. No engine lookups, no motor or `MobConfig` dependency, no allocations once its buffers are warm. |
| [PathFollowerSettings2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollowerSettings2D.cs) | Waypoint reach distance, arrival distance, nearest-cell search radius (-1 = the grid's) and `UseStraightLineShortcut`. |
| [PlayerNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/World/PlayerNavigation.cs) | Gameplay-scope lookup of the navigation a player paths on, since players live in the Gameplay scope and cannot inject a world's. `Of(transform)`: for a registered player in a world (`PlayerWorlds`), that world's `LoadedWorld.Navigation` through `Worlds`; null while the world is not loaded and for a player in no world. Read by `PlayerMotor2D` on every `MoveTo`. It replaced `ActiveNavigationGrid` (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 3); its area half went with the areas in Phase 9. |

Path follower flow ([PathFollower2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollower2D.cs)): world goal (moved off the cliff face, `MoveOffCliffFace`) -> cells -> `FindPath` into a reused buffer -> smooth (skip intermediate cells only when the straight line is traversable and no more expensive than the route) -> waypoints (last one is the exact goal unless partial; the first is dropped when already within the reach distance) -> `Tick` steers toward the next waypoint, using the arrival distance for the final one, and grows `StalledTime` while the body covers less than 20% of `moveSpeed`. A failed build clears the path and the goal cell. When the navigation's `Version` has moved on since the path was built (a tile edit, a build, or in a world a chunk's page loading or unloading), the next `Tick` checks the remaining segments once (`TryGetLineCost` from the current cell through each waypoint) and, if one is blocked, rebuilds toward the same goal with the same `allowPartial`; with no route left the path clears and `Tick` returns zero. Changes that open a shorter route are left to the owner's own repaths. `CanReach` = goal walkable + same region as the nearest walkable cell to the start. `MobPathAgent2D` calls it with `Time.fixedDeltaTime` and the motor's speed.

Straight-line shortcut (`UseStraightLineShortcut`): when the Bresenham line from the start cell to the goal cell is traversable and its cost is no more than the octile heuristic (so no route can be cheaper), the follower skips A* and uses the exact goal as its only waypoint. It is off for mobs: on random grids about 9% of the queries where it applies gave different waypoints than A* plus smoothing (smoothing stops at the first intermediate cell it cannot see, and A* breaks ties between equal-cost routes differently from the line), so turning it on would change mob paths. It is meant for player clicks.

Costs: 10 per straight step by default, diagonals x1.4.

## World

- A world's chunk scenes (see Worlds and streaming below) each hold a `Grid` at the world's origin with ground layers (plain data tilemaps, only those of the materials the chunk and its neighbours' borders use, in the shared draw order of `TerrainStyle.LayerOrder`: grass first, then dry grass, meadow, tall grass, snow, dirt, sand, mud, ice, the path layers (gravel, plaza, stone), then shallows, water, the ocean and lava over them, and planks over everything, each with a `NavigationTerrainSource2D` whose priority is its draw order) drawn by a `TerrainBlendRenderer` on the `Grid` (see Blended terrain below), optionally a `CollisionTilemap` on the `Obstacles` layer (a DualGrid walls data tilemap from `Walls.prefab`; paint it with the Terrain Brush, which is the Dual Grid Brush there, with the Dual Grid Brush itself, or through `AreaTerrain`), `NavigationGrid2D` (with the `CollisionTilemap` as its blocking tilemap), optionally a `RaisedTilemap` of raised ground and ramps (`RaisedGroundTile`s, [TerrainTypes.md](TerrainTypes.md)'s Phase 10) that the terrain renderer draws as plateaus with cliffs and the navigation reads for their cliff, an `AreaTerrain` for runtime edits, [MobSpawnPoint](../Assets/Scripts/World/MobSpawnPoint.cs)s (mob prefab, gizmo, `PersistentId`) and a `ChunkLifetimeScope`. A world's entries are data on its `WorldDefinition`, not scene objects. A standalone grid (a Scene Generator scratch scene) is set up the same way without a `WorldChunk`.
- [MobSpawning](../Assets/Scripts/World/MobSpawning.cs): static, what `ChunkMobSpawner` spawns with: `HasAuthority` (offline or host) and `IsClient`, `CollectNetworkPrefabs` (the distinct networked mob prefabs of a set of spawn points), `Instantiate` (one spawn point's mob through a resolver, named after its prefab, moved into a scene; null with an error without a prefab), `NetworkSpawn` (in a session) and `Despawn` (network-despawned in a session, destroyed offline, nothing for a mob already gone).
- [EntrySlots](../Assets/Scripts/World/EntrySlots.cs): static, where players arriving at one place of a world stand so they do not stand on each other: slot 0 on the place, then alternately right and left, `GameplaySettings.EntrySlotSpacing` (0.6) apart (`SlotPosition`); `FreeSlot` gives the first slot no other registered player of that world stands within half a spacing of, whatever the players' order in the registry. `WorldStreaming.EnterAsync` and `ArriveAsync` (the session's start, trips) and `PlayerSpawner.SpawnRemotePlayer` (a joining player) place players with it.
- Project layers include `Obstacles` (collision tilemaps; `MobConfig.obstacleLayerMask` uses it for line of sight), `Player` and `Enemy`. Separation only senses colliders on the mob's own layer. `Player` vs `Player` collisions are off in the 2D physics matrix, so players walk through each other; everything else collides.
- Blended terrain ([Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain), shaders in [Assets/Shaders](../Assets/Shaders), spec and phases in [TerrainBlending.md](TerrainBlending.md)): one shader draws a grid's ground layers at 32 px per unit, in one of two looks: crisp (designed rounded edges with rims, outlines, shadows and tufts) or soft (shapes blended from the cells' weights and the materials' heights), switched by a developer toggle on the renderer. Every chunk's ground is drawn this way, each chunk listing only the layers it and its neighbours' borders use, a subsequence of the style's layer order (Phase 2 of [TerrainTypes.md](TerrainTypes.md)), with `TerrainStyle.asset`: Testlands' and the Sandbox's chunks draw grass, meadow, tall grass, dirt, sand, gravel, stone and water among them, the SandboxInterior's dirt and plaza, and the stress world's its `GrassTilemap` only, all saved in the crisp look. Water and the shallows are animated (their glints, sparkles and the swell of their shore rims) in the shader, from each machine's own clock, and so are snow (sparkles only), ice (glints and sparkles) and the ocean (its wave crests travelling towards the shore, sparkles and the swell of its surf); no world lists snow, ice or the ocean yet (the generated areas that did went in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9; their generators still lay them, TerrainTypes.md's Phases 7 and 8). Raised ground, plateaus with a cliff face, a ledge and ramps, is drawn over the layers from the grid's raised tilemap; the Sandbox and Testlands have a plateau each (Phase 10). The ground layers are plain data tilemaps (no `TilemapRenderer`) holding their material's plain tile (`Tile_Grass` and so on), written by the scene generator, the Terrain Brush, `AreaTerrain` and `StressTestMap`; the dual-grid ground (modules, rule tiles, 64 px tilesets and render tilemaps) went in Phase 4. The Terrain Brush, the Tile Palette's default, previews a stroke on these layers through the renderer (see Editor tools).

  | Type / asset | Role |
  |---|---|
  | [TerrainBlendRenderer](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRenderer.cs) | `[ExecuteAlways]` on a chunk's (or a standalone) `Grid`. Lists its ground layers bottom first (data tilemap and `TerrainMaterial` asset, at most 32 (`TerrainControlMap.MaxLayers`), drawn in the shader's slower wide variant past 16; several layers may draw the same material; the order is the draw order and also the navigation priority: each layer's navigation source, `NavigationSourceOf` (the `NavigationTerrainSource2D` on its tilemap, when `FindLayerDrawing` finds this renderer and layer for the tilemap, that is the nearest renderer above it listing it, at its first listing), must have its index as priority, `NavigationPriorityOf`; `HasNavigationPrioritiesInStep` tells, its inspector keeps them in step, and a mismatch is logged as an error at each build while the ground is still drawn, since the drawing never reads the priority), its `TerrainStyle`, the chunk size (16), the sorting layer and order (`Terrain`, 0) and its `mode` (`TerrainBlendRenderer.BlendMode`: `Crisp` or `Soft`, serialized, a developer toggle that is not saved per machine; chunks are saved as `Crisp`). When enabled it builds a `TerrainControlMap` (narrow, `R16`; wide, `RGBA32`, when it lists more than `TerrainControlMap.NarrowLayers` (16) layers, or whenever the `internal` seam `SetAlwaysWide(true)` asks, so tests and checks can compare the wide path with the narrow one on the same layers; `IsWide` tells which; see [TerrainLayers32.md](TerrainLayers32.md)), an albedo, a cell field and a height `Texture2DArray` (a `TerrainMaterialArrays`: those of its style's layer order, shared with every other renderer of the style, when it was given `SharedTerrainArrays` (`Construct`, which its chunk's scope injects, see below) holding every material it draws, else its own, a slice per layer; `SharesArrays` tells which), the lookup texture of the style and the layers' materials with each layer's slice of the arrays, a `TerrainCoastMap` for its first layer whose material has waves (built again whenever the control map uploads, with the bridge layers' bits as a `uint`; a chunk with a baked coast map uses that instead, see below), a runtime material of the style's shader with the `_TERRAIN_SOFT` keyword set from the mode and `_TERRAIN_WIDE` when built wide (both in `ApplyMode`), and a `MeshRenderer` quad per chunk of a fixed 16-cell grid over the control map's bounds (a world chunk's: over its own cells only), each a child object flagged `HideAndDontSave`, so nothing is saved into the scene and the Scene view never picks the ground, on the default rendering layer and, built with raised ground, the style's `CliffShadowLayers`, so its cliffs cast the sun's shadow (Lighting.md's Phase 4); chunks the bounds no longer reach (after an undo shrinks them) are destroyed. The arrays keep no copy in memory (uploaded empty and made unreadable before the copies). Draws nothing where no layer is filled. Subscribes to `Tilemap.tilemapPositionsChanged` (and, in the editor, `Undo.undoRedoPerformed`, since an undo restores tilemaps without reporting the cells) in `OnEnable`: a change patches the control map at once, adds chunks when its bounds grow, and subscribes once to `RenderPipelineManager.beginContextRendering`, which uploads the texture and unsubscribes, so nothing runs per frame while no cell changes. The `Mode` property switches the keyword on the runtime material at once, in edit and play mode, rebuilding nothing and allocating nothing (the keyword is looked up on every switch, since one kept across a reimport of the shader stops working); set from code it marks nothing changed. Inspector changes to it switch the keyword too and rebuild it after the editor's current update (`EditorApplication.delayCall`) only when something the build reads changed since the last build (its style, its layers' tilemaps and materials, the chunk size, the sorting), so flipping the mode in the inspector rebuilds nothing; changes to its style or to one of its layers' materials (their editor-only `Validated` events), and to one of its layers' navigation sources (which call its `HandleNavigationSourceChanged`), always rebuild it; it follows its style and materials even when it could not build, so fixing one rebuilds it. The chunks are a `TerrainChunkSet`, the build's checks `TerrainBlendChecks` and the editor's rebuild decisions `TerrainBlendRebuilds` (rows below); the renderer keeps the layers and their navigation rules, the build, following tile changes and undo, previews and the seams. A chunk of a seamless world (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2; its `WorldChunk` sets this up) is drawn through `internal` `SetChunk(cells, skirtWidth, coastMap, glowMap, glowArea)` and `SetSkirt(sources)`, neither saved: its control map covers a fixed area, `ChunkControlArea(cells, skirt)` (its cells and the skirt around them plus the margin, 74 x 74 cells for a 64-cell chunk and a 4-cell skirt), its quads only its own cells, and it also reads `TerrainSkirtSource`s, its loaded neighbours' ground layers and raised ground in a strip along each border, each ground source counted for its first layer of the same material. A material a skirt source shows (with a tile in its strip) that none of its layers draws adds a built layer at its place in the style's order (`BuiltMaterials`; its own layer list is untouched), and a tile landing in such a source's strip, or raised ground in a raised source's while it draws none, rebuilds it; a skirt that changes nothing else only reads the control map and elevation map again. The skirt never touches its tilemaps, so navigation never reads it. With a wave layer and a baked coast map of the control map's size (`IsCoastBaked`, `CoastTexture`) it draws that map and builds no `TerrainCoastMap`; with a glowing layer and a baked glow map fitting its area inside its cells (`SetChunk(cells, skirtWidth, coastMap, glowMap, glowArea)`, `IsGlowBaked`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5) its glow light draws that map and builds none, nor again as its skirt changes; a map of another size is warned about and one is built instead. It builds only once its own `OnEnable` ran (another component enabled before it in the same load, a `WorldChunk`, only stores its settings), so a scene load or domain reload never builds it twice or leaks chunk quads. `OnDisable` unsubscribes and destroys the chunks, meshes, material and textures, releasing shared arrays instead of destroying them. `Construct(shared)` on a built renderer rebuilds it onto the shared arrays, or onto its own with null. When a layer's material glows (`TerrainMaterial.Glows`: lava, Lighting.md's Phase 5) it builds a `TerrainGlowMap` of every glowing layer's cells over its cells (a world chunk's own, else the control map's bounds), its skirt included, within the first glowing material's reach, again whenever the control map uploads, and draws it through a Sprite Light 2D on a child object flagged `HideAndDontSave` (`GlowLight`, null when nothing glows: off and at no strength until `PropLighting` burns it at `GlowStrength`, the material's; blend style 0, the `Default`, `Terrain` and `Entities` sorting layers, no shadows, its cookie the map's sprite, its corner on the map's first cell, in the material's glow colour), destroyed when nothing glows any more; seam `GlowMap`. The build, each of its parts (`TerrainBlendRenderer.ControlMap`, `.CoastMap`, `.GlowMap`, `.Arrays`, `.ElevationMap`, `.Lut`, `.Material`, `.Chunks`), the skirt's re-read in `SetSkirt` (`.Skirt`) and the teardown (`.Teardown`) each have a profiler marker, which the streaming benchmark records (WorldScale.md's Phase 2). A brush's hover preview goes through `internal` `IsDrawing(tilemap)` (built, and built with that tilemap among its layers), `SetPreview(tilemap, cells, filled)` and `ClearPreview()`. They set or clear the control map's preview bits without touching the tilemaps, navigation or anything saved, add chunks when filled preview cells grow the bounds, and schedule the upload before the next render like a tile change (one helper, `FollowControlMapChange`, does this bookkeeping for tile changes, undos and previews). `SetPreview` replaces any earlier preview and finds the layer among the tilemaps the renderer was built from, returning false and changing nothing for a tilemap it is not drawing (one added in the inspector before the rebuild). A preview lasts until cleared or rebuilt. The bounds a preview grew stay while previews replace each other, and `ClearPreview` shrinks them and the chunks back by reading the layers again. Moving a preview inside the bounds allocates nothing. Logs an error for each problem that stops it (no style or shader, no layers or more than 32, a graphics device that cannot sample the format of the control map it builds, `R16_UNorm` or, wide, `R8G8B8A8_UNorm` (`TerrainControlMap.IsFormatSupported(wide)`, the message naming `TerrainControlMap.FormatOf(wide)`), a wide build for a shader without `_TERRAIN_WIDE`, a layer without a tilemap, a tilemap listed twice, a layer without a material, a material without an albedo or without the cell field its cell mode needs, a texture (the height included) that is not repeat x 32 px RGBA32 with sRGB off, an unknown sorting layer; a missing height is not an error, the soft look moves that material's edges by nothing), and warns when the mode is soft and the shader has no `_TERRAIN_SOFT` keyword, when it is built wide and the shader (reimported since) has no `_TERRAIN_WIDE`, and when a layer's tilemap has an enabled `TilemapRenderer` (it would draw its tiles' sprites, the palette swatches, beside the ground), except when completely unconfigured outside play mode; a material several layers draw is checked, and reported, once. `LayerIndexOf(tilemap)` gives the layer drawing a tilemap. Animated materials move in the shader from the frame's time (URP's `_TimeParameters`), with nothing done per frame here; `SetFixedTime(seconds)` draws them at a fixed time instead, through `_TerrainInfo`'s last two values, for repeatable checks, and null goes back to the frame's time (not saved). `internal` seams for tests and the probe: `ControlMap`, `IsWide`, `SetAlwaysWide` (rebuilds when it changes; not saved), `RuntimeMaterial`, the arrays (`AlbedoArray`, `FieldArray`, `HeightArray`), `Lut`, `Chunks`, `FlushPendingChanges`, `SetFixedTime`, `AnimationFramesPerSecond` (the fastest animated material it built from, 0 for none), `SetDebugView` (topmost visible layer as a pure colour, `TB_DEBUG_COLOURS` in the HLSL: each channel off, at a quarter, half, three quarters or full with at least one full, so the 32 colours differ whatever the lights' intensity; layers 0 to 6 light red, green and blue fully by the bits of the layer plus one, layers 7 to 12 one channel full and another at half, layers 13 to 15 two full and the third at half, and in the wide variant layers 16 to 21 and 22 to 27 are layers 7 to 12 with the second channel at a quarter and at three quarters, 28 to 30 one channel full and the others at half, 31 blue full and the others at three quarters, so the first 16 keep their colours), `IsFlushScheduled`, `HandledChangeCount`, `RenderCallbackCount`, and in the editor `HandledUndoCount`, `IsRebuildScheduled` and `RunScheduledRebuild`. Its optional raised tilemap (`raisedTilemap`, `RaisedTilemap`, `Configure(style, raised, layers)`), whose cells are raised ground or ramps, is read into a `TerrainElevationMap` (`_TerrainRaised`; seam `ElevationMap`) at each build, again when the control map's bounds change and after an undo, and when one of its tiles changes, before the next render as for a layer's; with one, the lookup texture holds the style's cliff settings and the runtime material has the `_TERRAIN_RAISED` keyword, set with the look's keyword (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes). A change of it in the inspector rebuilds. Its build when it is enabled runs under the `TerrainBlendRenderer.Build` profiler marker, which the streaming benchmark records. For its layers whose material turns to its runs it builds a `TerrainTurnMap` (`_TerrainTurns`, `TurnMap`), rebuilt with the control map and whenever such a layer's tiles change, a facing changed in a filled cell included though no control bit changes. `SetRaisedPreview` and `ClearRaisedPreview` show a brush's raised ground through its elevation map before the next render, and `IsDrawingRaised` says whether it reads a tilemap as raised ground. In the editor a switch to soft keeps the crisp keyword until the soft look's variants are compiled (`SoftWarmUp`, a `TerrainBlendSoftWarmUp`), and `CompileModeNow` compiles them at once for a check that renders right away (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
  | [TerrainMaterialArrays](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialArrays.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). The albedo, cell field and height `Texture2DArray`s of a list of materials, a slice per material in its order (`SliceOf`, `HoldsAll`), each as large as the largest material, uploaded empty, made unreadable and filled on the GPU with `Graphics.CopyTexture` (a material without a cell field or height leaves its slice empty), `DontSave`. `Dispose` destroys them (`IsDisposed`). A renderer builds them over its own layers' materials, or draws with those `SharedTerrainArrays` built over its style's layer order. |
  | [SharedTerrainArrays](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/SharedTerrainArrays.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`), a Gameplay scope singleton handed to each chunk's renderer (WorldScale.md's Phase 2). `Acquire(style)` returns the `TerrainMaterialArrays` of the style's layer order (its non-null materials, each once), built on the first call and counted per user; null without a style, for a style with no layer order, or once disposed. `Release(arrays)` counts a user less and destroys them with the last. `Dispose` (the scope's) destroys every set. In the editor it follows the `Validated` events of the style and its order's materials: a change retires the style's set, so the renderers rebuilding after it (they follow the same events) get a new one, and the old goes with its last user. `AliveCount` is the number of sets alive. The project's style orders 18 materials, so each of its arrays holds 18 slices of 512 px (its largest material's repeat). |
  | [TerrainChunkSet](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainChunkSet.cs) | Plain C# (`internal`), the renderer's chunk quads: `Fit(bounds, empty, material, sortingLayerId, sortingOrder, renderingLayerMask)` destroys the chunks the bounds no longer reach (all of them when empty), creates those it needs on a fixed grid of `ChunkSize` cells (child objects of the renderer, `HideAndDontSave`, a `MeshRenderer` with shadows, probes and occlusion off, named after the renderer and on its layer, meshes `DontSave`) and fits each quad to the bounds, every chunk on the rendering layers given; `Clear` destroys them all; `Renderers` lists them. The renderer makes one at each build with its chunk size and calls `Fit` when the control map's bounds move. |
  | [TerrainBlendChecks](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendChecks.cs) | Static (`internal`): `IsUsable(renderer, grid, sortingLayerName, wide)`, the checks a renderer runs before it builds a narrow or wide control map, with the errors and warnings listed under `TerrainBlendRenderer` above, each logged against the renderer or the material or texture at fault. It also warns, once a build, when the layers leave the style's `LayerOrder`: a material the order does not list, or a layer listed above one whose material the order puts above its own (`WarnOffLayerOrder`); a style with no order checks nothing (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
  | [TerrainBlendRebuilds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRebuilds.cs) | Editor only (`#if UNITY_EDITOR`, plain C#): when an inspector change rebuilds a renderer. `Remember` what a build read (style, layers' tilemaps and materials, chunk size, sorting) and `Changed` against it; `Follow` the style's and the layers' materials' `Validated` events (once per material) and `StopFollowing`; `Schedule` the rebuild on `EditorApplication.delayCall`, once however often asked, and `RunScheduled` (the renderer's `RunScheduledRebuild`), which rebuilds through the renderer unless it was destroyed or disabled since. |
  | [TerrainControlMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainControlMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). A point-filtered texture with a texel per cell over the layers' filled cells plus a margin of one empty cell (so edge bands stay on the chunks), bit i for layer i, built narrow or wide by an explicit choice (the constructor's `wide`, `IsWide`): narrow an `R16` (`R16_UNorm`) texel, up to `NarrowLayers` (16), which the shader reads as `round(r * 65535)`; wide an `RGBA32` (`R8G8B8A8_UNorm`, linear) texel, up to `WideLayers` (32), its four bytes the bits from red up, which the shader's `_TERRAIN_WIDE` variant reads as `r | g << 8 | b << 16 | a << 24`, each channel `round(c * 255)`; more layers than its format holds throw. `MaxLayers` (32) is the most a renderer draws, the wide variant's cap. Cells, `GetCell`, `GetShownCell` and the preview bits are `uint` either way, and `Apply` writes each texel's bytes lowest first. `FormatOf(wide)` gives the format and `IsFormatSupported(wide)` tells whether the device can sample it (the format and its layout are checked against Unity's, D3D's and Vulkan's docs in TerrainLayers32.md's Decisions). `Rebuild` reads every layer (tight bounds, whatever the tilemaps' own bounds; z = 0 only); `Refresh(layer, positions)` reads changed cells (ignoring z other than 0), growing the bounds first (by the cells needed plus 8 more on each growing side) when a filled cell lands outside them or in the margin; changed cells wait for `Apply`, which writes them into the texture's data and uploads once (`UploadCount`), allocating nothing inside the bounds. Preview bits (`SetPreview`, `ClearPreview`, `HasPreview`; the Terrain Brush's hover, through the renderer) show in the texture without touching the real bits (`GetCell` vs `GetShownCell`); a filled preview cell outside the bounds or in the margin grows them like a filled tile. A layer may also read `Source`s (tilemap, layer, cells; `SetSources`, `Sources`, `Reads`), extra tilemaps counted only inside their cells, so a cell is filled for a layer when its own tilemap or a source covering it holds a tile; `Refresh(tilemap, positions)` reads changed cells for every layer that has the tilemap as its own or a source. Built over a fixed area (the constructor's `fixedArea`, `HasArea`, `Area`; a world chunk's), the bounds are that area while any cell in it is filled and nothing outside it is read. |
  | [TerrainGlowMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainGlowMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`), Lighting.md's Phase 5. How strongly each spot near glowing ground is lit by it, the cookie of a renderer's glow light: `TexelsPerCell` (4) texels per cell side over `Area`, the cells within the reach of a glowing cell inside the area it is built for, each texel (1 - distance / reach) squared in its colour channels (alpha 1), 1 on the glowing cells and 0 from the reach on, an RGBA32 texture (linear, bilinear, clamped) and a sprite of it at `TexelsPerCell` pixels per unit, pivoted on its corner. `Build(controlMap, glowLayers, area, reach)` (a bit per glowing layer) works out the exact distance from each texel's centre to the nearest texel centre of a glowing cell over the area grown by the reach (a distance transform along rows then columns), so glowing cells in a chunk's skirt light into it and two chunks' maps meet without a seam; returns whether anything glows (`IsEmpty` otherwise), reallocating only when its size changes; `Build(glows, bounds, area, reach)` does the same from any cells (a predicate over the cells inside `bounds`), which the world's glow bake reads from the whole world. `UseBaked(texture, area)` takes a map baked beside a world's chunk instead (LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): its sprite is made over the asset, which it does not own or destroy. `EncodedAt(x, y)` reads a texel of the last build. |
| [TerrainCoastMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainCoastMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). How far each spot of a renderer's area lies from the coast of its layer with waves: a bilinear `R16` texture, `TexelsPerCell` (8) texels per cell side over the control map's bounds, holding the signed distance in pixels (positive at sea) as (distance - `MinDistance`) / `DistanceRange`; 1 x 1 without a layer with waves. A cell is the sea's where that layer or one above it that is no bridge is filled, or where it is empty, so the map's edge is no coast. `Build(controlMap, waveLayer, bridges)` (`bridges` a `uint`, a bit per bridge layer, so a narrow or wide map's layers all count) takes chamfer distances both ways, blurs them three times over 3 texels and, blending in from 8 px out over 50 px, over 8 texels, so waves following it bend gently near the coast and forget its steps out at sea; it works them out again only when a cell changed sides since the last build (about 70 ms for the Coast in the editor's debug code). `ComputeFromCells(sea, width, height)` works the distances out from a sea flag per cell of a window instead, with no texture and touching no Unity object, so on any thread, and `EncodedAt(x, y)` reads a texel, clamped at the edge: the editor's coast bake (`WorldCoastBake`), which encodes many chunks at once on worker threads (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6). The blur's running sums are doubles, so a texel does not depend on where its line starts and a window of the world gives the texels a map of the whole world gives. See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes. A build of the same size after the coast moved works the distances out in full (two chamfer passes) but blurs and encodes again only the texels whose signed distance changed, widened by `Reach` (the far blur's 3 passes of 8 texels), from a window twice as wide whose inner edges' errors stop short of them, every other texel keeping what a whole build gives it (`LastRebuilt`; KnownGaps.md's Phase 6): on a 72-cell map in the editor a whole build takes 116 ms and a cell of coast moved 39 ms. |
  | [TerrainElevationMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainElevationMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). Which cells of a renderer's area are raised ground: a point-filtered `R8` texel per cell over the control map's bounds holding what the cell of the raised tilemap is (`RaisedGround.KindOf`: 0 level, 1 raised, 2 a ramp), 1 x 1 and level without a raised tilemap or with an empty control map. `Build(controlMap, raised)` reads the tilemap again, and `Build(controlMap, raised, skirt)` also the raised `TerrainSkirtSource`s where the cell is level on its own tilemap, picked out once per build so a cell looks only at them (looking at every source for every cell took the Overworld's chunk (3, 3) 11 ms in a player, WorldScale.md's Phase 2); `GetCell` reads the last build. About 3 KiB for the Coast. A brush's preview (`SetPreview`, `ClearPreview`) overrides the cells it shows, from the next build (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
  | [TerrainTile](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainTile.cs) | A ground layer's tile, a `Tile` keeping a reference to the `TerrainMaterial` it paints, so the editor finds a material's tile and a tile's material by reference however either is named (`TerrainTileWriter.TileOf`, `MaterialOf`), and its `Facing` (`Direction`): automatic, or for a material that turns to its runs fixed east to west or north to south (`FacingOf` for any tile). Its sprite is a palette swatch, never drawn (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
  | [TerrainTurnMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainTurnMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`): an R8 texel per cell over the control map's bounds holding the `TerrainTile.Facing` of the tile on the highest layer that turns to its runs filled there, read from its own tilemap or, past the chunk's cells, from that layer's skirt source; automatic where none is, so the runs decide; 1 x 1 without such a layer (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). `GetCell` reads the last build. |
  | [TerrainBlendSoftWarmUp](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendSoftWarmUp.cs) | Editor only (`#if UNITY_EDITOR`, internal): keeps a renderer switched to soft drawing crisp until a copy of its material with the soft keyword has every pass compiled, unlit and lit, compiling what is missing in the background (`ShaderUtil.CompilePass`) and calling the renderer back from the editor's update (`Poll`); `CompileNow` waits for them; once found compiled for a material it answers without checking. `IsCompiled` is a seam for tests (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
  | [TerrainSkirtSource](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainSkirtSource.cs) | Readonly struct: one tilemap of a neighbouring chunk a renderer also reads, only in `Cells` (the strip along the border): a ground layer drawing `Material`, or raised ground (`Raised(tilemap, cells)`, `IsRaised`); `Contains`, `HasAnyTile`. |
| [RaisedGround](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/RaisedGround.cs) | Static: the rules of raised ground the renderer, navigation and the scene generator share. `KindOf(tile)` (`Level` without a tile, `Ramp` for a `RaisedGroundTile` that is a ramp, else `Raised`), `KindAt(raised, cell)`, `CanCross(from, to, step)` (a step between level ground and raised ground, ramps included, is refused, except between a ramp and the level cell right below it; navigation bakes it into cliff sides), `RowsCovered(faceHeight)` and `RowsReached(faceHeight)` (how many rows of cells a face that many cells tall covers whole, and reaches into at all), `IsOnFace(raised, cell, rows)` and its twin over a layout's cell sets (a raised cell at most `rows` cells above level ground, raised all the way down, whose lowest raised cell, the face's foot, is no ramp: above a ramp the slope rises instead), `OffTheFace(raised, point, faceHeight)` (a point on the cliff face moved straight up to the face's top, `faceHeight` cells above its foot); `IsOnFace` and `OffTheFace` also take any `IKinds` (a raised tilemap's is `TilemapKinds`), through which navigation reads raised ground across chunk borders; and `IsCliff` over a layout's cell sets: the plateau's rim, a raised cell that is no ramp with level ground among its 8 neighbours, which the generator keeps mobs and props off and counts as blocked when it checks what the spawn reaches. |
| [RaisedGroundTile](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/RaisedGroundTile.cs) | A `Tile` for a raised tilemap: raised ground, or a ramp (`IsRamp`), raised ground that slopes down to the ground below along its bottom edge and can be walked up there. Its sprite is a palette swatch only. `internal static Create(isRamp)` for tests. |
| [TerrainMaterial](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterial.cs) | ScriptableObject, one per ground material, read-only at runtime; its asset name is its name, and `MaterialId` its stable id in saves (its asset's GUID, kept by the editor's `OnValidate` and `EnsureId`, `test-<name>` for one built by `Create`), which saved terrain edits name their layer by (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5). Albedo, optional cell field and height textures, repeat in cells, outer and inner corner radii, cell mode (`TerrainMaterial.CellMode`: none / whole chips / cut stones) with base colour, keep margin (whole chips: how far clear of the rim all of a kept chip must lie; cut stones: how far inside the rim a kept stone's centre must lie), cut gap and cut bevel, the soft look's height scale (how many pixels the height texture moves its edges either way, and each whole stone's centre; at most `MaxHeightScale`, 2.5 px, which the inspector's range and the `HeightScale` getter hold to, and `OnValidate` warns about an edited asset holding more; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 2 notes for the bound), its animation (frames per second, 0 for a still material, `IsAnimated` above it; glint colour, period and duty; sparkle colour, period and duty; rim swell in pixels and swell period; swash, up to `MaxSwash` (12 px), how far past its edge it washes up what it meets as each of its waves arrives; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 5 notes), its waves, under a header of their own (spacing in pixels, 0 for none, up to `MaxWaveSpacing`; period; width; angle in degrees; the distance from the coast within which they run whole; stretch, how much of each shows further out; wave, crest and trough colours; the swash's foam, foam shade, film and film back colours and how wet it leaves the shore; `HasWaves` when animated with a spacing; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes), its sinking (sink depth in pixels, 0 for a material level with the ground, up to `MaxSinkDepth`; bank shadow), its bank colour (the face it shows where a sunken material cuts into it), its sway, under a header of its own in the inspector (amplitude up to `MaxSwayAmplitude`, period, gust length in cells; `Sways` when animated with an amplitude; `WholeClumps`, off by default: each weed takes its gust at the cell of its foot and only its own pixels lean, the ground inside its box staying still), its reflection, under a header of its own (`Reflection`, 0 to 1, how strongly it reflects the lights; `SetReflection` for tests; Lighting.md's Phase 10), its relief, under a header of its own (`Relief`, a `TerrainMaterial.BevelRelief`: the bevel and its width, the rounded look and its radius, the shaded side and from how far it faces away, the mortar's width, as its stone recipe paints them, all zero for none; `HasRelief` with cut stones and a height texture, whose alpha holds which way each stone pixel's bevel faces; `SetRelief` for tests; Lighting.md's Phase 11), its glow, under a header of its own (strength, 0 for none, reach in cells up to `MaxGlowReach` (4, a chunk's skirt) and colour; `Glows` when both are above 0; `SetGlow` for tests; Lighting.md's Phase 5), its placement (`Bridge`: it lies on what is painted under it, so the layers under it count its cells only where they are painted there instead of going on beneath it; `TurnsToRuns`: its textures turn a quarter in cells where its layer runs further left and right than up and down; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes), and a default `TerrainStyle.EdgeSettings`. The textures, repeat, cell mode, base colour and relief, and an animated recipe's glint and sparkle colours, are written by its terrain material recipe (see Editor tools). `internal static Create` builds one in memory for tests. In the editor its `OnValidate` and `NotifyChanged` (which the recipe writer calls after rewriting the textures) raise `Validated` for the renderers drawing it. |
  | [TerrainStyle](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainStyle.cs) | ScriptableObject, read-only at runtime: the shader; shared edge noise (px), pair margin (1.5 px) and pair blend (1.5 px); the layer order (`LayerOrder`, `LayerOrderIndexOf`): every ground material, bottom first, the project-wide draw order each grid's layers follow as a subsequence, so a pair is always drawn the same way up (TerrainTypes.md's Decisions; the scene generator's writer and Convert Ground enforce it, the renderer draws whatever it lists); and pair overrides, each an upper `TerrainMaterial` over a lower one or over nothing (no lower material) with its `EdgeSettings`. `EdgeSettings`: rim width and rim `Gradient` (colours from the edge inwards, alpha how much of the interior it covers), outline colour and whether it is drawn, shadow colour, alpha and width, tuft height; for the soft look a band (width up to 4 px and colour) that replaces the outline and the rim, and whole stones (over a material with cut stones, each stone drawn whole or left out, decided at its centre moved by its own height). `GetEdge(upper, lower)` (upper required) returns the override for that pair of material assets or the upper material's default edge; overrides are keyed by asset, so another material of the same name and settings does not match. An override whose lower material is missing (`PairOverride.HasMissingLower`: set, but its asset deleted or the object destroyed, which Unity's `==` calls null) never applies, so it cannot pass for one over nothing. `internal static Create` builds one in memory for tests, with or without a layer order. In the editor its `OnValidate` warns about overrides without an upper material or with a missing lower one and about empty or repeated entries in the layer order, and raises `Validated` for the renderers using it. Its Raised ground settings are raised ground's look, the same for every material, whose bank colour the face takes: the cliff's height (inside the raised cells), fringe, outer and inner radius, band rows, band shade, depth shade, pebble chance and light, foot and outline shades, rim light and ledge width, and the ramp's shade (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes); the cliff casts the sun's shadow, from the chunks on `CliffShadowLayers` (`Sun Shadow Cliffs`), which replaced the shadow it painted below its face (Lighting.md's Phase 4). |
  | [TerrainBlendLut](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendLut.cs) | Static: the style and the layers' materials as shader data for both looks, an RGBA float texture read with `Load`, 37 texels wide (`Width`). Its layout has one name per column and row type, in C# constants and in the `#define`s of [TerrainBlendLut.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlendLut.hlsl) (`TB_` and the constant's name in capitals with underscores: `LayerBankColourColumn` is `TB_LAYER_BANK_COLOUR_COLUMN`), which `TerrainBlendLutTests` checks agree; the shader reads no column by number. Row `SharedRow` (0) the shared settings at `SharedColumn` (edge noise, pair margin, pair blend, and the low 16 bits of the bridge layers, `BridgeBits`, a `uint`: bit i for a layer i drawing a bridge material) and at `SharedHighBridgesColumn` the high 16 bits in its first value (empty for 16 layers or fewer, so a narrow renderer's lookup texture is as before; each half is a whole number a float holds exactly, and the shader reads the high half only with `_TERRAIN_WIDE`); a row per layer (`LayerRow`, the shader's `TBLayerRow`): `LayerShapeColumn` (radii, repeat in pixels, cell mode), `LayerChipsColumn` (keep margin, cut gap, cut bevel, 1 when the material has cut stones and one of the layer's pairs shows them whole), `LayerBaseColumn` (base colour), `LayerHeightColumn` (height scale, 0 without a height texture; 1 when the material turns to its runs), for an animated material `LayerAnimationColumn` (frames per second, glint period, glint duty, rim swell), `LayerGlintColumn` (glint colour, swell period), `LayerSparkleColumn` (sparkle colour, sparkle period) and `LayerSparkleTimingColumn` (sparkle duty, swash), zero for a still one; `LayerBankColumn` (sink depth, bank shadow), zero for a level one; `LayerBankColourColumn` its bank colour; `LayerSwayColumn` (sway amplitude, sway period, gust length, 1 when its clumps lean whole) for a swaying one, zero otherwise; for a material with waves `LayerWavesColumn` (spacing, period, width, tangent of the angle), `LayerWaveColourColumn` (wave colour, whole within), `LayerWaveCrestColumn` (crest colour, stretch), `LayerWaveTroughColumn` (trough colour), `LayerWashFoamColumn` (foam colour, wet), `LayerWashFoamShadeColumn`, `LayerWashFilmColumn` and `LayerWashFilmBackColumn` (their colours), zero otherwise; `LayerSliceColumn` the slice of the texture arrays holding the layer's material in its first value (`BuildTexels(…, slices)`, `Create(…, slices)`; the layer's own index without them); `LayerReflectionColumn` its material's reflection in its first value (Lighting.md's Phase 10), `LayerReliefColumn` (bevel, bevel width, radial, radial radius) and `LayerReliefShadeColumn` (shaded side, shaded side from, mortar width) its material's relief, zero without (Phase 11); a row per pair of an upper layer over a lower one or over nothing (`PairRow`; the shader's `TBFirstPairRow` is a layer's pair over nothing, the others following): `PairShadowColumn` (shadow colour, alpha), `PairOutlineColumn` (outline colour, drawn), `PairWidthsColumn` (shadow width, rim width, tuft height, whole stones, only over cut stones), `PairFillColumn` (the rim's outer colour; alpha 0 when the edge is seamless), `PairBandColumn` (the soft band's colour, its width), then from `PairRampColumn` the rim sampled every `RampStep` (half a pixel), `RampTexels` (32) texels. For layers drawn over raised ground (`BuildTexels(…, raised)`, `Create(…, raised)`), row 0 also holds the style's cliff settings at `SharedCliffColumn` (height, fringe, outer and inner radius), `SharedCliffFaceColumn` (band rows, band shade, depth shade, pebble chance), `SharedCliffEdgeColumn` (foot shade, outline shade, rim light, pebble light), `SharedRampColumn` (ramp shade) and `SharedCliffRimColumn` (ledge width); they are empty otherwise, and the shader then draws no cliffs. |
  | [TerrainBlend.shadergraph](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlend.shadergraph), [TerrainBlend.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlend.hlsl) | Sprite Custom Lit graph (`TopDownRPG/Terrain/TerrainBlend`, Disable Color Tint on; Lighting.md's Phase 1) whose file-mode Custom Function (Use Pragmas on, so the light keyword `TerrainBlend.hlsl` declares under `#if SHADERPASS == SHADERPASS_SPRITELIT` reaches the lit pass, and only it) calls `TerrainBlend_float` with the world position, the control map, the lookup texture, the three arrays (each layer read at its slice from the lookup texture, `TBSlice`, since shared arrays hold the style's materials in its order), `_ControlRect` (world origin and size of the control map) and `_TerrainInfo` (the layer count the renderer was built with, debug view). Its keyword "Terrain Soft" (`_TERRAIN_SOFT`) is a local, fragment-only `multi_compile`, so builds keep both looks although only code ever enables it; so is "Terrain Raised" (`_TERRAIN_RAISED`), which the renderer enables when it has a raised tilemap, so a grid without raised ground runs a variant without its code (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes), and so is "Terrain Wide" (`_TERRAIN_WIDE`), which the renderer enables when it builds a wide control map: under it `TBCell` decodes the RGBA32 texel, `TerrainBlend_float` adds the bridge bits' high half from `TB_SHARED_HIGH_BRIDGES_COLUMN`, and `TBNearBridgeEdge` builds its mask of the bridges from two layers up as `2u << min(m + 1, 31)`, never shifting by 32 or more (without it, `1u << min(m + 2, 31)` as before). `TB_MAX_LAYERS` is 32 with it and 16 without it, and so are the debug colours, so without the keyword every variant compiles to the same code as before the keyword and grids of 16 layers or fewer draw exactly as they did. In the wide variant the per-pixel arrays, every layer's distance `d` and the pair weights `w`, are static variables every function reads in place, where the narrow variant passes them as parameters, which FXC copies into each function; the macros `TB_D_PARAM`, `TB_D_OUT_PARAM`, `TB_W_PARAM`, `TB_W_OUT_PARAM`, `TB_D_ARG` and `TB_W_ARG` of `TerrainBlendInputs.hlsl` name those parameters and arguments, expanding to nothing in the wide variant. The wide variant draws the 16-layer stress scene about twice as long as the narrow one in crisp and 1.35 times in soft, and 32 layers 2.1 and 1.65 times (TerrainLayers32.md's Phase 2 and 3 notes). The HLSL is split by concern (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 11): `TerrainBlend.hlsl`, the file the graph calls, holds `TerrainBlend_float` (the composite) and includes, in order, `TerrainBlendInputs.hlsl` (the lookup texture's named columns from `TerrainBlendLut.hlsl`, the row functions `TBLayerRow` and `TBFirstPairRow`, the other defines and the shared structs `TBInputs`, `TBCorner`, `TBEdge`, `TBCentre`; `TBLut`, `TBShape`, `TBToOutput`), `TerrainBlendShape.hlsl` (control map cells, masks and bridges, `TBLayersAt`), `TerrainBlendLook.hlsl`, which includes one look by the keyword: `TerrainBlendCrisp.hlsl` or `TerrainBlendSoft.hlsl`, each defining the same look functions (`TBDistanceTerms`, `TBLayerDistance`, `TBOutward`, `TBEdgeDirection` for banks and tufts, `TBAddPairEdge` and `TBRampTexel` for a pair's part of the edge and the rim, `TBAddCentrePair`, `TBCentreOutward` and `TBChipNearest` for chips and stones, `TBOpaqueInside`; crisp also `TBGradient`, soft also `TBCellHeights`, `TBSoftDistance` and `TBDepthAt`), `TerrainBlendCoverage.hlsl` (every layer's distance, `TBVisible`, the bridge checks), `TerrainBlendRuns.hlsl` (turning to runs), `TerrainBlendEdges.hlsl` (pair weights, edge settings, rims, `TBSeamlessBelow`, tufts), `TerrainBlendCells.hlsl` (`TBCentreAt`: what a chip or stone sees at its centre), `TerrainBlendAnimation.hlsl` (animation time, glints and sparkles, rim swell), `TerrainBlendSway.hlsl` (sway and the clump border check), `TerrainBlendBanks.hlsl` (sunken banks), `TerrainBlendInterior.hlsl` (`TBInterior`; `TBLayerInterior`, which works the interior out once per layer for the edge pixel and the inside; `TBSeamlessEdge`, `TBInside`, which draws a layer's waves over its inside), `TerrainBlendWaves.hlsl` (`TBCoastDistance`, which samples the coast map, `_TerrainCoast`, a texture the runtime material sets and the include declares, the graph listing it as the hidden property "Terrain Coast" (`_TerrainCoast`) with its HLSL declaration overridden to "Do Not Declare", like "Terrain Raised" (`_TerrainRaised`): a texture a material sets that its shader's Properties do not list is not bound per material by the SRP Batcher, so with several chunk renderers every chunk read one material's elevation and coast maps (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 notes); `TBWaves`, a layer's waves, measured along the coast from each pixel's foot on it (`TBCoastFoot`, `TBWaveTurn`; LightingGaps.md's Phase 1); `TBWash`, its swash past its edge, which each look draws there, over the pair's shadow in crisp and where it covers at least half a pixel in soft, the swash and the shadow both held back where a sunken layer's bank stands, so the sea never washes over the top of its bank; TerrainTypes.md's ocean notes), `TerrainBlendLayer.hlsl`, which includes `TerrainBlendLayerCrisp.hlsl` or `TerrainBlendLayerSoft.hlsl` (each look's `TBLayer`) by the keyword, `TerrainBlendDebug.hlsl` (the debug colours) and `TerrainBlendReflection.hlsl` (water's reflections, see Lighting), with `TerrainBlendRelief.hlsl` (the stones' relief, see Lighting) included by the interior, then `Assets/Shaders/Include/PixelLight.hlsl`, whose `PixelLit` lights the composite and the debug colours once at the art pixel's centre (see Lighting); each includes what it uses, and only the two selector files name the look's keyword (`_TERRAIN_WIDE` is named where it changes the code: `TerrainBlendShape.hlsl`, `TerrainBlend.hlsl` and `TerrainBlendCoverage.hlsl`). The HLSL (`TB_MAX_LAYERS` 16, or 32 with `_TERRAIN_WIDE`) snaps to the 32 px grid, reads the four cells around the nearest corner (each a 16-bit texel read as `round(r * 65535)`, or with `_TERRAIN_WIDE` an RGBA32 texel read byte by byte), computes the signed distance of each layer up to the highest one filled in those four cells (`TBLayersAt`; the layers above cover none of them, so a grid pays per pixel only for the layers painted there) with cumulative masks, in which a cell counts for a layer when it or a layer above it is filled, except a bridge layer above it (`TBCounted`, from row 0's bridge bits), which lies on what is painted under it (crisp: the rounded shape distance pushed by the shared edge noise; soft: the bilinear weight of the four cells, less a bump on diagonal pairs, plus the heights of the cells' top materials mixed by the same weights, as a distance whose 0.5 is where the weight passes one half), chooses which lower layers show (a lower layer only where its edge lies the pair margin beyond the next layer up that covers the corner patch: `TBNextPresent` over `TBPresentLayers`, the layers up to the highest one filled there that is no bridge and the bridges filled there, so empty layers listed between a bridge and what it lies on change nothing, and neither do they in the pair weights, the chips' centres or the sway's rooting, KnownGaps.md's Phase 6); where a bridge ends on the edge of a layer painted under it, which the next layer up does not then hide, that layer draws its edge pixel and its inside but neither its shadow nor its tufts (`TBBridgesAt`, `TBNearBridgeEdge`), blends each edge's pair settings over the pair blend width, and composites bottom up: shadow, outline, rim gradient over the interior, chips and stones decided at their centre with the pair found there (a whole chip kept only where its reach, read from the cell field and stretched by the edge noise's steepest slope, keeps all of it clear of the rim; stones left out or cut along the rim and bevelled to the light), and attached tufts on straight edges. An animated layer (its layer row's frames per second above 0) lights its glint and sparkle pixels, those whose height texel's blue channel holds a glint phase (1 to 127) or a sparkle phase (128 to 255), with its glint or sparkle colour for that one's duty fraction of each of its periods (half strength over its first and last quarter), and reads its rims up to its rim swell further in, swelling in and out over its swell period at a phase that drifts along the edge with the edge noise's pattern; its time is `_TimeParameters.x` (or `_TerrainInfo.w` when `_TerrainInfo.z` is set), stepped to its frames per second. A swaying layer (sway amplitude above 0) reads its albedo from a texel moved left by its sway (`TBSwayTexel`: a 0-to-1 gust rolling over the cells picks a pose, the tips' lean in whole pixels up to the amplitude, and a pixel leans by the pose times the square of its weight in the height's blue channel, rounded, at the frames per second), and its blue channel then holds no glints; where its clumps lean whole (its sway row's last value) the gust is taken at the cell of the clump's foot (the pixel's row less its box row from the height's green channel), and a leaning pixel that would show a texel the cell field holds unchanged (the ground) shows the field under itself instead, so only the clump's own pixels move. A pixel of a clump's box (the height's green channel above 0: its row from the row under the clump's base, plus one) shows the cell field's colour, the ground under the clumps, unless the clump's foot and its top (the field's alpha: the box's rows above its base) both lie on the layer where it shows (`TBOpenOn`: inside the layer past its edge pixel, with no layer above covering it), so a clump rooted under a path or beyond its layer's edge leaves no tip above it and one growing up under a path is not cut by it. A seamless pair (its rim's outer colour with alpha 0) draws the edge pixel with the layer's own interior under the rim's first texel (`TBSeamlessEdge`), so a seamless pair with a rim (deep water over shallows) starts its rim at the edge. A sunken layer (`TBInside`) draws its bank inside its edge, the sink depth times how much the edge faces up the screen (the bare shape's direction in crisp, so the bank keeps its height along a straight edge) and how much of it lies over another layer along a pair that is not seamless (`TBSeamlessBelow`), so none over nothing or over a layer of the same surface (shallows), in the bank colours of the layers it meets blended by their pair weights (`TBBankFace`, leaving out seamless pairs), its lowest pixel darker, from the edge pixel in (`TBEdgeColour`, in place of the outline or the rim's outer colour); the rim starts below the bank, and the bank's shadow darkens the layer over as many pixels again. In soft every pixel is opaque or clear: the edge pixel is opaque, no shadow is drawn, a pair's band replaces its outline and rim, whole chips are kept by the depth where they come nearest the edge, and over a pair with whole stones (where the stone's centre lies 10 px inside the lower layer) each stone is drawn whole or left clear, inside or outside the shape. A layer that turns to its runs (`TBTurned`) lies as the tile painted in the pixel's cell fixes it (`TBFacingCell`, the renderer's turn map `_TerrainTurns`: east to west unturned, north to south turned, KnownGaps.md's Phase 6), and where the tile leaves it automatic reads its albedo, cell field and height turned a quarter (`TBSampled`: the texture's x up the world, its top to the left) in a cell where its layer runs further left and right than up and down, counting up to `TB_RUN_REACH` (3) cells each way with a loop over control map reads, ties left as they are, and turns the offsets it reads back into the world (`TBUnturn`). Colours are treated as sRGB and converted for the target. After the layer loop, with `_TERRAIN_RAISED` and when row 0's cliff height is above 0, `TerrainBlendRaised.hlsl` draws raised ground over the composite (`TBRaisedOver`, `TBRaised`): it reads `_TerrainRaised` (the elevation map, a texture the runtime material sets and the include declares; the graph lists it as the hidden property "Terrain Raised" with "Do Not Declare", see below), the raised ground's distance at the pixel's corner patch with each look's `TBRaisedDistance` (crisp the rounded shape with the cliff's radii and no edge noise, soft the cells' bilinear weight), and how many rows a pixel lies above the cliff's foot by halving the cliff's height (`TBRowsAboveFoot`); within the cliff's height of the foot it draws the cliff face (`TBCliffFace`: the ground hanging over it, banded earth in the bank colour of the composite's top layer, a dark foot) or, where the foot lies in a ramp's cell, the ramp's slope over the face's whole height, up through the raised cells above the ramp (`TBRampFace`), elsewhere on raised ground the outline and the ledge along the sides and back (`TBCliffRim`); the cliff's shadow is the sun's (`SunShadowCliffs.shader`, Lighting.md's Phase 4). The debug view does not draw it. |
  | [Assets/Shaders/Include](../Assets/Shaders/Include) | Shared helpers: `PixelSnap.hlsl` (`SnapToArtPixel`, `WrapPixel`), `PeriodicNoise.hlsl` (`PeriodicEdgeNoise`: three waves repeating every 128 px, slope under 0.33 per pixel at amplitude 1), `RoundedCorners.hlsl` (`CornerShapeDistance`: the exact distance to the filled cells around a corner, outer corners rounded, inner ones filleted), `PixelLight.hlsl`, `PixelRelief.hlsl` and `SunShadow.hlsl` (see Lighting). |
  | [TerrainStyle.asset](../Assets/Settings/Terrain/TerrainStyle.asset) | The terrain shader and the shared settings (edge noise 1 px, pair margin and pair blend 1.5 px). Its layer order: `Grass`, `DryGrass`, `Weeds`, `Meadow`, `TallGrass`, `Snow`, `Dirt`, `Sand`, `Mud`, `Ice`, `Gravel`, `Plaza`, `Stone`, `Shallows`, `Water`, `Ocean`, `Lava`, `Planks`. Pair overrides: stone over gravel: in crisp a 0.75 px rim of mortar (98, 88, 76), no outline, a neutral shadow (46, 40, 32) at 0.28 over a pixel, so the cobbles look set into the gravel; in soft whole stones. Plaza over gravel and stone over plaza: the same edge, so the street looks laid into the square. Water over sand: water's shore rim without its outline, and a wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.5 over 2.5 px. Dry grass over grass: seamless, no outline, rim or shadow, with 3 px tufts in straw (164, 145, 98). Sand, mud, gravel and stone over dirt: their own default edges with an earth shadow (66, 46, 28) instead of a green one. See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 notes. Shallows over sand: their shore with the wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.45 over 2 px. Water over shallows: seamless, no outline or shadow, a 4 px rim of two opaque steps, (56, 126, 172) then (48, 114, 166), so the lake steps down from the shallows into deep water (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 4 notes). The layer order ends with `Planks`, after the water. Planks over water and over shallows: their outline with a shadow cast on the water, (12, 30, 48) at 0.5 over 2.5 px (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes). Meadow over grass: seamless, with no outline, rim, shadow or tufts, since the meadow's ground is the grass's; meadow over dry grass: seamless with 3 px green tufts (52, 128, 50) (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 6 notes). Weeds over grass and the meadow over weeds: seamless the same way, all three sharing the grass's ground; weeds over dry grass: seamless with the same green tufts (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 12 notes). Dirt, gravel, stone and ice over snow: their own edges with a soft blue shade (150, 172, 210) at 0.35 over 3.5 px instead of their shadow, so the snow looks banked against them, dirt's without its straw tufts and with a 1.5 px rim of wet earth (118, 96, 72) under an outline (92, 72, 50); water over ice takes its default edge (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 7 notes). The ocean comes after the water: ocean over sand draws its surf without an outline and a wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.5 over 3 px; ocean over nothing (the map's edge, where the sea goes on) draws no outline, rim or shadow (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes). Lava comes after the ocean, with no overrides: its default edge reads the same over every ground it meets (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9 notes). Raised ground: a 64 px cliff, two cells (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 14), with a fringe of up to 3 px, radii 14 and 11 px, bands of 3 rows every other one at 0.82, darkening by 0.3 to a foot at 0.6, pebbles at 1.25 on 1.2 % of the face, an outline at 0.55, a rim light of 0.2, a 3 px ledge, a ramp shade of 0.18, and a shadow of 0.4 over 4 px (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes). |
  | [Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials](../Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials) | `Grass`, `DryGrass`, `Weeds`, `Meadow`, `TallGrass`, `Snow`, `Dirt`, `Sand`, `Mud`, `Ice`, `Gravel`, `Plaza`, `Stone`, `Shallows`, `Water`, `Ocean`, `Lava` and `Planks`. Dry grass is grass's edge in straw colours (rim (192, 175, 128) to (154, 133, 89), outline (95, 79, 51)) and level with it. Dirt: a 3 px rim from straw (190, 168, 92) fading into the earth, an outline (112, 94, 50), a green shadow (34, 70, 30) at 0.3 and 4.5 px tufts, so it meets grass with a fringe of dry blades, kept in soft (no band); a height scale of 2 px; bank colour (100, 76, 52). The plaza has stone's settings (radii, cut stones, keep margin, cut gap and bevel, height scale, edge). See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 notes. The grass is still (not animated, no clumps, no cell field); weeds and dry grass sway (8 frames per second, 2 px, 2.5 s, gusts 12 cells long): the clumps of weeds and stalks their recipes draw lean in the wind. Weeds are the grass's edge, radii, height scale and bank colour with the clumps (merged into the grass in TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3, separated again in Phase 12). Sand and mud have gravel's kind of edge in their own colours (a 1.5 px rim fading in, an outline, a green shadow, a 1.5 px band in soft) and a height scale of 2 px; water an outline, an 8 px shore rim in fixed steps from foam to shallows and a faint shadow, no soft band, a height scale of 1.5 px, and its animation (8 frames per second; glints in the strokes' highlight (140, 196, 228) every 3 s for 35 % of it; sparkles (226, 246, 242) every 4 s for a fifth of it; a 1.5 px rim swell over 4.5 s), and it is sunken 4.5 px under a bank with a 0.35 shadow; bank colours: grass (96, 74, 50), the dirt under it, sand (178, 146, 96), mud (82, 60, 40), gravel (118, 94, 64), stone (86, 76, 64), shallows (140, 118, 82); see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 5 notes. The shallows are lighter water over a sandy bed with the water's radii: a soft shore (no outline, a pixel of foam (214, 236, 232), two of pale water (156, 206, 214) at half cover, a faint wet shadow (24, 44, 30) at 0.25), a height scale of 1.5 px, animated like the water (8 frames per second; glints (168, 218, 236) every 2.5 s for 35 % of it; sparkles (232, 250, 246) every 4 s for a fifth of it; a 1 px rim swell over 3.5 s) and sunken 1.5 px under a bank with a 0.25 shadow; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 4 notes. Grass, gravel and stone have today's pair rules as their default edges: grass over nothing (outline, the light fringe bands as a fixed-mode rim gradient 9 px deep, a green fringe shadow, 4.5 px tufts), gravel over grass (darker dirt rim fading over 1.75 px, outline, green shadow; whole chips kept only where all of them lies clear of the rim), stone over grass (earthy rim fading into mortar over 1.5 px, outline, green shadow; stones left out only where their centre lies more than 0.5 px into the rim, the rest cut along it). Base colours (35, 114, 40), (146, 122, 88) and (98, 88, 76). Soft: height scales 2.5, 2 and 1.5 px; grass over nothing keeps its outline and fringe, gravel draws a 1.5 px band of dirt (126, 104, 76), stone a 1.5 px band of mortar (98, 88, 76). The planks are boards cut along the rim (cut stones, keep margin -0.5 px, no cut gap, bevel 18), with 2 px corner radii, level (no sink), a bridge that turns to its runs; their edge: no rim, an outline (50, 35, 24) and an earthy shadow (28, 20, 12) at 0.35 over 1.5 px, no soft band; a height scale of 0, so their edges stay straight in the soft look; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes. The meadow is the grass with flowers: the grass's edge, radii, height scale and bank colour, still (no animation, since Phase 12), so its flowers stand still. Tall grass sways in the same wind 3 px (`MaxSwayAmplitude`), its clumps leaning whole, and draws an edge of its own over every material and nothing: a 2 px rim from (104, 162, 58) into its blades, an outline (26, 62, 18), a shadow (22, 54, 18) at 0.35 over 1.5 px and 5 px tufts, a fringe of blades, kept in soft (no band); see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 6 notes. Snow has the water's radii, a height scale of 2 px and sparkles only (8 frames per second; white sparkles every 4.5 s for a quarter of it), level, bank colour (182, 198, 222); its edge over everything: no outline, a 2.5 px rim fading from (196, 212, 236) and a white frost shadow (240, 245, 252) at 0.35 over 1.5 px. Ice has the water's radii, a height scale of 1.5 px, glints (244, 252, 255) every 3.5 s for 30 % of it and white sparkles every 5 s for a fifth of it, level, bank colour (136, 178, 208); its edge over everything: an outline (84, 128, 166), a 2 px bright bevel from (220, 238, 250) and a shadow (36, 56, 78) at 0.3 over a pixel; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 7 notes. The ocean has the water's radii and height scale and its recipe's dark tones with no glints or sparkles lit (both duties 0); its waves (8 frames per second; 48 px apart, rolling in every 3.2 s at 8.5 degrees, 10 px wide in (44, 94, 150) with a crest (140, 184, 218) and a trough (14, 40, 80), whole within 40 px of the coast, in stretches further out at a stretch of 0.05), its swash up to 10 px (foam (240, 244, 248) and its shade (214, 226, 238), the film (150, 192, 222) and its back (112, 166, 204), the shore 15 % darker as it dries), no rim swell, sunk 6 px (`MaxSinkDepth`) under a bank that casts no shadow (0, since TerrainTypes.md's swash note of 2026-11-03), bank colour (140, 118, 82); its edge over grass and every other material without an override: an outline (36, 62, 64), an 8 px pale band in fixed steps ((126, 180, 212), (100, 158, 200), (74, 132, 182), (50, 104, 160), 2 px each) and a faint shadow (18, 40, 30) at 0.25 over a pixel, no soft band; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes. Lava has the water's radii and a height scale of 1.5 px; its bubbles glint (250, 160, 60) and their rims sparkle (130, 26, 18), both every 4 s for a fifth of it, so each bubble swells, opens into a ring and goes out (8 frames per second); a 1 px rim swell over 3 s; sunk 1.5 px under a bank with a 0.35 shadow, bank colour (70, 34, 28); its edge over every material and nothing: an outline (48, 20, 18), a 4.5 px molten rim in fixed steps (yellow (255, 214, 110) to 2 px, orange (240, 132, 40) to 3 px, then (220, 70, 26) at 0.6 to 4.5 px, the first pixel under the outline) and a warm glow (150, 52, 24) at 0.4 over 4 px on what it meets, no soft band; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9 notes. Lava glows at night (Lighting.md's Phase 5): strength 1, reach 2.5 cells, colour (255, 130, 50). |
  | [Assets/Textures/Terrain](../Assets/Textures/Terrain) | A folder per material, named after it: `Grass/`, `DryGrass/`, `Weeds/`, `Meadow/`, `TallGrass/`, `Snow/`, `Dirt/`, `Sand/`, `Mud/`, `Ice/`, `Shallows/`, `Water/`, `Ocean/` and `Lava/` (albedo and height each), `Gravel/`, `Plaza/`, `Stone/` and `Planks/` (albedo, height and cell field each; dry grass's, the weeds', the meadow's and tall grass's field is the ground under their clumps; the grass has none). 128 px textures (4 cells; grass's, dry grass's, the weeds', the meadow's and tall grass's 256 px, 8 cells; water's, the shallows' and the planks' 256 px, 8 cells; mud's, sand's, snow's, ice's, the ocean's and lava's 512 px, 16 cells), written by their recipes in `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Recipes` through `TerrainMaterialWriter` and imported by `TerrainTextureDefaults`. The renderer copies the heights into its height array for the soft look, which reads R to move the edges and G to move whole stones. The cell field's contract, which the shader reads, per texel: R and G the offset in whole pixels (x right, y up) to the centre pixel of the texel's chip or stone (its pixel nearest its seed, which points at itself), plus 128; B 128 plus the amount added to the material's base colour to give the base under the chip or stone (dirt or mortar), which is exactly what the albedo shows wherever no chip or stone covers it; A times 16, at least 1, for whole chips the chip's reach (the distance from its centre pixel to its farthest pixel, its shadow included, the same over the whole chip) and for cut stones the texel's distance to its stone's cell edge; A 0 marks a texel of no chip. The planks point every pixel straight across its board, at the board's middle row in its own column, since a board can be longer than a dock is wide (its centre pixel then points at itself as the contract asks). Height: R a smooth height whose slope stays under 0.2 per pixel (the recipes keep it under 0.1, as `materials.py` did; the soft look's height scale bound of 2.5 px holds for slopes up to 0.1, see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 2 notes), G one random value per chip or stone (0.5 where there is none, and for grass), B an animated material's glint or sparkle (0 for neither, 1 plus 127 times a glint's phase, or 128 plus 128 times a sparkle's; only the water highlights, ocean waves and lava bubbles recipes write any, snow's only sparkles). |
  | [Assets/Tiles/Ground](../Assets/Tiles/Ground) | `Tile_Grass`, `Tile_DryGrass`, `Tile_Weeds`, `Tile_Meadow`, `Tile_TallGrass`, `Tile_Snow`, `Tile_Dirt`, `Tile_Sand`, `Tile_Mud`, `Tile_Ice`, `Tile_Gravel`, `Tile_Plaza`, `Tile_Stone`, `Tile_Shallows`, `Tile_Water`, `Tile_Ocean`, `Tile_Lava` and `Tile_Planks`: the plain `Tile` of each ground material, without a collider, held by the palette of the same name in `Assets/Tiles/Palettes` (`Grass_Palette` and so on) (`Walls_Palette` holds the walls' data tile). Each tile's sprite is its material's swatch in [Assets/Sprites/Terrain](../Assets/Sprites/Terrain) (`grass_swatch.png` and so on: the albedo's bottom-left 32 px cell, a single 32 PPU sprite), seen only in the Tile Palette, since ground layers are data tilemaps without a `TilemapRenderer`. Written by `TerrainTileWriter` (see Editor tools). The ground layers hold them: the scene generator, the brushes, `StressTestMap` and `AreaTerrain` (as the ground layers' fill tile) write them. `Assets/Tiles/Raised` holds `Tile_Raised` and `Tile_Ramp`, the `RaisedGroundTile`s of raised ground and of a ramp (swatches `raised_swatch.png` and `ramp_swatch.png`, generated), held by `Raised_Palette`; the scene generator writes them on a grid's `RaisedTilemap`. |
- Dual-grid walls ([Assets/Scripts/World/DualGrid](../Assets/Scripts/World/DualGrid), editor tools in [Assets/Editor/DualGrid](../Assets/Editor/DualGrid)): a `DualGridTilemapModule` on the painted data tilemap keeps a half-cell-offset render tilemap of `DualGridRuleTile`s in sync, each render cell picking its sprite from the four data cells around its corner. Since Phase 4 of TerrainBlending.md only the walls use it (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`, whose 32 px sheet `WallSheetRecipe` generates; see Editor tools). Adopted from skner's DualGrid 2.1.0 (MIT, license kept beside the code). On a chunk of a seamless world its `WorldChunk` gives the module its cells and its loaded neighbours' wall modules (`internal SetChunk(cells, neighbours)`, `HasChunk`, `ChunkCells`): its rules read a neighbour's data cells past its border (`DataTileForRules`, which `DualGridRuleTile` reads through the module), it draws the render tiles on its own cells, and those along its upper and right border only while the chunk owning them is not loaded (`OwnsRenderTile`), refreshes its border when a neighbour comes or goes, and follows a neighbour's data changes along it (in edit mode `DualGridTilemapPersistentListener` forwards them, `ReadsNeighbourData`). So a wall across a border shows the same sprites as on one grid, each drawn once. Full description, authoring steps and editor behaviour in [DualGrid.md](DualGrid.md).
- Runtime terrain: [AreaTerrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/AreaTerrain.cs) on a world chunk's `Grid` lists the tilemaps gameplay may change (a dual-grid data tilemap, painted through its module, or a plain tilemap with a fill tile) and its sync prefab (`AssignSyncPrefabIfMissing`, editor only, for the authoring tools). Internal `TrySetCells(layer, cells, filled)` (the editor's dev menu and tests; nothing in gameplay edits terrain yet) works only with authority, writes the tiles in bulk and records a [TerrainEdit](../Assets/Scripts/World/TerrainEdit.cs) per cell (layer index, cell, filled; `INetworkSerializable`), raising `EditRecorded(index)`; `ApplyReplicated(edits)` applies the host's edits on a client without recording them; `RestoreEdits(saved)` applies saved edits (`GameSaveData.CellEdit`), each on the layer its name finds, and records them as just made (skipping, with a warning, names it has no layer of). `KeyOf(index)` names a layer as saves do (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5): a ground layer by the id of the material the grid's `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws on its tilemap (`TerrainMaterial.MaterialId`), the walls (a dual-grid data tilemap) as `walls` (`WallsKey`), any other tilemap by its name after `tilemap:`; `IndexOfKey(key)` finds a layer back. [NetworkAreaTerrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/NetworkAreaTerrain.cs) (on [AreaTerrainSync.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Network/AreaTerrainSync.prefab)) replicates a chunk's; `ChunkTerrainSync` (see Worlds and streaming) spawns it. Every chunk of the worlds lists its own ground layer (the one `CreateChunk` made it with, grass by default) and its walls when it has them (`WorldAuthoring.SetUpTerrainEdits`, which `CreateChunk`, `AddWalls` and the brush's walls copy run), and a generated chunk the base grounds it lays. On the wire a layer is its index in `AreaTerrain`'s own list, not in the terrain renderer, which every machine of a build loads alike and so agrees on; in saves it is named by `KeyOf`, so the world generation writer and Sync layer lists may take a ground layer a chunk no longer lays out of the list, its layer then removed, and saved edits of the other layers still land on theirs (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5). The scene generator's writer and Convert Ground never remove a tilemap `AreaTerrain` lists. A chunk's edits persist in its world's `WorldState`: `ChunkTerrainSync` restores them as the chunk loads, before its sync object spawns, and records each new one.
