Nothing in gameplay edits terrain yet; in play mode the editor menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/Dev/Toggle Terrain Cell At View Centre` (`AreaTerrainDevMenu`) makes edits by hand (see [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#checking-runtime-terrain-edits)).
- Props ([PropGeneration.md](PropGeneration.md)): natural props generated in the editor from recipes (see Editor tools, Props/), each look a sheet `Assets/Sprites/Props/<name>.png` and a prefab per variant, `Assets/Prefabs/Props/<Trees|Bushes|Rocks|Wood|Plants>/<name>_<variant>.prefab`. So far the 32 looks of PropGeneration.md's Phases 1 to 3, four variants each: the woods', the forest floor's and the shores', the Snowfield's and the Lava Fields' (see ScriptableObject configs); and the light props of Lighting.md's Phases 5 and 6 in `Assets/Prefabs/Props/Lights`, `Campfire` (four variants), `LampPost` (one), `Torch` and `Brazier` (two each); and the markers of KnownGaps.md's Phase 3 in `Assets/Prefabs/Props/Markers`, `Waystone_0` (one prefab for the waystone's two states, see Worlds and streaming), `Doorway_0`, `Signpost_0` and `Signpost_1` (pointing west and east) and `Doormat_0` to `Doormat_2` (across two cells, along two, on one), which the World Editor's Place markers step places (`WorldMarkers`, Editor tools). Every sheet with a glow (the light props' and, since Lighting.md's Phase 6, the charred trees' and stumps', whose embers glow) lies in `Assets/Sprites/GlowingProps`, outside the atlas (its sprites would have a secondary texture the others lack), with its glow in `Assets/Textures/Props/<name>_Glow.png`, the sheet's secondary texture `_GlowTex`; a campfire's, torch's and brazier's sheet and glow hold six frames of its moving flame in rows over the sliced first, its sprites meshed as full rectangles, and its prefabs draw with `SpritePixelLitFlames.mat` (see Lighting). Every other sheet is packed in one Sprite Atlas, `Assets/Sprites/Props/Props.spriteatlasv2` (the whole folder, a single 1024 px page, point filtered and uncompressed). The six area generators place them through their scatter layers (PropGeneration.md's Phase 5, SceneGeneration.md), the Lakeside and the Village Forest drying the bushes that stand on their dry grass and tall grass, and world generation writes the regions' props and the wilds' forest into the Overworld's chunks, under each chunk grid's `GeneratedProps` (that plan's Phase 6); The Clearing, the Snowfield, the Coast and the Lava Fields lay a campsite of a campfire and seats from their own stumps, the Village Forest lamp posts along its street, and world generation a lamp post on each waystone's plaza (Lighting.md's Phase 5); the Lava Fields a brazier at either end of its causeway, the Coast one on its plateau, the Lakeside a torch at its dock's foot and the Snowfield one at its trail's end, and world generation a pair of torches at each gate and lamp posts along the stone roads near the gates (Phase 6). Testlands' chunk (0, 0) shows the woods' variants on the grass south of the road by its `start` entry, under its `Props` root. A prop is laid out like the player and mob prefabs: the root stands at the prop's base, and a `Visuals` child on the root holds the `SpriteRenderer` (on the `Entities` sorting layer, its sprite pivoted on the prop's base pixel at 32 PPU like the characters, so roots, a rock's front and the outline reach below the root) sorting at its pivot, the root (Sprite Sort Point Pivot), as units sort at their feet, so props and units sort against each other by where they stand; a tall sprite is not sorted by its centre, which would draw a tree over a player standing behind it. Its `Visuals` is on the `Sun Shadow Props` rendering layer, so its sprite casts the sun's shadow (Lighting.md's Phase 4); a prop lying flat that casts none (`Stones_Small`) draws on the default layer alone. A light prop also has a `Light` child on the ground under its flame or lantern, holding its point Light 2D, off, and the [PropLight](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/PropLight.cs) that `PropLighting` burns it by (see Lighting); nothing runs on it. Every prop is a variant of one of two bases:
  - [Prop.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Props/Prop.prefab): the root on `Default` with nothing on it, for walk-through props (grass, flowers, mushrooms).
  - [BlockingProp.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Props/BlockingProp.prefab): the root on the `Obstacles` layer with a solid `BoxCollider2D` lying under the sprite (in the base, standing on the root). Each variant sets its size and offset, and, unless it is a light prop, carries on its `Visuals` a Shadow Caster 2D over that footprint, off, which `PropLighting` wakes while a burning light reaches it (Lighting.md's Phase 7). It is the prop's equivalent of the feet collider on a unit's root. The `Visuals` child stays on `Default`, because the camera culls `Obstacles`; this is the same split as the collision tilemap and its render tilemap. Bodies collide with the footprint, mobs cannot see through it (their line of sight uses `Obstacles`), and the chunk's `NavigationGrid2D` blocks every cell it overlaps, so paths go around it. Place props with the prop placement tool (see Editor tools), which snaps each footprint onto exactly the cells it needs; Snap Selected Props To Grid fixes props moved by hand.

  A generated variant's footprint is measured from its drawing (`PropBaker`): around the base, the columns and rows the ground-touching pixels reach (a tree's trunk foot and roots, the lower body of a bush, rock or stump), widened to lie evenly about the base, so the root lands on whole pixels when `PropGridSnapping` centres the footprint on its cells.

  A prop's sprite sorts by the custom axis at its pivot, its base, with no script running on it (a blocking prop's light shadow caster, its only script, is off until a burning light wakes it), so no prop does anything when it starts or per frame. Each has a [PropFade](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/PropFade.cs) on its root: the renderer that fades (its `Visuals`) and the box its drawing covers around the root, which the prop writer measures from the drawing for the looks that fade (trees and spires) and leaves empty for the others. [PropFading](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/PropFading.cs) (Gameplay scope, `ITickable`, with [PropFadeSettings](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/PropFadeSettings.cs)) fades the props hiding this machine's local player, on this machine only: each loaded chunk hands it its props as it loads and takes them out as it unloads ([ChunkPropFading](../Assets/Scripts/World/Props/ChunkPropFading.cs)), and it keeps them in buckets of 4 units by where they stand, so each frame it looks only at the few just in front of the player. A prop hides the player when it stands lower (so it draws over the player) and its drawing covers at least the settings' share of the player's body (a box above its feet); it fades toward the settings' opacity over their fade time, by `Time.deltaTime` since it is only how it looks, and back once it no longer hides the player, after which it is forgotten. Its shadow does not fade, since the caster shader reads its sprite's pixels, not its colour. Other machines' players never fade anything. `Add`, `Remove` (destroyed props included, by entity id), `Shown`, and an internal `Fade(viewer, feet, deltaTime)` that `Tick` calls with the local player, for tests.

  Props are static scenery placed in chunk scenes, identical on every machine, so nothing about them is networked; fading is local.
- Adding a world: with the World Editor (`Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Editor`, see Editor tools); the steps are in [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md#authoring-a-world).

### Worlds and streaming

Seamless worlds, specified in [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md) (Phases 1 to 8 so far: worlds and chunks streaming offline, terrain and walls drawn across chunk borders, navigation across chunks, authoring (the World Editor, the Terrain Brush across chunk borders, ownership validation and persistent ids), mobs per chunk, world exits with trips between worlds offline, multiplayer: each player's world replicated, trips by RPC, per-chunk readiness and release, home-chunk routing and per-chunk terrain sync, persistence: world states, mob respawns, terrain edits and players' places saved and continued, see Save data; and death and respawn: waystones discovered per player, a dead player sent to the nearest one on a trip within its world, nothing restarting, and the area path gone, every session in worlds). A world is a root scene and up to 16 x 16 chunk scenes of 64 x 64 cells, at a fixed origin in one global layout; chunk scenes hold their content at world positions (each chunk's `Grid` sits at the world's origin, its tilemaps in cells counted from it). Code in `Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds` and `Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming`. The worlds (Scenes, above): Testlands, the PlayMode tests' starting world, the Sandbox and its interior, the dev stress world, all painted by hand, and the Overworld, the starting world, generated in the editor ([WorldGeneration.md](WorldGeneration.md)) into chunk scenes that stream, draw, navigate, spawn and save as the painted ones do.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [WorldDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/WorldDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject (Core): `WorldId` (its asset GUID, set by the editor), `DisplayName` (the name players see, without an article: "Entering the Overworld"; UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, content rule 5), the root scene's `SceneDefinition`, `Origin` (world units of its cell (0, 0)), `SizeInChunks` (at most `MaxChunksPerSide`, 16), `Chunks` (coordinates and `SceneDefinition` each; a missing chunk has no scene and loads nothing), `Entries` (name, display name, unique within the world's entries, position from the origin, waystone; the first is `DefaultEntry`), and under Navigation its search limits, `MaxSearchCells` (4096, `DefaultMaxSearchCells`) and `NearestCellSearchRadius` (8, `DefaultNearestCellSearchRadius`), which its `WorldNavigation` takes (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5; `SetSearchLimits` for tests). `TryGetChunkScene`, `HasChunk`, `HasChunkAt(worldPosition)`, `FindEntry` (the default, with a warning, for an unknown name), `EntryPosition(entryName)` (that entry's world position, or the world's middle without entries), `OwnsScene(path)`, `ToWorld`/`ToLocal`, `LocalBounds`. Editor-only `Configure`, `SetChunk`, `SetEntries`; `internal static Create` builds one with transient scenes for tests. |
| [ChunkCoords](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/ChunkCoords.cs) | Static (Core): `Size` (64), `ChunkOf(cell)` (rounding down, below zero too), `CellOf(position)`, `Bounds(chunk)`, `Around(cell, extents)`, `AddOverlapping`, `Overlaps`, `SquaredDistance(cell, chunk)`. |
| [IWorldSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/IWorldSession.cs) | Core interface `GameFlow` enters and leaves worlds through: `IsActive`, `EnterAsync(world, position)`, `PrepareAsync(world, position, playerId)` (loads a world around a place for this machine's simulation without moving anyone, the host before spawning a joining player there, and returns where to place that player: the position, or walkable ground near it when the player can no longer stand there), `JoinAsync()` (a joined client's start, returning the world the host put its player in, or null when the session ended first), `ArriveAsync(world, entryName)` (the end of a local player's trip), `LeaveAsync()`. |
| [ChunkStreamer](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkStreamer.cs) | Plain C#, the decisions. Interests ([StreamInterest](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/StreamInterest.cs): a world and a world position) come in with the game time (`SetInterests`); `TryNext` gives one [StreamAction](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/StreamAction.cs) at a time (`LoadWorld`, `LoadChunk`, `UnloadChunk`, `UnloadWorld`) and `Complete(success)` records how it ended (a failed world or chunk is not tried again). Order: a world a player is in; the missing chunk nearest a player within the view plus the load margin; a loaded chunk no player keeps within the view plus the margin plus the unload hysteresis (only in worlds someone is in); then, for a world nobody has been in for the empty-world grace, its chunks and itself. A chunk its `IChunkHolds` (the host's session) says a client holds never unloads, so neither does its world, until it is released or `ReleaseAll` (the session ending) lets everything go. `ReleaseAll` lets everything go (`Resume` streams again); `IsSettled(interest)` (the world and every chunk around loaded, none of them being unloaded; a chunk that failed counts as done, and so does a world whose root scene failed, whose chunks are never tried, so nothing waits on it for ever), `IsWorldLoaded`, `IsChunkLoaded`, `IsBusy`, `IsEmpty`; static `ExtentsOf(viewCells, margin)`, the cells a rectangle reaches around a player's cell. |
| [WorldStreaming](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldStreaming.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`) and `IWorldSession`. Each frame, while a session runs, it gives the streamer (made with the session as its `IChunkHolds`) the players it streams for (every registered player in a world with `IGameAuthority`, so the host streams for every player in every world; else the local one; their world from `PlayerWorlds`) and runs its next action: a world's root scene loaded additively under the Gameplay scope with the `WorldDefinition` enqueued, a chunk under its world's scope with a `ChunkIdentity` enqueued, recorded in `Worlds`; unloads remove them from `Worlds` first. A failure is logged and completes the action as failed. The client's half of the handshake (`IChunkHandshake`, nothing offline or on the host): a loaded chunk is reported ready with the local player's trip count; when that count changes, every loaded chunk of the player's new world is reported again (a chunk kept from an earlier visit is up, and its report from before the trip may have been ignored), but not one whose release waits for the host, which unloads and is reported as it loads again; and an unload first releases the chunk and waits for the host's acknowledgement. `EnterAsync` waits for the local player (up to 600 frames), puts every player this machine simulates in the world and teleports it to a free slot (`EntrySlots`) at the start position (an entry's or a saved place), snaps the camera, and waits until `IsSettled` there, then, with authority, when the local player cannot stand there (`WalkablePlaces.TryMove`, by its `PlayerIds` id), moves them all on to the place it gives and waits until settled there too; `PrepareAsync` streams a place as an interest of its own (`preparations`) until `IsSettled` there, then drops it (at once when no session streams), and with authority asks `WalkablePlaces` about the joining player's id, streaming the moved place the same way and returning it; `JoinAsync` waits (up to 60 s of real time, or until the session ends) for the host to spawn the local player in a world, snaps the camera and waits until `IsSettled` around the player; `LeaveAsync` releases everything, forgets the trips' arrivals, waits until the streamer is empty and takes the players out of their worlds. It also streams each held trip arrival (`TripArrivals.AddInterests`) as an interest of its own, so a trip's target world and the chunks around its entry load while the traveller's screen fades out. `ArriveAsync`, the end of the local player's trip, teleports the local player (already in the target world, put there by the trip's authority) to a free slot at the entry, snaps the camera and waits until `IsSettled` there, and, for a dead player (a respawn's trip), until it is alive again, which the host decides once it stands there, at most `GameplaySettings.TripProtectionSeconds`, so its screen fades in on the living player; the world it left is untouched and waits out the empty-world grace like any world nobody is in. `IsSettled(world, position)` tells whether a world and the chunks around a position in it are loaded (the streaming benchmark waits on it). |
| [Worlds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/Worlds.cs), [LoadedWorld](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/LoadedWorld.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the loaded worlds: each `LoadedWorld` has its definition, root scene, scope, `WorldLighting`, `Navigation` (the `WorldNavigation` its scope registers, resolved by `WorldStreaming` as the root scene loads) and the scenes of its loaded chunks. `TryGet`, events `WorldLoaded`, `WorldUnloading`, `ChunkLoaded`, `ChunkUnloading`. |
| [PlayerWorlds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/PlayerWorlds.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of the world each registered player is in, with its trip count and its latest trip's entry (`Get`, `TripOf`, `EntryOf`, `Changed` when the world or the trip count changes): `Set` (no trip; taking a player out of every world forgets its trips), `Travel(player, world, entry)` (an approved trip: one more, even back into the same world), `Apply(player, world, trip, entry)` (a client following the host, through `PlayerWorldSync`); forgets a player that leaves the registry. |
| [ChunkIdentity](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkIdentity.cs) | Which chunk a chunk scene is (world, coordinates), registered into its scope. |
| [WorldLighting](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldLighting.cs) | Marker on the object of a world's root scene that holds its global lights (`SetShown`). In play mode it hides its object in `Awake` (execution order -1000, before the lights under it are enabled), since URP logs an error whenever a second global light is enabled, as when a trip's target world loads beside the one on screen; `LocalWorldPresentation` shows the world on screen. `IsShown` while its object is active. Its `Profile` ([WorldLightingProfile](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldLighting.cs), Lighting.md's Phase 3): `Outdoor` (the default, every world but one), whose global lights under it take the day's sky at an intensity of 1 (`ApplySky`, from `DayNightPresenter`; the lights found the first time and kept), or `Indoor` (`SandboxInterior`), whose keep their authored light. `GlobalLight`: the light its global lights give as the light texture holds it, the first one's colour times its intensity (black without one), which water's reflections take for the sky indoors (Lighting.md's Phase 10). As its world loads it turns its global lights' shadows off (Lighting.md's Phase 7): URP draws no global light's shadow, but would still draw a shadow texture for one near a caster. Internal `Configure(profile)` for tests. |
| [LocalWorldPresentation](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/LocalWorldPresentation.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, with the `WorldStreamingSettings`): shows the lighting of the presented world and hides every other loaded world's, as a world loads (at once, so two global lights never draw together) and whenever the local player, its world or its cell change. The presented world (`PresentedWorld`) is the local player's world once the player stands on that world's cells: a trip puts the player in its target world before moving it there, so the world being left stays lit and drawn behind the fade out until the player arrives. URP warns about two global lights on a sorting layer and lights everything with one of them, whatever world it is in (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 0). Only the local world draws: it records each loaded chunk's `WorldChunk` (`ChunkLoaded`, `ChunkUnloading`) and enables a chunk's terrain once the local player's interest rectangle (`LoadExtents`) overlaps it, keeping it until it leaves the rectangle widened by the hysteresis (`KeepExtents`), and disables every chunk of another world (`ShouldDraw`), so the host builds no ground for chunks it loads for other players. Each frame it only compares the local player's cell with the last one. |
| [WorldChunk](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldChunk.cs) | `[ExecuteAlways]`, `[DefaultExecutionOrder(-100)]` on a chunk scene's `Grid`: its `World`, `Coords` (`Cells`), the `CoastMap` baked for it and `CoastSource`, what that map was baked from (`WorldCoastBake.SourceOf`; serialized; `Configure`), and the `GlowMap` baked for it, the cells it covers (`GlowArea`) and `GlowSource` (`WorldGlowBake.SourceOf`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5). When enabled it makes the grid's terrain renderer a chunk with a `SkirtCells` (4) skirt, its coast map and its glow map and its wall modules chunk modules, then links with each of its eight neighbours of the same world whose scene is open (`SceneManager.GetSceneByPath` of the world's chunk scene, so at runtime and in the editor alike): each gives the other its ground layers and raised ground in the strip along their border and its wall modules of the same rule tile. Disabled or unloaded, it unlinks and its neighbours draw their own edges again. `Link`, `Unlink` (`internal`), `Neighbours`, `RefreshLinks` (after an editor tool changes its layers or walls). In play mode its terrain starts disabled (`IsDrawn`, `SetDrawn`) until `LocalWorldPresentation` shows it. |
| [WorldStreamingSettings](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldStreamingSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject: `ViewCells` (20 x 12), `LoadMargin` (12), `UnloadHysteresis` (8), `EmptyWorldGraceSeconds` (10); `LoadExtents` and `KeepExtents` (`ChunkStreamer.ExtentsOf`: 22 x 18 and 30 x 26 cells each side), `CreateStreamer`. The margins come from WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 load times. `KeepsHomeChunkLoaded(config, out problem)` is the leash rule: a mob's `LeashCells` plus its `DetectionRadius` under the load extents' smaller side (18), so any player it can detect has its home chunk in its interest rectangle, and plus its `LoseTargetDistance` under the keep extents' smaller side (26), so that chunk never unloads under it while it chases. `MobLeashConstraintTests` checks every `MobConfig` asset against the project's settings (the Weasel's 8 + 6 and 8 + 8). |
| [ChunkMobSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkMobSpawner.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point (`IPostStartable`, so it runs after `ChunkNavigation.Start` added the chunk's page). Offline or on the host, `PostStart` spawns one mob per spawn point of the chunk through the chunk's scope (`MobSpawning`) into the chunk's scene, gives it the chunk's area as its home (`HomeArea`, `MobController.SetHome`) and network-spawns it in a session as an object of its chunk (`Spawn(instance, homeChunk)`: visible to the clients holding the chunk; `Mobs` lists them). It despawns them when `Worlds.ChunkUnloading` names its chunk, before the scene unloads, and anything left when its scope is disposed. A mob stays in its home chunk's scene its whole life, wherever it walks, so it goes with that chunk. In a session `RegisterNetworkPrefabs` (the scope's build callback) registers each distinct mob prefab for its chunk with `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`, on a client so the host's mobs arrive in this chunk's scope, on the host so its spawns carry their home chunk, and `Dispose` unregisters them. On a client it spawns nothing. Persistence, offline and on the host: it follows each mob's `Health.Died` and records the death in its chunk's record of the world's `WorldState` by the spawn point's `PersistentId`, at `SessionSave.Time` (`WorldState.RecordMobDeath`; a spawn point without an id warns, and its mob comes back at every load); `ShouldSpawn` (private) skips a spawn point whose mob `StaysDead` by its `MobConfig` (`Respawns`, `RespawnDelaySeconds`), whichever chunk's record holds the death (`TryFindMobDeath`: a regenerated world can move a spawn point to another chunk). |
| [ChunkNetworkPrefabs](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkNetworkPrefabs.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the network prefabs the loaded chunks of this machine's worlds register in a session (`Register(prefab, chunk, resolver, scene)`, `Unregister`, `IsRegistered`). Netcode keeps one handler per prefab while several chunks list the same mob, so it installs one routing handler per prefab through `INetworkObjectSpawner` when the first chunk registers it and removes it when the last one unregisters. The handler is a `NetworkPrefabInstanceHandlerWithData<NetworkChunkId>`: the host's spawns carry their home chunk as instantiation data, and a client's copy is created through the `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` of that chunk, wherever the object stands (a mob that walked into a neighbour still lands in its home chunk's scene); a copy without one, or whose chunk is not registered (with a warning), goes to the registered chunk holding its spawn position, or the nearest. The host registers too, since Netcode sets instantiation data only for a prefab with such a handler; it never instantiates through it. `Dispose` removes every handler. |
| [ChunkTerrainSync](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkTerrainSync.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point for a chunk with an `AreaTerrain`, per-chunk terrain sync. In a session `RegisterNetworkPrefab` (the scope's build callback) registers the terrain's sync prefab for the chunk with `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`, so a client's copy is injected with this chunk's terrain. Offline and on the host `Start` restores the chunk's edits from its record in the world's `WorldState` (`AreaTerrain.RestoreEdits`, so they are the terrain's own and replicate) and records every later edit there, its layer named by `AreaTerrain.KeyOf` (`EditRecorded`, until disposed). On the host it then instantiates the sync prefab through the chunk's scope into the chunk's scene and spawns it as an object of the chunk (`Spawned`), and despawns it when `Worlds.ChunkUnloading` names the chunk or the scope is disposed. On a client it restores and records nothing. |
| [WorldPlayers](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldPlayers.cs) | The players in one world that its mobs consider, an `IPlayerRegistry` (its `World`): the Gameplay scope's registry narrowed by `PlayerWorlds`, leaving out the travellers `TripProtection` keeps out of play, following players into and out of the world and of play (`PlayerAdded`, `PlayerRemoved`) without allocating per frame. The world's scope registers it as its `IPlayerRegistry`, so the chunks' mobs consider only the players in their world, and never a traveller before its machine has faded in. |
| [PersistentId](../Assets/Scripts/World/PersistentId.cs) | A stable id for a placed object whose state persists (`Id`, `HasId`): a GUID string the editor assigns once and keeps unique within the world (`PersistentIds`, `PersistentIdWatcher`). Every `MobSpawnPoint` and `WorldExit` in a chunk scene has one (the Sandbox's twelve spawn points got theirs in Phase 5). A trip request names its exit by it (`WorldExits.TryFind`), and the world state keys killed mobs by it. |
| [WorldExit](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/WorldExit.cs) | A marker in a chunk scene (`[RequireComponent(typeof(PersistentId))]`, a cyan gizmo): `TargetWorld`, `TargetEntry` (empty for the default entry), `SizeInCells`, `Id` (its persistent id), `CellsIn(world)` (its lower-left cell holds its position), and `Marker`, what shows it in game (`ExitMarker`: `Signpost`, the default, for a road's exit to another world, `Door` for an exit into an interior, `Doormat` for one out of an interior, or `None`; KnownGaps.md's Phase 3), whose prop the World Editor's Place markers step places. Placed under an `Exits` root by `WorldAuthoring.AddExit`; validated as owned by its chunk. The Sandbox's exit into its interior shows a doorway, the interior's a doormat, the road exits between Testlands and the Sandbox signposts. |
| [WorldExits](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/WorldExits.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the exits in the loaded chunks of every loaded world: it reads a chunk's exits (`SceneQuery`) on `Worlds.ChunkLoaded` and forgets them on `ChunkUnloading`. `TryFind(world, id)`, `TryFindAt(world, cell)`, `Add` (tests), `Count`. |
| [TripRequest](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripRequest.cs), [TripApproval](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripApproval.cs) | Plain data: a player, an exit id and, for a client's request, the position its own machine has for the player (`HasPosition`); a player, its target world, the entry's name and its position. |
| [ITripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ITripAuthority.cs), [LocalTripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/LocalTripAuthority.cs) | Where a player's machine sends trip requests (`Request`) and hears back which trips go (`Approved`). `LocalTripAuthority` is the checks, offline and on the host: `TryApprove` refuses a dead player (its machine may not know yet; it travels only to its respawn's waystone, through `Send`), checks that the exit is loaded in the player's world, that the player stands within `ReachCells` (1) of its cells (at the request's position when it has one, else its body's), that it leads to a world `GameScenes` lists, and that the world has the entry (an unknown name falls back to the default, with `FindEntry`'s warning), and refuses a player whose trip is under way; then it holds the arrival in `TripArrivals` (so the target loads), puts the player in the target world for one more trip (`PlayerWorlds.Travel`), protects it on that trip (`TripProtection.Protect`) and gives the approval with the resolved entry, through `Send(player, world, entry)` (internal), which does that last part without any check for a trip the authority starts itself (a respawn). Its own `Request` (tests) logs a refusal with the reason. `ReportArrived(player, trip)`: the player's machine faded in at the end of that trip, which ends its protection (`TripProtection.End`). |
| [NetworkTripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/NetworkTripAuthority.cs), [ITripLink](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ITripLink.cs) | The Gameplay scope's `ITripAuthority`. Offline and on the host `Request` goes to `Approve`: `LocalTripAuthority.TryApprove`, then `Approved` (a refusal is a warning); a client's player hears of its trip through its networked world instead. On a client (`SendsToHost`: in a session, not hosting) `Request` goes through the player's `ITripLink` (`PlayerWorldSync`; `LinkFinder`, internal, swaps the lookup in tests) with the position this machine has for the player, a warning without one; `Deliver(approval)`, from `PlayerWorldSync`, holds the arrival in `TripArrivals` and raises `Approved`. `Send(player, world, entry)` (offline or the host): a trip no request asked for, a dead player's to its waystone (`PlayerRespawner`), through `LocalTripAuthority.Send`, then `Approved`; a client's player hears of it through its networked world, as of any trip. `ReportArrived(player, trip)`, the player's machine faded in at the end of a trip: on a client it goes to the host through the player's `ITripLink` (`SendTripArrived`; a warning without one), offline and on the host it ends the trip's protection (`LocalTripAuthority.ReportArrived`). |
| [TripProtection](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripProtection.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`ITickable`), the `IUnitProtection` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 2): the travellers kept out of play where game state is decided. `LocalTripAuthority` protects a player as it sends it on a trip (`Protect`, for the trip `PlayerWorlds` has it on, from then until `GameplaySettings.TripProtectionSeconds` of game time later; a new trip replaces it), and its machine's fade-in report ends it (`End(player, trip)`, nothing for an earlier trip's report); `Tick` ends those past their time limit with a warning, and a player leaving the registry leaves its protection. Out of play, a player is no player of its world for the mobs (`WorldPlayers`), `HitService` ignores hits on it and `StatusEffectService` holds its statuses still. `IsProtected(player)`, `IsProtected(receiver)` (by its `Health`), `Changed`, `Count`. Nothing is protected on a client. |
| [TripArrivals](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripArrivals.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of the trips under way: per player, the target world and entry position (`Hold`, `IsHeld`, `TryGet`, `Release`, `Clear`). `AddInterests(interests, playerWorlds)` adds each to the streamed interests and drops it once the player stands within two cells of the entry in that world (`HasArrived`), or is gone; allocates nothing once warm. |
| [WaystoneRules](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/WaystoneRules.cs) | Static (Core), the waystone rules over a player's discoveries (a list of `GameSaveData.Waystone`): `IsDiscovered` (the world's default entry always, a waystone entry once the list names it in that world, a plain entry never), `Contains`, `TryFindNearest(world, position, discovered)` (the discovered entry nearest in a straight line, the first listed on a tie; false without entries), `TryFindDiscovery(world, position, reach, discovered)` (the first undiscovered waystone, in list order, whose cell lies within `reach` cells of the position's along each axis). |
| [WaystoneDiscovery](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneDiscovery.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`), offline and on the host only (it has every player's position): each frame it compares every living player's cell in its world with the last one, allocating nothing once warm, and on a new cell asks `WaystoneRules.TryFindDiscovery` with `GameplaySettings.WaystoneReachCells` (2). A discovery is recorded in the `SessionSave` under the player's id (`PlayerIds`), raised on `WaystoneNotices`, and sent to the player's machine through its `IWaystoneLink` (`PlayerWorldSync`; `LinkFinder`, internal, swaps the lookup in tests). |
| [WaystoneNotices](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneNotices.cs), [IWaystoneLink](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/IWaystoneLink.cs) | Gameplay-scope news of discoveries on this machine (`Discovered(player, entryName)`, `Raise`): every player's offline and on the host, its own player's on a client (raised by `PlayerWorldSync` from the host's RPC); and how the host tells a client's machine (`SendWaystoneDiscovered(worldId, entryName)`). |
| [KnownWaystones](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/KnownWaystones.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of the waystones this machine's own player discovered (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3), which the stones show: where game state is decided, the `SessionSave`'s under this install's player id (`IPlayerIdSource.LocalPlayerId`), `Changed` raised on each discovery (`WaystoneNotices`); on a client, the list the host replicates on its player (`Replicate`, from `PlayerWorldSync`, each waystone once), `Changed` raised on each list. `Discovered`, `IsDiscovered(world, entry)` (`WaystoneRules.IsDiscovered`: the default entry always). |
| [WaystoneMarker](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneMarker.cs) | On a waystone's stone (`Waystone_0`, a `BlockingProp` variant the prop writer makes from the `Waystone` marker recipe; KnownGaps.md's Phase 3): the entry it names (`EntryName`, set by Place markers), its renderer and the sleeping and awake slices of its one sheet (so switching keeps the sheet's glow and normals), and its `Light` child, inactive as written. `SetAwake(awake)` shows the awake slice, whose runes glow, and sets the light active, or the sleeping slice and the light inactive; the light's `PropLight` reaches `PropLighting` with its chunk's (found inactive too), which writes it while it is inactive, so it burns at the night's share as soon as it wakes. `IsAwake`; `Configure` (the writer, tests), `SetEntry` (tests). Runs nothing. |
| [WaystoneMarkers](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneMarkers.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `IDisposable`): shows each loaded chunk's stones (`SceneQuery` on `Worlds.ChunkLoaded`, forgotten on `ChunkUnloading`) as `KnownWaystones` has them, awake once discovered, and looks at all of them again on its `Changed`; a stone naming a plain entry or none sleeps. So the host and every client each show their own player's discoveries. `Add(world, chunk, marker)` (tests), `ShowsAwake(world, entryName)`, `Count`. Nothing per frame, nothing sent. |
| [PlayerIds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/PlayerIds.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of each registered player's player id (`IdOf`): its owner's on the host while it is a spawned network object, this install's otherwise (`IPlayerIdSource`), learned as it registers, while its owner is still known, and kept until another player registers after it leaves, so it stays readable while the registry announces the removal. `GameSaveRecorder`, `WaystoneDiscovery` and `PlayerRespawner` key a player's place and waystones by it. |
| [ExitTriggerGuard](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ExitTriggerGuard.cs) | Plain re-trigger protection: `Step(onExit)` fires once when the player steps onto an exit from a cell that is none; it starts unarmed and `Disarm` (after a trip) waits for the player to stand on no exit again. |
| [LocalTripTraveller](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/LocalTripTraveller.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`), the local player's side of trips. Each frame outside transitions it compares the local player's cell with the last one; on a new cell it asks `WorldExits.TryFindAt` and the guard, and requests a trip through `ITripAuthority` when the guard fires (on a client, an RPC to the host). On an approval for the local player it waits for any running transition, stops the player (`ClearOrders`) and runs `GameFlow.TravelAsync`, then, faded in, reports the trip's end (`ITripAuthority.ReportArrived` with the trip count it was approved on), which ends the player's protection; a session that ended meanwhile releases the arrival instead. `IsTravelling`. |

Online (Phase 7) every machine knows each player's world, a client streams only its own player's world and the host every player's, and a client sees a chunk's mobs and terrain edits only while it holds that chunk (see Networking). A player walking into a world exit travels to another world (Phase 6): the trip is a local transition (`GameFlow.TravelAsync`) with gameplay input off and game time running, through which the host keeps the traveller out of play (`TripProtection`: no mob sees it, no hit lands, its statuses stand still) until its machine reports the fade in, and the world left behind stays loaded for the empty-world grace (10 s), so a trip straight back reloads nothing. A player in a world paths on its world's `WorldNavigation` (Phase 3): each loaded chunk's page joined to its loaded neighbours', so steps, costs, the cliff and regions run across borders and a path ends at the edge of the loaded chunks. Each chunk spawns its mobs as it loads and takes them out before it unloads (Phase 4): they path on the same navigation, consider only the players in their world, patrol inside their home chunk and give up on a target or a path more than their leash (8 cells) beyond it. Ground, raised ground and walls draw across chunk borders (Phase 2): each chunk's `WorldChunk` links its renderer and walls with its loaded neighbours', and each chunk's coast map is baked from the whole world by `WorldAuthoring.BakeCoastMaps` (`WorldCoastBake`).
- Scene generation: `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Scene Generator` fills the active scene's standalone grid (a scratch scene its New scratch scene button makes, `WorldAuthoring.CreateScratchScene`) from an `AreaGenerator` asset; it refuses a world chunk's grid, since writing layouts into worlds belongs to the world generation plan (WorldStreaming.md's Later). It repaints the terrain layers as plain data tilemaps (creating the missing ones), keeps any other layer the grid draws that is painted, at its place in the style's layer order, and removes the empty ones, sets up the grid's terrain renderer and `AreaTerrain`'s fill tiles for them, and replaces the props under `Props` and every mob spawn point, as one undo step. Its Convert Ground sets a grid's ground layers up as the generator's without repainting them, creating the ones the grid lacks and keeping or removing the others the same way (`GroundLayerMigration`). Generation runs in the editor and its result is saved in the scene. The six generated areas (the Clearing, the Village Forest, the Lakeside, the Snowfield, the Coast and the Lava Fields) were made this way and went with the area path in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9; their generator assets and seeds stay, for world generation to bring them back as regions of the first world. See [SceneGeneration.md](SceneGeneration.md).

## Dev tools

### Status panel

[StatusDebugPanel](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Statuses/StatusDebugPanel.cs) (`TopDownRPG.DevTools`, so editor and development builds only): created once per play session by `[RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod]` and kept in `DontDestroyOnLoad`. F2 toggles an IMGUI list of the status catalog (buffs `+`, debuffs `-`); F3 applies the selected status to the unit under the cursor (`PointerTargetPicker` rules) and F4 to the local player; a button clears the local player's statuses; the last outcome is shown. Statuses go through `StatusEffectService` without a source, so faction rules do not apply, and only with authority (offline or host; a client gets `NoAuthority`). Because the Gameplay scope cannot reference DevTools, the panel finds the `GameplayLifetimeScope` itself (cached; accepted, see Known gaps and oddities) and resolves `StatusEffectService`, `CombatSettings`, `LocalPlayerTracker`, `PointerTargetPicker` and `Camera` from its container when used. `ApplyToSelf(definition)` and `ApplyToUnitAt(screenPoint, definition)` are public for tests.

### Day and night panel

[DayNightDebugPanel](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Lighting/DayNightDebugPanel.cs) (`TopDownRPG.DevTools`): created once per play session like the status panel. F7 toggles an IMGUI panel at the bottom left with a slider over the cycle (where the day is, in minutes after sunrise, and whether the sun is up) and a button to follow the session's time again. It moves the Gameplay scope's `DayClock` through `ShowPhase` and `FollowSession`, so this machine's sky alone changes and nothing is saved or sent. `ShowTimeOfDay(phase)` is public for tests. It finds the Gameplay scope itself (cached) and resolves the clock from it.

### Lighting panel

[LightingDebugPanel](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Lighting/LightingDebugPanel.cs) (`TopDownRPG.DevTools`): created once per play session like the status panel; while closed its `OnGUI` only watches for F8, and nothing in Gameplay runs for it. F8 toggles an IMGUI panel at the top right, under the HUD's weapon, with a readout and the key map, the overrides' states in it. Its keys, none that gameplay binds by default (Docs/KeyBindings.md) nor the other panels' F2 to F7: `3` to `0`, `-` and `=` jump to the settings' ten light keys in order (sunrise, early morning, morning, noon, afternoon, evening, sunset, dusk, night, before dawn); `,` and `.` step the time of day by 1/100 of the cycle (15 s of the shipped 25 minutes), 1/1000 with Shift; `P` holds it or lets it run; `F` runs it 1, 10 or 60 times the session's; Backspace follows the session again; `Z` keeps the sun's shadows off or follows the day; `M` cycles the clouds through following the world clock, covering the most the day and the region allow, and none (Lighting.md's Phase 8); `G` cycles water's reflections through following the day, as at night, and none (Phase 10); `H` keeps relief under the lights off or draws it (Phase 11); `X` and `C` cycle the prop lights and the lava's glow through following the day, on and off; `V` and `B` keep the flicker and the glowing pixels off or follow (the flicker following the player's Reduce flashing too, which stills it whatever the panel says, DesignSystem.md's Phase 6); `N` keeps the prop lights' shadows off or follows; `T` and Shift+`T` teleport the local player to the next and previous entry of its world (the Sandbox's showcase spots, see Scenes). Everything is local to this machine and nothing is saved or sent. Its logic is [LightingDebugControls](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Lighting/LightingDebugControls.cs), plain C# over the Gameplay scope's `DayClock`, `DayNightPresenter` and `PropLighting` (each optional), the current world (`GameFlow.CurrentWorld`) and a teleport (`PlayerController.Teleport` on the `LocalPlayerTracker`'s player), through their `internal` seams (Gameplay grants DevTools its internals): `ShowKey(index)`, `StepTime(steps, fine)`, `ToggleHold` (back to the rate it ran at), `NextRate`, `FollowSession`, `CycleSunShadows`, `CycleClouds`, `CycleReflections`, `CycleRelief`, `CyclePropLights`, `CycleGroundGlow`, `CycleFlicker`, `CycleGlowingPixels`, `CycleLightShadows`, `Teleport(steps)` (from the last entry it sent the player to, round either way), `SpotText`, `RateText`, `KeyName`, `StateText` and `Readout(fps)`: the frame rate, the time of day by its keys, how it runs, whether the sun is up and casts, the sky's and the sun's colours, the region skies' tint at the local player, the clouds' cover and the region's share of it, the night lights' share, how many chunks' lights `PropLighting` holds and how many props' casters they keep awake, and the spot. The panel's public `Controls()` makes them for each Gameplay scope it finds (cached, as the other panels find theirs), so tests drive it without input.

### Weapon panel

[WeaponDebugPanel](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Weapons/WeaponDebugPanel.cs) (`TopDownRPG.DevTools`): created once per play session like the status panel. F5 equips the next weapon of the local player's catalog (`EquipNext`, wrapping round); F6 toggles an IMGUI list of the catalog at the top right with a button per weapon, the equipped one marked. It switches through the player's own `PlayerWeaponController.Equip`, so the switch reaches the other machines as any would. It finds the Gameplay scope itself (cached) and resolves `LocalPlayerTracker` from it.

### Stress test

Assembly `TopDownRPG.DevTools`, the dev-only stress world `Assets/Dev/Worlds/StressTest` (see Scenes), mob variant `Weasel_StressTest.prefab` (a variant of the Weasel, drawn by its baked set) using `Mob_StressTest` (zero attack damage, so it never attacks), in `Assets/Dev/StressTest`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [StressTestMap](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestMap.cs) | `[DefaultExecutionOrder(-1000)]`; in `Awake` generates a seeded map of `width` x `height` cells (128 x 128, the whole stress world) from its grid's cell (0, 0), walled along its outer ring and inside in an Open, Scattered or Corridors layout, its middle kept clear: it fills the plain ground tilemap (`groundTilemap`, the chunk's `GrassTilemap`, a layer of its `TerrainBlendRenderer`) with the ground tile (`groundTile`, `Tile_Grass`) in one batch and paints the walls through the collision tilemap's module (`DualGridTilemapModule.SetDataTiles`). On a chunk's grid (with a `WorldChunk`) it paints only that chunk's cells, every chunk laying the map out alike from the seed so walls run on across borders, and leaves the navigation page to `ChunkNavigation`, which builds it in `Start`; on a standalone grid it rebuilds the `NavigationGrid2D`. Logs an error and generates nothing when a reference is missing. |
| [StressTestSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestSpawner.cs) | In the stress world's root scene, injected from its world scope (listed in the `WorldLifetimeScope`'s auto-inject objects). Once every chunk of the world has its page in the `WorldNavigation`, a seeded spawn of its initial mobs through the world's scope (so they path on its navigation and see its players) on reachable cells near itself and across the world, then it stops updating; `SpawnNearPlayer`, `SpawnAcrossMap`, `Mobs`, `MobCount`, `ClearMobs`. Its mobs have no home chunk, so no leash holds them. |
| [StressTestOverlay](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestOverlay.cs) | IMGUI overlay: FPS, mob counts per state, `ProfilerRecorder` stats for FindPath/BuildPath and GC alloc per frame, spawn/clear buttons. |

Play it by opening `World_StressTest.unity` (or one of its chunks) and pressing Play: Boot From Main boots into any world asset, listed in `GameScenes` or not.

### Streaming benchmark

In `Assets/Scripts/Dev/Benchmark` ([WorldScale.md](WorldScale.md)'s Phase 0), shared by the editor's PlayMode benchmark and a development player.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [StreamingBenchmark](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Benchmark/StreamingBenchmark.cs) | Plain class over a world, its `LoadedWorld`, `WorldStreaming` and the local `PlayerController`. `Run()` places the player on the Clearing's entry, waits for the chunks around it (`WorldStreaming.IsSettled`) and 120 warm frames, then carries it a quarter of a cell a frame along `Route` (the Clearing, the Village Forest, the Coast, the Snowfield, the Clearing, the Lakeside, the Lava Fields), deactivating the mobs each frame. `Line` holds the frame and main-thread times (mean, 99th percentile, worst), each chunk load's worst frame of it and the 3 after it with the scene activation (`Application.LoadLevelAsync Integrate`) and ground build (`TerrainBlendRenderer.Build`, every build, rebuilds for a neighbour's skirt included) in them, the mean over the loads of each of the build's parts and of the neighbours' skirt re-reads and teardowns (their `TerrainBlendRenderer.` markers, WorldScale.md's Phase 2), the memory allocated a frame, the draw calls, the most chunks and props loaded, and the memory used once warm and at most. `LoadDetails` holds each load's chunk, props and times, its ground's parts included, and `Failure` why a run stopped. `Mean`, `Max` and `Percentile` are its statistics. |
| [StreamingBenchmarkRunner](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Benchmark/StreamingBenchmarkRunner.cs) | Created before the first scene loads when the command line holds `-streamingBenchmark <file>` (and optionally `-streamingBenchmarkLabel <label>`). It enqueues `BenchmarkSaveStore`s as the save and settings stores for the Main scope, waits for the main menu and its transition, reads the memory used there, presses New Game, runs the benchmark, appends its lines to the file and quits (exit code 1 on a failure). It finds the Main and Gameplay scopes when it needs them, like the debug panels. |
| [BenchmarkSaveStore](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Benchmark/BenchmarkSaveStore.cs), [BenchmarkSaveFolder](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Benchmark/BenchmarkSaveFolder.cs) | In-memory `ISaveStore` and `ISaveFolder` (with no earlier build's save file) a run starts empty with, so it touches no save or settings file. |

Run it in a Windows development build in batchmode (no window, CPU only), with `-batchmode -logFile <log> -streamingBenchmark <file>`. First add `player-connection-listen-address=127.0.0.1` and `player-connection-multicast=false` to the build's `boot.config`, so the development player raises no firewall prompt.

## Editor tools

Menu root: `Tools/TopDownRPG/`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayModeBootstrapper](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/PlayModeBootstrapper.cs) | Boot From Main toggle (EditorPrefs), saves modified scenes before Play, records open scenes as an `EditorBootRequest`, skips Test Runner sessions. |
| [ProjectScenes](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/ProjectScenes.cs) | Constants for Main scene path, GameScenes asset path, menu root. |
| [SceneSetMenu](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneSetMenu.cs) | `Open Scenes/` menu: Main; Main + Main Menu; Main + Gameplay + Starting World (its root scene). |
| [SceneBuildValidator](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneBuildValidator.cs) | Pre-build check (and `Validate Build Scenes` menu): Main first in build list, every `SceneDefinition` assigned and enabled in the build, but those under `DevFolder` (`Assets/Dev/`: the stress world's, played from the editor only). |
| [SceneDefinitionEditor](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneDefinitionEditor.cs) | Inspector with a `SceneAsset` picker + build-list warning. |
| [SceneDefinitionPathSync](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneDefinitionPathSync.cs) | `AssetPostprocessor` refreshing every `SceneDefinition`'s path when scenes move. |
| [VContainerDiagnosticsBuildGuard](../Assets/Editor/VContainerDiagnosticsBuildGuard.cs) | Turns off VContainer diagnostics for non-development builds (VContainer honours `EnableDiagnostics` in players too) and restores it afterwards: in its postprocess callback, and, since Unity skips postprocess callbacks when a build fails, also from an `EditorApplication.delayCall` that re-queues itself until no player build runs. |
| [DesignTables](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignTables.cs) | Static (DesignSystem.md's Phase 1): `Tools/TopDownRPG/Design/Write Design Tables`. `Build()` returns one [DesignTable](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignTable.cs) (a file name and its Markdown) per kind, built from [DesignContent](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignContent.cs) (`FromProject`: `CombatSettings.asset` and its status catalog, `WeaponCatalog.asset`, every ability and class under `Assets/Data`, a [DesignUnit](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignUnit.cs) per unit prefab in `Assets/Prefabs/Player` and `Assets/Prefabs/Mobs`, and `UiTheme.asset` with every themed text and bar fill, `UiThemeUsage.Collect()` and `CollectFills()`); `Build(content)` takes content tests build from `Create`; `Write()` writes them into `Docs/Generated` (LF, no byte order mark): `DamageTypes.md` (popup colours and their contrast on the theme's `Outline`, the resolution rules), `Weapons.md` (each weapon's attack, period and damage per second, then hits and time to kill against each mob), `Abilities.md`, `StatusEffects.md` (rules and periodic totals, then how each shows) `Units.md` (players and mobs, then each mob's hits and time to kill against each player) and `UiTheme.md` (DesignSystem.md's Phase 4: each colour role with its opacity, each text size with its font, UI px and body height at 1080p, each pair of text and background roles a themed text uses with its contrast and threshold, from `UiThemeUsage.Measure`, and, since UiRevamp.md's Phase 1, each pair of a bar's fill and track roles against 3:1, from `UiThemeUsage.MeasureFills`). Invariant culture, rows in catalog order or by name. [DesignMath](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignMath.cs) holds the derived columns: `AttackPeriod` (the cooldown, or the time until the attack may be cut short when longer; a mob's recovery always plays out), `HitsToKill`, `TimeToKill` ((hits - 1) x period + windup), `PeriodicTicks` (as `StatusEffectSet` ticks), `Resolve` (a hit through `DamageMath` against a profile's base defence) and `ContrastRatio` (WCAG 2). |
| [UiFonts](../Assets/Editor/UI/UiFonts.cs) | Static (DesignSystem.md's Phase 4): `Tools/TopDownRPG/UI/Write Fonts` writes, from each Galmuri TTF in `Assets/Fonts`, a static SDF font asset (sampled at 6 atlas pixels per font pixel, the font's em in UI px times 6, with 12 pixels of padding; 1024 x 1024 for Galmuri7 and Galmuri9, 2048 x 1024 for Galmuri11 and Galmuri14) holding `Characters`, and an outlined material preset beside it (`OUTLINE_ON`, black, width and face dilation 0.25, which puts the outline one font pixel outside a whole face: it reaches 2 x width x padding atlas pixels, measured on previews). Rewrites existing assets in place, keeping their GUIDs and references; a second run writes the same bytes. |
| [UiSkinRecipe](../Assets/Editor/UI/UiSkinRecipe.cs) / [UiSkinWriter](../Assets/Editor/UI/UiSkinWriter.cs) / [UiSkinRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/UI/UiSkinRecipeEditor.cs) | The UI skin (DesignSystem.md's Phase 5), generated like the terrain materials and the walls: `UiSkinRecipe` (editor-only ScriptableObject, `Assets/Settings/UI/Recipe_UiSkin.asset`: the corner cut, the raised panel's lip height and grey, the slot frame's inner line, the markers' half-axes, line, arms, outline and the hover brackets' share) draws each `UiSkinPiece` without touching an asset (`Generate(piece)`, a `SkinSprite`: its size, greyscale pixels and 9-slice border), the same settings always giving the same pixels: `Panel`, `PanelFrame` and `PanelRaised` (6 px, 2 px borders, square corners since UiRevamp.md's Phase 3, where they were cut by 1 px and 8 px; the raised one 2 px taller, its lip at 0.6 grey), `SlotFrame` (with a black line at 40% inside it), `BarFrame` (3 px, 1 px borders) and `BarFill` (3 px), `MarkerMove`, `MarkerAttackMove` and `MarkerHover` (43 x 23, one canvas, 2 px lines outlined in black at 50%) `Pip` (5 px), `Check` (8 x 5, a check mark in 2 px strokes, a toggle row's mark; DesignSystem.md's Phase 6), and since UiRevamp.md's Phase 1 `SlotCorners` (11 px: an L of 5 px arms, 1 px thick, in each corner, sliced at the arm, a queued slot's brackets) and `Hatch` (a 4 px tile with one diagonal rising to the right, shown tiled, a blocked slot's hatch); the recipe's Slot states settings hold the arm, the line and the hatch spacing; and since Phase 2 `BarTick` (1 x 3) and `Notch` (a 5 px right triangle filling its top row and right column), shown whole, from the Health and statuses settings. `UiSkinWriter.Generate(recipe)` writes each PNG into `Assets/Sprites/UI/Skin` when its bytes changed and sets it as a single sprite with a centred pivot, a full-rect mesh and the piece's border, which `PixelArtTextureDefaults` imports at 32 PPU with point filtering; an unchanged sprite is not reimported. Its inspector previews every piece and has Generate. |
| [UiThemeUsage](../Assets/Editor/UI/UiThemeUsage.cs) | Static: `Collect()` reads every `ThemedText` in the prefabs under `Assets/Prefabs` and in `MainMenu.unity` and `Gameplay.unity` (opened beside the open scenes and closed again when they were not open); `Measure(theme, uses)` gives the contrast of each pair of text and background roles at the smallest size it is used at, a translucent background counted over white and over black (the lower), a text on `ControlNormal` read in the normal, highlighted, selected and disabled states (a placeholder not in the last), against `Threshold` (4.5:1 below Title, 3:1 at Title and above and for disabled text); `CollectFills()` reads every `ThemedGraphic` that names a track in the same prefabs and scenes, and `MeasureFills(theme, uses)` gives each pair of fill and track roles once against `FillContrast` (3:1, a large shape that carries meaning), the fill drawn over the track and a translucent track over white and over black (`FillRatio`, the lower; UiRevamp.md's Phase 1); `BodyHeight(style)`, the top of a d to the bottom of a p in UI px. Used by `DesignTables` and `UiThemeTests`. |
| [DesignSearches](../Assets/Editor/Design/DesignSearches.cs) | Static (DesignSystem.md's Phase 2): the tuning views, `Tools/TopDownRPG/Design/Tune/` Weapons, Abilities, Statuses and Mob Configs. Each opens the Search window (`SearchService.ShowWindow`) on `ViewState(kind)`: the asset provider's query `t=<Type>` (`QueryFor`; `t:` would also find prefabs holding a component whose name starts with it) in the table view, with `Table(context, kind)`: the name column, then a column per serialized field of the assets found but the script (`SearchColumn.Enumerate`), editable in place. No saved search assets: `SearchQueryAsset` is internal in Unity 6.4. |
| [PixelArtTextureDefaults](../Assets/Editor/PixelArtTextureDefaults.cs) | `AssetPostprocessor` for `Assets/Sprites/`: sprite, 32 PPU, point filter, no mips, uncompressed RGBA32, each platform override's maximum size at least the default one's (so a sheet the writer raised past 2048 px keeps its size); menu `Tools/Textures/Apply Pixel Art Defaults To Sprites`. |
| [PixelArtEditorSnapSettings](../Assets/Editor/PixelArtEditorSnapSettings.cs) | Sets editor move snap to 1/32; menu `Tools/Pixel Art/Apply Move Snap (1/32)`. |
| [TerrainBlendRendererEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRendererEditor.cs) | Inspector of `TerrainBlendRenderer`: its settings, with each layer's navigation priority kept equal to its index, the draw order. Whenever the inspector changes the renderer (reordering, adding or removing layers), it sets the priority of each layer's navigation source (`TerrainBlendRenderer.NavigationSourceOf`) in the same undo step (`SyncNavigationPriorities`); when they disagree anyway (`HasNavigationPrioritiesInStep`), an error box and Set Navigation Priorities From Draw Order fix them. |
| [NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor.cs) | Inspector of `NavigationTerrainSource2D`: its settings, with the priority read-only and a note when a `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws its tilemap as a ground layer (`TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing`), since that layer's place sets it. |
| [TerrainMaterialRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialRecipe.cs) and its kinds | Editor-only ScriptableObjects that generate a `TerrainMaterial`'s textures, like `AreaGenerator` generates an area. The abstract recipe holds the material it fills, a seed, the repeat in cells (32 px each, up to 16) and the waves of the smooth height (normalised into 0-1, and scaled down when the red channel's slope could reach 0.1 per pixel); `Generate(seed)` draws every random value from one `System.Random` seeded with it and returns a [TerrainTextures](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTextures.cs): plain pixel arrays, rows from the bottom (y up), with the base colour and cell mode, touching no asset. Waves count whole periods across the texture, so everything repeats with it. Kinds (each a material's look; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 1b and Phase 5 notes): [SandRipplesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/SandRipplesRecipe.cs) (sand colour, tint waves and grit; ripples, whole across the texture along x and y, bent by wobble waves and shown where cover waves rise above a level, each a shaded trough under a lit crest of a band width; specks on a jittered grid, light or dark; clumps, none by default: low bumps on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance, an ellipse of a height between two values and a stretch times as wide, its middle in the middle colour and its outer half lit where it faces the light and shaded where it faces away, kept apart; sparkles, none by default: pixels off the clumps' shade on a third grid, each with a sparkle phase, the sparkle colour handed to the material; the Snow material is this recipe with both), [MudPuddlesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/MudPuddlesRecipe.cs) (mud colour, tint waves and grit; puddles where puddle waves (many short ones, set for 16 cells) rise above a level, in wetter mud above a lower level, both levels lowered and the mud darkened by cluster waves scaled by a cluster amount and shade, their pool, bank shade on the top-left edge, wet rim on the bottom-right one and two-pixel reflections; ridges on a jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a length between the shortest and longest, a lit row over a shaded row a pixel to its right; lumps two pixels square on another, lit from the top left; ridges and lumps off the puddles and never touching another mark; cracks, none by default, on a third jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a length between the fewest and most pixels, each pixel a step from the last that turns an eighth of a turn left or right at a chance, in a crack colour with a lit lip on the pixel below and right of each crack pixel, also off the puddles and clear of other marks; the Dirt material is this recipe with no puddles or wet patches), [WeedsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/WeedsRecipe.cs) (the grass recipe's textures repeated over its own repeat, or the grass colour with grit without one; `Recipe_Grass`, `Recipe_DryGrass`, `Recipe_Meadow` and `Recipe_TallGrass` are this kind: weed clumps over `Recipe_GrassGround`, dry stalks over `Recipe_DryGrassGround`, weeds and flowers over `Recipe_GrassGround` and tall blades over `Recipe_TallGrassGround`, grass recipes of hatched blades that write no material; weed spots on a jittered grid kept at a chance plus density waves, each a clump of blades with feet, heights, curves and seed heads, in stem, two leaf colours and tip over a shadow; flowers, none by default (their random draws are made only with a flower chance): at the flower chance plus the density waves times the flower drift a kept spot is a flower cluster instead, stems between the fewest and most, of a stem height, spread and curve, each topped by one flower head glyph ('o' petal, 'c' heart) in one petal and heart colour, picked by slow colour waves whose middle half is cut into even bands (or at the mix chance any colour), so clusters of one colour gather in drifts, a cluster's sway weight reaching its top at its lowest head so every head leans whole; every pixel of a weed's box, sway margin included, given its row's weight in the height's blue channel and its row from the shadow row under the base, plus one, in the green, boxes inside one cell column and a pixel apart; the cell field is the ground without the weeds, its alpha a box's height in rows above its base, 255 outside the boxes), [WaterHighlightsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/WaterHighlightsRecipe.cs) (pond and lake water, and the ice made with it: the water, darker and lighter tones, cut from tone waves at the darker and lighter shares; for shallow water, none by default, a bed tone where bed waves are highest, at the bed share, under the strokes, and pebbles on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance, on plain water off the strokes and sparkles; the Shallows material is this recipe with both; stroke spots per world cell on a jittered grid, each kept at a chance plus density waves scaled by a density amount, with a length between the shortest and longest, a glint phase, and at a chance a companion half as long a row above, shifted a third of its length to one side and glinting a twentieth of a period after it; a stroke left out when it would touch another, bowed up a pixel in its middle from a length on (off by default), in the resting colour, its glint phases falling by the spread from its middle to its ends; sparkle spots off the strokes at a chance, each with a sparkle phase; a slant, 0 by default, that has each stroke climb that many rows per pixel, rounded (1 for the Ice material's diagonal glare); shines, none by default: shine spots on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a half-width between two values, a squat ellipse the shine squash times as tall in the edge colour round a core in the shine colour, the pixels within half of it from a point a quarter of the way towards the light whose four neighbours are in the shine, left out where it would lie on a stroke or beside one, on a sparkle or on or beside another shine, as the Ice material's are; the highlight and sparkle colours handed to the material as its glint and sparkle colours; with no strokes it writes only sparkle phases, as snow's), [OceanWavesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/OceanWavesRecipe.cs) (the Ocean material's: dark water in three flat tones cut from a tone field at shares, as the water highlights recipe's; crest patches in families, each with a smooth mask, a pixel in the patches of the family whose mask is highest and above the crest level (below the crest level plus the fade, only at a chance that grows with the mask, so a patch's ends break up); a few sparkles outside the patches; the crests' and the sparkles' colours handed to the material. The crests' glint phases, which the material no longer lights (its glint duty is 0), and the settings that shaped only them (bend, arch, crests across) were dropped in KnownGaps.md's Phase 6, the bend's random draws still made (`BendDraws`) so the height and sparkles stay as they were: the ocean's height lost only its blue channel's glint phases, and renders of the Overworld's chunk (4, 0) came out the same, pixel for pixel, in both looks at two times), [LavaBubblesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/LavaBubblesRecipe.cs) (the Lava material's: a surface in a red and a darker red where a slow tint noise is highest, at the darker share; hot patches where a patch noise roughened by a finer one is highest, at the hot share, with hotter cores at the core share, each connected patch (wrapping) in its own orange, its hue and brightness moved by up to the hue and value ranges; dark specks where a speck noise is highest, on the cooler half of the surface only; the noises are tileable value noise with no direction; bubbles per cell, each a disc of a radius between two values placed a pixel clear of the others, its pixels' glint phases falling behind by the swell delay per pixel from its middle, so it swells from a point and opens into a ring as its middle goes out first, the outermost pixel of a bubble of two pixels' radius or more a sparkle phase on the same timing, its dark rim; the bubble and rim colours handed to the material as its glint and sparkle colours, which needs the material's sparkle period and duty to match its glint's), [PlankBoardsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/PlankBoardsRecipe.cs) (planks: rows of boards along x, boards per cell (a gap row under each), joists per cell (nail heads), butt joints on the cell borders at lengths of whole cells between the shortest and longest, each kept off the two rows below's where a redraw allows; a palette colour and tint per board; a bevel lighter along the top row and left end and darker along the bottom row and right end; grain streaks per board per cell, dark at a chance, else light; knots at a chance; a nail on the middle row over each joist at a chance; the cell field points each pixel straight across its board at its middle row in the same column, alpha its distance to the board's edge, the gap colour the base; the Planks material), [GrassTuftsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/GrassTuftsRecipe.cs) (grass colour, tint waves and grit; tuft spots on a jittered grid, their chance plus density waves, blade and tip colours and the tuft glyphs, top row drawn highest; colour patches, none by default: slow waves that shift the ground, blades and tips together towards a low and a high colour by up to an amount, so the hue varies as well as the lightness, as the DryGrass material's straw does), [GravelChipsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/GravelChipsRecipe.cs) (dirt colour, tint waves and grit; chip spots, chance and density waves, the light, grey and dark chip colours with the dark chance, colour jitter, radius and stretch ranges, chip grit, bevel and where it starts, the one-pixel shadow; overlapping chips give a pixel to the chip it lies deepest in), [StoneCobblesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/StoneCobblesRecipe.cs) (mortar colour, width and grit; stones per cell and jitter, a seed offset in pixels (0 by default; with no jitter, half a pixel puts the cell edges on pixel centres for one-pixel mortar, as the Plaza material's square slabs have), the palette, bevel and its width, the rounded look, the shaded side, which it hands its material as its relief, writing in the height's alpha which way each stone pixel's bevel faces from its seed (`TerrainArt.FacingByte`: 1 plus the direction in 255 steps of a turn, 0 on the mortar; Lighting.md's Phase 11); each pixel's nearest seed and exact distance to its stone's cell edge, found by searching the seed grid ring by ring around the pixel until no farther ring can hold a nearer seed or edge, so a pixel's cost does not grow with the number of stones: about 30 ms at 128 px, 110 ms at 256 px). A chip's or stone's centre pixel is its pixel nearest its seed. Shared maths in [TerrainArt](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainArt.cs): periodic noise, tileable value noise (`PeriodicValueNoise`, a lattice read with a cubic B-spline), the value below which a share of a field lies (`Level`), jittered seeds, the per-pixel hash, shading with halves rounded up, wrapping, light from the top left as in the shader, waves' total amplitude, the height texture's pixels (with optional glint and sparkle phases, `GlintByte` and `SparkleByte`), and cracks (`CrackSteps`, `CrackDirections` drawing a crack's length and turns, `CrackPixels` its groove and its lip below and right), the mud recipe's. Ported from `tools/terrain-art/materials.py`, which is gone: same parameters and look, other random draws. |
| [TerrainMaterialWriter](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialWriter.cs) | Static. `Generate(recipe)` writes the recipe's textures for its seed into the material's folder under `Assets/Textures/Terrain`, at `TargetPaths`: for each texture the PNG the material already uses there (so a renamed material keeps its files and GUIDs), else `<material name in lower case>_albedo.png`, `_height.png` and, with chips, stones or clumps, `_field.png` (`PathsIn`) in `FolderOf(material)`: the folder of the material's textures already there, so they stay together and a renamed material keeps its folder, else a new folder named after the material, which `EnsureFolder` creates (the tile writer uses it too). It refuses, with an error and without writing, a path that is another material's texture (`MaterialUsingTexture`, which searches every `TerrainMaterial` asset) or one file for two textures. `WriteFiles` encodes every PNG before touching a file and writes all of them or none: when one write fails, the files already written get their previous bytes back (new ones are deleted) and the error is rethrown. Then the files are reimported so `TerrainTextureDefaults` applies, and `Apply` points the material at them and writes the base colour, repeat and cell mode they were made for through its serialized data, without undo, and calls its `NotifyChanged`, so the renderers drawing it rebuild even when nothing on it changed; the material is saved. Last, `TerrainTileWriter.RefreshSwatch` rewrites the swatch of the material's ground tile when it has one. `EncodePng` gives the bytes, of a square or of a width and height (the character sheets'); the same seed writes byte-identical files. `Generate(recipe, textures)` writes textures already generated for the recipe's seed (the inspector's preview) the same way. |
| [TerrainTileWriter](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTileWriter.cs) | Static. A ground material's tiles are `TerrainTile`s keeping a reference to it, found by it: `TileOf(material, facing)` is the first `TerrainTile` of the project, by path, of that material and facing, wherever it lies and however it is named, and `MaterialOf(tile)` the material a `TerrainTile` keeps (null for any other tile). `Write(material)` writes a ground material's swatches from its albedo PNG, then its tiles (`FacingsOf`: automatic, and for a material that turns to its runs east to west and north to south): each tile found by reference is pointed at its swatch, rewritten where its sprite is, without a collider; a missing one is created at `TilePath` (`Assets/Tiles/Ground/Tile_<material name>.asset`, then `_EastWest` or `_NorthSouth` for a fixed facing) with its swatch at `SwatchPath` (`Assets/Sprites/Terrain/<texture base name>[_eastwest or _northsouth]_swatch.png`, imported by `PixelArtTextureDefaults` and made a single sprite), and a plain `Tile` already at its path is turned into a `TerrainTile` in place (`Convert`, the script field set as the inspector sets it, the GUID kept, so scenes and palettes keep it). A swatch is `SwatchPixels(size, albedo, facing)`: the albedo's bottom-left 32 px cell, for north to south that cell turned a quarter as the shader turns it, and for a fixed facing a two-headed arrow along the boards drawn over it (`DrawArrow`, light and outlined). A material that turns to its runs then has its palette (`PalettePath`, `Assets/Tiles/Palettes/<material name>_Palette.prefab`) hold its fixed tiles in the cells right of its automatic one, saved only when a cell changed. It refuses, with an error, a material outside `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials` (new tiles and swatches are named after materials, whose names are unique only there), a material without a square albedo PNG of at least a cell, and a missing tile's path holding anything but a tile. `RefreshSwatch(material, textures)` rewrites the swatches of a material that has a tile and points its tiles at them again (the material writer's Generate calls it). A swatch whose file already holds the same bytes is neither rewritten nor reimported. Menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Write Ground Tiles` writes the tiles of every material in the folder. A material renamed keeps its tile and swatch (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
| [TerrainBrush](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBrush.cs) | The Tile Palette's default brush, `[CustomGridBrush(true, true, true, "Terrain Brush")]`, which also hides Unity's default brush. A `DualGridBrush`: on a tilemap with a `DualGridTilemapModule` (the walls) it writes the module's data tile into every cell of a stroke (flood fill included), and on a plain tilemap (the ground layers) the palette's tile, as the grid brush does. What it adds is its editor's preview on ground layers and, on a chunk of a world, strokes across chunk borders: its `BoxFill`, `BoxErase` (so paint and erase, which the grid brush runs through them) and `FloodFill` hand a target under a `WorldChunk` to `WorldBrushStroke`, and leave any other target (a standalone area) to the Dual Grid Brush as before, but for a flood fill of a plain ground layer or raised ground there, which it fills by itself (`FloodCells`: the start cell and every cell joined to it, 4-connected, holding the same tile, within the tilemap's cell bounds with the start cell taken in, as Unity's flood fill goes), so its editor's preview shows exactly what it fills, and for walls box filled there, under which it then paints the ground beside them (`WallGround.FillUnder`; KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). The Dual Grid Brush stays in the brush list. |
| [TerrainBrushEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBrushEditor.cs) | Editor of `TerrainBrush`, a `DualGridBrushEditor`. A ground layer here is a target tilemap that the renderer `TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing` finds is drawing right now (`IsDrawing`). On one, `PaintPreview`, `ErasePreview` and `BoxFillPreview` replace the last preview with the stroke's cells, filled or empty, through the renderer's `SetPreview`, never touching the tilemap. On a ground layer of a chunk the preview shows on the renderer of each chunk it covers, the target's and its linked neighbours' (`ShowOnChunk`), on their layer of the same material, each given the cells within its own cells and skirt so the preview blends across the border; a chunk that lacks the layer shows none until the stroke adds it. A paint with the Paint tool or a box shows empty while shift is held, since the click then erases (`PaintPreview(…, erasing)`, `BoxPreview`). Pressing or releasing shift redoes a hovered paint or box preview, which Unity's refresh would not (`FollowShift`). Only the cells the click writes are shown: z = 0, and on a plain layer when filling only the cells whose brush cell holds a tile. A flood fill shows the cells it would fill (`TryShowFloodPreview`: `WorldBrushStroke.FloodCells` on a chunk, `TerrainBrush.FloodCells` elsewhere, the cells the brush then fills). On raised ground (a tilemap a renderer reads as its raised ground, `IsDrawingRaised`) a paint, an erase, a box or a flood fill previews through the renderer's elevation map (`SetRaisedPreview`), each cell raised as the brush's tile there would raise it, or level for an erase, on the chunk's renderer and its linked neighbours' on a seamless world. On the walls of a chunk the paint, erase, box and flood fill previews use the walls of every open linked chunk the stroke covers (`ShowWalls`): each cell's editor preview tile on the data tilemap of the chunk owning it, each render cell around them on the module drawing it (`OwnsRenderTile`), whose rule reads the preview tiles of both chunks, so the corners along a border come out right (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6).
