The previews (ground, raised and walls, `ClearAllPreviews`) clear on commit (`RegisterUndo`), on stroke end (`FollowStroke`: the mouse released), pointer leave, tool deactivation, undo or redo, brush or paint target change (`GridPaintingState` events), scene closing, before an assembly reload, and when the editor is disabled. During `OnPaintSceneGUI` a clear waits for its end and is dropped when a new preview replaced the old one first, so a hover refresh uploads once and never shrinks a grown map in between. A clear that happens, or a preview that added chunks, has the Scene view repaint. A hover update allocates nothing. On every other tilemap, including the layers of a renderer that is off or has not built, it is the Dual Grid Brush's editor. In the Tile Palette (`OnPaintInspectorGUI`) it warns when a picked tile is a ground material's (`TerrainTileWriter.MaterialOf`) that the target layer is not drawn with (`PaletteProblem`): it names the area's layer for that material, or, when the area has none (`MissingMaterial`), says so and shows a button, "Add the <material> layer", which adds it (`AddMissingLayer`: `GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer`, one undo step, then on a chunk `WorldChunk.RefreshLinks`, so its neighbours' skirts read the new layer) and makes it the paint target, since each area lists only the layers it uses and Unity's palette list cannot be filtered (TerrainLayers32.md's Phase 4). See TerrainBlending.md's Phase 3 notes and TerrainTypes.md's Phase 2 notes. |
| [TerrainMaterialRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialRecipeEditor.cs) | Inspector of every recipe: its settings; previews of the albedo, the cell field (each chip or stone in its own hue, brighter where its alpha is larger, its centre pixel white, texels of no chip dark) and the height's red channel (glint pixels in a hue by their phase, sparkle pixels white), regenerated after every change and after an undo or redo that changed the recipe (its serialized settings are compared, so undos elsewhere cost nothing); a line with the size, base colour and cell mode; and Generate, disabled without a material, which runs `TerrainMaterialWriter.Generate` once the inspector is drawn and names the files it writes. Beside it Auto Generate (`AutoGenerate`, an editor preference, off by default; turning it on generates once): the textures are written `AutoGenerateDelay` (0.4 s) after the last change or undo, each change pushing it back, from the preview when it matches the current settings (else rebuilt first), so the ground in the open scenes follows the settings while they are tuned; one still waiting when the inspector closes is written right after. `ScheduleAutoGenerate`, `IsAutoGenerateScheduled` and `FollowAutoGenerate(now)` are internal hooks. |
| [TerrainSceneViewAnimation](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainSceneViewAnimation.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside play mode, while an enabled `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws an animated material, repaints every Scene view at twice the fastest one's frames per second (`FramesPerSecond(renderers)`, from their `AnimationFramesPerSecond`), whichever window has focus, so water keeps moving there; otherwise it looks twice a second. Menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Animate In Scene View` turns it off and on (an editor preference, on by default). Unity throttles editor updates while it is in the background, which slows it there. |
| [TilePaletteFollower](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TilePaletteFollower.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: when the Tile Palette's paint target changes (`GridPaintingState.scenePaintTargetChanged`), selects the palette holding a tile the target paints: a dual-grid layer's data tile, then the tile of the material a `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws the layer with (`TerrainTileWriter.TilePath`, so an empty layer finds its palette), then the tiles painted on it; with none it leaves the palette alone. `PaletteFor(target, palettes)` is the choice; it works with any set of layers an area lists. Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Match Palette To Tilemap turns it off (an editor preference, on by default). |
| [TerrainTextureDefaults](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTextureDefaults.cs) | `AssetPostprocessor` for `Assets/Textures/Terrain/` and its material folders (the files `TerrainMaterialWriter` writes): default texture type, sRGB off, point filtering, repeat wrapping, no mipmaps or NPOT scaling, uncompressed RGBA32, so `TerrainBlendRenderer` can copy the textures into its arrays as they are. It also gives a chunk's baked coast and glow maps (`WorldAuthoring.IsCoastMapPath`, `IsGlowMapPath`) their import settings (`ApplyCoastImport`, `ApplyGlowImport`) as they first import, so a new map imports once, not first as a compressed sprite (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6). |
| [TerrainBlendProbe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendProbe.cs) | Editor checks for a `TerrainBlendRenderer`, ported from the Phase 0 spike's probe. `Render` draws a world rectangle through a temporary camera (`RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest`) at whole screen pixels per art pixel, with shaders compiled synchronously (`ShaderUtil.allowAsyncCompilation` off; every material it draws and the terrain's compiled first, unlit and with the global light keyword `USE_SHAPE_LIGHT_TYPE_0`, which `CompilePass` alone leaves out) and props, or every renderer in the open scenes but the chunks (other areas' chunks included, found with `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll`), hidden through `Renderer.forceRenderingOff`; it clears the dirty mark that leaves on each renderer, so it leaves the scene as it found it. `Capture` writes a PNG; `CheckBlobs` renders the debug view over the control map, where its grid places it, at a fixed time, and lists per layer (named after its material) detached blobs under a size and holes, telling up to 32 layers apart by `DebugClass` (each channel read as the nearest quarter of the brightest one and looked up in `DebugColours`, the HLSL's `TB_DEBUG_COLOURS` in quarters; a colour that is no layer's counts as "unknown colour"), or says that nothing is built or that nothing was drawn yet (menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Check Blobs In Open Areas`, which checks every renderer in both looks); `MeasureShimmer` and `MeasureCost` repeat Phase 0's measurements; `MeasureRebuildAndModeSwitch` times a full rebuild and a switch of the look. `BuildStressScene(layers)` builds the scene those measurements share into the active scene, so every session measures the same ground ([TerrainLayers32.md](TerrainLayers32.md)'s Decisions), and returns its renderer: a white Global Light 2D at 0.85, as in the areas, and a grid at the origin whose renderer draws that many layers of `TerrainStyle.asset` in the crisp look, grass over 72 x 48 cells and above it the style's later materials in turn, starting over after the last, each painted as four discs of radius 2 to 5 cells inside the grass at spots drawn from a `System.Random` seeded with the layer's index, so a scene of more layers is one of fewer with more on top (16 layers reach the ocean; planks, a bridge, fall at layer 17); the caller opens an empty scene first and saves nothing. `InMode(renderer, mode, check)` runs any of them in a given look and puts the renderer's mode back afterwards, through the `Mode` property, so checks leave no scene diff. Chunks created or rebuilt in the current editor update are not drawn until the next one, so render in a later call after (re)building. |
| [NavigationGridEditModeRefresh](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/NavigationGridEditModeRefresh.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside Play Mode, forwards tile changes to every built `NavigationGrid2D` (`HandleTilemapPositionsChanged`), so painting updates the walkable-cell gizmo and `MovementBlockerGizmo` cell by cell (a raised cell on a chunk's border reaches the page across it when both are in one map). The grid also rebuilds before its next gizmo draw after an inspector change to it or one of its terrain sources (`OnValidate` -> `MarkGizmoGridStale`). |
| [MovementBlockerGizmo](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/MovementBlockerGizmo.cs) | Static `[DrawGizmo]` drawer for every enabled `NavigationGrid2D`, selected or not, in edit and play mode (the Scene view, and the Game view with gizmos on): what keeps players off each cell of the grid's walkable bounds (a chunk's cells on a chunk's grid, a page per chunk), under the movement profile of the project's `PlayerControlSettings` (the first found; mobs use the same profile today). Blocked cells are filled by `NavigationGrid2D.Blocker`: terrain the profile cannot walk (water, ocean, lava) magenta, walls red, blocking props orange, cells the cliff face of raised ground covers whole yellow, no ground grey; a white line runs along every side where a walkable cell meets a blocked one, the cell beyond the bounds read from the grid's navigation (nothing outside an area or in a chunk not loaded, the neighbouring chunk's page when it is, so no line runs along a border the ground crosses: in play mode through the world's navigation, outside it through `EditModeWorldNavigation`), and a yellow one along the cliff of raised ground (`GetCliffSides`), which blocks steps rather than cells. Cells outside the bounds are not drawn (nothing there is walkable). `BlockerMap` caches each grid's fill as runs of neighbouring cells along a row with the same blocker (`BlockedRun`) plus the edge and cliff lines (`LineList`s, `Edges` and `Cliffs`), rebuilt when the grid's `Version` or the profile (or its `Version`) changes: on the Coast 62 runs, 220 edge lines and 40 cliff lines, built in about 4 ms. Toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Gizmos/Movement Blockers` (EditorPrefs, on by default). |
| [PropGridSnapping](../Assets/Editor/World/PropGridSnapping.cs) | Static rule for where a prop's root goes on a `GridLayout`. A blocking prop's footprint (the solid `BoxCollider2D` on its root, `TryGetFootprint`) is centred on the fewest whole cells that hold it (`CellsSpanned`, allowing for `NavigationGrid2D`'s edge inset), on the run nearest to where it is now, and stands on the bottom edge of the cell its bottom falls in; snapping a snapped position changes nothing. A walk-through prop rounds to the 1/32 pixel grid. `BlockedCells` gives the cells the navigation grid will block, with the same inset rule. `RootOnCells` places a footprint on the cells starting at a given bottom-left cell (what `SnapBlocking` does once it has picked them), and `CellsCovered` gives how many columns and rows a snapped footprint blocks; the scene generator uses both. |
| [PropPlacementWindow](../Assets/Editor/World/PropPlacementWindow.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/Props/Prop Placement`: a palette of the prefabs in `Assets/Prefabs/Props/<category>` (the generated props; the two base prefabs at its top are left out) with sprite thumbnails, a search field and a category filter. While one is picked, the Scene view keeps its clicks: a translucent preview follows the pointer at the snapped position, the cells it would block are drawn green, or red when a collider on the grid's obstacle layers (another prop, a wall) is already there, and a left click places it under the `Props` root object (created when missing) of the scene it goes in as one undo step. A red spot refuses the click. Escape or Stop ends placing. With world chunks open, a prop goes in the chunk whose cells hold the pointer (`OwningChunk`), and a footprint crossing that chunk's border, or a walk-through prop rooted past it, is red too (`FitsChunk`, the validation's rule); otherwise it targets the scene holding a `NavigationGrid2D` (the active scene first). It has the grid's gizmos rebuild after each change. While the selection holds props world generation wrote (under a grid's `GeneratedProps`, `GeneratedPropsIn`), a note says that moving or deleting them is hand work, which the next write refuses unless the chunk is locked or the write forced; props placed with the window are kept by the writer. `Tools/TopDownRPG/Props/Snap Selected Props To Grid`, also a button in the window, snaps the selected prop instances (or the props a selected child belongs to) with undo. |

waystones cyan and gates yellow crosses; each chunk's layer count by its top-left corner, `CountOrigin`, black, red past 16 and blue when allowed wide) and `WorldGeneratorWindow` (`Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Generator`, a thin window over the session: the asset picker, Recompose and the world; its `AdventurerCarryPlace`, a hip or the back; the side, place and angles; a scabbard, a sling or a quiver and its place; how much further out it sits over a cape), drawn under each cape; the head, its hair's size and face tilt and its ears; the face, the eyes' place, height, width and glint, the brows' gap, width and tilt, the mouth with its width and curve, and the blush; and its palette; drawn by [AdventurerPainter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AdventurerPainter.cs), which paints a face feature only where it faces the camera, raises each lock's tip by the pose's `HairLift` times its length, and draws the pose's expressions: `HeadTilt` tips the face back, `EyesClosed` over 0.5 leaves each eye its lowest row without its glint, and `MouthOpen` opens the mouth that many rows below its line, in the brows' colour; it draws one layer at a time (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6), every layer taking the same drawing steps in the same order and drawing only its own: per leg the legs' then the boots', per side the top's then the belt, the buckle, per arm the sleeves then the hand (the hands' layer since Phase 7), the bag's strap and its pouch behind the right hip, the cape, the collar, the weapon, then the ears, the hair's ball (cleared inside the face's hairline), the bare head and face, the locks (but those whose roots the headwear covers) and the headwear, the cast's light, and last the weapon's gear (`DrawCarried`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7): in a clip without a weapon the weapon stowed, the sword's hilt over its scabbard at the left hip, the greatsword and the longbow on the back with a sling or a quiver, further out over a cape; in its own clips only what stays on; in another weapon's nothing)), the motions ([CharacterMotion](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterMotion.cs), abstract: a pose per frame, a frame's duration at natural speed, the gait it shows (`Gait`, a runtime `CharacterGait`, `None` by default), whether it is an idle animation (`IsIdleAnimation`, false by default) and its ground speed (how fast a planted foot moves back, in rig units per second, 0 by default), smears and effects, `GaitFrames` (how many gait frames it is drawn over, 0 by default), the colours of a cast's `Light` and `SmearPoseAt` (the pose a smear samples on a frame); [GaitMotion](../Assets/Editor/Characters/GaitMotion.cs): one parametric stride, each foot sliding back while planted and swinging forward along a cubic curve, the pelvis's bob, the lean, arm swing and pump, the shoulders' twist and the cape's flow, strides scaled by the recipe's legs and swings by its arms, frame 0 with the left foot planted in front; its style, all off by default ([PlayerCharacter.md](PlayerCharacter.md)'s Phase 2): `bounce` (the bob from a smooth wave to a bounce, round at its top and sharp at its bottom), `bobOffset` (the bob turning later than mid-stance, so its top and bottom land on frames), `headFollow` and `headLag`, `hairFollow` and `hairLag` (the head and the hair's tips following the bob late), `stretch` (the torso longer at the bob's top, shorter at its bottom) and `armArc` (the hands rising at both ends of their swing); `PoseAtPhase` the pose anywhere in the cycle; its gait (walk, run or sprint) and its ground speed, the stride over the time a foot is planted; [IdleMotion](../Assets/Editor/Characters/IdleMotion.cs): a breathing loop on the standing pose, the pelvis sinking and rising once, a slight lean and hand sway, the cape rippling a whole number of times, both feet planted, the left a little in front, gait `Idle`; one frame holds the standing pose still; [KeyedMotion](../Assets/Editor/Characters/KeyedMotion.cs): `MotionKey` poses (the adventurer's face's expressions among them: the chin tipped back, the eyes shut, the mouth open, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3) eased into each other (`Ease`: in-out, out, in, linear, step) and sampled at the frames' times, the contact frame, smears (`MotionSmear`, sampled `SmearSamples` times over a span of motion time) and effects (`MotionEffect`: dust, an arrow's release, and a cast's light at the hands it names (`CastHands`: right, left, both, between): a gathering glow sized by the share of the gather, a star on the release, a broken ring and sparks after it, in its `Light` colours), `idleAnimation` (an idle animation: no contact frame, written into its clip as `IsIdleAnimation`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 3), its style, off by default (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 4): `headFollow` and `headLag`, `hairFollow` and `hairLag` (the head and the hair's tips following the head's own rise and fall late) and each key's `stretch` (the torso longer, or shorter when negative); a key's `spin` turns the whole character, weapon included, from the way it faces, in degrees counterclockwise seen from above (`CharacterPose.Spin`), and `PosedOn`, the body its keys were posed on: baked on a body of other sizes, `FitToBody` fits each pose to it (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 1: the pelvis and feet scaled by the legs' length over the ankles and from the hips, the hands kept where they were from the shoulders, scaled by the arms' length; a body of the same sizes, or none named, keeps the pose); [OverGaitMotion](../Assets/Editor/Characters/OverGaitMotion.cs): a keyed motion drawn over a gait's frames (Animation.md's Phase 5), frame g * K + f its frame f over gait frame g, the gait's feet, pelvis and cape and the keyed motion's twist, arms and weapon (its hands kept relative to the shoulders, its lean plus a share of the gait's, the hips countering the gait's shoulders; the keyed motion's spin, and its stretch and head's and hair's lift added to the gait's, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 4); its frame durations, release frame, smears, effects and light are the keyed motion's, smears sampled over the frame's gait frame), [CharacterWeaponArt](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterWeaponArt.cs) (a blade, one- or two-handed, or a bow: its sizes (a bow's limb thickness at the grip and the tips, `LimbRadius`), its colours and its smears' and dust's, and `Carry`, how it changes a gait's pose, which no clip uses since Animation.md's Phase 3: the holding hand's place and swing, the blade's or bow's direction, the twist kept, the elbows' poles; `TakesLeftHand`, a bow or a two-handed blade), [CharacterLanternArt](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterLanternArt.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject, `Assets/Settings/Characters/Lantern.asset`, named by a recipe's `Lantern`, none for a character without one, Lighting.md's Phase 9: the carried lantern's sizes times `Size` (the bail, the roof and knob, the glass, the base, the corner bars, the wick), how far below the hand it hangs and the hip hook it hangs from while a weapon takes the left hand, its lean there, its swing with a gait's stride (`CharacterPose.LanternSwing`, which `GaitMotion` sets and `OverGaitMotion` takes from the legs), and its iron, brass, glass and wick colours (`Ramp`)), [CharacterRig](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterRig.cs) (`Solve` turns a [CharacterPose](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterPose.cs) into `CharacterJoints`: the spine's lean and twist, the torso stretched or squashed by the pose's `Stretch`, the head raised by its `HeadLift`, the hips following the keys or turning a third against the shoulders, legs and arms as two-bone chains (`Reach`), the left hand on a two-handed grip; `StandingPose`), [CharacterCanvas](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterCanvas.cs) (the shared `ArtCanvas`, see Art/, in rig units, its material and part ids the characters' `CharacterMaterial` and `CharacterPart`, with typed overloads of the drawing calls and accessors (`MaterialAt`, `PartAt`, `SetMaterial`, `Count`) and `Shade(ramps, light, shadow, gap)`, which gives the canvas a table built from the ramps and `CharacterMaterials`; nothing is hidden under the ground, so every frame is byte for byte what it was before the canvas was shared; `CharacterMaterials` says which materials are flat, outlined, the weapon's, a cast's light (`LightRim`, `LightGlow`, `LightCore`, flat in the motion's light colours) or the lantern's (`LanternIron`, `LanternBrass`, and `LanternGlass` and `LanternWick`, flat, unlined and glowing, `Glows`), in parts of its own; `MarkLantern` records where the lantern's light shines from (`LanternCentre`, `HasLantern`); the Weasel's are `Fur`, `FurLight`, `FurDark`, `Nose`, `EarInside` (flat), `Wraps` and `WrapsBand`, its band and eyes the rogue's `Sash` and `Eye` in its own colours; the adventurer's are `Skin` and `Hair` (shaded and outlined), `Brow`, `Mouth`, `Blush`, `EyeGlint` and `MouthInside` (the inside of an open mouth, which the brows' material drew until PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5; flat, unlined), `Strap` and `Pouch` (the satchel's, drawn as `Belt` until then), `Headwear` (the hood and the cap, in a part of its own, Phase 6), with the rogue's `Eye`, `Jerkin` (its tunic), `Shirt`, `Trousers`, `Boots`, `Belt`, `Fittings` and `Gloves` in its own colours), [CharacterPainter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterPainter.cs) (abstract: a body plan's body below the head, then the weapon with its smear and effects, the head, and last a cast's light, just in front of the hands it names, the weapon and the light each a drawing step of the body's layer (`PaintsShared`); `DrawCarried(pose, joints)`, a last drawing step for what a body plan carries outside its hands, nothing by default (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7); `PaintLantern(lantern, facing, pose, joints)` draws a carried lantern over a painted frame, hanging plumb from the left hand or from the hip hook while the weapon takes that hand, and marks its glass's middle; its kinds draw the body and the head: [RoguePainter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/RoguePainter.cs), the rogue's outfit: legs and boots, torso, belt with its buckle, sash, pouch and dagger, arms, mantle, the cape's strips (an outer layer and a red lining, the hem rising and streaming with the flow), the scarf, and the hood, whose face is laid out by angle so it lines up in every direction; [WeaselPainter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaselPainter.cs), the Weasel: furred legs with wrapped shins and long paws, the tail, the torso and neck with the pale belly and throat, arms with wrapped forearms, and the head with its snout and nose, ears and the band's knot and tails, its band, chin and eyes laid out by angle like the rogue's face, the eyes only where the head's front shows), [CharacterBaker](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterBaker.cs) (sets the scale so the standing character facing south is the target height, outline included: estimated from its height at one pixel per rig unit, then moved in steps of half a percent while rounding leaves it a pixel off (the rogue's needed none); `Bake(clip)` gives a `CharacterSheet` (each frame drawn facing its direction turned by its pose's spin, and a smear's samples turned by their own spin against the frame's, so a spin's smear is a ring while the light stays put): a row per direction (`CharacterDirections`, in Gameplay: E, NE, N, NW, W, SW, S, SE, counterclockwise from east, E in the top row), a column per frame, the frames whose drawing reaches their edge, and each frame's cast light (`LightCentres` in pixels from the feet and `LightStrengths`, from `CharacterCanvas.MarkLight`, which the painter calls where it draws a cast's light, at `MotionEffect.LightStrength`: a gathering glow its size, the release's star 1, the ring 0.5, the sparks 0.25); `Shade` colours a frame, a cast's light in its motion's colours and the lantern in its art's; for a recipe with a lantern, `Bake` shades each frame, paints the lantern over it (`DrawLantern`) and shades it again, the pixels that changed the sheet's `LanternPixels` (which of them glow in `LanternGlow`) and each frame's light `LanternCentres`, so the sheet itself stays as without; for a recipe with dyes, each frame's palette map (`PaletteMap(canvas)`: the frame shaded again with each material's four tones as its colours, so every pixel, outline and inner lines included, names the material and tone it was coloured with, turned into the recipe's dye times four plus the tone, 0 for a material without a dye) laid out as the sheet (`CharacterSheet.PaletteMap`) and the dyes it names (`Dyes`; PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5); for a recipe with parts (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6), `Outfit` (the parts picked, the recipe's own until set) and `LayerKeys()` (the body's layer, then every option of every slot, the hair once per headwear: 32 for the adventurer), `BakeLayers(clip, keys)` draws each layer of every frame from one pose on a canvas of its own and keeps its pixels before shading, the depths quantized as a layer file keeps them (`CharacterLayer`), the body's with the material table, a weapon's gear with one of its weapon's colours for each clip without a weapon (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 7), and `Bake` composes the outfit's layers with the runtime's `CharacterComposer`, so the sheet and its palette map for any outfit are what the game composes, the lights taken from the body's layer; `Render` merges the outfit's layers by depth (`ArtCanvas.MergeFrom`); `Render` gives one frame unshaded, `StandingHeight` the standing character's height; about 0.25 s a clip of 64 to 72 frames), [CharacterSheetWriter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterSheetWriter.cs) (`Generate(recipe)` logs and refuses a recipe that cannot bake, bakes every clip before writing, writes `Assets/Sprites/Characters/<name>/<name>_<clip>.png` when its bytes changed, imports it at 32 PPU (`PixelsPerUnit`) and slices it into `<name>_<clip>_<direction>_<frame>` at `CharacterSheet.FrameRect` with a custom pivot on the feet through the sprite editor's data provider, keeping existing slices' IDs and removing slices no longer baked; an unchanged sheet is not reimported, and a sheet larger than its import's maximum size gets the size it needs (`WriteSheet(sheetName, clipName, frameSize, feet, columns, pixels)`, shared with the Aseprite recipe, through `SpriteSheetFiles.WriteSheet`; `SheetSprites` finds a written sheet's slices by direction and frame; the paths and names also take a sheet and a clip name); for a recipe with a lantern, each clip's layer (`WriteLantern`, which also takes a clip's layer and glow as arrays for the Aseprite recipe and returns whether it wrote: `PackLantern` cuts every frame's layer to the box round its pixels into cells of the clip's largest box, the sheet's layout, a slice per frame where the lantern shows pivoted on the feet; written as `<name>_<clip>Lantern.png` with its packed glow `Assets/Textures/Characters/<name>/<name>_<clip>Lantern_Glow.png` (`GlowRoot`, `GlowPath`) as its `_GlowTex`; deleted for a recipe without); for a sheet with a palette map, the map (`WritePaletteMap`, through `SpriteSheetFiles.WritePaletteMap`) as `Assets/Textures/Characters/<name>/<name>_<clip>_Palette.png` (`PaletteMapPath`), the sheet's only secondary texture `_PaletteMap`, deleted and taken off the sheet for a sheet without (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5); for a recipe with parts, its parts (`WriteParts`, PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6): every layer of `LayerKeys()` baked for every clip and written as `<sheets>/Parts/<name>_Body.bytes` and `<name>_<slot>_<option>[_Under<option>].bytes` (`LayerName`), one file for layers alike, none for an option drawing nothing, files no longer written deleted, and `<name>_Parts.asset`, the `CharacterParts` naming them over the set, each rewritten only when it changed; then the runtime `CharacterAnimationSet` `<name>_AnimationSet.asset` beside the sheets (`SetPath`; `BuildSet(recipe, baker, sheets)` without touching an asset: the dyes the sheets' palette maps carry, and per clip the slices by direction and frame, whether it is an idle animation, its light colour and each frame's light (`Lights(sheet)`: the motion's light colours' rim tone, `ColourRamp.Shadow`, and each frame's light in units, none for a motion without light colours or a clip drawing none), the lantern's layer per sprite and its light (`LanternLights(sheet)`: each frame's centre in units at full), its motion's frame durations, gait, contact frame (`CharacterMotion.ContactFrame`: a keyed motion's, -1 for the others), its gait frames (a motion drawn over a gait: its sheet's columns run gait frame by gait frame) and whether a weapon is drawn, and `NaturalSpeed`, the motion's ground speed at the baker's scale over 32, which holds east or west), created, or copied over the existing asset when it differs so its GUID stays (`WriteSet(path, set)`, shared too, through `SpriteSheetFiles.WriteAsset`, returning whether it wrote)) and [CharacterRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterRecipeEditor.cs) (the inspector of every kind of recipe: the settings, a picked clip's sheet and one picked direction playing at the motion's frame durations, baked again when the recipe, the clip's motion or its weapon changes (their serialized settings are compared on each layout), a cast drawn over the run playing over one picked run frame (`Gait frame`, the first by default, its frames `g * K` to `g * K + K - 1`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 7; `PlayingFrame`) rather than every column in a row, the scale and standing height, a warning listing the frames at their edge, and Generate, which writes the sheets and the set). The weapon a character holds is drawn by [WeaponPainter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaponPainter.cs) (a blade with its smear and dust, or its hilt alone along a crossguard axis given (`DrawBladeAcross`, a blade stowed or sheathed), a bow with a nocked arrow and its release, an arrow on its own, in a space a caller's mapping turns into the canvas's world; `CharacterPainter` passes its facing), which also draws a weapon's other sprites (Animation.md's Phase 4): [WeaponSpriteRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaponSpriteRecipe.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject: the weapon art and the character whose shading and scale it uses; the HUD icon's name, size, the angle the weapon points at in the screen's plane, its margin, an optional fixed scale (0 fits the icon), a bow's draw and arrow; for a bow, the arrow's sheet name, direction count, frame size and view, [ArrowView](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaponSpriteRecipe.cs) `ThreeQuarter` (flying level in the game's view, turned so it points at its direction's angle on the screen) or `ScreenPlane`), [WeaponSpriteWriter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaponSpriteWriter.cs) (`BakeIcon` and `BakeArrow` touch no asset; `IconScaleOf` the largest scale inside the margin; `Generate` writes `Assets/Sprites/Weapons/<icon>.png`, one sprite pivoted on its middle, and a bow's `<arrow>.png`, a row of frames sliced into `<arrow>_<direction>` pivoted on the tip at each frame's middle, plus its `DirectionalSprites` `<arrow>_Directions.asset`, keeping slice IDs and the set's GUID, through `WriteDirections(sheetName, frameSize, pivot, count, pixels)` (a thing drawn in directions, one frame each in a row sliced into `<sheet>_<direction>` on the pivot, and its set; shared with the Aseprite projectile recipe; returns the paths it wrote; `SheetPath`, `DirectionSliceName`, `DirectionsPath` by sheet name), and every sheet through [SpriteSheetFiles](../Assets/Editor/SpriteSheetFiles.cs): `WriteSheet(path, width, height, pixels, slices)` writes a generated sheet when its bytes changed, imports it with `CharacterSheetWriter.ApplyImportSettings`, raises its import's maximum size to fit, and gives it exactly the slices asked for, each with its pivot, through the sprite editor's data provider (existing slices keeping their IDs by name, the others removed; a sheet already imported gets its new slices before its new pixels import, so one that lost frames never imports with slices past its edge), reimporting only when anything changed and returning whether it did; `WriteAsset(path, set)` creates the asset beside a sheet or copies a changed one over it, keeping its GUID, and returns whether it wrote; `SpritesByName(path)`; `Slice.PivotOn(pixel, frameSize)`, a pixel's bottom left corner as a share of the frame; `WriteGlow(path, width, height, pixels)` writes a glow beside a sheet when its bytes changed and imports it as a plain point-filtered, uncompressed texture without sprites, a new one first as one clear pixel with those settings, since the project's 2D import cuts a new file into sprites on its first import and its `.meta` would keep every cut sprite's ID; `WriteNormals(path, width, height, pixels)` writes a prop sheet's normals the same way, linear (Lighting.md's Phase 11); `WritePaletteMap(path, width, height, codes)` writes a character sheet's palette map the same way, its bytes as grey levels imported as a single channel, red (R8, linear), so the texture holds them exactly (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 5); and `SetSecondaryTextures(sheet, textures)` gives the sheet exactly those secondary textures (`GlowTextureName`, `_GlowTex`; `NormalTextureName`, `_NormalTex`; `CharacterPaletteMap.MapTextureName`, `_PaletteMap`), for the props' glows, the lantern's and the characters' palette maps. Every generated sheet goes through it: the characters', the effects', the weapon sprites' and those drawn in Aseprite) and [WeaponSpriteRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Characters/WeaponSpriteRecipeEditor.cs) (its inspector: the icon and the arrow's directions baked again when the recipe, the weapon or the character changes, and Generate). |
| [Characters/ (Aseprite)](../Assets/Editor/Characters) | Characters drawn by hand in Aseprite ([AsepriteCharacters.md](AsepriteCharacters.md), Phase 1), copied into sheets and a set exactly as the baker writes them, so the runtime plays them the same, and what the effect and projectile recipes drawn in Aseprite share with them (Phase 3; the effects' are under [Effects/](../Assets/Editor/Effects)). One so far: the Bandit, a stand-in until a character is drawn by hand ([AsepriteRecipe_Bandit](../Assets/Settings/Characters/AsepriteRecipe_Bandit.asset), drawn with the player prefab, its 17 files under `Assets/Art/Characters/Bandit`, the rogue's baked clips recoloured and written by the tests' `AsepriteFileWriter`, its sheets and set under `Assets/Sprites/Characters/Bandit`; AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 2 notes). [AsepriteCharacterRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteCharacterRecipe.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject: the character's name (letters and digits), the prefab drawn with the set (optional) and the clips, each an [AsepriteClip](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteCharacterRecipe.cs): an `.aseprite` file under `ArtRoot` (`Assets/Art/Characters`) named `<character>_<clip>.aseprite`, which names the clip (`ClipName`), the gait (`None` for an attack or a cast), whether it is armed, a walk's, run's or sprint's natural speed (`Moves`) and the feet pixel from the canvas's bottom left; `CanGenerate` refuses a bad name, no clips, a clip without such a file, outside `ArtRoot` or listed twice, a moving gait without its natural speed or negative feet; `Create` for tests), [AsepriteClipReader](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteClipReader.cs) (`Read(path, gait, feet)` reads a clip's file, `ReadEffect(path, directional, pivot)` an effect's (no mark, every frame as long as the first) and `ReadDirections(path, tip)` a projectile's (a frame per direction, durations not read, no mark), each with the importer's `AsepriteReader`, after checking its header so a file that is not one is reported without the parser's error, and gives an [AsepriteClipFrames](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteClipReader.cs): the canvas size, the directions (eight, or one for a file read whole) and frames a direction, a sheet a row per direction, the first on top (`FrameRect`; eight laid out as the baker's, from the tags named `E` to `SE`, other tags ignored; one row of every frame of the file, its tags ignored, for an effect facing one way and a projectile), the first direction's durations in seconds, the frame marked `contact` (on any cel, hidden or not, a linked cel carrying its target's user data) or -1, and the problems, each naming the file and where it has them the tag, the frame in it and the frame in the file, counted from 1 as Aseprite shows them (the frame in the file alone for a file read whole): a direction tag missing, doubled, not forward, past the frames or overlapping another; directions of different lengths or durations; a gait, an effect or a projectile with a mark, an action with none, several or one on another frame than `E`'s; an effect's frame not as long as its first; a file without frames; an empty frame; a layer drawing past the canvas; a drawn layer not blending normally, a group around one not normal at full opacity, a visible tilemap layer; the feet (pivot, tip) outside the canvas; for a clip's lantern, a layer of its names doubled or not an image layer, a `LanternGlow` or `LanternLight` layer without a `Lantern` one, a lantern without its light's layer, not blending normally or under a drawn layer, a frame whose `LanternLight` layer has no pixel or several; a file with any gives no sheet. A frame is its visible image layers in layer order, hidden layers, layers in a hidden group and reference layers left out, each cel drawn with Aseprite's normal blend (`rgba_blender_normal`, its integer arithmetic) at the cel's opacity times the layer's (pixman's `MUL_UN8`), a linked cel from the cel it links to; a cel's z-index is not read (the parser keeps it internal); transparent pixels are written clear. A clip's lantern (AsepriteCharacters.md's Phase 4) is read from three layers by name, shown or hidden, and never drawn into its frames: `Lantern` (`LanternLayer`), drawn as a frame is, as `LanternPixels` laid out as the sheet; `LanternGlow`, whose pixels over the lantern's mark them glowing in `LanternGlow`; `LanternLight`, one pixel a frame whose middle, from the feet, is the frame's `LanternCentres`; `HasLantern`, and `CarriedPixels()`, the sheet with the layer drawn over it, which the preview shows; an effect's or a projectile's file draws layers of those names as any other), [AsepriteCharacterWriter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteCharacterWriter.cs) (`Generate(recipe)` returns an `AsepriteReport`: nothing when the recipe cannot generate; else each clip read, a clip with problems reported and left out, the others written through `CharacterSheetWriter.WriteSheet` (the slices pivoted on the clip's feet) and the set through `WriteSet` at `CharacterSheetWriter.SetPath(name)`, its clips the files' durations, gaits, armed flags, contact frames and natural speeds; a clip carrying a lantern also has its layer and glow written through `CharacterSheetWriter.WriteLantern` (`<name>_<clip>Lantern.png`, packed as the rogue's) and its lantern sprites and lights (`LanternLights(centres)`) in the set, and a clip without one loses those written before; then the clips the prefab plays and the set lacks; an unchanged sheet or set is not rewritten), [CharacterClipNeeds](../Assets/Editor/Characters/CharacterClipNeeds.cs) (`Of(prefab)`: each clip a prefab plays with what plays it: a player's class abilities' casts, each catalog weapon's first attack (a set without one of its other attacks' clips plays the first's) and abilities' casts, a mob config's attack; a Continue cast's clip over the run is not needed; `Missing(prefab, clips)` one line per lacking clip), [AsepriteImportSettings](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteImportSettings.cs) (`AssetPostprocessor` for `.aseprite` files under its `ArtRoot`, `Assets/Art` (`IsProjectArt`: the characters', effects' and projectiles' files), applied before each import and kept in the file's .meta: Animated Sprite mode, Merge Frame, hidden layers left out, 32 PPU, point filtering, no mipmaps, uncompressed for the active build target, no model prefab, no animation clips; the importer's texture and sprites only preview the file, so no pivot is set; other Aseprite files keep the importer's defaults), [AsepriteRegeneration](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteRegeneration.cs) (`AssetPostprocessor`: after an import of `.aseprite` files under `Assets/Art`, every Aseprite recipe asset (character, effect or projectile, found by type and read through `IAsepriteRecipe`) using one of them is queued (`Queued`), once however many of its files came in, and generated on the editor's next update (`Flush`, internal, returning the recipes generated), writing only what changed, logging what it wrote and the problems as a warning on the recipe; the queue is static, as Unity calls postprocessors statically; AsepriteCharacters.md's Phases 2 and 3), [IAsepriteRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Characters/IAsepriteRecipe.cs) (the three recipe kinds: `FilePaths` and `Generate()`, returning an `AsepriteReport`: `Problems`, each naming the file, tag and frame where it has them, `Written`, the paths that changed, and `LogGenerated(recipe)`, the inspectors' log of a run), [AsepritePreview](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepritePreview.cs) (internal, the three inspectors' preview: one file read as Generate reads it, read again only when the recipe's settings or the file (its write time and length) changed, its problems, or its sheet in a point-filtered texture: a frame drawn three times its size on the grass's colour with the pivot pixel's corner crossed, a clip's lantern over it (`DrawFrame`), a direction playing at the file's durations (`DrawPlaying`, `NeedsRepaint`) and the whole sheet (`DrawSheet`)), [AsepriteCharacterRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteCharacterRecipeEditor.cs) (the recipe's inspector: the settings, a picked clip read from its file as Generate reads it, its problems or one direction playing with the feet crossed and its sheet, the recipe's own problem, and Generate, which logs what it wrote and shows the problems found until the next run), [AsepriteProjectileRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteProjectileRecipe.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject, Phase 3: the sheet's name (letters, digits and underscores), its file `<sheet>.aseprite` under `ArtRoot` (`Assets/Art/Weapons`), the directions (32 by default), a frame each counterclockwise from east, and the tip pixel from the canvas's bottom left; `CanGenerate` refuses a bad name, a missing or misnamed file, one outside `ArtRoot`, no direction or a negative tip; `Create` for tests), [AsepriteProjectileWriter](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteProjectileWriter.cs) (`Generate(recipe)`: nothing when the recipe cannot generate or the file has a problem, a frame count other than the directions' among them (internal `Read`); else the row written with its `DirectionalSprites` through `WeaponSpriteWriter.WriteDirections`, pivoted on the tip, so a recipe of the baked arrow's name writes over it keeping its slices' IDs and its set's GUID) and [AsepriteProjectileRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Characters/AsepriteProjectileRecipeEditor.cs) (its inspector: the file read as Generate reads it, a picked direction drawn large with the tip crossed and its angle, every direction in a row, and Generate). |
| [Art/](../Assets/Editor/Art) | The canvas the characters and the props share ([PropGeneration.md](PropGeneration.md), Phase 1). [ArtCanvas](../Assets/Editor/Art/ArtCanvas.cs): a frame drawn through the game's 3/4 view (pitched 30 degrees: `ViewPitch`, `SinPitch`, `CosPitch`, `ViewRight`, `ViewUp`, `ViewForward`, `ToView`, `ToWorld`) at a scale in pixels per unit, the world origin on the pivot pixel's bottom left corner. It draws, depth-tested, spheres (their normals optionally leaning toward an `ArtEnvelope`'s), capsules (chains of spheres), flat discs, single dots and triangles facing the camera, ellipsoids at any orientation, convex solids (where every plane of a list holds, each pixel shaded by the face its view ray enters) and fields marched along each pixel's view ray (below zero inside, refined by bisection, their gradient the normal, a material per pixel from its position and normal), a sphere or an ellipsoid optionally moved in the depth test alone by a depth offset (a crown's lower clumps drawn in front); each pixel keeps its depth, material id, part id, view and world normal and world position (`MaterialIdAt`, `PartIdAt`, `NormalAt`, `WorldNormalAt`, `WorldPositionAt`, `DepthAt`, `SetMaterialId`, `CountId`), and `HidesBelowGround` leaves out surfaces under z = 0 (props sink into the ground; the characters keep it off). A character drawn a layer at a time (PlayerCharacter.md's Phase 6) is drawn in steps (`BeginStep(draws)`, `Drawing`; every drawing call does nothing in a step it does not draw), each pixel keeping its step (`StepAt`), and `MergeFrom(layer)` takes a layer's pixels that lie nearer, or as near from an earlier step, so merging the layers gives what one canvas drawn with all of them would; `ClearPixel` empties a pixel and `BaseTone` gives a pixel's tone before lines as `Shade` would for a plain material. `Shade(materials, light, shadow, gap, noise offset)` colours it through a table of [ArtMaterial](../Assets/Editor/Art/ArtTypes.cs)s: a material's cover first (another id taken where the world normal's up component plus value noise passes a threshold), then flat materials in their middle tone and the others in the light tone above the light threshold facing the light (from (-0.6, 0.8) in the screen plane, as the terrain's), the shadow tone at or below the shadow threshold and the middle tone between (or by how high they lie, for a material with a tone height), moved by the material's tone offset and its `ArtPattern` (`Bark` grooves along the up axis, `Speckle`, `Rings` around it, a pine's needle `Streaks` running out from it, basalt's `Joints` across it) within the three tones, read in world positions or in the material's pattern frame (an axis through an origin taking the up axis's place: a fallen log's grooves along it, its rings around its axis); inner lines where another outlined part lies nearer by more than the gap; and an outline in the darkest colour of the nearest outlined part beside each empty pixel. A material may glow (`ArtMaterial.Glow`, the share of its own colour it is drawn at whatever the light) and flicker (`Flicker`, the colours its pixels may flicker to), which the canvas leaves to the prop baker's glow (Lighting.md's Phase 5). `ArtNoise`: a lattice hash and value noise. |
| [Props/](../Assets/Editor/Props) | The prop baker ([PropGeneration.md](PropGeneration.md), Phases 1 and 2): natural props drawn by code on the shared canvas and written as a sheet and prefabs. [PropRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropRecipe.cs) (editor-only, abstract ScriptableObject: the prop's name, its `PropCategory` (`Trees`, `Bushes`, `Rocks`, `Wood`, `Plants`, `Lights`, `Markers`: the prefabs' folder), a seed, the variant count, the frame size and base pixel, whether it blocks, the contact height (how high a drawn surface still counts as touching the ground, for the footprint), the characters' shading thresholds and inner line gap, whether it casts the sun's shadow (`CastsShadow`), whether it fades while it hides the local player (`Fades`) and the preview terrains; `Draw(drawing)` per kind; `CanGenerate`, with what a kind asks besides (`KindProblem`); `Frames`, how many frames each variant's sheet holds, 1 but for a moving flame; `PrefabCount`, how many prefabs it writes, every variant's but for a recipe whose variants are states of one prop; `StreamIndex(index)`, the variant whose random stream a variant draws from, its own but for variants sharing one shape) and its kinds: [TreeRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/TreeRecipe.cs) (a `TreeForm`: a round crown, a pine's tiers on a short trunk, or bare limbs; the tree's height, the crown's width (a pine's tiers'), height on screen and depth and how far its middle wanders over the trunk, the trunk's radius and taper, branches, roots, the round crown's clumps, the pine's tiers, the bare tree's limbs, grooved bark, leaves or needles with their streaks, an optional cover and, on a bare tree, embers (`PropEmbers`, the charred tree's)), [BushRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/BushRecipe.cs) (width, height, depth, how high its middle sits, its crown, flowers, twigs and hanging strands, its leaves with an optional pattern, its twigs' colours), [RockRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/RockRecipe.cs) (a `RockForm`: lumps, each a convex solid inside an octagon around its ellipsoid, cut by random planes leaning up and flattened on top, leaning on a body; the lumps scattered apart around a middle stone as small stones; or a crag, blocks with flat sides stacked into a spire, each narrower, a little off the one under it and its top tilted, the last pointed, with a smaller spire beside it that does not touch the ground for the footprint; the width with its lumps or smaller spire, height, depth, sink; speckled or jointed stone; an optional cover, moss by default), [WoodRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/WoodRecipe.cs) (a `WoodForm`: a stump, a field with a flared foot, lobes and a tilted jagged top, whose surfaces facing up are its cut end with rings, and roots; or a log, a field around an axis turned from lying east and west, bowed, tapering, knotted, with jagged broken ends where the surface faces along it, its bark grooved along it and its ends ringed around it, and branch stubs; bark and cut end colours; an optional cover on both, none by default; on a stump, embers in its bark and cut end (`PropEmbers`, the charred stumps')) and [PlantRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/PlantRecipe.cs) (a `PlantForm` with a section each: `PlantFronds`, a fern's arching fronds of unlined stems and flattened ellipsoid leaflets, each frond its own part; `PlantBlades`, a tuft of unlined blades toned by height, leaning out and drooping, with cattail heads on stiffer stems or none; `PlantCaps`, a cluster of mushrooms, each a stem and a cap marched as a field, its underside in the stems' colours as gills, with spots; and the main, second and spot colours; plants touch the ground only when made to block) and [LightRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/LightRecipe.cs) (Lighting.md's Phases 5 and 6; a `LightForm`: a campfire, a ring of ellipsoid stones round a bed of glowing coals, logs leaning together over it, charred where they meet, and tongues of flame in three flat, unlined, glowing layers, each drawn nearer the camera than the last and each layer's pixels flickering to the next layer's tone; a lamp post, an iron foot, a wooden post, an iron plate, a box of flat glowing glass between iron corner bars, an iron roof with a knob and the wick's glowing flame showing through the glass; a torch, a wooden pole staked among three stones, an iron collar and a wrapped head burning, its tongues moved sideways together to reach as far either side of the head; or a brazier, three splayed iron legs under a ten-sided iron bowl heaped with glowing coals, burning; the campfire's, torch's and brazier's tongues of flame drawn by one helper, which, while the flame moves (`flameMoves`, on but for a lamp post, whose `Frames` is then `FlameFrames`, 6), swells and sways each tongue round its loop at the drawing's phase from offsets of its own and has the tallest shed a lick of flame; the ring's, logs', flame's, post's, lantern's, pole's, head's, bowl's and legs' sizes; the light's colour, strength, radius and flicker share (`LightColour`, `LightIntensity`, `LightRadius`, `LightFlicker`); the colours; it marks its light at the flame's or the lantern's middle; `LightRecipe` and `MarkerRecipe` give the writer their light through [IPropLightSettings](../Assets/Editor/Props/IPropLightSettings.cs): colour, strength, radius, flicker and whether it casts) and [MarkerRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Props/MarkerRecipe.cs) (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3; a `MarkerForm`: a waystone, a slate standing stone 1.5 cells tall, tapering, its corners chamfered, hewn by a few random faces, each tilted up so it never narrows toward its foot, rounded on top and leaning, on an octagonal plinth that alone touches the ground, three diamond runes carved down its face, its two variants its states (`DormantVariant`, the runes flat and dark; `AwakeVariant`, the runes and a halo round them glowing), drawn from one stream (`StreamIndex` 0) and written as one prefab (`PrefabCount` 1), its light, (140, 205, 255) at 0.9, radius 3, casting nothing, marked at the runes in both; a doorway, a threshold, jambs and a lintel with a keystone round a studded plank door; a signpost, a capped post among three stones with an arrow board, `SignpostWest` then `SignpostEast`; or a doormat, a border round a speckled striped field with a fringe, `DoormatAcross` two cells wide, `DoormatAlong` two tall and `DoormatSingle` on one; `KindProblem` refuses fewer variants than its states, directions or sizes). [PropShapes](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropShapes.cs): the serializable `PropCrown` (a core ellipsoid one tone darker and clumps of three sizes on its surface where it faces the camera, each its own part, their normals leaning toward the crown's envelope, stretched taller than wide for a willow's, each recorded as a `PropClump` when asked; optionally each drawn in front of the clumps above it (`tilt`), picked from several places as the one farthest from the clumps already set (`candidates`) and sized from small at the crown's top to large at its foot (`taper`), as the broadleaf trees' are), `PropRoots` (capsules splayed from a trunk's foot into the ground, in the trunk's part), `PropFlowers` (a heart pixel and four petals in flat colours on pixels a clump shows, facing the camera, all of one `PropFlowerColour` a variant picks, the meadow's four by default, lined in the leaves' outline so they change no line), `PropTwigs` (bare unlined capsules poking out past a crown, some forking), `PropStrands` (unlined curves hanging from a crown's surface facing the camera to just above the ground, toned by height), `PropCover` (a cover of moss, snow or ash: whether it lies on the prop, its ramp, the facing past which it lies and its noise; `Add` puts its material in the drawing, `Under` builds a material that takes it), `PropPine` (a pine's tiers around the up axis, each a field marched in steps of half a pixel, its radius growing as a power of its depth below its apex, its lower edge cut in teeth hanging lower and reaching further, the radii shrinking to the pointier top tier, each tier its own part) and `PropEmbers` (embers smouldering in charred wood, Lighting.md's Phase 6: off unless enabled, a flat glowing material of their own in the ramp's middle tone, touching the ground as the wood did so the footprint stays, at a glow of 0.75 and steady by default, or flickering to the ramp's shadow and light tones; `Add` puts it in the drawing, `Apply` turns the pixels of the bark materials along cracks of value noise near its middle, inside coarser patches shrinking up to the embers' height (0.7 cells), and of the cut end materials in patches, into embers) and `PropLimbs` (bare branching: limbs of short capsule segments turned by a jitter and pulled back toward their aim, the trunk a share of the height leaning within a limit, forks of two or three at the end of each limb until its depth runs out, side branches on its upper part, each limb its own part, lined while thick and unlined as twigs). [PropDrawing](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropDrawing.cs): one variant being drawn: its canvas, its random stream, the material table (each marked as touching the ground or not), new part ids and random helpers, and where a light prop's light shines from and how strongly (`MarkLight`, `HasLight`, `LightAt`, `LightStrength`), the phase of a moving flame's loop it draws (`Phase`, 0 for the first frame) and the variant's index (`Variant`, which a waystone draws its state by). [PropBaker](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropBaker.cs) (static, touching no asset): `Bake(recipe, index)` seeds a `System.Random` from the seed and the index of the variant whose stream it shares (`VariantSeed`, `PropRecipe.StreamIndex`), draws the variant onto an `ArtCanvas` of the frame at 32 px a cell with the ground hiding what is under it (`Render`), shades it with cover noise moved by its stream, and returns a `PropVariant`: its pixels, its glow (`Glow`, `Glows`: each drawn pixel's alpha its material's glow and its colour one of the material's flicker colours, picked by a hash of where it lies, black where it does not flicker; all clear for a prop that does not glow), its footprint (around the base, the columns and rows the ground-touching pixels below the contact height reach, the rows mirrored above the base, widened to lie evenly about it), its drawing's bounds, whether it reaches the frame's edge, and a light prop's light (`HasLight`, `LightOffset`: on the ground under where the drawing marked it, in units from the base; `LightStrength`); a recipe of more than one frame bakes each frame from the same stream at its share of the loop, the variant holding them (`FramePixels`, `FrameGlow`, `FrameNormals`, the first being `Pixels`, `Glow` and `Normals`: each drawn pixel's normal, `NormalsOf`, x right, y up the screen and z toward the camera as 0 to 255, opaque, clear on the outlines; `AtEdge` for any frame), its footprint, drawing and light measured on the first; `BakeAll`. [PropWriter](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropWriter.cs) (static): `Generate(recipe)` bakes every variant first and refuses, logging why and writing nothing, a recipe that cannot bake, a drawing at its frame's edge, a blocking variant touching no ground, a prefab left of a variant past the count, or an existing prefab of the other base (`Refusal`); then writes `Assets/Sprites/Props/<name>.png` through `SpriteSheetFiles` (the variants in a row, sliced into `<name>_<variant>` pivoted on the base pixel, slice IDs kept) and `Assets/Prefabs/Props/<category>/<name>_<variant>.prefab`, a variant of `BlockingProp` or `Prop` whose `Visuals` draw its slice on the base's rendering layers, or on the default one alone when the recipe casts no shadow, whose `PropFade` covers its drawing when the recipe fades (`PropRecipe.Fades`: trees and spires) and whose collider is its footprint and whose `Visuals`, for a blocking prop that is no light prop, carry a Shadow Caster 2D shaped by URP's shape editor as the footprint's box, off, without URP's edge clipping or trim, casting onto the `Default` and `Terrain` sorting layers only (`ApplyCaster`, Lighting.md's Phase 7), created once and updated in place only when it differs, so GUIDs and placed instances stay; for a prop that glows (any variant's glow: the light props, the charred wood) the sheet goes in `Assets/Sprites/GlowingProps` (`GlowingSpritesFolder`), outside the atlas, moved there with its slices and GUID from the props' folder when it was written there (and back once it stops glowing), its glow, laid out as the sheet, in `Assets/Textures/Props/<name>_Glow.png` (`GlowFolder`, `GlowPath`; a plain texture, sRGB, point filtered, clamped, without mipmaps or compression) as the sheet's secondary texture `_GlowTex` (both removed when no variant glows any more); every sheet's normals, laid out as it (`PropVariant.FrameNormals`), in `Assets/Textures/Props/<name>_Normal.png` (`NormalsPath`; the same, but linear, `SpriteSheetFiles.WriteNormals`) as its secondary texture `_NormalTex`, after the glow (Lighting.md's Phase 11), so every sheet in the atlas has one and the atlas packs them into a page of their own, linear; a moving flame's sheet and glow hold its frames in rows over the first, which alone is sliced, its sprites meshed as full rectangles so a frame reaching past the first's outline draws (tight otherwise), and its prefabs' `Visuals` draw with the flames material (`FlamesMaterialPath`, `SpritePixelLitFlames.mat`, a copy of the base's material made when missing, its `_FlameFrames`, `FlameFramesName`, kept at the recipe's frames), every other prefab with the base's; a light prop's prefabs (a recipe with `IPropLightSettings` whose drawing marked a light) get a `Light` child (`LightName`) where its light stands, a point Light 2D on blend style 0 and the `Default`, `Terrain` and `Entities` sorting layers, casting hard shadows at full (Phase 7) when the recipe's light casts, off, in the recipe's colour, strength and radius, and its `PropLight`; it writes a prefab for each of the recipe's first `PrefabCount` variants, and a waystone's one gets a `WaystoneMarker` holding its sleeping and awake slices and its `Light` child, made inactive (`ApplyWaystone`), its `Visuals` a caster over its footprint like any blocking prop's since its light casts nothing; it returns the paths it wrote (none when nothing changed), and writing into the props' folder makes sure the props' Sprite Atlas is there (`AtlasPath`, `EnsureAtlas`: the folder packed with 2 px of padding, neither rotated nor tight, point filtered, no mipmaps, RGBA32 up to 4096 px); internal overloads write into other folders, for tests, one with a glowing sprites folder of its own. [PropRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Props/PropRecipeEditor.cs): the inspector of every kind: the settings, then every variant on a strip of each preview terrain (the albedo read from its PNG and tiled, the variants with each footprint centred on its cells, the player's (the adventurer's) idle frame facing south, all at the worlds' 0.85 light) at 1x and at 3x with the cells, the blocked cells and the footprints drawn over, baked again when the recipe changes; a line per variant with its size and footprint, the bake time, why it cannot be written, and Generate. |
| [Lighting/](../Assets/Editor/Lighting) | `LightShadowResources` (the 2D renderer's projected shadow shader, ours, set again on the editor's load when lost and by `Tools/TopDownRPG/Lighting/Use Art-Grid Light Shadows`), `NightPreview` and `TimeOfDayOverlay` (a time of day previewed in the Scene view), `PropShadowRedraw` (the props' sun shadows drawn again on each change in the editor) and `ShadowCasterSceneSaving` (keeping the Shadow Caster 2Ds' editor meshes out of saved scenes); see Lighting. |
| [Effects/](../Assets/Editor/Effects) | The effect recipes (Animation.md's Phase 6): one-shot effects drawn by code straight onto the pixel grid and baked into sheets, as the weapon sprites are. [EffectRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Effects/EffectRecipe.cs) (abstract editor-only ScriptableObject: the sheet's name and frame time; the frame size, frame, variant and direction counts and the pivot; `BakeFrame(variant, direction, frame)`, `CanGenerate`); [ImpactEffectRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Effects/ImpactEffectRecipe.cs) (the spark star: frame size, directions, rays (every other one long, one along the hit), scale, outline shade, an `ImpactStarFrame` per frame (core radius, where rays start, the long and short rays' tips, how much of each ray is drawn back from its tip, its hot middle, its shade) and an `ImpactPalette` per damage type in `DamageType`'s order (hot, light, middle, dark, and `CastsLight`: Fire, Frost, Lightning and Poison light in their middle colour, `LightColour(variant)`), in [EffectArtTypes.cs](../Assets/Editor/Effects/EffectArtTypes.cs)); [PuffEffectRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Effects/PuffEffectRecipe.cs) (the death puff: frame size, the feet pixel corner it pivots on, frame count, a `ColourRamp`, the light's direction and the facings that take the light and shadow colours, a white pop on the first frame, `PuffBlob`s (place above the feet, radius, delay) that swell, grow, rise, spread and shrink at the recipe's rates, and an ordered dither dissolving it from a time on, its outline going partway); [EffectPainter](../Assets/Editor/Effects/EffectPainter.cs) (static and pure: `Star` draws a star frame, the core then each ray with its hot middle, outlined in the darkest colour darkened by the outline shade; `Puff` draws a puff frame, later blobs over earlier ones, each pixel shaded by how its blob's edge faces the light); [EffectSheetWriter](../Assets/Editor/Effects/EffectSheetWriter.cs) (`Bake` touches no asset: a row per variant and direction, the first at the top, a column per frame; `Generate` writes it through `Write(sheetName, frameSize, pivot, variants, directions, frames, frameSeconds, pixels, lightColours)`, with each variant's `EffectRecipe.LightColour` (clear by default), shared with the Aseprite effect recipe (which gives none): `Assets/Sprites/Effects/<sheet>.png` through `SpriteSheetFiles`, sliced into `<sheet>_<variant>_<direction>_<frame>` at the pivot, and the runtime `EffectSprites` `<sheet>_Sprites.asset` beside it, keeping its GUID, returning the paths that changed; `SheetPath`, `SpritesPath` and `SliceName` by sheet name) and [EffectRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Effects/EffectRecipeEditor.cs) (the inspector of every effect recipe: the first variant's first direction frame by frame, baked again when the recipe changes, and Generate). Generated: `Impact_Spark` (6 variants, 8 directions, 4 frames of 40 px) and `DeathPuff_Smoke` (10 frames of 48 x 56 px). Effects drawn by hand in Aseprite ([AsepriteCharacters.md](AsepriteCharacters.md), Phase 3; none in the game yet): [AsepriteEffectRecipe](../Assets/Editor/Effects/AsepriteEffectRecipe.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject: the sheet's name (letters, digits and underscores), its variants (`AsepriteEffectVariants`: `One`, or `DamageTypes`, one per damage type in `DamageType`'s order), whether it is drawn in the eight directions (tags `E` to `SE`) or facing one way (every frame of the file), the pivot pixel from the canvas's bottom left, and a file per variant under `ArtRoot` (`Assets/Art/Effects`) named `FileNameOf(variant)`: `<sheet>.aseprite`, or `<sheet>_<damage type>.aseprite`; `CanGenerate` refuses a bad name, a file count other than the variants', a file missing, misnamed (so out of order) or outside `ArtRoot`, or a negative pivot; `Create` for tests), [AsepriteEffectWriter](../Assets/Editor/Effects/AsepriteEffectWriter.cs) (`Generate(recipe)` returns an `AsepriteReport`: nothing when the recipe cannot generate or any variant's file has a problem or differs from the first in canvas, frames a direction or frame duration (internal `Read`, which gives the whole sheet); else the variants' rows stacked as `Bake` lays them, written through `EffectSheetWriter.Write` with the file's frame duration and the pivot, so a recipe of a baked effect's name writes over it keeping its slices' IDs and its set's GUID) and [AsepriteEffectRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/Effects/AsepriteEffectRecipeEditor.cs) (its inspector, through `AsepritePreview`: a picked variant read from its file as Generate reads it, its problems or one direction playing with the pivot crossed and the variant's sheet, the recipe's own problem, and Generate, which logs what it wrote and shows the problems found until the next run). |
| [CollisionTileGizmo](../Assets/Editor/World/CollisionTileGizmo.cs) | Static `[DrawGizmo]` drawer for every enabled `TilemapCollider2D`, selected or not, in edit and play mode (the Scene view, and the Game view with gizmos on): a translucent red fill and an outline on each cell whose tile has a collider (`CollectCollisionCells`: the filled cells of the tilemap's bounds whose collider type is not `None`), drawn whole even for a `Sprite` collider. Shows the walls' `CollisionTilemap`, which has no renderer and sits on the culled `Obstacles` layer. Reuses its buffers across repaints. Toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Gizmos/Collision Tiles` (EditorPrefs, on by default). |
| [WorldAuthoring](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldAuthoring.cs) | Static, the steps the World Editor runs. `NameProblem(name)`: letters and digits, starting with a letter, and no folder yet. `CreateWorld(name, origin, sizeInChunks, parentFolder)`: `Assets/Worlds/<name>/` (or another parent folder: the stress world's is `Assets/Dev/Worlds`) with the root scene `World_<name>.unity` (a `WorldLifetimeScope`, and `Lighting` with `WorldLighting` and a white Global Light 2D at 0.85), its `SceneDefinition` beside it, and the `WorldDefinition` `<name>.asset`. `CreateChunk(world, coords, ground)`: `Chunks/World_<name>_<x>_<y>.unity` with a `ChunkLifetimeScope` and a `Grid` at the world's origin whose `TerrainBlendRenderer` (the project's style) draws an empty layer of the chunk's own ground (grass when none is given; the world generation writer gives the chunk's, `WorldGenerationWriter.GroundOf`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), which its `AreaTerrain` lists (`SetUpTerrainEdits(grid, ground)`), its `SceneDefinition` (written with its scene, so it needs no save of its own), and its entry in the world. `CreateFilledChunk(world, coords, ground, fill)` (internal) creates it the same way but has `fill` write the grid, inactive and drawing with `EditModeTerrainArrays`' shared arrays, before the scene's one save, and saves no other asset, the caller saving the world: how `WorldGenerationWriter.Write` creates a missing chunk (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6); it returns null, making no definition, when the scene cannot be saved. Every scene goes into the build list, but a dev world's (under `SceneBuildValidator.DevFolder`). The scenes are made beside the open ones, which keep their unsaved changes: a clean untitled scene, beside which Unity opens no new scene, is closed first (replaced when it is the only scene open), and both refuse, making nothing, while an untitled scene has unsaved changes. `CreateChunk` is also how the world generation writer makes a chunk the world lacks, and it also puts a `WorldChunk` on the grid and sets up its navigation (`SetUpNavigation(grid)`: a `NavigationGrid2D` reading the ground layers under the grid, blocked by the walls and the `Obstacles` layer, reading the raised tilemap the renderer draws; `AddWalls` adds the walls to it). `Paint(world, material, cells)` paints world-local cells into the open chunk scenes that own them, adding the material's layer where missing (`GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer`) and to each open neighbour whose skirt the cells reach; `PaintWalls(world, cells, filled)` through each chunk's wall module, adding the walls (`AddWalls`: an unlinked copy of `Walls.prefab`, `WallsPrefabPath`, an empty `CollisionTilemap` with its light shadow caster) where missing; `AddWallShadows(walls)` gives walls without one the walls prefab's Shadow Caster 2D, shaped by their own collider, untrimmed (Lighting.md's Phase 7), and brings one casting on other sorting layers onto the ground's alone (`PropWriter.ShadowedSortingLayerIds`, the props' casters' too, KnownGaps.md's Phase 7); `PaintRaised(world, cells, tile)` into each chunk's raised tilemap (`AreaGroundSetup.SetUpRaised` where missing). `AddRaised(grid)` sets up a chunk's raised tilemap on its own, as one undo step. Whole-world steps, opening the chunk scenes that are not open and saving the ones they change, together (`SaveScenes`: `EditorSceneManager.SaveScenes`, one asset refresh for them all, each scene marked changed first, WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6): `MarkChunks(world)` gives every chunk its `WorldChunk` (how the Sandbox's got theirs), `SetUpNavigation(world)` sets up every chunk's navigation (how both test worlds' chunks got theirs), `SyncLayerLists(world)` adds to each chunk a layer for every material within its skirt it lacks, in the style's order, and removes each layer holding no tile whose material nothing within its skirt shows any more, with its `AreaTerrain` entry, the layers left set up as they were (`AreaGroundSetup.SetUpLayers`; KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), a line per layer added or removed, and `BakeCoastMaps(world)` bakes each chunk's coast map (`WorldCoastBake`) into a 16-bit PNG beside its scene (`CoastMapPath`: `World_<name>_<x>_<y>_Coast.png`, imported linear, uncompressed `R16`, bilinear, clamped, unreadable), sets it on the chunk's `WorldChunk`, and removes the map of a chunk that shows no waves, reading each stale chunk's sea on the main thread and encoding them all at once on worker threads (`WorldCoastBake.Encode`), writing every map, then importing them in one batch of asset changes (a new map taking its settings from `TerrainTextureDefaults` as it first imports) and setting the import settings of any still lacking them in a second (`ImportMaps`, a map imported as anything but a texture an error, WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6), returning a line per chunk with its encoding's and its writing's times, one for the encoding and one for the import and the saves; with `onlyChanged` it bakes only the chunks whose `WorldChunk.CoastSource` differs from what they would be baked from now (`IsCoastStale`), and `StaleCoastMaps(world)` lists them without baking (opening the chunk scenes and closing them unchanged). `BakeGlowMaps(world, onlyChanged)` and `StaleGlowMaps(world)` do the same for each chunk's glow map (`WorldGlowBake`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): an RGBA32 PNG beside its scene (`GlowMapPath`: `World_<name>_<x>_<y>_Glow.png`, imported linear, uncompressed, bilinear, clamped, unreadable), set on its `WorldChunk` with the cells it covers, removed from a chunk with nothing glowing within reach, and stale (`IsGlowStale`) when its `GlowSource` differs from what it would be baked from now. `StaleMapProblems(world)` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5): a problem line per chunk whose coast or glow map is out of date, reading the world's ground once, which `WorldValidation.ValidateWorld` adds, so a build stops on one. `MarkMapsStale(marker, coast, glow)`: what the map was baked from becomes `StaleSource` (`stale`), recorded for undo, so `IsCoastStale` and `IsGlowStale` find the map stale until the next bake records what it was baked from; the Terrain Brush marks its chunks so as it paints the sea, its shore or lava (`WorldBrushStroke`). `MarkedStale(world)`: the open chunks so marked, for the World Editor. Opening and closing: `OpenChunk(world, coords, withNeighbours)` opens a chunk's scene and its eight neighbours' beside the open scenes (closing an untitled scene without changes; opening beside the others keeps loaded assets alive) and makes it active, `OpenRoot`, `OpenChunkCoords`, `OpenScenesExcept` and `CloseChunks(world, keep, root)` (the caller asks to save first; the last scene open stays), `OpenChunkScenes`/`CloseScenes` (saving the changed scenes together first unless told not to, then hiding every chunk's terrain, `WorldChunk.SetDrawn(false)`, so a chunk still open whose neighbour closes draws no border again)/`SaveScenes` for whole-world steps; `IsCoastMapPath`/`IsGlowMapPath` tell a chunk's baked maps by their path. `ListInGameScenes(world)`. World exits and entries: `AddExit(world, cell, target, entryName, size)` places a `WorldExit` with a new `PersistentId` on a world cell of the open chunk owning it, under that scene's `Exits` root (`ExitsRootName`, made when missing), as one undo step; `ConfigureExit` sets where an exit leads, its size and its marker (a signpost unless given) through its serialized object; `OpenExits(world)` lists the exits of the open chunks; `SetEntry(world, name, position, waystone)` replaces (keeping its display name) or appends an entry and saves the world. `CreateScratchScene()`: after offering to save the open scenes, a new unsaved scene with a Global Light 2D and a standalone grid at the origin set up as a chunk's (an empty grass layer drawn by the terrain renderer, its navigation grid, its `AreaTerrain` listing it, the walls) without a `WorldChunk`, for the Scene Generator. `OpenChunkGrid`, `MaterialNamed`, `ChunkSceneName`. `PaintWalls` paints the ground of their neighbours under walls it fills beside ground (`WallGround.FillUnder`), and `SetUpGround`, which the scratch scene and a new chunk use, is internal for the tests (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
| [WallGround](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/WallGround.cs) | Static, the ground under an area's walls (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6, chosen with the user over a wider fillet on the walls' inner corners): `FillUnder(cells, rendererOf)` paints, under each wall cell just painted with no ground on its renderer's layers, the ground most of its eight neighbours show on top (`GroundBeside`, the first in the style's order on a tie) with the tile a neighbour holds of it, read before anything is painted, on that renderer's layer of it (added when missing), each tilemap recorded for undo in the current group; `BareCells` lists the wall cells beside ground with none under them, for Validate; `TopAt` is a cell's top ground. The Terrain Brush (on a chunk through `WorldBrushStroke`, else after the Dual Grid Brush's box fill) and `WorldAuthoring.PaintWalls` call it. |
| [WorldGlowBake](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldGlowBake.cs) | Static (LightingGaps.md's Phase 5): a chunk's glow map from the whole world's ground (`WorldCoastBake.WorldCells`). `BakeChunk(world, chunkMaterials, chunkCells, textureName, out area)` builds a `TerrainGlowMap` over the chunk's cells from the world's cells of the chunk's layers' glowing materials, within the reach of the first of them (`GlowingMaterialOf`), and returns it as a readable linear RGBA32 texture with the cells it covers, or null when nothing glows within reach: what the chunk's renderer builds with its neighbours loaded, whose skirt (4 cells) reaches past the glow's reach. `SourceOf` hashes the first glowing material, its reach and which cells glow within that reach of the chunk, or `NoGlow`. |
| [WorldCoastBake](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldCoastBake.cs) | Static: a chunk's coast map from the whole world's ground. `WorldCells` holds a bit per material per cell of the world (`Add(tilemap, material, cells)`, `BitsAt`, `MaterialsIn`, `Covered`: the filled cells and the control map's margin, as one renderer over the world would cover them). `BakeChunk(world, style, chunkMaterials, chunkCells, out width, out height)` takes the first material with waves in the style's order among the chunk's layers and the ground within its skirt (null when none), flags each cell of a window `Reach` (40) cells around the chunk, clipped to `Covered`, as sea when it is empty or holds that material or a non-bridge one above it, builds a `TerrainCoastMap` over the window and crops it to the chunk's control map (clamping past the window's edge): the texels a renderer over the whole world gives those cells, exactly. It is `ReadSea` (the window's sea flags as a `ChunkSea`, on the main thread, since it reads the materials; null when the chunk shows no waves) then `Encode(sea)` (`TerrainCoastMap.ComputeFromCells`, touching no Unity object), which `WorldAuthoring.BakeCoastMaps` runs for every stale chunk at once on worker threads (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 6). `SourceOf` hashes what a chunk's map is baked from (the material with waves, the window and which of its cells are sea), or `NoWaves`, so equal sources bake equal maps; a change of ground that is no sea, under the waves' place in the order or past the reach leaves it as it was. `ToTexture` makes the R16 texture. About 220 ms per chunk of a 2 x 2 world in the editor (20 µs per cell of the window). |
| [WorldEditorWindow](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldEditorWindow.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Editor`.
