A picker of every world in the project; New world: a name (`NameProblem`), a size in chunks and the first free origin (`WorldLayout.TryFindFreeOrigin`, or one typed in, checked against the layout as it is typed), creating every chunk and listing it in `GameScenes` when asked; the picked world's origin and size with any layout problem, and a button listing it in `GameScenes` when it is not; a grid of its chunks, north up, each closed, open or missing, the selected one created, opened with its neighbours or alone, or kept while the others close (asking to save changed scenes first), and, while open, given walls (`AddWalls`) or raised ground (`AddRaised`) when it lacks them, the root scene opened and every chunk closed; the world's entries (name, position, waystone; the first is the default) edited in place, with a warning for one on no chunk; the exits of the open chunks, each with its target world, a popup of that world's entries (or its default entry), its size, its marker and a Select button, and Add exit, which places one in the selected open chunk at the Scene view's pivot when it lies on that chunk (its middle otherwise) and selects it; and the whole-world steps: Sync layer lists, Check maps, Bake changed maps, Bake every map (the coast maps and, since LightingGaps.md's Phase 5, the glow maps), Place markers (`WorldMarkers.Place`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 3), Repair ids and Validate, each reporting its lines under the window. Each exit's row also has its marker (`WorldExit.Marker`), a popup. Its only standing work is a flag set when a tile of an open chunk of the picked world changes (`Tilemap.tilemapTileChanged`), which shows a hint to check the coasts, and, once the stroke is done (a delay call), the list of open chunks the brush marked out of date (`WorldAuthoring.MarkedStale`), which a warning names until the maps are baked (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5). |
| [WorldBrushStroke](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldBrushStroke.cs) | Static, the Terrain Brush's strokes on a chunk. `TryResolve(target)` gives a tilemap under a `WorldChunk` naming a world its `Target`: the chunk and a `Kind`, `Ground` (a layer the chunk's renderer draws, with its material), `Raised` (the renderer's raised tilemap), `Walls` (any other dual-grid tilemap) or `Other`. `Apply(target, changes)` splits the stroke's `TileChangeData` by the chunk owning each cell (chunks are found among the open scenes, `OpenChunks`) and writes each part to that chunk's tilemap of the same kind: the layer drawing the material, added where missing (`GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer` in the current undo group) and added also to each open neighbour whose skirt the painted cells reach; the walls of the same rule tile (`WallsLike`), an empty copy of the target's walls where missing, joined to the chunk's navigation; the raised tilemap (`AreaGroundSetup.SetUpRaised` where missing). Each part records its tilemaps, and for walls the open neighbours' render tilemaps, in the current undo group, so the stroke is one undo step across scenes. A part of a chunk not open or outside the world, or of another chunk for an `Other` target, is skipped and named in one warning; returns how many cells it skipped. `FloodFill(target, start, fill)` fills the 4-connected cells of the kind holding the start's tile across the open chunks of the world (a chunk lacking the tilemap counts as empty), warning when it stopped at the border of a chunk that is not open, and applies them as one stroke. A ground stroke that paints a material with waves, paints or erases a cell holding one, paints a cell holding no ground (sea to a coast map) or erases a cell's last ground marks the coast map of every open chunk whose bake reaches one of its cells (`WorldCoastBake.Reach`, 40 cells) out of date, and one that paints a glowing material (lava) or a cell holding one the glow map of every open chunk within the glow's reach (`TerrainMaterial.MaxGlowReach`), in the stroke's undo group (`WorldAuthoring.MarkMapsStale`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 5); a closed chunk is found by Check maps, Validate and the build. `LayerOf`, `WallsLike`. Walls it paints on cells with no ground beside ground get the ground of their neighbours under them (`WallGround.FillUnder`, in the same undo group), and `FloodCells` gives the cells a flood fill writes, which the brush's flood preview shows (KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
| [WorldLayout](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldLayout.cs) | Static, the global layout (WorldStreaming.md's World origins): `RectOf` (a world's rectangle in units), `Gap` (the distance between two rectangles), `Problem(origin, size, others, self)` (a rectangle leaving `Extent`, 16 384 units around the origin, or within `MinimumGap`, 1024 units, of another world's, naming it), `Validate(worlds)` (every world leaving the layout and every pair too close), `TryFindFreeOrigin(size, worlds)` (the first slot of a grid `SlotSize`, 2048 units, apart, row by row from the origin's, where the world fits) and `AllWorlds()`. |
| [WorldValidation](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldValidation.cs) | Static, the ownership checks, a line per problem naming the object (scene and hierarchy path). `ValidateChunk(chunk)`: every `SpawnPoint`, `MobSpawnPoint`, object with a `PersistentId` and walk-through prop (a child of the scene's `Props` root, or of the chunk grid's `GeneratedProps`) on one of the chunk's cells; every blocking prop's footprint (`PropGridSnapping.TryGetFootprint`) blocking only its cells (`FootprintInside`, with `PropGridSnapping.BlockedCells`, the navigation's rule); every mob spawn point with a `PersistentId`, and every id set; every `WorldExit` covering only the chunk's cells and leading to a world the listed worlds hold (`GameScenes`', or a list a test passes) at an entry it has (or its default entry for an empty name). `ValidateIds(ids)`: a line per shared id naming its holders. `ValidateWorld(world, allWorlds)`: the origin against the layout, each chunk scene's `WorldChunk` naming the world and its coordinates, `ValidateChunk` for each, ids unique across the world, every chunk's waystone and exit markers those Place markers would write (`WorldMarkers.Stale`), and every chunk's coast and glow maps up to date (`WorldAuthoring.StaleMapProblems`, KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), opening the chunk scenes that are not open and closing them unchanged. `ValidateChunk` checks the markers under a chunk grid's `Markers` root as props, their footprints inside its cells. `ValidateAll()` for every world. A chunk's wall cells beside ground with no ground under them, where the ground's corner leaves a gap at the wall, are a problem too (`CheckWallGround`, `WallGround.BareCells`, reading the neighbours' ground in the chunks open beside it; KnownGaps.md's Phase 6). |
| [WorldMarkers](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldMarkers.cs) | Static, the World Editor's Place markers step (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3): what shows a world's waystones and exits, written from the world's data into every chunk scene (locked ones too: markers are world data) under its grid's `Markers` root (`RootName`). `Place(world)` opens the chunk scenes that are not open, writes, saves the ones it changed together (`WorldAuthoring.SaveScenes`) and closes them, a line per changed chunk and per marker with no free cell; `Plan(world, grids, report)` gives each chunk's `Placement`s (prefab, root position, name, entry, cell); `WriteChunk(grid, placements)` makes the root's instances the plan's in order, reusing one of the same name and prefab and changing it only where it differs, so a second run changes no file, and destroys the rest (the root too when nothing is left); `Differs` and `Stale(world)` (the chunks out of step, which Validate reports). A waystone entry gets `Waystone_0` (named `Waystone_<entry>`, its `WaystoneMarker` naming the entry) on the first free cell of `WaystoneOffsets` from its entry's cell, two north first, rooted as the props' snapping roots a blocking prop (`PropGridSnapping.RootOnCells`); an exit by its `WorldExit.Marker` (`PrefabFor`): a signpost on the first free cell of `SignpostRows` above, then below, its middle column, one row clear first, `Signpost_0` in the world's western half and `Signpost_1` in its eastern; a doorway on the middle of its bottom row; a doormat on its middle, across two cells, along two or on one by its shape. A cell is free when the navigation of the chunk owning it walks it for `TerrainMovement_Default` (built for the check when the editor's world navigation has not, and unbuilt after), counting a cell blocked only by markers' colliders as walked, and it lies on no exit, no mob spawn point and no entry's row of arrival slots (its cell and one either side). |
| [WorldBuildValidator](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldBuildValidator.cs) | Pre-build check (`IPreprocessBuildWithReport`, after `SceneBuildValidator`) failing a build with any `WorldValidation.ValidateAll` problem, listed, a coast or glow map out of date among them (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5); also `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Validate Worlds`, which logs each problem as an error. About 0.2 s for the three worlds before the maps were checked; their check takes 1.5 s for the four hand-made worlds together, and the Overworld's Validate 1.6 to 2.2 s. |
| [PersistentIds](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/PersistentIds.cs) | Static: `NewId` (a GUID without dashes), `SetId` (through the serialized object, with undo), `AddToSpawnPoints(roots)` (a `PersistentId` on every `MobSpawnPoint` lacking one), `MakeUnique(ids)` (an id for each without one and for each later holder of a shared one, the first keeping it), `RepairNew(fresh, existing)` (a new id for each fresh object whose id is missing or held by another, so a duplicate changes and its original keeps its own), `Duplicates`, `IsChunkScene`, `InWorld`, `Repair(world)` (the World Editor's Repair ids over every chunk scene, saving the ones it changed together, and only those: a chunk open with changes of its own stays unsaved; it keeps the derived ids the world generation writer sets, since they are set and unique), `PathOf`. |
| [PersistentIdWatcher](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/PersistentIdWatcher.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside play mode, on `ObjectChangeEvents` for a created hierarchy (a new object, a duplicate, a paste) or a changed object structure in a chunk scene, gives its mob spawn points their `PersistentId` and repairs its ids against every loaded one (`RepairNew`), logging each new id. Changes made by scripts without undo raise no event, so as a chunk scene saves (`EditorSceneManager.sceneSaving`, outside play mode) it also gives the scene's spawn points their ids and makes its ids set and unique within it, the first holder of an id keeping it (`RepairScene`, internal; `PersistentIds.MakeUnique`), logging each new id (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5); an id the scene shares with another chunk is left to Repair ids and Validate, since one scene cannot tell which holder is the original. The world generation writer sets each generated spawn point's derived id as it makes it, so the watcher, should it see one, keeps it. |
| [EditModeTerrainArrays](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/EditModeTerrainArrays.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5): outside Play Mode, hands the `TerrainBlendRenderer` of every world chunk opened in the editor (`EditorSceneManager.sceneOpened`: the World Editor, the World Generator's writes and whole-world steps, the tests, by hand) one editor-wide `SharedTerrainArrays` (`Arrays`, internal), as a chunk's scope hands it the Gameplay scope's in play; a renderer drawing with shared arrays already, one given its own by a caller and chunks built in memory are left alone. A renderer builds its own arrays as its scene opens and swaps to the shared ones at the end of that open, so at most one chunk's own are alive at a time. Every set goes before an assembly reload (`Dispose`), and the chunks open after a reload or back from Play Mode get the new set (a delay call). On the Overworld, the whole-world steps' peak above the editor's memory went from 0.90 GB to 0.27 GB, and the 20 chunks open from 0.97 GB to 0.14 GB, in the same time (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5 notes). |
| [EditModeWorldNavigation](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/EditModeWorldNavigation.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside Play Mode, joins the `NavigationGrid2D` pages of the open chunks of each world in a `WorldNavigation` of its own (one pool, up to `NavigationPagePool.DefaultSlots` chunks), as the world's navigation joins them in play, so the movement blocker gizmo draws no line along a border the ground crosses and a cliff across a border only where the game has one. A chunk takes part when its `WorldChunk` and grid are enabled in the scene its world lists for those coordinates (chunks built in memory by tests stand alone). `Sync` runs once per editor update at most after a scene opens or the hierarchy changes; a closing scene's pages leave first, and every page leaves before Play Mode and an assembly reload (`LeaveAll`). `IsJoined(grid)`, and `NavigationOf(world)`, the joined navigation of a world's open chunks (`OverworldTests` walks it). |
| [AreaTerrainDevMenu](../Assets/Editor/World/AreaTerrainDevMenu.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/Dev/Toggle Terrain Cell At View Centre`: in play mode, toggles the cell at the Scene view's pivot (or the main camera's position when no Scene view is open) on the selected tilemap, at z = 0 (`CellAt`), through its area's `AreaTerrain` (`Toggle`), so a host's edit is recorded and replicated and a client is refused. For checking runtime terrain edits by hand (see [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#checking-runtime-terrain-edits)). |
| [DualGrid/](../Assets/Editor/DualGrid) | Dual-grid tooling: inspectors for the module, rule tile and data tile, the Dual Grid Brush (listed beside the default Terrain Brush, which derives from it), the rule tile preview, automatic tiling rules, `Assets/Create/2D/Tiles/Dual Grid Rule Tile` and `GameObject/2D Object/Tilemap/Dual Grid Tilemap`, and `DualGridTilemapPersistentListener` (edit-mode render refresh). `RestrictedTransformEditor`/`RestrictedTilemapEditor` replace every `Transform` and `Tilemap` inspector to lock render tilemaps. See [DualGrid.md](DualGrid.md). |

## ScriptableObject configs

| Type | Assets | Consumed by |
|---|---|---|
| `GameScenes` | `Assets/Data/Scenes/GameScenes.asset` (starting world the Overworld at `clearing`) | `MainLifetimeScope`, `GameFlow`, `BootFlow`, `PlayerStarts` (`FindWorldById`, the starting world and entry), `LocalTripAuthority` (the listed worlds), editor scene tools; `SceneBootTestHelper` registers a copy starting at Testlands' `start` (`CopyStartingAt`) |
| `NetworkSettings` | `Assets/Data/NetworkSettings.asset` | `MainLifetimeScope` -> `NetworkSession`, `MainMenuController` |
| `UiScaleSettings` | `Assets/Data/UI/UiScale.asset` (480 x 270 layout, 390 lines a zoom, 2 size steps) | `MainLifetimeScope` -> `UiScale` |
| `UiTheme` | `Assets/Data/UI/UiTheme.asset` ([UiTheme.md](Generated/UiTheme.md)) | Referenced directly by every `ThemedText`, `ThemedGraphic` and `ThemedSelectable` and by `AbilitySlotView`, `MoveMarker` and `WeaponSlotView`; not registered in a scope |
| `HudSettings` | `Assets/Data/UI/HudSettings.asset` (a failure's words show 1.2 s; a notice 3 s; no ticks (`TickHealth` 0), a loss shown 0.4 s, low at 30% pulsing once a second down to 35%) | Gameplay scope -> `AbilityBarPresenter`, `NoticePresenter`, `PlayerHudPresenter` |
| `SceneDefinition` | `Assets/Data/Scenes/Scene_*.asset`; each world's beside its scenes under `Assets/Worlds` | `GameScenes`, `WorldDefinition`, `GameFlow`, `WorldStreaming`, `SceneLoader` |
| `GameplaySettings` | `Assets/Data/GameplaySettings.asset` | `PlayerDeathHandler` (respawn delay), `WorldStreaming` and `PlayerSpawner` (entry slot spacing), `GameSaveRecorder` (autosave interval), `WaystoneDiscovery` (reach), `NoticePresenter` (the respawn delay it counts down, the waystone notice's text), `WalkablePlaces` (saved-place search radius, 16 cells) |
| `WorldStreamingSettings` | `Assets/Data/WorldStreamingSettings.asset` (view 20 x 12, load margin 12, unload hysteresis 8, grace 10 s) | Gameplay scope -> `WorldStreaming` (its `ChunkStreamer`) and `LocalWorldPresentation` |
| `WorldDefinition` | `Assets/Worlds/Sandbox/Sandbox.asset`, `Assets/Worlds/SandboxInterior/SandboxInterior.asset`, `Assets/Worlds/Testlands/Testlands.asset`, `Assets/Worlds/Overworld/Overworld.asset` | `GameScenes.worlds`, `GameFlow`, `BootFlow`, `WorldStreaming`, `ChunkStreamer`, `NoticePresenter` (display names) |
| `CombatSettings` | `Assets/Data/Combat/CombatSettings.asset` | Gameplay scope -> `DamageService`, `DamagePopupPresenter` (popup colours, the periodic and allies' popup sizes), `HitFeedbackPresenter` (the immune colour, a tick's type colour), `AttackService` and `AbilityService` (unit layers), `ProjectileService` (unit and projectile wall layers) |
| `HitFeedbackSettings` | `Assets/Data/Combat/HitFeedbackSettings.asset` | Gameplay scope -> `HitFeedbackPresenter` |
| `DayNightSettings` | `Assets/Data/Lighting/DayNight.asset` (a 20-minute day and a 5-minute night, a new game in the morning, ten light keys with the night lights' share, flickering 8 times a second with 36 % of flickering pixels shown, clouds 14 units across covering up to 45 %, see Lighting) | Gameplay scope -> `DayClock`, `DayClockSync`, `DayNightPresenter`, `PropLighting` |
| `RegionSky` | `Assets/Data/Lighting/Skies/Sky_<region>.asset`, one per region with a sky of its own: the Village Forest, the Lakeside, the Snowfield, the Coast and the Lava Fields (see Lighting) | the area generators (`AreaGenerator.Sky`) -> the world generator's writer -> each chunk's `ChunkSky` -> `RegionSkies` |
| `PropFadeSettings` | `Assets/Data/PropFadeSettings.asset` (fades to 45% over 0.15 s; a body 0.5 wide and 1.3 tall above the feet; a prop covering a quarter of it fades) | Gameplay scope -> `PropFading` |
| `CombatProfile` | `Assets/Data/Combat/Profile_*.asset` | Player and Weasel prefabs (`DamageReceiver`) -> `DamageService` |
| `PlayerWeapon` | `Assets/Data/Weapons/Sword.asset`, `Greatsword.asset`, `Longbow.asset` | `PlayerWeaponController` (the attack timeline, cooldown, cancel rules and clips), `PlayerController` (attack range), `AttackService` (a melee hit and its area), `ProjectileService` (a projectile), HUD |
| `WeaponCatalog` | `Assets/Data/Weapons/WeaponCatalog.asset` (Sword, Greatsword, Longbow) | Player prefab (`PlayerWeaponController`) -> `PlayerNetworkSync` (weapon ids on the wire), `WeaponDebugPanel` |
| `AbilityDefinition` | `Assets/Data/Abilities/Blank_*.asset` | `PlayerClass`, `PlayerWeapon`, `PlayerAbilities`, `PlayerOrders`, `PlayerCastVisuals` (its cast clips and timeline) |
| `PlayerClass` | `Assets/Data/Classes/Class_Placeholder.asset` | Player prefab (`PlayerAbilities`) |
| `StatusEffectDefinition` | `Assets/Data/StatusEffects/Status_*.asset` | `StatusEffectCatalog`, `PlayerWeapon` and `MobConfig` on-hit statuses (none yet) |
| `StatusEffectCatalog` | `Assets/Data/StatusEffects/StatusEffectCatalog.asset` | `CombatSettings` -> `NetworkStatusEffects` |
| `RebindableActionList` | `Assets/Data/Input/RebindableActions.asset` | `MainLifetimeScope` -> `InputBindings` |
| `PlayerControlSettings` | `Assets/Data/PlayerControlSettings.asset` | Player prefab (`PlayerController` -> `PlayerOrders`, `PlayerUnitQueries`, `PlayerMotor2D`), Gameplay scope -> `PointerTargetPicker` |
| `MobConfig` | `Assets/Settings/AI/Mob_Default.asset`, `Assets/Dev/StressTest/Mob_StressTest.asset` (both respawn after 300 s) | All mob components and states; `ChunkMobSpawner` (Respawn header: `respawns`, off for mobs that stay dead such as bosses, and `respawnDelaySeconds`) |
| `TerrainMovementProfile2D` | `Assets/Settings/AI/TerrainMovement_Default.asset` (grass walkable at 10, snow at 15, mud and shallows at 20, water, the ocean and lava not walkable, edge cost 5; sand, ice, gravel, stone and planks have no rule, so they are walkable at the default 10) | `MobConfig.movementProfile`, `PlayerControlSettings` -> navigation |
| `TerrainStyle` | `Assets/Settings/Terrain/TerrainStyle.asset` | `TerrainBlendRenderer` (every area's `Grid`), the `AreaGenerator` assets (their terrain style) |

| `GrassTuftsRecipe`, `GravelChipsRecipe`, `StoneCobblesRecipe`, `SandRipplesRecipe`, `MudPuddlesRecipe`, `WaterHighlightsRecipe`, `OceanWavesRecipe`, `LavaBubblesRecipe`, `WeedsRecipe`, `PlankBoardsRecipe` (`TerrainMaterialRecipe`s, editor-only) | `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Recipes/Recipe_GrassGround.asset` (`GrassTuftsRecipe`, 20261012, repeating over 8 cells: green blades and tufts, the grass's textures and the ground the weeds and the meadow draw over), `Recipe_Weeds.asset` (`WeedsRecipe`, 20261019, 8 cells, weed clumps over `Recipe_GrassGround.asset`; ## Cross-system interactions

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph Main scope
        BootFlow --> GameFlow
        GameFlow --> SceneLoader
        GameFlow --> ScreenFader
        GameplayInputGate -- TransitionStarted/Finished --> GameFlow
        GameplayInputGate --> PlayerInputService
    end
    subgraph Gameplay scope
        PlayerController -- IPlayerCommandSource --> LocalPlayerCommandSource
        LocalPlayerCommandSource -- IPlayerInput --> PlayerInputService
        LocalPlayerCommandSource --> PointerTargetPicker
        LocalPlayerCommandSource -- unit filters --> LocalPlayerTracker
        PlayerController --> PlayerOrders -- IAbilityCaster --> PlayerAbilities --> AbilityService
        PlayerAbilities -- CastStarted --> PlayerNetworkSync -- CastRpc --> PlayerAbilities
        PlayerAbilities -- CastEnded --> PlayerNetworkSync -- CastCancelledRpc --> PlayerAbilities
        PlayerAbilities -- CastStarted/CastEnded/RemoteCastStarted/RemoteCastCancelled --> PlayerCastVisuals -- PlayCast --> CharacterVisuals
        PlayerController --> PlayerMotor2D -- Of --> PlayerNavigation
        PlayerMotor2D -- SetLocomotion --> CharacterVisuals --> CharacterAnimator
        PlayerController --> PlayerWeaponController
        PlayerWeaponController -- PlayAction --> CharacterVisuals
        PlayerWeaponController -- at contact --> AttackService --> HitService --> DamageService
        PlayerWeaponController -- at contact --> ProjectileService --> HitService
        PlayerWeaponController -- EquippedWeaponChanged --> PlayerNetworkSync
        DamageService --> Health
        DamageService -- DamageApplied --> CombatEvents
        CombatEvents --> DamagePopupPresenter --> DamagePopupLayer
        CombatEvents -- DamageApplied --> HitFeedbackPresenter --> HitFeedback
        HitFeedbackPresenter --> EffectPool
        HitFeedbackPresenter -- ICameraKick --> CameraFollow2D
        HitFeedback -- Hold --> CharacterVisuals
        Health -- Damaged/Died --> PlayerHudPresenter --> PlayerHudView
        PlayerWeaponController -- EquippedWeaponChanged --> PlayerHudPresenter
        Health -- Died --> PlayerDeathHandler -- TrySendToWaystone / TryRestore --> PlayerRespawner
        PlayerRespawner -- Send --> NetworkTripAuthority -- Approved --> LocalTripTraveller
        LocalTripTraveller -- ReportArrived --> NetworkTripAuthority -- End --> TripProtection
        HitService -- IUnitProtection --> TripProtection
        StatusEffectService -- IUnitProtection --> TripProtection
        WaystoneDiscovery -- Discover --> SessionSave
        WaystoneDiscovery -- Raise --> WaystoneNotices --> NoticePresenter
        WaystoneNotices --> KnownWaystones -- Changed --> WaystoneMarkers -- SetAwake --> WaystoneMarker
        PlayerWorldSync -- Replicate --> KnownWaystones
        GameplayEntryPoint --> CameraFollow2D
    end
    subgraph World and chunk scopes
        ChunkMobSpawner -- resolver.Instantiate, SetHome --> MobController
        MobController -- INavigation2D --> WorldNavigation
        MobPerception2D -- IPlayerRegistry --> WorldPlayers
        TripProtection -- Changed --> WorldPlayers
        MobController --> MeleeDamageDealer -- at contact --> AttackService
        MeleeDamageDealer -- AttackStarted/Cancelled --> MobController -- PlayAttack/StopAttack --> MobMotor2D
        MobMotor2D -- SetLocomotion/PlayAction --> MobVisuals[CharacterVisuals]
        MeleeDamageDealer -- AttackStarted/Contacted/Cancelled --> MobNetworkSync
        Health -- Died --> DestroyMobOnDeath --> EffectPool
    end
    MainMenuController -- StartWorldGameAsync / StartJoinedGameAsync --> GameFlow
    MainMenuController -- TryLoad, StartNew / StartFrom --> SessionSave
    MainMenuController -- TryGetStart --> PlayerStarts
    GameSaveRecorder -- WorldSessionEnding --> GameFlow
    GameSaveRecorder -- Write --> GameSave --> ISaveFolder
    ChunkMobSpawner -- RecordMobDeath / StaysDead --> WorldState
    ChunkTerrainSync -- RestoreEdits / RecordTerrainEdit --> WorldState
    MainMenuController -- StartHost / JoinAsync --> NetworkSession
    NetworkSessionLifecycle -- ConnectionLost / TransitionFinished --> NetworkSession
```

Events summary:

| Event | Publisher | Subscribers |
|---|---|---|
| `GameFlow.TransitionStarted` / `TransitionFinished` | `GameFlow` | `GameplayInputGate`, `TransitionTimeFreeze` (offline only, and not for trips, `IsTravelling`), `NetworkSessionLifecycle` (finished only) |
| `GameFlow.WorldSessionEnding` | `GameFlow` (leaving a world session) | `GameSaveRecorder` |
| `Application.quitting` | Unity | `NetworkSession`, `GameSaveRecorder` |
| `ITripAuthority.Approved` | `NetworkTripAuthority` (offline or on the host once `LocalTripAuthority` checked a request, held the arrival and set the player's world, or once it sent a dead player to its waystone, `Send`; on a client when the host's trip arrives through `PlayerWorldSync`) | `LocalTripTraveller` (the local player's trip: `GameFlow.TravelAsync`) |
| `WaystoneNotices.Discovered` | `WaystoneDiscovery` (offline or the host, every player's), `PlayerWorldSync.WaystoneDiscoveredRpc` (a client, its own player's) | `NoticePresenter` (the local player's), `KnownWaystones` (where game state is decided) |
| `KnownWaystones.Changed` | `KnownWaystones` (a discovery where game state is decided; a replicated list on a client) | `WaystoneMarkers` |
| `NetworkSession.ConnectionLost` | `NetworkSession` | `NetworkSessionLifecycle` |
| `NetworkSession.ClientJoined` | `NetworkSession` (host) | `GameplayPlayers` |
| `TripProtection.Changed` | `TripProtection` (a trip protecting a player; its machine's fade in, the time limit or the player leaving ending it) | `WorldPlayers` |
| `PlayerWorlds.Changed` | `PlayerWorlds` | `WorldPlayers`, `PlayerWorldSync` (host), `WorldStreaming` (the local player's trips, for its chunk reports), `GameSaveRecorder` (a player entering a world) |
| `PlayerController.Teleported` | `PlayerController.Teleport` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `MeleeDamageDealer.AttackStarted`, `AttackContacted`, `AttackCancelled` | `MeleeDamageDealer.TryStartAttack`, `Tick` (contact), `CancelAttack` (a stun, death, a rebuild) | `MobController` (start and cancel, onto its motor), `MobNetworkSync` (host) |
| `LocalPlayerTracker.Changed` | `PlayerBinder` | `GameplayEntryPoint`, `PlayerHudPresenter` |
| `PlayerWeaponController.AttackStarted`, `AttackContacted`, `AttackCancelled` | `PlayerWeaponController.TryAttack`, `Tick` (contact, a recovery cut short), `Order`/`CancelAttack` (an early end) | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `Health.Damaged` | `Health` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `Health.Healed` | `Health.Heal` (via `DamageService`, with the heal's source) | `NetworkHealth` (host), `PlayerHudPresenter` |
| `DamageReceiver.ImmuneHit` | `DamageService.ApplyDamage` (authority) | `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `Health.Died` | `Health` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `PlayerDeathHandler`, `DisableOnDeath`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`, `ChunkMobSpawner` (its mobs, offline or on the host) |
| `Health.Restored` | `Health.Restore` (via `PlayerRespawner`, or `NetworkHealth` on clients) | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `DisableOnDeath`, `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `CombatEvents.DamageApplied` / `HealApplied` | `DamageService` | `DamagePopupPresenter`, `HitFeedbackPresenter` (damage only) |
| `CombatEvents.HitPredicted` | `AttackService.Predict` (a client's own melee contact, KnownGaps.md's Phase 4) | `HitFeedbackPresenter` |
| `CombatEvents.StatusBlocked` | `StatusEffectService` (authority, or replicated on clients) | `DamagePopupPresenter` |
| `StatusEffects.Changed` | `StatusEffectSet` | `NetworkStatusEffects` (host), `StatusBarPresenter`, `StatusVisuals`, `WorldStatusIcons` |
| `StatusEffects.Blocked` | `StatusEffectService.Apply` (authority) | `NetworkStatusEffects` (host) |
| `PlayerRegistry.PlayerAdded` / `PlayerRemoved` | `PlayerRegistry` | `PlayerDeathHandler`, `PlayerIds` (added only: learning ids), `GameSaveRecorder` (removed only: a leaving player's place and weapon), `TripProtection` (removed only), `WaystoneDiscovery` (removed only) |
| `AreaTerrain.EditRecorded` | `AreaTerrain` (authority) | `NetworkAreaTerrain` (host), `ChunkTerrainSync` (offline or host, into the world state) |
| `PlayerWeaponController.EquippedWeaponChanged` | `PlayerWeaponController` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `PlayerAbilities`, `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `ProjectileService.UnitStruck` | `ProjectileService` (a projectile stopping at a unit, on every machine) | tests |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastStarted` | `PlayerAbilities.TryCast` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastFailed` / `SlotsChanged` | `PlayerAbilities` (failures come from `PlayerOrders` too) | `AbilityBarPresenter` |
| `InputBindings.BindingsChanged` | `InputBindings` (a change, a reset, or a keyboard layout change) | `AbilityBarPresenter`, `OptionsMenuController` |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastEnded` | `PlayerAbilities.CompleteCast` / `CancelCast` (called by `PlayerOrders`) | `PlayerCastVisuals`, `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner: a cancel) |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastCompleted` | `PlayerAbilities.CompleteCast` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner: the completion to every other machine) |
| `PlayerAbilities.RemoteCastStarted` / `RemoteCastCompleted` / `RemoteCastCancelled` | `PlayerAbilities.PlayRemoteCast` / `CompleteRemoteCast` / `StopRemoteCast` (a copy, from `PlayerNetworkSync`'s cast messages) | `PlayerCastVisuals` |
| `AbilityService.CastApplied` / `CastResolved` | `AbilityService.Apply` / `Resolve` (authority only) | tests |

## Tests

Tests build their own grids, tilemaps and GameObjects and destroy them in `TearDown`. When the editor is driven by a tool (UnityMCP), run them through [ProjectTestRun](../Assets/Editor/Testing/ProjectTestRun.cs) rather than UnityMCP's `run_tests`, so one call starts a run and one wait reads a short summary (see CLAUDE.md for when to run what):

- `ProjectTestRun.Start(mode, fixtures, categories, tests, slowest)`, from execute_code: `mode` is `EditMode` or `PlayMode`, and the other three are lists separated by semicolons, of test classes, categories and full test names, that narrow the run (all empty: the whole suite of that mode). It returns `started ...`, or `refused: ...` while the editor compiles, imports or plays, while a run is going, or while an open scene has unsaved changes, since it makes an empty scene active first (EditMode tests touch the active scene). It runs through the Test Framework's `TestRunnerApi`.
- When the run ends it writes `Temp/TestRunSummary.txt` (`SummaryPath`, written whole through a temporary file and deleted when a run starts): `PASSED` or `FAILED` with the filter, the counts and the run's length, the slowest test classes and tests with their times (`slowest` of each, 8 by default), and a `FAIL` line per failed test with the first line of its message; `ERROR` and the message when the run could not go. Its callbacks are registered on every domain load, so a PlayMode run writes it too.
- Waiting: a shell loop until the file exists, run in the background, which then prints it and `git status` of ProjectSettings and the scenes (a run can leave churn there).
- Checks that run for every cell or pixel (the area generation and terrain recipe tests) go through an `Expect(condition, message)` helper rather than `Assert.That`, which costs a few microseconds a call even when it passes, its message formatted and a constraint result built; `Expect` formats its message only on failure.
- Explicit tests run only when named, when their class is or when their category is: the pathfinding benchmarks (`Benchmark`), the network benchmarks (`NetworkBench`: the remote player's smoothness, the Network Simulator's preset in one process and KnownGaps.md's Phase 4 timings) and the terrain shader's compile test (`ShaderCompile`, nearly four minutes, with a timeout of its own past the Test Framework's default of three minutes), run after a shader change and before merging one.

| [PixelLightTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/Editor/PixelLightTests.cs) | Crisp light (Lighting.md's Phase 1): the pixel-lit sprite shader, and the sun shadows' caster and cliffs shaders, import cleanly and every pass compiles for D3D, with and without the light keyword where it has it, without errors or warnings; only the `Universal2D` pass of it and of the terrain graph declares `USE_SHAPE_LIGHT_TYPE_0`; `SpritePixelLit.mat` has the pixel-lit shader and is the 2D renderer's default for a new sprite. Rendered through `TerrainBlendProbe.Render` at 3 screen pixels per art pixel with lights the test makes: under a tinted global light alone, a sprite of many colours and clear pixels draws exactly the pixels URP's `Sprite-Lit-Default` draws, and a terrain of many colours (read linear) draws its unlit value times a light of (0.75, 0.75, 0) to within the 8-bit rounding; under a white point light over a dim global one, every art pixel of a one-colour terrain and of two white sprites on it (one flipped, one cut from the middle of a larger texture, as the atlas packs props) is one colour, while the light falls off across them. And under the shipped day's afternoon key, sky and sun together, set on a world's global light by its outdoor lighting, with the afternoon's sun casting, a terrain of many colours and a sprite casting its shadow on it draw exactly the pixels the authored white at 0.85 draws wherever the shadow does not fall, and darker where it does (Lighting.md's Phases 3 and 4). Renders from later editor updates until something is drawn. At night a sprite's glowing pixels draw at their own colour and the others as without a glow, and at a flicker share of one every flickering pixel shows its flicker colour (Lighting.md's Phase 5); with the glowing pixels off (`_GlowFlicker.z` 1, the lighting panel's) every pixel draws as without a glow. A moving flame's sprite, on a material of four frames, draws its first frame while nothing flickers and at each step the frame `PropLighting.FlameFrameAt` picks for where it stands, two flames side by side on frames of their own (Lighting.md's Phase 6). A white point light casting over a grey ground with a wall's box Shadow Caster 2D east of it darkens the ground behind the wall and leaves the ground before it as it was, every art pixel one colour, and with the camera moved by quarters of a screen pixel no art pixel changes by more than 2 levels; the renderer's projected shadow shader is `LightShadowProjected.shader`, which compiles cleanly (Lighting.md's Phase 7), and holding it the editor leaves URP's resources alone (`RestoreIfLost`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5). The clouds' shader compiles cleanly too (Phase 8). Water under a point light standing above it on the screen, its reflections on, draws the art pixels below the light, toward the camera, brighter than with them off, every art pixel one colour, at another time of the water's moved, and those above the light and beside its reach as with them off; water with no light but the sky, and ground that reflects nothing under a point light, draw the same pixels with the reflections on and off (Phase 10). The project's cobbles under a point light over a night sky turn their bevels to it, those facing it brighter and those facing away darker than painted, every art pixel one colour, and the ground beyond its reach draws as with relief off; under the shipped afternoon's sky and sun relief moves no pixel more than 4 steps and hardly any more than one, and at sunrise the bevels facing the sun brighten and those facing away darken; a sprite's normals light its half facing a point light and darken its half facing away, a sprite without normals and the sky alone as with relief off (Phase 11). on a written seed 1, the lava source of its terrain type at the last priority, every lava cell blocked, a step onto open earth costing grass's 10 and every mob and trail cell joined to the spawn; `AreaLayoutWriter` on a scene whose ground layer is still dual-grid: every layer a plain data tilemap at its place (no module, render tilemap or Tilemap Renderer left, no record of the old tiles), the layer's tile in exactly the layout's cells, a navigation source of its terrain type with its index as priority, the grid's `TerrainBlendRenderer` drawing the layers in order with their materials and the generator's style, `AreaTerrain` filling the grass layer with its tile, old props and mob spawn points replaced, the start spawn moved, then a built `NavigationGrid2D` blocking exactly the footprint cells and connecting every mob to the spawn; the renderer the writer added holding exactly the new layout's cells after one layout is written over another, cells the new one leaves empty included; a Village Forest area whose renderer also draws painted water and sand layers and an empty dry grass layer above its own, out of order, written again into the shared order (grass, dirt, sand, gravel, plaza, stone, water) with the painted layers kept with their cells and materials, the empty one removed, each layer at its place among the grid's children with its index as priority, and the renderer building; an area listing only grass, mud and water (a `TestAreaGenerator` of the Lakeside's layers) drawing its three layers and navigating as before, water blocking and mud dearer than grass. A `TestAreaGenerator` of 33 layers (the Clearing's grass tile and type, each with its own test material, in a test style whose layer order lists them) refused with an error and nothing written, and one of 32 written, its renderer building all 32 wide and each layer's cell holding its own bit alone; layers out of the style's order or drawn with a material it does not list refused with an error and nothing written. On the Coast over 20 seeds, its plateau on the grass off the coast, the paths and the spawn's disc by its margin, in one piece three cells thick, its ramp the ramp width wide in its bottom row with cliff on either side and two rows of walkable ground in front, no prop on its face, its ramp, the row above it or in front of it, no footprint across its edge, no mob on its cliff, the ramp and the top above it reached from the spawn and no cell of it reached without the ramp; every generator's written layout holding its raised ground on the raised tilemap, ramp tiles in the ramps, drawn by the renderer and read by the navigation grid, whose cliff stops every step up the plateau's face but the ramp's while blocking no cell; the plateau off in the detail stream case, and raised ground in the determinism case. `TearDown` reverts the writer's undo group before destroying the test objects, so the Test Runner's undo revert after a run cannot bring the objects the writer destroyed back into the open scene |

Fakes and seams:
- [ManualClock](../Assets/Tests/Editor/ManualClock.cs): `IClock` with settable `Time` and `Advance`.
- [TestWorldGenerators](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestWorldGenerators.cs): unsaved world generators and worlds (at (8192, 0)) with the project's style, profile, ground materials and Weasel, only read; the Overworld's Phase 0 settings (`Overworld`), with the wilds' prop settings of the project's Overworld generator (`WithWildsProps`); chunk grids built in memory as `WorldAuthoring.CreateChunk` builds them, with grass or a ground given (`NewChunk`); `Destroy` clears their undo and destroys what it made. `WorldGeneratorTests`, `WorldGenerationWriterTests`, `WorldGeneratorWindowTests` and `ChunkedGroundRenderTests` share it.
- [TestCombat](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestCombat.cs): a shared default `CombatSettings`, `CreateDamageService(authority, events)`, `CreateStatusService(...)` (on a `ManualClock` by default, with a given `IUnitProtection` or none) and `CreateHitService(authority, events)`. [TestThemes](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestThemes.cs): UI themes from `UiTheme.Create`, every role in a colour of its own and each size in the project's Galmuri fonts (read, never written); `Shared` for tests that only read it. [FakeUnitProtection](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeUnitProtection.cs): an `IUnitProtection` whose `Protected` set the test fills.
- [TestTerrainStyle](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestTerrainStyle.cs): a `TerrainStyle` built in memory with the project's terrain shader (only read) and given pair overrides, and `TerrainMaterial`s built in memory: plain grass, gravel (whole chips) and stone (cut stones) materials with fresh 128 px textures (albedo, a flat height and, with chips or stones, a cell field), or one of a given name and cell mode.
- [TestPropPrefabs](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestPropPrefabs.cs): built props saved in a temporary folder (`Assets/Tests/TestPropPrefabs`, removed by `Delete`) as variants of `Prop` and `BlockingProp` without art, with footprints the sizes of the props the generators were built with (a plant and stools for seats, a campfire, a building, a fountain, a fence, a lamp post, barrels and tables), which `AreaGenerationTests` gives the generators' copies for the steps the assets leave unset.
- [AsepriteFileWriter](../Assets/Tests/Editor/AsepriteFileWriter.cs): writes `.aseprite` files from the published format (github.com/aseprite/aseprite, `docs/ase-file-specs.md`) so tests need no hand-drawn art: the 128-byte header (32 bits per pixel, the layers' opacity valid, square pixels, a 16 x 16 grid, no palette), then per frame its header and chunks: the layers (image layers and groups, visible or hidden, with a blend mode, an opacity and a child level) and the tags (each with its loop direction) in the first frame, and each frame's cels in layer order, zlib-compressed as Aseprite saves them, each followed by its user data text when it has one, or a link to another frame's cel (whose user data Aseprite writes only after the cel linked to). `AddLayer(name, visible, blendMode, opacity, group)`, `AddGroup(name, visible, opacity, group)` (a group's layers added right after it), `AddFrame(milliseconds)`, `AddTag(name, from, to, loop)`, `SetCel(layer, frame, rect, pixels, userData, opacity)` (from the canvas's top left, rows from the top, as the file stores them; a cel may reach past the canvas), `SetLinkedCel(layer, frame, linkedFrame)`, `SetImage` (a whole canvas cropped to its pixels that are not fully transparent; none leaves no cel), `Write(path)`; `FromSheet(sheet)` writes a baked `CharacterSheet` in AsepriteCharacters.md's conventions (a canvas the size of a frame with the feet on the baked pivot's pixel, one layer `Body`, each direction's frames under a tag named after it in `CharacterDirections` order, durations rounded to whole milliseconds, `ContactMarker` (`contact`) on the contact frame's cel in every direction; a sheet carrying a lantern adds its three layers above the body: `Lantern`, then hidden `LanternGlow` (its glowing pixels in `GlowMark`) and `LanternLight` (the pixel holding each frame's light in `LightMark`)); `FromSheetRows(sheet, sheetWidth, size, firstRow, rows, columns, milliseconds, tagOf)` writes rows of frames cut from a baked effect or weapon sheet, row after row on one layer, each row's frames under the tag `tagOf` names (an impact's variant: its eight rows tagged by direction; the death puff and the arrow: one row untagged).
- [TestAreaGenerator](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestAreaGenerator.cs): an `AreaGenerator` built in memory by `Create(style, layers)` (named TestGenerator), whose layouts fill cell (i, 0) of each layer i and nothing else, for writer cases the project's generators cannot give (such as 16 or 17 layers).
- [FakePlayerInput](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakePlayerInput.cs): settable `IPlayerInput`. [TestPlayerControlSettings](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestPlayerControlSettings.cs): builds a `PlayerControlSettings` (layers `Enemy`/`Player`) and assigns it to a `PlayerController`.
- [FakeInputBindingLabels](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeInputBindingLabels.cs): settable `IInputBindingLabels`. [FakeMenuInput](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeMenuInput.cs): settable `IMenuInput`. [FakeSessionStatus](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeSessionStatus.cs): settable `ISessionStatus`.
- `MemorySaveStore` ([EditMode](../Assets/Tests/Editor/MemorySaveStore.cs), [PlayMode](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/MemorySaveStore.cs)): in-memory `ISaveStore` with `Contents` and `WriteCount`, for the settings. `MemorySaveFolder` ([EditMode](../Assets/Tests/Editor/MemorySaveFolder.cs), [PlayMode](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/MemorySaveFolder.cs)): in-memory `ISaveFolder` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 5), its files by name (`Read`, `KeptCopiesOf`), the earlier builds' file (`LegacyStore`, `RetiredLegacy` once moved aside), `WriteCount` across its files, `Contents` (the file written last; set, a session's save seeded as written before the test, `Seed`). [ManualWallClock](../Assets/Tests/Editor/ManualWallClock.cs): `IWallClock` set by hand, `Advance`. `GameSaveTests` builds its own `GameScenes` and `SceneDefinition`s with fake GUIDs.
- `SystemRandom` with a fixed seed stands in for `IRandom`.
- A real `PlayerRegistry` of `PlayerHandle`s over test transforms stands in for the session players.
- Mobs are driven manually: `MobController.Configure`/`Construct`, then `TickStateMachine(dt)` / `FixedTickStateMachine()`.
- `internal` hooks via `InternalsVisibleTo`: `DamageReceiver.SetProfile`, `StatusEffects.SetHold`, `StatusEffectService.Advance`/`TrackedUnitCount`, `DamagePopupLayer.Configure`/`ActiveCount`, `PlayerHudView.ConfigureReferences`, `AbilityBarView`/`AbilitySlotView.ConfigureReferences` and the slot's parts, `PointerFeedbackLayer.Configure` and its state, `WorldHealthBar.Configure`/`Refresh`/`FillFraction`/`IsShown`, `FloatingDamageText.Advance`/`Refresh`, `GameSaveRecorder.TryGetPlace`, `PlayerController.Tick`/`Orders`, `PlayerWeaponController.State`/`SetCatalog`, `MeleeDamageDealer.State`/`Clock`, `MobMotor2D.DesiredVelocity`, `CharacterVisuals.Animator`/`Tick`, `ProjectileService.Advance`/`Flying` (and its constructor over `IProjectileQueries`), `ProjectileVisual.Configure`, `ProjectileVisualPool.CreatedCount`, `HitFeedback.Configure`/`Tick` and its counters, `HitFeedbackPresenter.Show`, `EffectPlayer.Configure`/`Tick`, `EffectPool.Played`/`CreatedCount`, `CameraFollow2D.AdvanceKick`, `PlayerMotor2D.FixedTick`/`Follower`, `PlayerAbilities.Configure`/`RecentCastCount`/`TryGetRecentCast`, `MobPathAgent2D.Waypoints`, `GridAStarPathfinder2D.LastExpandedCount`, `NavigationGrid2D.AreRegionsLabeled`, and in the editor assembly `TerrainBrushEditor.FollowStroke`/`FollowShift`/`PaintPreview(…, erasing)`/`BoxPreview` (outside IMGUI `Event.current` is null, so the grid brush editor's `OnPaintSceneGUI` cannot run in a test).

### PlayMode (`Assets/Tests/PlayMode`)

| File | Covers |
|---|---|
| [SceneBootTestHelper.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/SceneBootTestHelper.cs) | Helper: boots through `Main` like a build using `EditorBootRequest`, resolves services from `MainLifetimeScope` or the Gameplay scope (`ResolveFromGameplay`), finds a status in the catalog by name (`FindStatus`), timeout-guarded waits. Every boot registers a `MemorySaveStore` (the test's own, or a fresh one) into Main with `LifetimeScope.Enqueue`, and a fresh one under the `UserSettings.FileName` key, so tests never read or write the real save or settings file. Every boot also registers, after the asset, a copy of `GameScenes` starting at Testlands' `start` (`GameScenes.CopyStartingAt`, a `GameScenesCopy`, destroyed with the Main scope), since the Overworld is the starting world: the tests that start from the menu (New Game, Continue, Host Game, Join Game), the in-process host and client, which resolve `GameScenes` from the booted scopes, and a joining player the host does not know all start there. `BootIntoMainMenu(store, startingWorldPath, entry)` names another starting world, or keeps the project's own with a null path (`OverworldPlayModeTests`' menu cases). |
| [UiScalePlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/UiScalePlayModeTests.cs) | DesignSystem.md's Phase 3: the booted main menu's canvas at a constant pixel size of 32 reference pixels per unit and the zoom, following a new display at once; hosting with an in-process client, the host choosing Large at 1080p puts its HUD, popup and in-game menu canvases at zoom 4 and its health text, injected with the zoom, at 3/4 (UiRevamp.md's Phase 3), while the client stays connected, its settings at Normal and its copy of the host's player with the same look, colours and parts |
| [OptionsMenuPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/OptionsMenuPlayModeTests.cs) | Booted main menu: the options screen exists hidden with the Character header, its Look row, the adventurer's eight part rows and fifteen colour rows (PlayerCharacter.md's Phases 5 to 7), the Display header, its UI size row, its Reduce flashing and Reduce motion rows and its allies' damage numbers row, then its nine rows and three headers; Options hides the menu buttons and selects the first row, the Look row; Back shows them again with Options selected; the UI module and `InputBindings` use the session's `RuntimeInputActions` copy, whose UI actions are enabled |
| [GameMenuPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/GameMenuPlayModeTests.cs) | Solo: the menu key's map is live in game, the open menu pauses and blocks gameplay input until it closes; leaving from a pause reaches the main menu with time running and both maps off. Hosting: the menu pauses nothing, Leave asks first, and confirming ends the session in the menu |
| [GameSavePlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/GameSavePlayModeTests.cs) | New Game enters the starting world and saves the local player there by its player id (the current version, empty waystones, its starting weapon); booting straight into Testlands is a throwaway session: with a save stored, nothing is written (a save asked for returns false) as it enters and leaves, and Continue then loads that save as it was, a real session that saves again; without a save, with an old save of an earlier build (version 1, ignored with its warning) or with only a copy of this machine's place in a host's session that may still be there, Continue is disabled and not selected; Continue lands offline in the saved world and position holding the saved weapon, with the world clock and world state continued; Host Game with a save lands its player at its saved place holding its saved weapon and saves it there, and leaving saves a weapon switch; hosting the starting world with a save that knows a client's player id in the Sandbox, the joining `InProcessClient` spawns at its saved place only once the host loaded the Sandbox around it, is in the Sandbox on both machines, holds its saved weapon on both (the host's copy from the spawn on) and is saved with it, and when it leaves after walking its new place is kept |
| [WorldPersistencePlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/WorldPersistencePlayModeTests.cs) | In the Sandbox: a killed mob of the start chunk stays dead after the chunk unloads and loads again, and comes back once its respawn delay has passed (its death moved back on the world clock), its death forgotten; a hole opened in the start chunk's grass survives the chunk unloading, restored as the terrain's own edit; in a game continued from the menu, leaving to the menu saves the killed mob and the player's place, and Continue brings both back. Places no longer walkable (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 5): a place saved in the middle of the Sandbox's pond starts, on Continue, on the nearest walkable cell (7 cells west, every nearer cell water), with one warning, and the next save keeps it; hosting with a save that places a client's player id there, the joining `InProcessClient` spawns on that cell on the host, its own copy arrives there and the host saves it there; a place saved in the Overworld's open sea, with no walkable cell reaching the world's entries within 16 cells, starts at the Lakeside's waystone, the nearest the player discovered |
| [NetworkWorldsPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkWorldsPlayModeTests.cs) | Seamless worlds online with an in-process client. Hosting Testlands: a joined client gets both players, its own in Testlands on both machines and held, but no mob until it reports the chunk ready; then the mobs arrive with that home chunk and its player is released; with both players moved to another chunk the host keeps the held chunk (and the client its copies); the client's release hides the chunk's objects before the acknowledgement arrives, and the host then unloads it; a disconnect releases what the client held. In the Sandbox (the host starts there; the client starts in the starting world and the host sends it to the Sandbox's entry on its first trip): the client's trip into the interior goes by RPC with its own position, the host approves without fading or moving its player, counts the trip, loads the interior and holds the client's player; a report from before the trip is ignored and one for it shows the interior's objects; the host's own trip leaves the client where it is, with its mob copies, sees the host's player in the interior snapped to the entry, and the host keeps the Sandbox loaded |
| [NetworkTerrainPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkTerrainPlayModeTests.cs) | Hosting Testlands: the host walls off cells and empties a grass cell through the `AreaTerrain` of the chunk under its player, on open grass it finds near the entry, and the world's navigation follows; an in-process client that joins afterwards gets no sync object while it holds no chunk, and once it reports the chunk ready receives all four edits on its `NetworkAreaTerrain` copy, which arrives with that home chunk; a later edit of the same cell replaces its record there instead of adding one. The client shares the host's scene, so applying the edits on a separate client is only checked in EditMode and Multiplayer Play Mode |
| [NetworkMobsPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkMobsPlayModeTests.cs) | Hosted game plus in-process client: the host's mob is a spawned network object; the client's copy (the same network object id, since the area has several mobs) has its AI off and a kinematic body, follows the host's mob, mirrors its HP and publishes the hit for popups with its damage type, and an immune hit as an immune report without HP loss; the host's heal reaches the client's player and is published; a status on the host's mob reaches a late-joining client (with its effect on the copy's defence), a blocked status reaches it for the Immune popup, and a Burn on the client's player shows on its owner and its ticks arrive; the client's own player takes the host's damage, dies (its statuses cleared on the client too) and is respawned by the host; killing the mob despawns the client copy and plays the pooled death effect once on each; a stun on the host's mob shows on the client's copy; the host's mob attacking the host's player (Animation.md's Phase 7): a client joining mid-attack sees nothing replayed, then two next attacks on its copy, each its windup frames until the host's contact landed and then the contact frame, and a stun on the host mid-windup stops the copy's attack, which would otherwise hold its windup Its client reports its chunks again for each of its player's trips (`InProcessClient.FollowTrips`), as a real client's streaming does, so the respawn waits for them as it now must (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4). |
| [NetworkSmoothnessPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkSmoothnessPlayModeTests.cs) | With the area's mobs despawned and under simulated 150 ms / 20 ms / 4% loss, the client's player is sent on move orders 1000 units left or right (constant speed) and stopped with Stop: the host's copy starts within 0.8 s, never jumps back a visible pixel, never freezes for 0.6 s and keeps its speed within 50% on average over 3 runs. Plus the explicit `NetworkBench` benchmark described under Transform smoothing. |
| [InProcessClient.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/InProcessClient.cs) | Helper: a second `NetworkManager` cloned from the session's (without its simulator, joining with a given player id or a fresh one), with its own container (`BuildContainer`: camera, the player prefab's `PlayerControlSettings`, picker, `PlayerWorlds`, the trip services with a client `ISessionStatus` (and the trip protection, timed by the host's `GameplaySettings`, which nothing ticks), an empty `WorldNavigation`, so its player walks in straight lines, `AbilityService`, `AttackService`, a `ProjectileService` that nothing ticks (its arrows are visual only and never fly), the host's `CombatSettings`, `StatusEffectService`, `HitService`, an always-ready `IClientReadiness`, an `EffectPool`, in-memory `UserSettings` (whose look its own player follows) and, given the host's `HitFeedbackSettings`, a `HitFeedbackPresenter` started on a `RecordingCameraKick`, `CameraKick`), prefab handlers, player registry, local player tracker and scripted input (`TestInput.PointAt(world)` plus one-frame `MovePressedThisFrame`/`StopPressedThisFrame`/`AbilityPressedThisFrame`), acting as a joining client inside the test process. It shares the host's scenes and loads no world of its own: its handler for the mob and terrain sync prefabs of the host's loaded chunks takes their home chunk as instantiation data (`HomeChunks`), injects the copy with that chunk's `AreaTerrain` and keeps copies in `DontDestroyOnLoad`; `SendJoined`, `SendChunkReady(trip, chunk)`, `SendChunkReleasing(chunk)` (acknowledgements land in `Released`) and `SendReady` (joined, then every chunk the host has loaded ready for trip 0) stand in for a real client's streaming; `HostChunks`, `Resolve<T>()`; its container holds its own `KnownWaystones` and the stones' presenter, not started, which a test starts and hands a stone to. Never let the host reload the Gameplay scene while one is connected: that froze the editor every time (see [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#testing)). `FollowTrips()` (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4): from then on, each new trip of its own player reports again every chunk the host has loaded in the trip's world, tagged with the trip, as a real client's streaming does when a trip reaches it. |
| [InProcessHost.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/InProcessHost.cs) | Helper: a bare host cloned from the session's manager on its port, so the editor's own session is tested as a joining client: it approves every client or refuses remote ones, and with the player prefab, combat and gameplay settings and scene catalogue it has a host-side container (`InProcessClient.BuildContainer` with a host `ISessionStatus`) and spawns a joined client's player at `SpawnPosition` in `SpawnWorld` (unless `SpawnsJoinedPlayers` is off), making itself Netcode's singleton for the spawn. It records `Joined`, `Ready` (trip, chunk) and `Releasing`, acknowledges a release at once unless the chunk is in `HeldAcknowledgements` (then `Acknowledge`), and exposes `Players`, `PlayerWorlds` (so a test can send a trip) and `PlayerOf(clientId)`. |
| [NetworkDelay.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkDelay.cs) | Helper (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4): a delayed connection for the in-process host and clients, usable by any PlayMode test. `Install()` (before a session starts) sets Netcode's driver constructor hook (`UnityTransport.s_DriverConstructor`) to build every Unity Transport driver with its simulator stage on sent packets only and a fixed seed (`Seed` plus a count), and disables the Network Simulators while installed (each writes its preset over every driver as one is created; in one process its stage, on sent and received packets, delays each way twice); `Set(conditions)` gives every running and later driver half the round trip, give or take the jitter, and the loss, both ways alike, turning running drivers to sent packets too; `SetSending(manager, delay, jitter, loss)` sets one manager's way out (100 drops all it sends); `Dispose` builds drivers as before and gives running ones no delay. `Conditions`: `None`, `Fast` (50 ms round trip, ±5 ms each way, 1% loss each way), `Typical` (150, ±15, 2%), `Slow` (300, ±30, 4%). |
| [NetworkDelayPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkDelayPlayModeTests.cs) | The delayed connection measured with named-message pings the host echoes, 80 a round on the clock both machines share: at no delay and at Fast, Typical and Slow, an unreliable ping's round trip is the setting's within its jitter over the no-delay frames, each way about half, losing about what both ways' loss make; the reliable channel loses nothing and its quickest pings take the setting's round trip. Plus an explicit `NetworkBench` measurement of the Network Simulator's 150 ms preset on drivers Netcode built itself, logging a round trip of about 600 ms. |
| [NetworkTimingPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkTimingPlayModeTests.cs) | Explicit `NetworkBench` benchmarks under each delay setting (KnownGaps.md's Phase 4, whose notes have the numbers), logging and asserting nothing: how long the host's copy of a client's player holds its windup past its own before the contact arrives, and when its cast would release on its own clock and does release against the host resolving it; how often a client's own sword hits hold its contact frame, how long after its contact its hit shows and how often its own view at contact agrees with the host's hit, a Weasel standing in reach and sliding across it; and when the host's arrow leaves, lands and has its hit reach the client, the client's projectiles flown each frame by the benchmark. KnownGaps.md's Phase 7: the client's casts with a bufferable one pressed halfway through them (Bolt and Lunge then Whirl or Leap, Bolt and Charge then Sweep), with no delay and the Typical one, as its own player and the host's copy show them: whether the running cast's release showed, and when the buffered cast's clip and release showed, against the owner's completions and the host's resolves. |
| [NetworkAbilitiesPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/NetworkAbilitiesPlayModeTests.cs) | Hosted game with a connected in-process client: a host cast reaches the client's copy of the host's player (recorded as remote) and the client's `AbilityService` never applies anything; the client's enemy cast (R) on its copy of a mob (matched by network object id) reaches the host with that mob as target. Separately: the host's own cast is applied once; an in-process client, moved two units from the host's player (it never enters the area itself, so it starts on top of it), presses E on the host's player (an ally cast through its real command source and picker) and the host's `AbilityService` applies it exactly once, from the host's copy of the client's player, with the host's player as target; the client's facing toward the aim reaches that copy; the client's Smite damages and stuns the host's mob only once its cast completes (and both reach the client's copy), and its completed Mend fortifies the host's player |

| [DayNightPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/DayNightPlayModeTests.cs) | Day and night (Lighting.md's Phase 3): in a new offline game in Testlands the day clock's world time is the session's and the world on screen's global light is the clock's light at an intensity of 1; the dev panel moving the time of day to the night turns it to the night sky. Hosting from the menu with the mobs deactivated and an in-process client given a day clock of its own listening on its manager: as it joins it hears the host's world clock, which it then shows to within a quarter second, and the same time of day. At the Sandbox's `walls` spot at night the burning lights cast and wake the props' casters near them, the walls cast and the sky casts nothing, and the panel keeping the lights' shadows off lets every caster sleep (Lighting.md's Phase 7).
