Trip protection (KnownGaps.md's Phase 2), hosting Testlands: the host's own player sent to `start` with a mob by the entry is kept out of play through its whole trip (the mob never targets it, a hit on it is ignored, its health stays full) and, once faded in, the mob comes for it and hurts it; a client's player sent there likewise stays out of play on the host while the client moves it there and reports the chunk, until the client reports its fade in, after which the mob hurts it on both machines; hosting, a client's player walking to `north` has its discovery replicated to its machine alone, whose stone shows it awake while the host's sleeps, `start`'s awake, until the host's own player stands there; and a known client knows an earlier session's discovery from its spawn on, once (KnownGaps.md's Phase 3) KnownGaps.md's Phase 4, under the Typical delay: a client that reported `start`'s chunk at its join has its dead player sent to `start` within Testlands; the chunk stays held but not ready for that trip, and the player stays dead though the client moved it there, until the client reports the chunk during the trip, when the host restores it on both machines. The client respawn test deactivates the mobs once the client joined, as its neighbours do, since one bumping the host's player failed its exact check of the host's player now and then. |
| [OverworldPlayModeTests.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/OverworldPlayModeTests.cs) | Booting into the Overworld by its root scene (`SceneBootTestHelper.BootIntoWorld`): the player on the Clearing's waystone, the default entry; once the chunks around it settle, each loaded chunk drawn, linked with each loaded neighbour and holding one mob per spawn point. The player walking the Clearing's road east to its east gate, past the outline at x = 108, then on along the world road across the border into chunk (2, 1), each leg walkable and connected on the world's navigation, the mobs deactivated as their chunks load, and nothing logged but the editor boot's note that the session is a throwaway one. One case per region: sent to its waystone, the player discovers it (the Clearing's counting as discovered from the start), dies four cells away and comes back alive at it after the respawn delay. From the menu with the project's own `GameScenes` (`BootIntoMainMenu(null, null)`), the only tests not pinned to Testlands: New Game starts on the Clearing's waystone, and so do Host Game with no save and an in-process client the host does not know, on the next entry slot. An explicit benchmark (`Benchmark_StreamingAlongARouteThroughEveryRegion`, category Benchmark) runs `StreamingBenchmark` (DevTools, see Dev tools), which carries the player a quarter of a cell a frame from waystone to waystone through every region, and appends its two lines to `Temp/StreamingBenchmark.txt` (PropGeneration.md's Phase 6 notes, WorldScale.md's Phase 0 notes). 10 tests, about 42 s. |

## Known gaps and oddities

Factual observations against CLAUDE.md. [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md) closed the ones no other plan covered: each was fixed and taken off this list, or is marked accepted here with its reason. The six left unmarked are left to the plans its Where we start names: the fixed build version, every player visible to every client, the placeholder status icons, the camera off the art grid, lava only blocking and the editor's night as a preview.

- **A fixed build version**: it stays at the pre-alpha `0.1.0-prealpha` until the user asks for another (CLAUDE.md), so `JoinApproval` no longer refuses a client whose protocol, worlds or catalogs (`WeaponCatalog`, `StatusEffectCatalog`, which rely on it) differ from the host's; it matters from the first real multiplayer test with another client, planned for the first playable vertical slice. A content hash in the join payload is WorldGeneration.md's open question.

- **Chunk loads hitch in the editor** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 5: editor only, and a player's load fits a frame): an Overworld chunk's load has a worst frame of about 69 ms there, mostly the scene's activation, which grows by about 0.1 ms a prop. In a development player (batchmode, CPU only) it is about 12.6 ms on average and 16 ms at worst, under a 16.7 ms frame: activation 4 to 10 ms (about 0.005 ms a prop) and the ground's build 1 to 5 ms, since its texture arrays are shared per style (WorldScale.md's Phase 0 and Phase 2 notes). The GPU side of a load is not measured.
- **Every player is visible to every client**: players are never hidden, so a client receives the movement of players in other worlds, thousands of units away and never drawn. Fine for 2 to 4 players.
- **Wave crests stand on end at the Overworld's top edge** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 5: they lie 20 cells or more from every walkable cell, past every view, and the Coast region's joins smoothed in that phase left them as they were): in the open sea within about 12 cells of the world's top edge a few crests run north and south instead of along the waves (at cells x 3, 164, 184, 195, 216 and 234), lined up with the bumps of the Coast region's beach to the south: seams in the baked coast maps far from any shore. They lie 20 cells or more from every walkable cell, past the view (10 cells to either side, 5.6 up and down), so no player sees them; WorldGeneration.md's Phase 4 notes.
- **The dev panels find the Gameplay scope themselves** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 1): the status, day and night, lighting and weapon panels look the `GameplayLifetimeScope` up (`FindAnyObjectByType`, cached, at most once a second while there is none) and resolve their services from its container, as the streaming benchmark's runner finds the Main and Gameplay scopes, instead of being injected. No clean route injects them: they live outside every scope (made per play session in `DontDestroyOnLoad`, from DevTools, which Gameplay cannot reference and release builds leave out); VContainer's only hook into a scope built elsewhere, `LifetimeScope.Enqueue`, keeps its installers on one global stack whose every disposal pops the top one, whoever pushed it, so a panel holding an installer for the whole session would pop the runner's or a PlayMode test's in place of its own; and putting the panels in the Gameplay scene for its auto-inject list would leave missing scripts in release builds. The lookup is dev-only and costs nothing once found.
- **Remote arrows are visual and late** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 4: they land in step with the hit each machine learns of, a median 4 to 8 ms apart at every delay measured, where flown ahead by their message's age they would land a one-way trip before their hit shows): every machine flies the arrows it learns of, another player's from its contact message, so on a client they start one way late (a median 72 ms at a 150 ms round trip) and, where a mob's copy lags the host's mob, can stop where the host's arrow did not (a homing arrow follows its target's copy, so in the measurements every one struck it); only the host's arrow hits, and its result reaches the client through `NetworkHealth`.
- **TMP's dynamic fallback font changes at runtime** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7: package behaviour, which keeping UI text inside the static atlases' characters keeps out of commits): a glyph missing from the UI's static Galmuri atlases (`UiFonts.Characters`, DesignSystem.md's Phase 4) falls back through TMP's settings to `LiberationSans SDF` and then to its dynamic `LiberationSans SDF - Fallback`, which gains the glyph, which is package behaviour; in the editor that dirties the fallback asset. `Clear Dynamic Data On Build` empties it again on builds and when the editor closes, so the fallback is committed in that empty state. Keep UI text inside the Galmuri atlases' character set (add a character to `UiFonts.Characters` and the subsets, then run Write Fonts); if the fallback shows a diff with glyphs in it, find the text that used a character outside the atlases.
- **Status icons are placeholders**: no status has an icon sprite, so the HUD shows two-letter abbreviations on coloured squares (with a tooltip naming and describing the status since DesignSystem.md's Phase 5) and the world icons are plain coloured pips; every visual is the same tinted ring. Their art waits for DesignSystem.md (its Later), not KnownGaps.md.
- **A world's edge has no walls** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 2: bodies move only along paths, which go around it; the first move that leaves the paths, a knockback or a dash, brings the colliders, on a layer that stops bodies but not line of sight): where a world's ground stops (Testlands' and the Sandbox's outer border), nothing physical stops a body; paths keep players and mobs inside, and the default profile's edge cost keeps routes a cell in from the edge where there is room.
- **Feet slide going north or south** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 2, as Animation.md's Phase 2 chose): a gait's natural speed holds east and west. The 3/4 view shows ground depth at half its length while the world's ground is drawn at full length, so going north or south the planted feet keep up with half the player's speed at any rate, and with about four fifths on the diagonals (accepted in Animation.md's Phase 2).
- **A cast aimed aside runs toward its aim** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7: only split layers could turn the legs, which Animation.md's Phase 5 weighed and left and KnownGaps.md leaves out): during a Continue cast aimed at a unit (Smite) the player faces the aim, so its legs run that way, over the run, while the body moves elsewhere, as the run clip already did; only split layers could turn the legs (Animation.md's Phase 5 notes).
- **The walk and the sprint never show in play yet** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 2: they show once a slow or a haste reaches them, and the PlayMode test covers them): the player moves at 5 units per second, or 3 chilled, both in the run's band; only a slow under 0.44 or a haste over 1.2 would reach them, and no status, terrain or ability does yet. The PlayMode test makes its own.
- **Path followers only react to edits that block their path** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7, with the user: nothing in gameplay edits the terrain yet, only the dev menu, and a shorter route is taken at the owner's next repath): an edit that opens a shorter route is picked up at the owner's next repath.
- **The camera does not snap to the pixel grid, by choice**: `CameraFollow2D.snapToPixelGrid` is off in `Main`, as are the Pixel Perfect Camera's grid and pixel snapping. The camera moves by fractions of an art pixel, so at a whole zoom an art pixel at an edge is now and then drawn a screen pixel narrower or wider than the rest for a frame: in LightingGaps.md's Phase 7 spike, a 1.5 s walk at zoom 3 showed it in 59 of 90 frames, on 0.7 % of the colour runs on average and 1.9 % at most, seen only zoomed in. Snapping the camera alone (tried in terrain blending Phase 1) makes the moving player shake against the screen; snapping it and every sprite keeps each art pixel square but moves the view in whole art-pixel steps, 2 or 3 a frame at a run, which judders; and moving the snapped frame by what is left under an art pixel needs the sprites snapped in their shaders and the final picture shifted after the post-processing. The user kept today's camera (LightingGaps.md's Phase 7).
- **One layer with waves per area** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 6: only the ocean has waves, and a second sea in one area is not planned): a renderer builds its coast map for its first layer whose material has waves, so a second such layer would roll its waves toward the first one's coast.
- **Water blocks only through navigation** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 2, as a world's edge is): it has no collider, so nothing physical stops a body there, and neither do the ocean, lava and the cliff of raised ground. Bodies move only along paths today, which go around them; a knockback or a dash would need a collider on a layer that does not block line of sight.
- **Lava only blocks**: it does no damage yet; standing in or next to it burning a unit is planned in DamageAndStatusEffects.md (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9).
- **A layer beside a lower one of another look shows it under its corners** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 6: a documented painting rule, which the generators keep to): each layer's shape counts the cells of every layer above it, since lower layers are meant to go on under upper ones (a bridge, the planks, excepted: the layers under it count its cells only where they are painted there). Where an upper layer lies beside a lower one instead (mud or a road next to dry grass, with only grass under them), the lower one shows under the upper one's rounded corners where the two meet a third material: a notch in crisp, a spike in soft. Paint the lower layer under the upper one where they meet, or keep a cell between them, as the Lakeside's dry grass does from mud and paths, and its shallow ponds from every other look (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 and Phase 4 notes). The map's edge counts as the third material: where bands that lie over each other run out at it, as the Coast's beach and ocean do at its side edges, the lower band shows under the upper one's rounded corner there, a notch of a pixel or two, and draws its own edge over nothing up to it (the beach's outline along the map's edge).
- **Diagonal paths step from cell to cell** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 6: the crisp look's style, its corner rounding turning the steps into a wavy band): the path layers are filled cell by cell, so a road that wanders north or south climbs in cell steps. The crisp look's corner rounding turns the steps into a wavy band, but a diagonal never runs straight there (the soft look draws straight diagonals). The Clearing generator's `roadMeander` trades variety against steps.
- **Raised ground has one level** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 6: a second level is a feature, not a gap, and one of KnownGaps.md's non-goals): a plateau cannot stand on another; the Terrain Brush paints `Tile_Raised` and `Tile_Ramp` from `Raised_Palette` on an area's `RaisedTilemap` and previews them through the renderer's elevation map.
- **Props darken whole under the sun** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7, as LightingGaps.md's Phase 3 chose: read by column, a crown read its own trunk's shadow beside it): a prop darkens whole or not at all, by the art pixel two pixels sunward of its feet (Lighting.md's Phase 4 notes); read by column, as the units are since LightingGaps.md's Phase 3, a crown read its own trunk's shadow beside it. A unit no longer takes another unit's shadow, since it reads the mask without them.
- **Soft light shadows are off the art grid** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7: no light uses a soft shadow): a soft shadow's penumbra, which no light uses, is drawn as URP draws it and is not on the art grid (Lighting.md's Phase 7).
- **Restoring our shadow shader rewrites URP's settings** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7: `LightShadowResources` restores ours only when an upgrade lost it, and keeping only the shader's line of that diff is the whole cost): a URP upgrade may reset the projected shadow shader in URP's global settings to URP's, and the editor sets ours again on its next load (`LightShadowResources.RestoreIfLost`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 5), but saving the settings from the editor empties their `m_RuntimeSettings` list, so keep only the shader's line of that diff (Lighting.md's Phase 7).
- **Shadow Caster 2Ds make meshes in the editor** (accepted, [KnownGaps.md](KnownGaps.md)'s Phase 7: `ShadowCasterSceneSaving` keeps the meshes out of saved scenes, and the first dusk's 6 to 7 ms frame is URP making the light textures of the batch the casters split off, which no warm-up at the start keeps alive): every caster, a disabled one too, makes itself an empty shadow mesh as it wakes in the editor, and a scene saved with them wrote each as an unreferenced object of its own, so `ShadowCasterSceneSaving` marks them not to be saved first. In a player a prop's caster builds its mesh the first time a burning light wakes it, about 20 µs. The first wake of a session took 12 to 15 ms a frame at the Overworld's gate at dusk while Mono compiled URP's shadow code (Lighting.md's Phase 7), which `LightWarmUp` now runs at the session's start; that first dusk still takes one frame of about 6 to 7 ms, against 4 at most after it, while URP makes the screen-sized light and shadow textures of the batch the props' casters split off, which no warm-up at the start keeps alive until dusk (LightingGaps.md's Phase 5).
- **The editor's night is a preview**: the light props' lights and lava's glow are off as written and only `PropLighting` burns them in play; in the editor the Scene view's Time of Day overlay (`NightPreview`, LightingGaps.md's Phase 6) shows them, the sky, the sun and the reflections at a phase of the day, but without the region skies, the clouds or the props' lamp shadows (a caster woken in the editor writes its mesh's state into its scene), and with the preview off the editor shows them off and water reflecting nothing, as a probe render does unless it sets them. A renderer whose sprite is swapped to one with a glow after it first drew kept drawing without the glow in an edit-mode render test; no prop changes its sprite in play (Lighting.md's Phase 5 notes).
